# OksaSDR — status and roadmap

Updated with every build. The newest TestFlight build is at the top; what is
queued, waiting on someone, or still to verify follows.

## Next build

Next: an Android beta 123 with a preview on the phone first: **219** properly (the app's copy of Qt's device list shows a headset's call and music device both) with **220**, and **216** on Android and Linux. **209** and **213** are in the tree already for Android and Linux (this push's Android run 160 and Linux L44 built them as previews, not published). For the next iOS build: the microphone's permission text (216), and what the devices show of **215**, **216** and the iPad's Space (209). **210** waits for Justin's word.

Build 221 / Android beta 122, released 2026-11-26 to everyone: Android beta 122 at 18:31 (the fourth preview's build, run 159, with the Linux AppImage L39), build 221 to the TestFlight group at 19:02 (external testers after Apple's beta review). Build 221 with Android beta 122, the user's PuJu for every platform on 2026-11-26 at about 16:50, after their check of the fourth Android preview ("Kuulokkeen katkaisusta kaiuttimeen kesti melko pitkään mutta tuli ... 2. pieni katko 3. 4 toimii") and the list of what waited for an iOS build: "Otetaan iosille nuo 209, 213, 215, 216 ja 210 osalta odotetaan Justinin palautetta eli nyt PuJu jossa kaikki alustat". iOS build 221: **209** (Space as PTT can be switched off, and never keys with a screen reader's keys), **213** (the radio's fan speed on the meters), **215** (receive back in the background after an interruption) and **216** (the microphone open the whole time the app is connected and in front, on iOS). Android beta 122 is the fourth preview's build as the user tried it (run 159: **214**, **218**, **221**, **203**), not a new one: 209 and 213 are shared code and reach Android and Linux with the next beta, through a preview on the phone, and 216 there likewise (said to the user at once; no objection). Linux keeps build L39. Not run on a device: 215, and the iOS halves of 209 and 216; the notes say "should". 210 waits for Justin's word.

Android beta 122, prepared and pushed on 2026-11-26 as a PREVIEW for the user's own phone (the marker mobile/PREVIEW is in the tree; the user at about 08:20, after their own try of beta 121 that morning and my proposal to mend 214 and push it as a preview: "push"). Android only: **214**, the headset keeper after its first run on a device (a refused route is a try that failed, a headset off Android's list is looked for, a phone call's route is left alone, no renewing at the front with the link up). The code is under Q_OS_ANDROID, so no iOS build is made and build 216 stays the newest on TestFlight; the Linux AppImage is unchanged too. Compiled for arm64 Android with the release flags and read once more against the Java side; it cannot run here, which is what the preview is for. To everyone only on the user's word after the try (the notes for beta 122 are written). *The second preview, the same day:* the user tried the first one at 10:05 to 10:16 (the results are under 214), said of the delay at leaving the front that it is the fault, and to my question whether its cure goes into this build: "toinen esiversio". So beta 122 also carries **218** (the headset's link kept when the microphone closes: the route asked through an AudioManager of the app's own, the app's call mode taken again before the microphone closes, the quick way back, receive not opened twice). Android only as before; not run on a device, which is what this preview is for. *The third preview, the same day:* the second showed 218 working on the phone (no break when the app left the front, three times) and a new fault: the app stood still for 15 s when the headset was switched off with its media audio on (**221**), besides **219** (Speaker to Bluetooth media stays on the phone's speaker). The user to my proposal of a third preview with 221, 219 and 203's stacks at 8 s: "Tee esiversio". So beta 122 also carries **221** (the calls to Android's audio service waited for only a moment), **219** (Bluetooth media on the headset's music device by its id, and a log line naming where Android plays) and **203** (stacks at 8 s). Not run on a device. *The fourth preview, the same day:* the third showed **221** working on the phone (the headset switched off three times with media audio on: Android's audio service out for 15.1, 4.4 and 13.4 s, the app going on and the radio's connection kept each time) and 219's attempt failing: Qt shows the app one Bluetooth device of a headset only, so the music device could not be opened, and the attempt lost the sound after a headset's power cycle with Bluetooth media chosen. The fourth is the third without that attempt: **214**, **218**, **221** and **203**. 219 and 220 get their own build.

Build 216 / Android beta 121, released 2026-11-26 to everyone (PuJu, the user going to bed on 2026-11-25 at about 23:00, after the evening's summary of the prepared preview, which named what had not run on a device: "Tehdään PuJu sillä lähden nyt nukkumaan"). Its contents were picked by the user that evening while testing build 215 on the OnePlus Open, a Jabra Evolve2 65 and the iPad: **204** ("204 noin ok", their own drawing), **192** a ("192 a muutetaan"; the program name only with the Apple change), **201** (parts 1, 2 and 4: "pitäisi toimia tuossa kuuloketilassa. Niin kuin handsfree-sarjanakin toimii kuulokkeet"), and **181** b, **181** c, **192** the demo radio's name and **207** ("Otetaan kaikki nuo ehdottomasti 4 tähän buildiin mukaan"); **203**'s stall-watch stacks on Android are in it as built. One commit an item. Tested on the FLEX in the render tool and on the desktop as each item says. 201 and 203 are Android code that cannot run here and went out without a run on a device; so before the push both were made careful (201: only a link the app has seen up is mended, five tries, the round after the background only for a headset without media audio; 203: the stacks only when the app has stood 16 s), every Java call of the build was checked by name and signature against the Java side (a wrong one fails only on a device), and both compile for arm64 Android with the release toolchain. Found in that last reading and written into the release: the microphone held open for a headset streams to the radio all the time, as with VOX (up to some 50 MB an hour over mobile data), which the privacy text's microphone section, the manual and the Android notes now say; holding the link without the stream is **208**. For the release: the manual's picture of PTT's places with the row of Wide on its side; the Connect page's picture with the demo radio's new name, from now on rendered from settings of its own (each saved radio by its model, the SmartLink section as its sign-in form), so that the operator's own radio nickname is not in a public picture (it was in the picture published until now; older versions stay in the history).

Build 212 / Android beta 118, released 2026-11-24 to everyone without a preview first (PuJu, the user going to bed: "Kun kaikki kunnossa tee PuJu"; shown the Big buttons change in the morning: "Ehdottomasti tämä korjaus mukaan"), from the user's tests of beta 117 on the OnePlus Open the evening before. **142** PTT with the Controls drawer open at the side (the user, the opened phone with the Log tab and the keyboard up: "PTT:tä pystyy painamaan loginäkymän ollessa päällä vain etukannella. Sisäkannella se ei toimi koskaan ... PTT poistaa loginäkymän"; approved 2026-11-24: "Tee siis kohdat 1 ja 2"). Cause, read in Qt 6.12 and reproduced in the render tool at 751 x 659: a Drawer is modal and closes on a release outside it (QQuickDrawer: setModal(true), CloseOnEscape | CloseOnReleaseOutside; blockInput() blocks every press outside), so a press on the page's PTT, in full view under the side drawer since 2026-10-15 (82e4531c3: "ends above the page's PTT row, which stays usable"), never reached it and closed the drawer, with or without the keyboard. Fix: the side drawer carries a PTT copy as the drawer from the bottom does, a PttButton in a small non-modal popup over the page's button, stacked right above the drawer (it opens when the drawer has opened and only its size follows whether it shows, so a dialog opened from the drawer, Logbooks… / Setup, covers it also after the device is turned); in an own layout only where the drawer does not lie over the PTT block; kept while held. Everything else outside the drawer as before (a tap closes it, nothing tunes). The first plan (the drawer non-modal while the keyboard is up) was dropped: PTT stayed dead without the keyboard and the whole page went live under the drawer. Tested on the FLEX: with the transmit lock on, a 1.4 s hold on PTT arms it with the drawer open at 751 x 659, 1194 x 834, 1366 x 1024, 1024 x 1366 and 1470 x 890 (the copy exactly over the page's button; the drawer and the call field stay, typing goes on, OH2 → OH2TST; a tap outside closes the drawer and sends no tune), 698 x 712 and 834 x 1194 keep the bottom drawer's own PTT; on the air at 1 W (14.335 USB, no microphone in the test machine, so 0.0 W out): four presses keyed the radio and unkeyed on release, by mouse and by a finger, the keyboard up and down, the drawer open and closed; the CW key (14.052, two 0.3 s key-downs at 1 W), the dit paddle (two dits) and tap on / off through the copy as through the page's own button; FT8 running: no copy, the six buttons stay; an own layout: the copy over a PTT block clear of the drawer, none where the drawer covers the block; Setup opened from the drawer covers the copy and a press there keys nothing, also after turning the window from 834 x 1194 to 1194 x 834; the focus sweep with the drawer open (Audio, DSP, TX): no control keeps the keyboard. The radio left as found (14.074 DIGU, 100 W). Not tested here: that the call field keeps the keyboard under a finger (the render tool's synthesized touch reaches the focus filter about (5, 56) px off on this desktop; by mouse it holds, and on the devices the filter's press positions are right, as their logs show). Also in it: **142** with the on-screen keyboard up the page still kept the navigation bar's inset (under the keyboard): a band of about 60 pt between PTT and the keyboard ("musta raita ... ihan turhaa"), and in the opened phone's landscape the Log drawer showed only its tabs ("toisessa suunnassa koko logi ikkunaa ei näy"): no bottom inset while the keyboard is up; on the outer screen in landscape Android opened its keyboard full screen (extract mode: nothing of the app showed, and no keyboard room reached the app): QT_ANDROID_NO_FULLSCREEN_KEYBOARD, which Qt 6.12 turns into IME_FLAG_NO_FULLSCREEN; turned upright without a keyboard the Controls drawer filled the screen, likely from a small rectangle Android reports with its keyboard down: under 120 pt it no longer counts, and each change of the keyboard's room goes to the log with Android's numbers and the safe area. The user also found *Bluetooth media* plays through the headset when its Media audio is on (Mattias SM6YEC's Galaxy stays silent: his log asked for). Tested in the render tool with the inner screen's room less its status bar (751 x 659, 698 x 712, keyboard 316, two bar meters, text 1.15); the full-screen keyboard and the strip only on the device. Added in the night of 2026-11-24 (the user: "ennen pushia katsotaan sitten, mitä meillä on muuta tarjolla tähän buildiin. Eikä lähdetä pelkästään näillä korjauksilla"): the upstream fixes **148**–**154** (cherry-picked with -x: 150 7ff77ce9e, 149 cc345828d, 151 510599743, 148 23b24a306, 152 2212eaf8a, 154 942320a83 and 158082357; 153 ported by hand into MobileEngine::recallMemory) and **161**, Kent Winrich's Big buttons report of the same night. Upstream's own unit tests for them pass in the desktop test build (bandplan_usage_resource_test, wan_udp_keepalive_test, cwx_quote_test, cwx_panel_test, aetherd_meter_decode_test, tx_operation_integration_test, midi_settings_test, radiomodel_spot_status_test; midi_settings_test needed SettingsPaths.cpp in this fork). Tested on the FLEX: **153** with the radio's active slice made to differ from the app's (a second slice, then an injected status): the build before sent `memory apply 1` and `slice tune 0 7.066` and the memory landed on slice B (7.066 LSB, and the transmit slice) while A stayed at 7.074 DIGU; now `slice set 0 active=1` goes first (the radio answers `slice 1 active=0`, `slice 0 active=1`) and only A moves (7.066 LSB, filter −3036 to −28), B stays at 7.0763 DIGU. **149** and **150** on the air at 1 W on 14.052 CW: the straight key (`cw key 1 … client_handle=0x6B8D16B9`, the radio transmitting by SWCW, unkeyed on release) and the CW memory `VVV DE OH6OKSA "A"` (`cwx send` with DEL for the spaces and RS for the quotes, the radio's reply 0, `cwx sent=` 206 to 223, 18 characters; the app's own decoder copied VVV DE OH6OKSA "A). **152**: the strips at 1.8405, 14.105 and 3.610 show ALL. Found there: with Band plan lock on, the new label made the lock turn a DIGU slice to LSB at 1.8405 (FT8's 160 m row) and to USB at 14.105, where it had left the mode alone while the rows read DIGI; an all-mode row whose usage note names digimodes now sets no mode, tried both ways on the FLEX (without the rule DIGU → LSB at 1.8405 and 3.610 and → USB at 14.105; with it DIGU stays at all three, and the lock still sets LSB at 1.845 and USB at 14.150); Region 1's 3.600–3.620 changes with it (it set LSB before). **148** and **151**: a mock Power Genius XL and Tuner Genius XL with the handles 0x9A3B5C7D and 0xF0000002 link and show their readings, and an injected `spot 4242 triggered pan=0x40000000` changes nothing and warns of nothing; the radio's own amplifier meters and a real click from another program could not be made here (their decode is the unit tests'). **154** (demo radio, a copy of the settings): with the file whole, *Connect to it by itself* Off saves and keeps the 11 bindings; with the file cut after the third binding MidiSettings refuses the preference save (its new rule) and the app rewrites the file from the bindings in use, valid again with 3 bindings (a phone user cannot reach the file to restore it); not tried on Android. **161**: at 393 x 852 the ⋯ menu's item in and out, Exit by a tap (the hint) and by a hold; at 1194 x 834 View's button in, the Controls button's drawer and its *Exit Big buttons* out; at 834 x 1194 the ⋯ menu in and out; the hint was bold at first, which pushed the mode button off the phone's row: amber only, the row as before. The radio left as found after each run (14.074 DIGU, 100 W, one slice). Left out: **146** and **147** (see them).

Build 211 / Android beta 117 out the same evening: TestFlight 211 to TestGroup (iOS run 211; the xcode-27 runner came after 10.5 min in the queue), android-beta-117 (Android run 150) with OksaSDR-L34-x86_64.AppImage, beta 111's release removed (tag kept); FB post https://www.facebook.com/groups/1468327561855382/posts/1501531228535015/ (Mattias Åbom, Robin Bishop, Christoph Steinert, Ben Morris tagged); Mattias SM6YEC told by mail. The user's tests of beta 117 on the OnePlus Open: a Bluetooth headset on Auto gets receive back after PTT (also short taps, the microphone held a second), *Bluetooth media* plays when the headset's Media audio is on (Mattias's Galaxy stays silent there: his log asked for), Done logs the call, the PTT row's buttons work, under FT8 they stay. Found, open (to fix in the next build): **142** with the on-screen keyboard up a ~60 pt band under PTT (Android's navigation-bar inset), the inner screen's landscape Log drawer showing only its tabs, the outer screen in landscape showing only the keyboard (Android's full-screen extract keyboard), the drawer filling the inner screen turned upright; and on a tablet layout (the inner screen both ways, very likely the iPad in landscape too) **PTT does nothing while the Controls drawer is open**: the Drawer is modal and closes on a press outside it, so a press on the page's PTT only closes the drawer, the field and the keyboard go (the user: "Sisäkannella se ei toimi koskaan ... PTT poistaa loginäkymän"); fixed in the next build (above) with a PTT of the side drawer's own; the first plan, the drawer non-modal while the keyboard is up, was dropped.

Build 208 / Android beta 117, a preview again (207 / beta 116 reached only the Android artifact: both iOS attempts found no xcode-27 runner): what 207 has, and from the user's tests of beta 116 on the OnePlus Open with a Bluetooth headset, Calls only (2026-11-23 evening): **144** the hands-free fix confirmed in the log (after each PTT "communication device earpiece, hands-free link down", the link up 220-400 ms after the route was taken again, receive reopened on it, also after short taps); a press shorter than Android's 0.1-0.2 s to open the headset's microphone opened it after the release and shut it at once, leaving the headset chirping until a press of a second or more (the user: "nopea tangentti jättää jotain päälle", with Media audio on no chirp): a start whose stop came first no longer opens the microphone (m_micStopSeq); **142** Android's keyboard rectangle comes in device pixels ("keyboard 1413+1071 of 764", ratio 3.25), so no room was made: the opened phone upright had Big buttons' PTT under the keyboard (the user: Done right on both screens, PTT only on the inner one upright, landscape fine); scaled back on Android when its bottom lies past the window, the raw numbers in the Done log line; **155** TGXL relays sent 0 / 1 / 2 (Ben Morris KT4O, FB 2026-11-23 on firmware 1.2.44: "When I press the + on C1 actually changes the L value"; his log: twelve relay steps, the line without the relay), each step now logged with the relays before and 1.5 s after. Tested: the render tool's Done placement unchanged at 412 x 915 (the scaling only on Android with a ratio over 1.05); builds. Not tested: the scaling and the short press on the device (beta 117 preview), the relay change with genius-mock or a real TGXL (Ben, after the release).

Build 207 / Android beta 116, a preview for the user's own devices first (the user, 2026-11-23 afternoon, after testing 206 on their devices: "HF korjaus tähän samaan jos sinulla on siihen ratkaisu"): **141** Back to the call kept on an iPad keyboard and Android: the call field's key is Return with the choice on (EnterKey.type), as Qt on iOS lets a one-line field go after a Return on its default Done key unless the focus moved (QIOSTextResponder) and Android hides its keyboard on IME_ACTION_DONE, whatever logging did (the iPad's log: the field let go 69 ms after "logged", with either choice); **142** Big buttons' Log: Done no longer on the mode button (the placement now counts an item pressed through a TapHandler as a control), and Done's place goes to the engine's log (Engine.logUi; a QML console line never reached the developer link); **144** a headset with only Calls on (Media audio off): after PTT the hands-free route is taken again as at connecting (reassertHeadsetRoute: useHeadset), receive opens again and the link watch is armed, also after a short press; the log line gives the headset's state (communication device, hands-free link, media audio, audio mode), also at connecting. Tested: in the render tool Back to the call on and off by keyboard as in 206 (dry, 1366 x 1024); Done in Big buttons' Log in the call field's end and on the Log tab beside Rcvd at 412 x 915 and 915 x 412 with stand-in keyboards; Platform.java compiled against android.jar. Not tested (devices only): the iPad keyboard's Return, the Android keyboard's Return, a headset with Media audio off and on, a short PTT tap on it, Done on a real Android keyboard in a lower Controls drawer (its log line now tells). Also tested by the user on 206: the run line and names (141) on their devices, the late start (145) on the iPad (DL1TRK tapped 5.11 s in, 4.86 s left out; UA4HBJ logged).

Build 195 (released, below), chosen 2026-11-20 (the user: "1-3 ei muuta"): **12** follow-up (below), **109** POTA and WWFF spots on the display (Robin Bishop G5OUT; SOTA left out until the SOTA Management Team approves, its API terms), and the KPA1500's OPERATE readback in the log (Rich Yost N2RY's build 190 log, the ideas list). Promised: Sergio the power next build, Robin the spots "when it is in a build", Rich the log line. **109**: Controls › View › Park spots, **POTA** and **WWFF** (off by default, MobilePotaSpots / MobileWwffSpots): each list read once a minute while a radio is connected (MobileEnginePota.cpp, api.pota.app/spot/activator and spots.wwff.co/static/spots.json, the app's User-Agent; OKSASDR_POTA_SPOTS_URL / OKSASDR_WWFF_SPOTS_URL for stand-ins), a spot not seen before to the radio as the desktop's PotaClient does (source POTA / WWFF, "<ref> <park> <mode>" as the comment, POTA's expire / WWFF's 30 min from its time as the lifetime, PotaSpotColor #FFFF00 / WwffSpotColor #66FF66), through one queueRadioSpot shared with the cluster (its dedup: a call on the same frequency once a lifetime); an activator's park kept by call, so noteSpotCall (tuneToSpot) gives the Log tab lastSpotSig / lastSpotRef from a park spot or from any spot of a listed activator; the Log tab's spotted-call button fills Their park with the call. The line under the buttons: "Listed now (07:12 UTC): POTA 15, WWFF 2." or why a list was not read. Tested on the FLEX-6600M by the real buttons: the live lists (POTA 13-16, WWFF 1-2 at the time) on the radio; BA4SUA (POTA CN-1601, 14.0249 CW) tapped on 20 m: tuned, CW, the Log tab's call and Their park POTA; stand-in lists: a spot of OH0TEST from another source got POTA OH-0001 from the list, OH0WWF/P (WWFF OHFF-0001, FT8) tuned and filled; rendered at 393x852, 375x667, 1180x820 and --font-px 17; the test spots taken off the radio (spottest clearparks), the slice put back to 14.074 DIGU. Found with it: a spot tapped near the view's edge in DIGU retuned 2 kHz off (the spot's own tap tuned, the view followed the slice, and the display's tap no longer saw the tag under the finger): the display's tap now judges the overlays as they lay at the press. **The KPA1500's OPERATE readback**: a command not shown in the 3 s wait now logs what the readings say ("reads STANDBY, answering"), then "shows operate after 6.2 s, later than the wait" or "still does not show operate 33 s after the command"; tested against kpa1500-mock.py --operate-delay 5 / -1 (new). Docs: the manual (View › Park spots, the Log tab, the KPA500's and KAT500's Power, the experimental note), pictures park-spots (new), kpa500-controls (new), tab-kpa500, kat500-controls; update-manual-screenshots.sh no longer stops at "changes asked: 0" (grep -c under pipefail); README, privacy (the park lists), TestFlight and Android notes (beta 106).

**12** follow-up (found 2026-11-20 at night in Sergio Landoni LU7YS's log of build 194 on his real KPA500 and KAT500; ready in the working tree, not committed): *the KAT500 off* and *the KPA500's and KAT500's power*. The KPA500 worked on his devices (487 W, SWR 1.20, Standby / Operate, band following). The KAT500 answered its identity and firmware (02.16) but every reading stayed "–" and the controls dim: it was off (its own power, PS0), and off it answers only I, RV, SN and PS, which the model then asks every 5 s; the OFF badge showed only once a mode was read, so nothing said why. The log's "stopped answering" / "answers 02.16" every few seconds is the same: the 5 s reads land just past the model's 5 s freshness. Now: OFF in the badge and "The tuner is off" with what turns it on; with Control on a **Power On / Off** row (PS1 / PS0 through the model's setPower, confirmed, not while transmitting, the one command that works with the tuner off; the desktop applet has the same ON/OFF); readings from before it went off not shown (nor on the meters); 3 s more before "stopped answering". Tested against the stand-in (elecraft-mock.py: --kat-off, --delay-ms, kat mute / unmute) by the real buttons: 30-40 s off with 300-600 ms late answers without a flip, Power On → readings in 10 s, Power Off → the readings gone, a silent tuner still "stopped answering" and back; rendered at 1180x820, 393x852, 375x667. Sergio (mail 2026-11-20 00:54, away from the station): "the tuner was powered off! I managed to switch it on through AetherSDR, and that resolved the problem", and asks "Power ON/OFF buttons for both the KPA500 and KAT500 in OksaSDR Mobile, just as we have in AetherSDR on Windows" ("Operate/Standby is useful, of course, but it's not the same as actually powering the equipment on and off"). So also *the KPA500's power* (ready in the working tree the same night): with Control on a **Power On / Off** row on its Amp tab, as the desktop's Kpa500Applet power button and through the same Kpa500Connection::setPower: Off (^ON0;) while it answers as on, On (its bootloader's wake, a lone P; the rear power switch must be on) also while it is silent, as it is off, over the bridge's open link; each confirmed, none while transmitting (the connection's TX state brought up to date first, as it refuses then without a word). Off a KPA500 answers nothing and the connection then asks only ^ON; every 5 s, so silence alone proves nothing (it is "not answering" after 3 s whatever the amplifier did): On is done when a check gets ^ON1 (15 s at most: "the wake needs its rear power switch on and the bridge connected to it"), Off by ^ON0 or by the silence through two checks ("silent after Off, as the KPA500 is when it is off"), or "still answers as on" when a check gets ^ON1. The state line no longer says "Connecting to …" while the link is up and the amplifier silent: "Link to … open, but the amplifier does not answer: it is off (Power › On below wakes it), or the bridge is not connected to it". The stand-in's KPA500 is now silent off also to ^RVM (as the real one), with --kpa-off and kpa rear off / on. Tested by the real buttons against the stand-in (300 ms late answers) and the FLEX at 393x852: off → the link line, On → confirmed → "shows power on", STANDBY, 20m; Off → silent → "silent after Off"; the rear switch off → "has not answered in 15 s", on → On works; the question open, the radio keyed (USB 5 W, no audio, peak 0.0 W): the question closed, On dim, a tap sends nothing; put back DIGU, 100 W. Still to do for a build: the manual's KPA500 and KAT500 text and pictures (Power), the notes.

Build 171 / beta 83, released 2026-11-15 (the user, 2026-11-15: "Nämä kaikki seuraavaan buildiin" for 72 and 73, "Lisää 1, 2 ja 3" for 74, 67 (b) and 75, "Tehdään näin" widening 74, then "Ota mukaan" for two of Christoph Steinert DM5HF's points): **72** the KPA1500 tuned in STANDBY; **73** a MIDI Learn that takes another function's control asks; **74** the receive path to upstream's (audio put back in order, #6886, #6704); **75** Big buttons' Log; **67 (b)** a POTA self-spot; and the radio's voice keyer held off in RADE, a Mac's arrow keys in text fields. '**72**: kpaTune asks ^OS0 first when the amplifier reads OPERATE and keys the carrier only once the readback shows STANDBY (5 s, else "did not go to STANDBY", no carrier); ^OS is read every cycle with ^TP and ^FL, and an OPERATE reading stops the carrier; the result adds "Put it in OPERATE when you are ready." The other amplifiers checked (above). Tested on the FLEX at 10 W (14.345, put back to 14.154 USB) against kpa1500-mock.py --tune-seconds 2: the stand-in got ^OS0 then ^FT, two writes in all; "The amplifier tuned. Put it in OPERATE when you are ready." '**73**: MobileEngineMidi's learnCompleted undoes a learn whose source another function has (the desktop's overlap rule) and asks on the row; tested with ctr2-mock: Slice volume caught the VFO knob's CC 100 (asked, nothing changed), Learn again took CC 102 (mode 1), Replace moved CC 100 (the VFO knob's binding gone), each checked in the saved midi.settings, then put back. '**74**: #6886 and #6704 cherry-picked (clean but for two test-access lines); #6992's audio part by hand in PanadapterStream: VitaRxReorder.h as upstream's, the speaker streams (PCM and Opus) through it before decoding, a 30 ms precise flush timer, one Opus decoder per stream, a stream's window retired with its generation; a minute's line in the log when audio was out of order, concealed or too late. #6700 left out (the radio binds IPv4 only). Unit tests: vita_rx_reorder_test (upstream's), remote_audio_rx_reorder_test rewritten for the new behaviour (a late packet plays in its place; a gap waits 30 ms; later than that dropped), panadapter_late_packet_test and lan_udp_keepalive_test (upstream's) and the receive tests before: all pass. On this PC's path to the FLEX (site-to-site WireGuard over LTE): 3 min uncompressed, 3 min Opus and 2 min Opus played into a test sink: no sequence errors, no underruns, no reordering seen in those minutes (the minute's line stayed silent), so the gain is for paths that reorder (testers' VPNs, Wi-Fi). '**75**: Log beside Step (upright and on its side; in the top row on a low landscape phone, as Step), a log panel in the overlay (the call, the lookup, Sent / Rcvd with − / +, Log, Cancel, PTT taking the rest), "Logged CALL" over the frequency for 4 s; the call is Engine.workedCall, My park the engine's. Rendered at seven sizes (Log first in the top row ran past 360 px with the "Logged" line: moved beside Step, the line over the frequency); a QSO logged dry (W1AW 59 / 57 with the lookup), Esc cancels, the page has the keyboard after both; focus sweeps over the Log, Amp and View tabs, Big buttons and Radio Setup › MIDI: nothing keeps the keyboard. Manual: big-buttons.jpg, big-buttons-log.jpg. '**67 (b)**: MobileEnginePota.cpp, Self-spot by My reference; tested against a stand-in for the endpoint: one POST with the JSON above, User-Agent OksaSDR/<version>, no Origin or Referer; a second within 30 s refused ("Wait 24 s between spots."); the two-fer chooser. '**RADE and the voice keyer** (Christoph, build 170: "DVK does send all macros in SSB although RADE is on"): the radio's voice keyer plays through the radio's own analog chain, so F1-F12 now refuse it while RADE is on, with the reason; the app's own recordings take the microphone's place before the RADE encoder and go as RADE. '**A Mac's arrow keys** (Christoph: the arrows did not move the cursor in the FreeDV Reporter message field): unproven without a log; AppleControlKeys now also maps AppKit's function-key characters (0xF700-0xF703 arrows, Home, End, forward Delete, Page Up / Down) as it maps Backspace, and the keyboard diagnostics log a navigation key's code (never typed text), so his next log tells; on this PC a key with U+F702 moved the call field's cursor (W1AW to W1XAW). Tested on the FLEX for RADE: RADE on (the radio's DVK stood in by a licence status), F1 refused with the reason, RADE off put slice A back to USB 100-2800 Hz.

'**48**: the line "not shown" on the user's iPad and Android (build 169) was its switch: Off on both, never turned on (the user: "En ole kytkenyt päälle ios enkä android"; the Android's View tab over the devlink showed Off, and turned On it drew in 3D and 2D at -121 dBm). Build 166 / beta 78, a fix build (Kurt Hanisch DL5EAG, mail 2026-11-11: beta 77 dropped the network and hung, back on 76; the developer's two Android phones and the devlink log the same morning; build 165 was the branding commit's automatic build, the same app as 164): Big buttons' − / + took their size from their own text, which follows the size the layout gives, so a turn, a fold or any resize started a layout loop ("TuneButton: Binding loop detected for property implicitHeight", "GridLayout called polish() inside updatePolish()") in the overlay, hidden or not: the GUI thread stalled 1-8 s at a time, the ping showed seconds, the radio dropped the client and Android closed the app once. Reproduced on the desktop by resizing (stalls up to 8 s), gone with the fix: the buttons' starting size fixed, the overlay made only while on (a Loader). With it, as the user asked on the phones: a tap on the frequency opens frequency entry; on a phone on its side Step beside Exit, so PTT keeps its height (it was a sliver); Log's Developer link address on its own line on a phone (squeezed unreadable). And over SmartLink the slots-full question came back (the user: "Nythän ei edes tule sitä pyyntöä"): it was asked at once after the refusal while the connection was still closing, so its check for an idle app always failed; now asked when the app is idle, tried on the FLEX with the slots full (my session holding the second). And **58**, Big buttons with the slices, two meters and the main screen's slice features. Seen once and not reproduced since: the Connect page's ColumnLayout in a polish loop after a refused connection (slots full), on beta 77's binary only.

MobileEngineMidi.cpp registers the app's 48 functions under the desktop's ids (the .map table's: freq, tunestep, bands, modes, nr, mainmute as the app's mute, ptt, the paddles...); the VFO knob tunes by the step from its own target while it turns (the desktop's 250 ms); TX functions only with Transmit on, ended when the port goes (watched every 4 s: a Bluetooth controller switched off stays open but leaves the list). To test on devices: pairing and the CTR2 itself (Hanno), USB MIDI on an iPad. ''**52**: the SmartLink sign-in kept through network dropouts. SmartLinkClient tells a passing failure (no reply, a timeout, 408, 429, 5xx: lastAuthFailureTransient) from a refusal, and drops a login or refresh answer that comes after logout (a generation counter); the app keeps the sign-in on a passing failure ("SmartLink server not reachable. Retrying…"), tries again after 5 s doubling to 60 s, at once when the network or the app comes back, and when the server drops under a kept sign-in; a dropped remote session keeps its own 5 s retry; Android's secure store writes and removes with commit(). Tested on this PC: 45 s without a network (unshare -rn): kept, tries at 5, 10, 20 and 40 s, the stored sign-in unchanged; with the network it signs in from it; --smartlink-signout-test (Sign out while the refresh is under way): still signed out 10 s later (the kept sign-in put back from a copy afterwards). Manual: SmartLink paragraph. To test on a phone: airplane mode for a minute, and a sign-out then closing the app at once (Android).

'
'**10 (a)** FTx › DXpedition › Off / Hound / All Hz (FT8 only, this session only): a tap on the Fox (below 1000 Hz, or its CQ; a Hound above refused, a CQ to another continent refused) calls its base call in the odd slot, a TX Hz below 1000 moved to a random 1000-2650 (not saved; FT8's own Hz back when Hound goes off), four calls then a tap again; the Fox's report (standard or combined) sends R+report on the Fox's Hz, repeats 300 Hz off, also within 3 minutes after the calls stopped; its RR73 logs under the full call and halts at once; CQ, Keep CQ and Answer CQ off; Free looks at 1000 Hz and up in the odd slot; off on a band change or FT4; a warning on an FT8 calling frequency. '
'--ft8-test hound / hound,std / hound,late dry on the FLEX (no RF): CQ and a Hound refused, "<KH7Z> OH6OKSA" at a random Hz, R-07 at 360 then 660 Hz, RR73 logged once, the TX Hz setting kept (719). Manual: FT8 step 8 and FTx's Hound and contest paragraphs, ft8-hound.jpg, tab-digi.jpg. On the air 2026-11-10 (receive): KH8A's multi-stream message decoded on 18.095, "IC8AIK RR73; SQ2BXI <...> -12" at 366 Hz, -10 dB; KH8A runs MSHV's multi-answer (its spot: "QSY MSHV for EU"), which callers work in the normal mode, and it went to SSB before the Hound run (15 min at 10 W, its signal not heard again). On the air 2026-11-10 (transmit, 10 W): C8K Mozambique, WSJT-X F/H on 18.096 (two or three streams at 813-941 Hz, "LA9GX RR73; HA8ZB <C8K> +00"): a tap on its combined message called "<C8K> OH6OKSA" at 1319 Hz; the Fox answered "<OH6OKSA> C8K -15" four times (934, 875, 875, 878 Hz), R-05, R-08 and R-13 went each time on its Hz, then 300 Hz off (578 Hz) after it stopped; no RR73. WSJT-X's Fox cannot read a nonstandard call's R report: it queues the call from "<C8K> OH6OKSA" (decoder.f90), but foxRxSequencer finds the Hound by DecodedText::deCallAndGrid, whose word2 is [A-Z0-9/]+, so "C8K <OH6OKSA> R-13" gives an empty call; WSJT-X sends the same R message with a nonstandard call (genStdMsgs, t0b), and a free text "C8K OH6OKSA R-13" is over 13 characters. So a nonstandard call cannot complete a QSO with a WSJT-X Fox, in WSJT-X or here; standard calls are not affected. Done 2026-11-10 (the user: "Tee 1-4"): Hound's status says it when turned on with a nonstandard call without "/" (a compound call is matched by the Fox's compound loop), the manual's FTx Hound list and step 8 and the notes say it (checked on the FLEX with --ft8-test hound, dry, and rendered at 440x956 and 1366x1024: four and three lines); reported to the WSJT-X developers: https://github.com/WSJTX/wsjtx/issues/164 (the same code in master, v3.0.2). The test tool: hound,live first took a Hound's call to the Fox for the Fox ("C8K OH3MMF KP21" at 775 Hz: Hounds on dial 18.095 land below 1000 Hz on 18.096; a tap in the app does the same, as a double-click in WSJT-X), one "<OH3MMF> OH6OKSA" went out before the run was stopped; it now taps only rows the Fox sent. --ft8-test also puts each band's power back (it restored only the start band's: 17 m and 30 m had stayed at 10 and 50 W, put back to 100 W; checked with a dry CQ on 17 m). '
'**53** (first part): each license feature the radio reports goes to the log when it comes and when it changes, and the subscription (RadioModel::licenseFeatureNames, not the radio_id); on the FLEX: "radio license: SmartSDR" and 25 features, e.g. digital_voice_keyer off (plus), auto_agc_t off (ea). Next: Ian's log from his AU-510 / FLEX-8400, then the DVK choice. '
'**The Connect page at a large text size** (Ian Gilchrist VK1HF's build b5): "OksaSDR" shrinks to fit beside the version and the buttons (it ran over the version at 130 %, down to 16 px), and a radio card grows with its text; rendered with OKSASDR_TEXT_SCALE 1.3 / 1.35 and --font-px 21 / 22 at 360x800, 390x844 and 412x915 (it fits), and at 100 % the same pixels as before at 390x844, 1366x1024 and 1024x1366. Still to do: a QSO with a WSJT-X Fox from a standard call (a tester), the devices (30).

Build 153 / beta 66 (the user, 2026-11-04: the crash log "Lisää seuraavaan buildiin"; Juho Kontio's crash and Ben Morris's PGXL / TGXL comment from build 152): **The SmartLink crash at Connect** (Juho: multiFLEX off, AetherSDR at his remote station, build 152 still crashed at Connect; his .ips from build 150 a SIGSEGV from the SmartLink socket): reproduced by a simulated refusal of "client gui" with AddressSanitizer: WanConnection::processLine erased a response callback's map node after the callback, whose refusal had ended the session and cleared the map (heap use after free); the callback now leaves the map before it runs. Upstream main still has this and build 152's destructor bug (upstream not pursued). **No retry after the radio refuses the app** (it only met the same refusal), an error saying why, and **Another station is on the radio** (Main.qml): on the radio's network with multiFLEX off the engine pauses before registering (multiFlexConflictDetected; the app sat connected with nothing), over SmartLink before connecting when the licence allows one station (the desktop's confirmClientSlotAvailability; the FLEX-6600M reports licensed_clients=2) and after a refusal from SmartLink's station list; Disconnect it and connect / Cancel. Tested on the FLEX with the cabin iMac's AetherSDR on it, multiFLEX off and a refusal simulated by local test patches (removed). **The crash log** (LogBuffer): kept lines also to run.log as they come, a crash signal's name and call stack (backtrace / _Unwind_Backtrace, alternate stack, then the system's own handling), the next start's notice and Log › Previous run (Share / Copy); tested with SIGSEGV. **PGXL / TGXL wording** (Ben Morris, iPhone 17 Pro Max, real devices: Control on, but where to Operate?): where the buttons are under Control, the address as the device's own (the reported one in the empty field, "the radio reports none"), "Read directly … (the radio does not report it)", *Use the address the radio reports* / *Clear the address*; the iPad over LTE and WireGuard reached genius-mock.py at an entered address (10.9.0.5 → 192.168.1.232), as a VPN user would. Then (the user: "voidaanko simuloida tilanne jossa radio luulee, että meillä on oikeasti pgxl ja tgxl"): **genius-mock.py --radio** registers the stand-ins with the FLEX as paired devices do (4O3A's PGXL amplifier-to-radio API: a non-GUI client per device, amplifier create, keepalive, ping; no interlock, no meters), and the radio then connects to the devices' own ports and relays: the PGXL gets operate=0/1, the TGXL operate set= / bypass set= and autotune, and with --radio-tune the TGXL's autotune keys the radio's tune carrier (transmit tune 1 / 0) as the real tuner does. Tested from this PC and the user's iPad over the home network, WireGuard on LTE and SmartLink without a VPN: the address filled from the radio, Operate / Standby, Bypass, Tune keying the radio (15 W), the relays, the fan, the remote code asked, entered and kept, a mock restart as a link outage. **4O3A's TGXL protocol description** (upstream discussion #2595, WP4SK; the user: these are on main, read them) checked against all the PGXL / TGXL code by a separate read: relays numbered 1 = C1, 2 = L, 3 = C2 (we sent 0..2: L moved C1, C2 moved L, in builds up to 152 and on the desktop), 3way=1 in info for the 1x3 model (the radio maps it to one_by_three, seen on the FLEX), operate set= / bypass set= and the device's state= / bypass= direct for a tuner the radio does not report, swr of either sign, a port live with a band or a frequency whatever feeds it; left unverified by the documents: antA's base, the PGXL setup write and its authcode, the PGXL sequence past 255, the SO2R model's channel. **Ben's real cause** (his FB update: On took continuous tapping): every telemetry frame rebuilt Radio Setup's device rows (the state maps in the Repeater's model), so a tap landed on a replaced button; a fixed model now, and the Amp tab's relay rows a count. Also: the radio's refusal of a relayed amp / tuner verb shown (FlexBackend's reply sink; a Tune refused with 5000003C did nothing on screen), Radio Setup's device headings and texts (which code, why asked, what works without a direct link), the Forward bars 0-100 W under 100 W, fast polling from the key. **Split by XIT** (Sebastian DG5CST, e-mail: his FLEX-6400M's second slice held by AetherSDR at his remote PC; a Maestro user splits by XIT): Controls › TX › Split, SPLIT then sets the active slice's xit_on / xit_freq (1 kHz CW, 5 kHz voice; hold +1 / +2 / +5, XIT's ±9.999 kHz), read back from another client on the FLEX. **The decoder's text in the Macros panel** (Tyler K0OTC, e-mail: the panel covered the CW decoder). **RIT / XIT steps on their grid** (the user on Android: from 9999 the steps went …9, −1 past zero). Build 150 / beta 63 (the user, 2026-11-03: "Lisää 1-3 buildiin", and "tutkia muita mahdollisia focus ongelmia"): **Macros by PTT** (Tyler Akerman K0OTC, e-mail: the voice keyer at the bottom of the TX tab and the CW memories halfway down the CW tab took scrolling past sliders on a phone): a Macros button beside Controls (phone), before the controls button in a phone's landscape row, and beside PTT (tablet) opens MacrosPanel over the display, the F-keys' mapping: CW memories with the call being worked, the Digi tab's RTTY / PSK macros, the voice keyer; buttons only, not modal, PTT in use, closed by the drawer; checked on phones and tablets in USB, CW and RTTY without sending (Tyler: "That sounds like a perfect solution"). **Tune for the RF2K-S's own tuner** (Jeff Stinger, FB build 149: he tunes by sending a carrier and pressing Tune on the amplifier's screen over VNC, and on one phone the carrier stopped as the app left the screen): RF-KIT's API cannot start a tune (upstream's applet shows the tuner read only; RFC #6712's TUNE needs a community-modified server; the amplifier's TRX tune interface is Icom / Yaesu / Kenwood's; over TCI the RF2K-S takes only the frequency: upstream #2597 read its passive TCI client), so the Amp tab's Tune (Control on, asked first) puts it in STANDBY, keys the radio's TUNE at the TX tab's Tune power (4-39 W, the manual's 7.2) and keeps it with the app away for 20 s at most, stopping once the amplifier reports a tune done, in OPERATE, over 45 W, a link lost, Stop or 20 s. Layout sweep against build 149's: 27 sizes, no layout decision changed, no QML warnings (side by side caught the tablet's PTT row growing into the display, fixed before). nuo kaikki aina roadmapille"). A number stays with its item; a new item

smallest number to the largest ("aina pienimmästä isoimpaan numeroon").

Planned, not started. **From you:** nothing now; pick it by number when it is wanted. **Details:** (Planned: logging)
8. **Digi:** Contest macros with serial numbers **In short:** RTTY / PSK macros that count a contest serial number up by themselves. Planned, not started. **From you:** nothing now; pick it by number when it is wanted. **Details:** (Planned: the Digi tab)
9. **Digi:** More keyboard modes: Olivia, MFSK … **In short:** More keyboard modes beside RTTY and PSK, such as Olivia and MFSK. Planned, not started. **From you:** nothing now; pick it by number when it is wanted. **Details:** (Planned: the Digi tab)
10. **FTx:** Fox / Hound and SuperFox: working a DXpedition as a Hound **In short:** Working a DXpedition's Fox as a Hound in FT8. The classic Hound mode is in the app since build 163. SuperFox Hound (the newer DXpedition signal) is open and large, a build of its own. **From you:** nothing now; pick part (b) by number when a SuperFox DXpedition makes it worth a build. **Details:** (Michael Whaley AF0F 2026-10-29; Gianluca IK6QOO on FB 2026-11-09). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-09* (WSJT-X 2.7 source, the FT8 DXpedition Mode and SuperFox user guides): Hound only; being a Fox is the DXpedition's side (SuperFox needs an NCDXF key) and is not planned. No upstream code to take (the desktop leaves FT8 to WSJT-X) and no upstream issues. (a) **Classic Hound** (*in build 163, in progress*; still used, e.g. 5R8IC Madagascar "FT8 (f/h)" until 2026-12-20 on NG3K's list): ft8_lib does not decode the Fox's combined message (type 0.1, "K1ABC RR73; W9XYZ <KH1/KH7Z> -12", c28 c28 h10 r5; message.c returns "not handled"), so today the app cannot even show it: ~60 lines in our copy and a test with our own packer. A Hound switch in the FTx controls and the rules WSJT-X enforces: transmit only in the second (odd) period; call at 1000-3000 Hz (moved there if below); decode below 1000 Hz unless all are asked for; after the Fox's report, R+rpt sent automatically on the frequency the Fox called on (300-900 Hz), a repeat 300 Hz off; RR73 (alone or in a combined message) stops and logs; the operator confirms calling again every 2 minutes; directed CQs (CQ EU, ...) honoured; MSHV multi-stream messages read as WSJT-X does. Mobile: no heavier than FT8 today; the radio's TX filter must pass 300 Hz. Nonstandard Hound calls (the user's OH6OKSA): the call goes as "<KH7Z> OH6OKSA", WSJT-X's Fox takes it (lib/decoder.f90) and answers by hash, but does not read the R report "KH7Z <OH6OKSA> R-11" (on the air with C8K 2026-11-10: see build 163). Size: medium, one build; tested by decoding a live Fox at the cabin and a low-power QSO. (b) **SuperFox Hound** (WSJT-X 2.7+, the large DXpeditions): the even period carries one 1512 Hz wide signal from 750 Hz to up to 9 Hounds with a 20-bit one-time password; Hounds call with normal FT8 anywhere at 200-3000 Hz, no frequency moves, only after decoding the SuperFox. The decoder is WSJT-X's (GPLv3, as the app): ~800 lines of Fortran to port to C++ (sfrx_sub, qpc_sync, qpc_decode2, sfox_demod, sfox_remove_tone, sfox_unpack, ...) and ~1100 lines of C (the polar code, usable as is); removing FT8 signals first needs FT8 subtraction, which ft8_lib lacks (later). "Verified": the code checked at https://www.9dx.cc (NCDXF's server; the Fox's call, the time and the code, nothing of the user; no terms found, the guide offers it to receiving software): an option, named on the privacy page; or the code shown for a later check. Test audio: no SuperFox recordings published; WSJT-X's sfoxsim needs gfortran (not on this PC) or our own port of the encoder; no SuperFox DXpedition on NG3K's list now. Size: large, its own build after (a). Both: Hound only on the Fox's announced frequency, never the FT8 sub-bands (a warning when Hound is on on a standard FT8 frequency), and off on a band change.

19. **Radio:** Optional VOX mode, the microphone always open (*in build 188*, with this device's microphone) Asked again by Christoph Steinert DM5HF **In short:** VOX with this device's microphone is in since build 188. What is open is an anti-VOX of the app's own, so that the speaker's sound does not key the radio: an idea only. **From you:** nothing now. **Details:** (mail 2026-11-16, Mac with EarPods: "is there a VOX option? In the manual I found it, but not in the app? Would be cool, especially with EarPods"): the VOX in Controls › TX is the radio's own, shown only with the radio's microphone input; a VOX for this device's microphone needs the microphone always open (this item). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-18* (chosen for build 187, the user: "19 VOX laitteen mikille"): the radio's own VOX keys on whatever reaches its microphone input, and with mic input PC that is the audio the client streams (remote_audio_tx): the desktop (MainWindow_Session.cpp) streams the PC microphone all the time while PC is chosen ("remote_audio_tx still exists for VOX/met_in_rx"), so SmartSDR's and the desktop's VOX work with a PC headset; this app opens the device's microphone only while PTT is held (and for the TX tab's Mic meter, held off while VOX is on: "speech would key it"), so the radio hears nothing to key on, and the VOX rows show only with the radio's own inputs. Fix: with mic input PC and the radio's VOX on, the app keeps the device's microphone streaming to the radio (as the mic check does), and the VOX and Delay rows show for PC too; the radio keys and unkeys by its own VOX level and delay (nothing new on the radio's side); the app's own uses of the microphone that are not for the air stop the stream while they run (the voice log's listening, the voice keyer's recording), so they cannot key it; the stream stops when VOX goes off, the input changes, the app disconnects or goes to the background without background audio; a line under VOX says the microphone stays open and a headset is needed (a phone's speaker would key it with the receive audio). iOS shows its microphone indicator while it is open, as it should. Terms: none (the radio's own VOX); the privacy policy's Microphone section says the microphone is open only while PTT is held or a recording runs: it gets "or while VOX is on with this device's microphone". Test: on the FLEX, live at low power on a clear frequency (TX testing wanted): mic PC, VOX on, a recorded voice played into the PC's input keys the radio and the delay drops it; VOX off closes the microphone; the voice log does not key it. Size: small-medium. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-18)*: MobileEngine::updateMicCheck holds the microphone for VOX (with mic input PC, the radio's VOX on, not CW, not in standby, the radio able to transmit; re-checked on the phone settings, the transmit lock and the mode) and the Mic meter's check as before (never with VOX on); usingDeviceMicrophone and voxHoldsMicrophone for the TX tab, whose VOX and Delay rows now show with this device's microphone too (DEXP stays with the radio's inputs), with a line under Delay: the microphone open and a headset needed, or in standby that VOX cannot key until armed; the voice log's and the voice keyer's recordings send nothing to the radio (AudioEngine keeps a recording's audio to itself), and the Android recogniser closes the stream while it listens, as it did the meter's. The manual's TX tab and the privacy policy (the microphone open with VOX, and for the TX tab's meter) say so; the shot plan's phone command reads and sets the radio's phone settings. Tested on the FLEX, live at 10 W on 14.2365 USB (clear, the noise floor) with a virtual PipeWire microphone playing a Morse "TEST DE OH6OKSA" at 800 Hz twice: "mic check: microphone open for VOX", the radio keyed by its VOX on each word ("radio transmitting: yes (this app)") and dropped after its delay (13; Morse's word gaps are longer, so 14 key-ups for two plays; speech holds it), VOX off closed it; with the transmit lock on (SB) and VOX on the microphone stayed closed and the same Morse keyed nothing; frequency (14.155450 USB), power (100 W), VOX off, the lock off and the PC's default input restored. Rendered at 440x956 and the iPad's panel at 1180x820; the manual's tab-tx, fm-tx and tx-lock re-rendered on the FLEX (tx-lock's scroll 550 → 650 for the two rows). Christoph Steinert DM5HF (mail 2026-11-21, build 197 on the Mac and the iPad Pro): "simply great ... works with a good adjustments directly on Mac / iPad without headset"; a wish, "not priority": an *ANT-VOX* (anti-VOX) slider, so the receive audio from the device's own speaker does not key VOX. Rich Yost N2RY (mail 2026-11-21, AM and 10 m FM): "the VOX gets a little junky sometimes". To pre-analyse: the VOX is the radio's (it keys on the microphone audio the app streams), so an anti-VOX would be the app's own (the receive audio's level against the microphone's, or holding back the stream while only the speaker's echo comes in), and what iOS's voice processing (echo cancellation) already removes.

22. **Display:** Upstream FFT Smooth and De-comb (*in build 196 / Android beta 107*, the user 2026-11-20: "22 mukaan"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-20*: two of the desktop's Display panel sliders the app lacks; display only, the audio and the radio untouched. **FFT Smooth** **In short:** Two display sliders from the desktop, FFT Smooth and De-comb. Released in build 196. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (src/gui/FftTraceSmoothing.h, 63 lines, header only): each point the mean of its neighbours ±N logical px (0-16, 0 = Off) in linear power, back to dB, so the noise floor is not pulled down as a dB mean would. **De-comb** (src/gui/FftTraceDecomb.h + core/dsp/SpectralEnvelope.cpp, already in libaethercore with FftwPlannerLock.cpp, FFTW being in the iOS and Android builds): a zoomed-in SSB voice drawn as a comb of its pitch harmonics (100-250 Hz apart) is replaced by its cepstral true envelope over the peaks; one width in Hz (0-600, 0 = Off); over noise the trace stays as it was (clipped at the 20th percentile + 12 dB, blended in only above it); a steep edge rounds over about half the width. The desktop (SpectrumWidget::shapeFftTrace) runs both on the source bins, De-comb first, Smooth's half-width turned from px into bins (px × Hz per px / Hz per bin). The app today: SpectrumItem has only the time average (kTraceAverage 0.35, the desktop's SMOOTH_ALPHA) and the 3D view its row smoothing. Plan: SpectrumItem::onSpectrum keeps m_spectrum (the averaged bins) as it is for the levels, the noise floor and the CW / spot peak finding, and shapes a copy for drawing only (both draw paths, the scene graph's and the software one); the waterfall and the 3D view stay unshaped as on the desktop. Controls › View › Spectrum gets two sliders under Range: "FFT smooth  Off / N px" and "De-comb  Off / N Hz" (step 10 Hz), stored per device (MobileFftSmoothing, MobileFftDecombHz), off by default; the manual's View section gets both. Cost: De-comb is two DCTs of about 2048 points times up to 12 iterations per frame (about 0.15 ms on the desktop, a few times that on a phone at 25 fps); the plan for a new length only tries FFTW's planner lock, so a frame without it draws unshaped. To measure on the iPad and the OnePlus with the devlink log (frame time). Test on the FLEX: an SSB voice zoomed to 3 kHz with De-comb 0 / 150 / 300 Hz, the noise with Smooth 0 / 8 / 16 px, both off gives the trace bit for bit as before, CW peak tuning and the spots unchanged, a pan width change and a zoom. No terms. Size: small to medium. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-20)*: SpectrumItem::shapeTrace (De-comb, then Smooth, on a copy; m_spectrum untouched), MobileEngine fftSmoothing / fftDecombHz (MobileFftSmoothing, MobileFftDecombHz; the limits asserted equal to the desktop's), View › Spectrum "FFT smooth" and "De-comb" under Range, hidden with 3D on. Tested on the FLEX at 1366x1024 by dragging the sliders (a tap on a track does nothing since 101): an 80 m LSB voice at 3.694 zoomed to 5 kHz with De-comb Off / 140 / 280 Hz (the comb drawn as one outline, the noise beside it as it was) and Smooth Off / 8 / 16 px (16 gives the desktop's flat tops of a 33 px box), back to Off; at 393x852 the two sliders fit in the drawer.
23. **Display:** 3D with 768 columns on capable devices (*in build 196 / Android beta 107*, the user 2026-11-20: "23 niin että huomioidaan tehokkaat laitteet"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-20*: the desktop's 3D **In short:** A finer 3D display (768 columns) on devices that can draw it. Released in build 196. **From you:** nothing; the frame rate on your iPad and OnePlus is mine to read from the developer link's log when you next run 3D. **Details:** (DssRenderer) has 768 frequency columns; the app's Spectrum3DItem 512, "to spare a phone's GPU". 768 gives 50 % finer frequency detail, which shows on a wide view (an iPad at 1366 pt draws 512 columns 2.7 pt apart, 768 1.8 pt), hardly on a phone (393-430 pt upright), at about 50 % more mesh (104 rows × 1281 instead of 854 vertices) and texture upload. The shaders take texCols and meshCols as uniforms already; the C++ has kCols / kMeshCols as constants sizing std::array rows. Plan: the arrays sized for 768 and a column count chosen when the 3D view starts: 768 on a tablet or computer (the screen's shorter side 600 logical px or more: iPads, the Mac, Linux, Android tablets and a foldable's inner screen), 512 on a phone. The slow-GPU check (items 94, 99) first steps 768 down to 512 and keeps the 3D view, logged ("3D: 768 columns too slow, 512"), and only at 512 switches the view off as before. The column count in the log at start. Test: the render tool at 1366x1024 and 393x852 against the FLEX (768 / 512 in the log, the picture the same apart from detail), the step-down forced with a test switch, the iPad on the devlink log for frame times. No terms. Size: small. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-20)*: Spectrum3DItem rows sized for 768, m_cols chosen at creation (screen shorter side >= 600 → 768; AETHER_3D_COLS overrides), onGpuTooSlow steps 768 to 512 once (history cleared, the slow check warming up again) before switching the view off; the renderer makes its mesh and height texture again at the new size (buildResources / releaseResources), gets the columns at creation so the first build is right; AETHER_3D_SLOW_AFTER_MS (development) forces the step. Tested on the FLEX with the OpenGL RHI (llvmpipe) at 1366x1024: 768 columns drawn, the forced step at 35 s to 512 with the view kept and drawn again ("3D pipelines ready ... 512 columns"); llvmpipe's frame waits 768 / 512: median 1112 / 953 ms (a CPU renderer, only a relative cost: about +17 %); 393x852 with 512 drawn. To check: the iPad and the OnePlus on the devlink log.

37. **Radio:** Power per mode (*in build 190 / Android beta 102* as Controls › TX › Digital max), Sloan Davis **In short:** RF power remembered per kind of mode, with an upper limit for digital modes (Controls › TX › Digital max). Released in build 190. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (TestFlight, build 156, FLEX Aurora AU-520: low drive gave 140 W on FT8, SSB wants 65-70 %): RF power remembered per mode group (voice, CW, digital), with an optional upper limit for digital modes, set as the mode changes and shown when it does. Parked (the user, 2026-11-08): the radio already remembers the power per band, and within a band the radio's TX profiles (Prof tab) do it, which the user uses; with the set power now in the TX slice's tag (36) a forgotten power shows before a transmission. If it comes back, the clear part is a digital-modes limit (Digital max, off by default) rather than power per mode group. *Chosen for build 190* (the user, 2026-11-19, after "Mitä digtal max tekee": "37 mukaan"). *Done in the dev build (2026-11-19)*: MobileEngineDigitalMax.cpp, Controls › TX › **Digital max** under RF power (− / + in 5s, Off at 0, in the RF power's own unit: W, or % on an Aurora; kept on the device, MobileDigitalMax), a line under it with what it did. When the TX slice is in DIGU, DIGL or RTTY (FT8 / FT4, PSK, RTTY and RADE, which runs in DIGU / DIGL) and the power is above the limit, the power comes down to it; the power before comes back when the slice leaves those modes, only if the power is still the one the limit set (changed meanwhile, it stays); a power raised above the limit there (here or by another client) comes back down; the limit changed while it holds the power takes the power with it, up to the power before (found in the test: + from Off in DIGU put the power to 5 W and it stayed there, and USB then restored nothing, as the restore compared with the new limit). Off leaves the power where it is. Tested on the FLEX (power only, no transmission): in DIGU 100 W, + eight times: 5, 10 … 40 W following; USB: back to 100; DIGU: 40; 60 asked in DIGU: 40; USB: back to 100 (the power before the digital mode); 30 in USB, DIGU and USB: 30 kept; Off. DIGU and 100 W put back.

40. **Fixes:** The on-screen keyboard did not come for a focused text field, Sebastian DG5CST **In short:** On a tester's iPhone the on-screen keyboard did not come up for a text field until the app was started again. Not reproduced here. **From you:** nothing; it waits for the tester's log. **Details:** (e-mail, 2026-11-07, iPhone 15 Pro, iOS 27.0.1, build 157: the CW memory editor and the Log tab on 160 m CW, the field focused with its cursor; after an app restart it came). First guess a hardware keyboard (iOS shows no on-screen keyboard with one; build 158 logs them coming and going), but Sebastian answered (2026-11-07): nothing connected, a plain iPhone 15 Pro; he sends Log › Previous run if it comes again. So likelier the app's side: in Qt 6.12's iOS input context the keyboard comes only when Qt's text responder becomes UIKit's first responder at a focus change (showInputPanel is a no-op, the same focus object skips the reset), so a field that keeps the focus after the responder was lost, or a UIKit refusal (another responder or a presented view holding it), leaves the cursor in the field and no keyboard. Next: on iPhone / iPad too (now only on a Mac) log, when a field is focused or pressed and no keyboard shows within 0.5 s with no hardware keyboard, UIKit's first responder, the key window, a presented view and the app state; then recover as the Mac fix does (the input method updated, the field's focus dropped and taken again, the window activated), each step logged. Then (2026-11-07, 20.04) his screenshot of Log on build 158, a Mac screenshot ("Bildschirmfoto … um 19.03.07", the phone's clock 19:03): the iPhone seen on his Mac, likeliest through iPhone Mirroring, where the Mac's keyboard is the iPhone's and iOS shows no on-screen keyboard (Cmd-K toggles it), and "even" Share › Mail brought none, so iOS-wide, not the app. Asked: iPhone Mirroring? Typing with the Mac's keyboard into the field? The log by Copy or AirDrop to the Mac (build 158's keyboard lines). The diagnostic and recovery above wait for that answer. His log also shows the RF2K-S out of reach: "RfkitConnection: transport error on /info Operation timed out" and Qt's "QIODevice::read … device not open" every 9 s, worth one line per state change. Sebastian (e-mail, 2026-11-08): a plain iPhone, no mirroring, and it happened again: the app's side, so his log is needed (Log › Send to the developer, build 159).
41. **Radio:** Bluetooth PTT for operating with a Bluetooth headset or the car's hands-free (*(a) in build 200 / Android beta 111 as the learned PTT key, 121*; (b) to (d) open, (b) on Android is 128) **In short:** PTT from a Bluetooth button. A button that works as a keyboard key can be taught as the PTT key since build 200. Open: a headset's or a car's play / pause button as PTT (on Android this is 128), buttons with their own SDK (Flic), and PTT from the lock screen. **From you:** nothing now; pick a part by its letter when it is wanted. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-07), in steps: (a) a button that is a Bluetooth keyboard (page-turner pedals and PTT buttons in keyboard mode, Space / Enter) keys TX already with Space while the page has the keyboard; a *PTT key* learned by pressing the button once, so any key it sends serves, also while a drawer is open; (b) the headset's or car's play / pause button as PTT, off by default: iOS MPRemoteCommandCenter (toggle only, press on and press off: iOS gives no release), Android a MediaSession in RadioService (hold where the device sends press and release); with a TX time limit, a beep at TX start and end (the screen not watched), TX ended at once when the Bluetooth device goes, and never keyed by the "play" many car units send on connect; (c) BLE PTT buttons with an open SDK, hold-to-talk: Flic 2 / Flic Duo (official flic2lib for iOS and Android, press and release; a press queued while the button was out of reach, wasQueued, never keys TX; Android adds BLUETOOTH_SCAN) and the programmable button of BlueParrott / Jabra Perform headsets (Apache-2.0 SDK, iOS and Android); (d) later, TX from the lock screen with Apple's PushToTalk framework (an entitlement; the system starts the audio session; BLE accessory events and the system's talk button). Not: buttons whitelisted for Zello only (AINA, Pryme BLE: no public protocol), CallKit. To learn on devices first: what a headset and a car unit send while the Bluetooth microphone (HFP) is open, and whether a remote command reaches the app in the background. Step (a) waits until the user has a Bluetooth button for the tests (2026-11-08). A handset's own PTT key, asked by Rob Metallo VA3MET (mail 2026-11-20 01:51 "Is it possible to add PTT button mapping?", then 19:07 "Was trying on the F400 android 15 and couldn't get the built in ptt to work"; TestFlight: iPad (10th gen), iOS 26.2, build 196; the F400 on the Android beta): rugged and PoC handsets send their side PTT key as a key code (often vendor-specific) or as a vendor broadcast intent rather than as Space, so the learned *PTT key* of step (a) would take the key-code kind; the broadcast kind needs the vendor's intent names. To find out first: the F400's maker and what its key sends (a key-event log line in the app, or the vendor's PoC documentation). Rich Yost N2RY (mail 2026-11-21 01:23, "Is it possible to implement a Bluetooth PTT ? I use AirPods and it would allow me to not touch the screen in a round table env"): step (b) with AirPods; besides MPRemoteCommandCenter, iOS 17's input-mute handler for apps that use the microphone (the AirPods stem press mutes and unmutes, AVAudioApplication) is a candidate to verify on a device. Until then VOX with the AirPods microphone (Controls › TX › VOX) keys hands-free. Rich (mail 2026-11-21 14:24): "it was more of a Bluetooth button device I was thinking of": step (a); a button in keyboard mode that sends Space keys TX today (hold; Tap on / off under PTT for a button that sends a short press only). A reply sent 2026-11-21 says so and asks which button. Rob (mail 2026-11-21 17:56, two photos of About phone): the F400 is a **UNIWA F400** (model UNIWA_F400, Android 15, a MediaTek build); its key "works with" Zello, QSO 1 and "pocket sdr from Ria", so it reaches third-party apps: either as a key code or as a broadcast; Zello documents com.zello.ptt.down / com.zello.ptt.up (and com.zello.intent.buttonExtra1.*) for handset makers (support.zello.com, hardware partner integration). Next step (no build chosen): an Android beta that logs keys the app does not use (key code, scan code, device) and the common PTT broadcasts it receives, then Rob's log after one press; then the key as PTT, off by default, under Transmit from the controller. Reply drafted 2026-11-21. **Rob's log (mail 2026-11-21 21:42, Android beta 110 on the F400):** the PTT key is **KEYCODE_F2 (132), scan code 60, from "mtk-kpd"** (MediaTek's keypad, not a full keyboard), with its press and release (a short press 0.57 s, a long one 2.9 s), so hold-to-talk works; no push-to-talk broadcast came. Today that key is the app's F2: each press played the voice keyer's slot 2 ("dvk playback_start id=2" to his radio, 32 times in all, as the key's auto-repeat started it again and again); slot 2 was empty, so the radio did not transmit (no "transmitting: yes" in the log), but a recording there would have gone on air. Next: (a)'s learned *PTT key* (a key pressed once in Setup becomes PTT, held as long as the key, and no longer acts as F2), off by default, and F-keys' auto-repeat no longer re-triggering the voice keyer.
