48. ~~**Radio:** Auto AGC-T in the app, for every radio~~ *(a) in build 162, (b) in build 169 as Follow* (the user, 2026-11-08: as Thetis has it, so radios without FlexRadio's Early Access licence get it too, as the app's own CW keyer and voice keyer did; roadmap 32 is the radio's licensed one). The radio's AGC knee (agc_threshold 0-100) has no dBm mapping, so unlike Thetis (its AGC runs in the PC and knows the noise floor in dBm) the app finds it from what it hears: the engine already has the desktop's AgcTCalibrator (aethercore, docs/agc-t-calibration-design.md): on a quiet spot it steps AGC-T from high to low (~300 ms a step) and finds the knee where the post-AGC audio level (AudioEngine::levelChanged) starts to drop, with a quiet-spot check (S meter vs the noise floor, mobile NoiseFloorTracker). Planned: (a) ~~an **Auto** button by the AGC-T slider: one sweep of a few seconds, the value set just below the knee, remembered per band and AGC speed and set again on that band~~ *in build 162*; (b) ~~later, Thetis-like tracking: the noise floor followed between sweeps and AGC-T moved by the slope the sweep measured, a new sweep on a band change~~ *in build 169 as Follow* ("Follow the noise: AGC-T moves as the noise rises and falls, from Auto's measurement"; found done when the list was checked against the code 2026-11-25). Must not fight the app's per-mode AGC (voice slow, data / CW fast) or a radio that has its own Auto (32); receive only, tested on the FLEX on several bands. Size: a build of its own.
    *Build 170*: the line's switch also in the AGC panel (Noise line, under Follow) and 2 px in 2D, after the user found nothing in build 169 with the switch Off.
    ~~(b)~~ *in build 169*: View › Noise floor line and AGC › Follow (tested on the FLEX: 20 m USB 56 at -127 dBm, 1.00 dB a step, ANT2 -134 dBm moved it to 63 and ANT1 back to 57; CW 500 / 250 Hz, AGC off, FM, AM and a TUNE carrier held as planned; a flat sweep no longer stays at Measuring 100 %, a build 162 bug). (b) asked again by the user, 2026-11-13: AGC-T set automatically as in Thetis, the noise floor shown on the panadapter as its own line, and a switch to turn it on or off in case a device lacks the power. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-13*: Thetis (MW0LGE, since v2.8.11) has Auto AGC, which keeps the threshold over its noise-floor estimate, and Setup › Display › RX1 › "Show noise floor on panadapter", a dashed line at that estimate (Apache Labs forum threads "Thetis v2.8.11 - UPDATES", "Noise Floor Line - Gone?"). Here the noise floor is already estimated on every spectrum frame for the display's auto range (SpectrumItem.noiseFloorDbm, NoiseFloorTracker with the desktop's transient rejection: a lightning crash does not lift it), so neither part costs a device more than a line drawn and an occasional command: no power switch is needed for that, but two user switches are, as some want AGC-T where they put it: Controls › View › **Noise floor line** (the line with its dBm at the right edge, 2D and 3D) and an **Auto: follow the noise** choice by the AGC-T slider beside today's one-shot Auto. Tracking: after a sweep the app knows the knee at that floor and, from the sweep's curve below the knee, the dB per AGC-T step; when the floor at the slice (the passband's own bins, not the whole pan: the noise slopes across a wide pan) has moved 3 dB or more for 10 s, AGC-T moves by that much over the slope, at most once every few seconds, never while transmitting, and a band or mode-group change sweeps again (or recalls). Off at once when the slider is moved by hand (as Auto now) and whenever the radio's own Auto (32) is on. The radio's 0-100 knee has no dBm mapping, so the line shows the noise floor, not the AGC-T knee (a knee line would be a guess). Tests: on the FLEX across a floor change (80 m at dusk, 40 m at sunrise), the commands counted, the line against the trace in 2D and 3D, a phone and an iPad. Size: medium, a build of its own. *The other modes (the user, 2026-11-13: "48 pitää huomioida miten toimitaan muilla kuin phone modeilla")*: the app's mode groups today are voice (USB, LSB, AM, SAM and also FM, NFM), cw and data (DIGU, DIGL, RTTY, DFM, FDVU, FDVL); each group keeps its own swept value, slope and AGC speed (voice slow, CW and data fast). **CW**: a narrow filter (500 or 250 Hz) lets about 6-10 dB less noise into the passband than 2.9 kHz, so the knee and the slope are the CW group's own, measured with the CW filter; a filter width change of more than half sweeps again (or recalls the width's value); the passband floor is read from the filter's own bins (a 250 Hz filter is only a few bins: averaged over a longer time), and a keyed signal in the passband holds the tracking (the quiet check as the sweep's). **Data**: FT8 / FT4 put many signals in one 3 kHz passband, so the passband floor is the lowest bins' (the quantile the display floor uses), not the mean; the tracking moves AGC-T only between transmit periods and never within 2 s after TX; RTTY, PSK and FreeDV / RADE (on DIGU / DIGL) are one signal in the passband: the same rules as CW. FlexRadio advises AGC off or slow for multi-signal digital modes: with AGC off the slider is agc_off_level (a fixed gain, no knee), so neither the sweep nor the tracking runs there. **AM / SAM**: the carrier always sits in the passband, so the passband floor is read beside the carrier (the bins outside the AM filter on both sides) and the quiet check refuses a sweep on a station (it already does). **FM / NFM / DFM**: the radio has no AGC in FM (the desktop hides the AGC panel there, docs/agc-t-calibration-design.md): Auto and Auto: follow the noise are off and dimmed with a note, and FM moves out of the voice group into its own (no AGC); today's Auto button still offers a sweep in FM, fixed with this. **Every mode**: the noise floor line is display only and drawn in every mode; the tracking is held while the slice transmits, while the radio's own Auto (32) is on, and in AGC off. Tests on the FLEX: SSB 80 / 40 m, CW 500 and 250 Hz on a quiet spot and beside a keyed signal, FT8 on 20 m through TX periods, AM on a broadcast station, FM on 10 m (Auto dimmed).
49. ~~**Fixes:** The Macros button's repeat mark an empty box on Android (the user's OnePlus screenshot, beta 74, 2026-11-08: ⟳ U+27F3 is in none of the fonts Android draws)~~ *in build 162 / Android beta 75*: ↻ (U+21BB, in the bundled Noto Sans Math subset's arrows); docs-check (the pre-push hook) now refuses a QML symbol between U+2190 and U+2BFF outside the bundled fonts' ranges (checked: ⟳ put back is refused).
50. **Radio:** A KiwiSDR receiver as a receive antenna **In short:** Using a KiwiSDR web receiver as a receive antenna. Looked into: possible but large, and upstream's own version has an open audio fault. Verdict so far: not now. **From you:** nothing; say so if it should be planned all the same. **Details:** (Wayne Roth WA2N, mail 2026-11-08/09: "Scrape KiwiSDR as antenna from AetherSDR? Can Claude do that?"). Numbered 50 overnight without a pre-analysis, made an idea again by the user 2026-11-10 ("Kiwisdr numeroimattomaksi"), and 50 again since 2026-11-25, when every idea and proposal took a number. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-09*: possible but large. The engine code is in our tree (src/core KiwiSdrClient, KiwiSdrProtocol, KiwiSdrManager, KiwiPublicDirectory, KiwiSdrCredentialStore), but the desktop's glue is big (MainWindow_KiwiSdr.cpp 2427 lines, KiwiSdrApplet 356, KiwiPublicReceiverPicker 279) and the app would need its own: Kiwi receivers in the RX antenna chooser, a slice whose audio and waterfall come from the Kiwi (12 kHz audio, its own waterfall beside the radio's pan), the band and frequency following the slice. The public receiver list comes only from upstream's mirror (KiwiPublicDirectory kDirectoryUrl https://cdn.aethersdr.com/kiwi.json, made at the KiwiSDR maintainer's request, no fallback to kiwisdr.com), and each operator's ext_api policy applies. Upstream open: RFC #3613, #4993 (buzzing audio on selecting a Kiwi since 26.8.2), #5450 (the directory mirror), #7072, #7073. Verdict: not now; worth a number once upstream's audio issue is fixed and the mirror's terms for other clients are clear.
51. ~~**FTx:** The FT8 / FT4 contest and DXpedition messages shown in the decode list~~ *in build 163* (the user, 2026-11-09, with 10 (a)). ft8_lib decodes only the standard, nonstandard-call, free text and telemetry types and drops the rest ("not handled"), so the Fox's combined messages and a contest weekend's exchanges never show. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-09*: the types and their bits are WSJT-X's (lib/77bit/77bit.txt, packjt77.f90 unpack77): 0.1 DXpedition ("K1ABC RR73; W9XYZ <KH1/KH7Z> -12"), 0.3 / 0.4 ARRL Field Day ("WA9XYZ KA1ABC R 16A EMA"), 3 ARRL RTTY Roundup ("TU; W9XYZ K1ABC R 579 MA", serial or state / province), 5 EU VHF contest ("<PA3XYZ> <G4ABC/P> R 590003 IO91NP"); 0.2 and 0.6+ are undefined or WSPR only. Decoded in our copy of ft8_lib (~150 lines and WSJT-X's section and state tables, GPLv3 as the app), the message parser taught "TU;" and the combined form, so worked-before, colours and PSK Reporter read the right calls. Tested with a bit packer for each type through the decoder, and WSJT-X's sample recording from the 2018 FT8 Roundup (its 2019 FT4 samples are from before FT4's release and decode in nothing). Display only: answering in a contest is not part of it. No terms, no mobile limits. Size: small, with 10 (a) in build 163.
52. ~~**Fixes:** The SmartLink sign-in kept through network dropouts, and a sign-out that sticks~~ (*in build 164*; Ian Gilchrist VK1HF, mail 2026-11-09, from his own Android build on a FLEX-8400 and an Aurora AU-510 over SmartLink; his code GPLv3, offered as a branch to cherry-pick). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-10*: confirmed in our code: SmartLinkClient emits authFailed for any refresh reply error, a timeout or no network included (src/core/backends/flex/SmartLinkClient.cpp:155-159), and the app's authFailed handler then removes the kept sign-in (mobile/src/MobileEngine.cpp:1517), so a phone losing the network loses its SmartLink sign-in; Android's secure store writes and removes with apply() (mobile/android/src/fi/oh6oksa/oksasdr/Platform.java:73, 96), so a sign-out may not be saved if the app is closed at once. Ian also found a token refresh or login answer arriving after Sign out signing back in. Fix (his approach): only a real refusal (Auth0 4xx other than 408 / 429, or the server rejecting the session) forgets the sign-in; no network, a timeout, rate limiting or a 5xx keep it, with "not reachable, trying again", a backoff (5 s doubling to 60 s) and a retry when the network or the app comes back; a generation counter drops answers after Sign out; commit() for the secure store. Size: small-medium; tested by cutting the network on a phone (airplane mode) and with Sign out during a refresh.
53. ~~**Fixes:** The voice keyer on the radio's DVK without SmartSDR+~~ *done in builds 163 and 167* (closed 2026-11-16, the user: "eikös meillä ole jo oma voice keyr ilman lisenssiä?": the app's own voice keyer clips need no licence; the radio's DVK is used only when the radio reports digital_voice_keyer licensed, and every licence feature is in the log; a manual choice of the radio's DVK was never asked for) (Ian Gilchrist VK1HF: his radios used the radio's DVK instead of the app's clips, no SmartSDR+ licence). *Second part in build 167* (the user, 2026-11-11): the radio's DVK only once the radio reports digital_voice_keyer on: radioFeatureLicensed() is false while the radio has not said, so a radio without SmartSDR+, or one that never says, keeps the app's own clips, which always work (the desktop fails open, #4210, having no clips of its own). Since build 163 the app subscribes to the license status, and the FLEX here says "digital_voice_keyer off (plus)": the app's clips, as before. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-10*: radioFeatureLicensed() takes a feature the radio has not reported as licensed ("older firmware never says", mobile/src/MobileEngine.cpp:3039-3050), and dvkOnRadio() then picks the radio's DVK until it refuses. To learn first what his AU-510 / FLEX-8400 report: the app does not log the licence features today (MobileEngine.cpp logs only SmartLink's licensed clients), so a log of his could not tell (found 2026-11-10 from his logs); each feature the radio reports goes to the log (name, enabled, reason), then ask him for a log; then: the radio's DVK only once the radio says the feature is on, or a choice (his build: a setting, the app's clips by default). Size: small.
54. **Radio:** Bluetooth MIDI controllers as a VFO knob and buttons (*iOS in build 164*; *Android in beta 93*): Lynovation's CTR2-MIDI / CTR2-Dial **In short:** Bluetooth MIDI controllers (the CTR2-MIDI and others) as a VFO knob and buttons. Released: iOS in build 164, Android in beta 93. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Bluetooth LE and USB MIDI; used with SmartSDR for iOS / macOS), Hanno Vogels DK3HV, mail 2026-11-09; also Michael Walker, mail 2026-11-10 ("integrate the CRT series of controllers", with his idea of a screen for driving); numbered by the user 2026-11-10 ("Tee 1-4"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-09* (Lynovation's CTR2-MIDI manuals v3.00.01, our engine): (0) **No app change may be needed at home:** CTR2-MIDI firmware 2.00+ has a Flex mode that joins the radio over WiFi as a non-GUI client bound to the GUI client ("works with any Flex GUI client"; same LAN as the radio, no SmartLink); the app registers as a GUI client with an id ("client gui <id>"), so it should bind: Hanno to try it first. (1) **MIDI mode in the app** (needed over SmartLink or VPN): the engine already has the desktop's MidiControlManager (RtMidi 6.0, Learn, the relative VFO decoder, CW gates) and MidiSettings, built into the iOS engine with CoreMIDI; Android builds RtMidi as a dummy (RtMidi's AMIDI backend needs API 29, our minimum is 28). The CTR2 sends the knob as CC 100 "WheelA" (64 plus a speed offset), buttons as notes 1-6 and long presses 11-16; Lynovation ships an AetherSDR map for the desktop's import (their file, not bundled). To build: the app's own function list (VFO, step, band, mode, filter, MOX, CW paddles into the app's keyer), a MIDI page in Radio Setup (device, Learn, the map import), Bluetooth pairing (iOS CABTMIDICentralViewController and a Bluetooth permission text; USB MIDI on an iPad needs none; on a Mac Audio MIDI Setup pairs; Android a BLE scan, MidiManager.openBluetoothDevice and BLUETOOTH_SCAN), the privacy page. TX from MIDI (PTT, CW) off by default and ended when the device goes. Upstream open: #4402 (0/127 encoders), #3927 (VFO sensitivity), #743 (VFO step by notes), #6684, none blocking. No terms (MIDI is a standard). Test: no MIDI device here (a USB MIDI keyboard would do on an iPad or Android); Hanno has the CTR2; an iOS network MIDI session from this PC as a stand-in. Size: medium for iOS (one build), Android a second step. Hanno tried Flex mode 2026-11-10: it did not work in his network (a Fritz 7580, the radio on LAN, the CTR2 on WiFi), and WiFi never reaches a remote station; he uses the CTR2 over Bluetooth with another iPad app, very stable. Verdict: MIDI mode, iOS first. Michael Walker's CTR2 Mini worked with it, no issues (FB, 2026-11-12).
55. ~~**Display:** Big buttons, a screen of a few large controls for operating in the car, with gloves or at a glance~~ (*in build 164*; Michael Walker, mail 2026-11-10: "a mobile screen that only had a few buttons on it like up and down at the step rate so you could tap them easily without having to look at the phone", PTT about a third of an iPhone's screen at the bottom; the user, 2026-11-10: "voidaan miettiä"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-10*: one QML page over the operating screen, no new engine calls: the frequency and mode large, the S meter as a bar, **−** and **+** filling the middle (tuneBySteps by the step shown on them, held to repeat, a haptic tap each step), the step changed by a tap on it (stepList), PTT across the bottom third (setPtt, hold as the app's PTT; the app's rules: voice modes, the transmit lock), the screen kept awake; out by a long press on a small Exit, so a stray tap does not leave it. In View › Big buttons and the ⋯ menu. Portrait and landscape, phones and tablets. With the Bluetooth PTT (41) and the CTR2's knob (54) the eyes can stay off the screen. Terms: App Store guideline 1.4.5 ("Apps should not urge customers to ... use their devices in a way that risks physical harm"), so it is named and described as big buttons (the car mounted and used as local law allows, gloves, the field, low vision), never as for use while driving; CarPlay and Android Auto take only their own app categories (an Apple entitlement; ham radio is not one), so it is the phone's own screen. Size: small-medium, one build; tested by the user's path on every form factor (render), hold-to-repeat tuning and PTT on the FLEX (USB, no audio).

57. **Branding:** The app icons made again from the vector master **In short:** The app's icons made again from the vector master. Released in build 167. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-11: "Kirjaa", after the SVG master came in b8f30dd). *In build 167* (the user, 2026-11-11: "59, 53, 57 mukaan"): resources/branding/source/make-icons.sh makes the set (the mark trimmed from a 4096 px render, then placed by the measured sizes). Checked against the old set: every size the same, iOS and Play RGB without alpha, the Android foreground and round icons with alpha; the mark within 1-2 px of the old place, now centred; outside Android's 66 dp safe circle 537 px of the arrow's tip (the old set 8681), outside the 72 dp mask 238 (4265); side by side and a difference picture: the same mark with clean edges. On the devices after install: to do. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-11*: resources/branding/source/oksasdr-symbol.svg (traced from the 1024 px raster, two gradient paths, the white traces knock-outs) draws the same mark as the raster (side by side and a difference picture), with clean edges where the raster has compression noise and softness at 1024 px (the App Store's 1024 icon, the iPad's 167 and 152, Android's xxxhdpi). Its frame is tight: the mark takes about 93 % of the width and the upper arrow's tip touches the top edge, so `rsvg-convert -w 1024 -h 1024` alone is not an icon master: iOS's rounded corners could cut the right arrow, and Android's adaptive foreground needs the mark inside the safe zone. The set made again with today's margins, measured from the files: iOS the mark at 80 % on white, RGB without alpha (AppIcon-1024 and the 12 sizes Icon-20 … Icon-180, the Mac's too); Android the foreground's mark at 248 of 432 px (57 % of the 108 dp canvas, inside the 66 dp safe zone) over the white background layer, the legacy ic_launcher and ic_launcher_round at five densities, and the Play listing's play_store_512; made by a script beside the sources (rsvg-convert, magick), so later sizes come out the same. Under about 64 px the fine traces still run together, as in the raster: a simplified small mark is design work for later, not this item; also later, made possible by the vector: Android 13's themed icon (a one-colour monochrome layer) and iOS 18's dark and tinted icons. No terms beyond the stores' icon rules (iOS no alpha, Android the adaptive layers); no code. Test: old and new side by side at every size, the size and alpha checks, and on the devices after install (the iPad and iPhone home screens, the OnePlus launcher's mask). Size: small.
58. ~~**Display:** Big buttons with more to read and the main screen's features~~ (the user, 2026-11-11: "Mahtuisiko ... exit ja mode sanan väliin slice tieto tehoineen sekä palkkimittareita 2 kpl päällekkäin joissa valittavissa mitä ne näyttävät", then "Kaikki samaan 166:een"; *in build 166*). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-11*: the slice chip's TX tag with the set power (SliceBar), the meter readings and their chooser (MeterView) and the power slider and slice menu exist; shared, not copied ("vaikka näkymät muuttuvat niin ominaisuuksien pitää säilyä"): SlicePowerPopup and SliceMenu serve the main screen's chips and Big buttons'. Found on the way: the main screen's tap handlers took taps through Big buttons (a tap on a meter opened the mode list): the page's controls are off under it. Tested on the FLEX: one, two and three slices, the tag's slider and the hold menu on both screens, a 5 W tune on 28.500 with Power and SWR live. Size: small-medium.

59. **FTx:** A DX working several stations at once without Hound: its RR73 inside its message to the next ends and logs the QSO **In short:** A DX that works several stations at once without Hound mode sends its RR73 inside its message to the next station; the app now takes that as the end of the contact and logs it. Released in build 167. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Ben Morris KT4O, mail with his log 2026-11-11, build 166, iPad: KH8A on 30 m sent "RC3KK RR73; KT4O <KH8A> -16", which gave the report, then "KT4O RR73; JK1TMP <KH8A> -16", which the sequencer skipped, as it read the DXpedition form's RR73 only with Hound on; the app sent R-07 again until he pressed Stop and nothing was logged; the user, 2026-11-11: "59, 53, 57 mukaan"; *in build 167*). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-11*: WSJT-X 2.7 (widgets/mainwindow.cpp, readFromStdout and processMessage) takes a five-word message whose first word is its own base call in the normal mode too, as a "dual Fox style message, possibly from MSHV": it logs the QSO and goes to Tx6, no 73. The same here: in handleDigiDecodes, outside Hound, a DXpedition message with our call as its RR73's from the DX being worked (its hashed call known and the same base call, or not known) logs and ends the QSO (digiFinishQso: back to CQ with Keep CQ, as after a 73). No terms. Test: --ft8-test multi, dry on the FLEX: CQ KH8A answered, the report from "RC3KK RR73; OH6OKSA <KH8A> -16" (R-08 next), another DX's "OH6OKSA RR73; W9XYZ <KH1/KH7Z> -11" left alone, "OH6OKSA RR73; JK1TMP <KH8A> -16" -> "RR73 from KH8A: QSO logged" (a dry run keeps it out of the log), idle, no 73. Size: small.
60. **Fixes:** What a MIDI controller's VFO knob sends, in the log **In short:** A tester's MIDI knob tuned down whichever way it was turned. The log now shows what a knob sends, and a knob that sends a position instead of turns is named in Radio Setup › MIDI and not tuned with. Released in builds 167 and 168. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Hanno Vogels DK3HV, mail 2026-11-11, build 166, iPad Air 5, CTR2-MIDI over Bluetooth: "When I turn clockwise OR counterclockwise… frequency goes always downwards in selected steps"; the user, 2026-11-11, chosen with 59; *in build 167*). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-11*: the decoder (src/core/MidiRelativeCcDecoder.h) and midiTune take the CTR2's documented 64 ± speed (65 / 63) right both ways, through ctr2-mock too; down both ways fits a knob whose values decode negative either way (a Learn that caught a CC with a constant value, or 127 / 63, for example), so the cure waits for what his CTR2 sends. Asked by mail (Last message clockwise and counter-clockwise; Learn or Import a map…). Meanwhile the log names the VFO knob's binding when the port opens and, for each turn (1.5 s without its messages starts the next), its first six messages (CC, value, channel) and the first six steps they gave, so a log from Log › Send to the developer answers it. No terms. Test: ctr2-mock on this PC, +3, −3, +5×3 and −5×3 detents at a 1000 Hz step: "CC 100 = 65" with +2 and +1 steps, "63" −1, "67" +3, "61" −3, the slice back at 14.074000. Size: small. *Second part* (the user, 2026-11-11: "Otetaan nämä seuraavaan buildiin"; *in build 168*; the mark read "in progress" until 2026-11-25, when the code was checked: both parts are in since 5d19905d7), *pre-analysis 2026-11-11* (his pictures by mail: the VFO knob learned as Ch 1 CC #104; the CTR2-MIDI MIDI Mode Manual v3.00.01, Knob Mode Indications and the map defaults): a short press of the CTR2's knob steps its four knob modes, Home CC 100, Mode 1 CC 102, Mode 2 CC 104, Mode 3 CC 106 (push and turn the odd ones); Map 1 makes only CC 100 WheelA, every other knob function SliderA, a position 0-127 kept over power cycles. Learned for the VFO it is marked relative, the decoder locks on passing 63 / 65 (or 1 / 127), and a position below 64 then decodes negative whichever way it turns: his symptom. Told by mail the same day: long-press the knob back to Home (yellow LEDs off) and Learn the VFO knob again (CC 100). To build: a VFO knob that sends a position (consecutive values stepping by one, not centred on 64) named on Radio Setup › MIDI and in the log, with the CTR2's Home mode (CC 100) as the hint; not tuned by the position's change (a slider stops at 0 and 127, and a fast wheel could pass for one). Test: ctr2-mock with a slider mode. No terms. Size: small.

61. **Devices:** Elecraft KPA1500 on the Amp tab and the meters, read only first **In short:** The Elecraft KPA1500 amplifier on the Amp tab and the meters. Released from build 168 on and verified by a tester on his amplifier. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Rich Yost N2RY, tester request 2026-11-11, FLEX-6500: "What I would like to test: ... KPA1500 amplifier"; the user, 2026-11-11: "Otetaan nämä seuraavaan buildiin"; *in build 168*; the mark read "in progress" until 2026-11-25, when the code was checked: both parts are in since 5d19905d7). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-11* (Elecraft's KPA1500 Programming Reference V3, ftp.elecraft.com, a public vendor document; upstream #4097 and docs/architecture/kpa1500-amplifier-design.md; our engine): upstream's Kpa1500Protocol (framing, the status decode, tests) and Kpa1500Connection (TCP, a 500 ms poll, control and a bounded ^TX key nothing calls) are in src/core, unused by the app. The reference: a TCP server on port 1500 (^CP moves it) and a UDP server on the same port, "a single TCP client and any number of UDP clients may be concurrently active"; the amplifier never sends by itself; over UDP one command a packet, at most one answer. So the app reads it over UDP: Elecraft's own software (KPA1500 Utility / Remote) or a logger keeps the one TCP connection, and a lost answer only leaves a reading a tick old. Read: ^PWF / ^PWR / ^SW (tenths) / ^PWI (the drive) / ^VI (PA volts in tenths, amps) / ^TM / ^BN / ^FL (the fault table's text) / ^OS / ^AN / ^AM / ^AI (and the reference's ^AT0 typo) / ^TP / ^ON (main supplies) / ^RV; not the serial number. A reader of its own (mobile/src/Kpa1500Reader) that can build nothing but queries, paced one packet at a time. Control (the user, 2026-11-11, the same build: "kuten tehtiin rfkit"): off by default, Radio Setup › Amp › Control the amplifier opens the amplifier's TCP connection (upstream's Kpa1500Connection with its own poll off: commands only); ^OS0 / ^OS1, ^AN1 / ^AN2, ^AMI / ^AMB (the ATU MODE, as holding the front panel's button), ^FLC, ^FT / ^FE; each confirmed on the page, one at a time, never while the radio transmits, done when the UDP readback shows it (the amplifier answers no SET; 3 s, else said so). Operate's question names the radio's power (the owner's manual: 50-60 W for 1500 W, fault 60 "PWR INPUT" at 65 W). Tune: the manual's full search "Apply 10 to 50 W", in STBY or OPER (the PA bypassed while tuning): ^FT, then the radio's TUNE carrier at the TX tab's Tune power (10-50 W, else refused), ^TP and ^FL asked every cycle; done once ^TP reads 0 and a fresh ^FL shows no fault (a no match is fault 92 at the same moment), else ended on a fault, over 55 W, a lost link, Stop or 20 s, with ^FE. A control link the amplifier closes at once (its one TCP connection held by another program) shows as not connected and is tried every 5 s. Not done: keying over the network (^TX / ^RX, firmware 3.07: parallel with KEY IN, 5 ms lead before RF, the hot-switching question upstream left open); the amplifier stays on its KEY IN cable (Elecraft's Flex guide: TX1 to KEY IN, a KXUSB CAT cable in the radio's USB port for band data). App: Radio Setup › Amp › Elecraft (address, read on / off, control on / off, state, forget), the Amp tab's KPA1500 page (power, reflected, SWR, drive, temperature, voltage, current, band, antenna, ATU, operate, fault; with control Mode, Antenna, ATU, Tune, Clear fault), the meters' Amp readings (temperature red from 100 °C: it faults at 112), the logged power in OPERATE. Over SmartLink: no (a device on the operator's network: same network or VPN). Test: mobile/tools/kpa1500-mock.py (UDP and TCP, the reference's reply formats, acting on commands as the amplifier: keyed / fault / standby / supplies off / tune / no match scenes) with the FLEX connected; done on this PC: readings, fault, supplies off, silence and back, logged power (USB 5 W no audio), Operate / ANT2 / ATU bypass / clear (and a temperature fault not cleared), Tune keyed at 10 W on 14.345 USB (tuned; no match; Stop; 5 W refused), the TCP connection held; Rich on his amplifier. No terms (the reference and manual are public). Size: medium-large.

62. **Connect:** Two SmartLink accounts with favourite radios that connect in one tap (*(0)+(a)+(c) in build 178 / beta 93*; (b) later if at all) **In short:** Several SmartLink accounts on one device, switched with a tap, and favourite radios that connect in one tap. Released in build 178. Open is part (b), both accounts signed in at the same moment with one merged list: uncertain and large. **From you:** nothing; (b) only if someone asks for it. **Details:** (Ian Gilchrist VK1HF's own build, mail 2026-11-09; the user, 2026-11-11: "Tutki myös tuo 2 smartlink tiliä onko mahdollista"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-11*: possible with no new sign-in or protocol work. Today one SmartLinkClient (src/core/backends/flex) signs in with Auth0's password grant, keeps the refresh token in the secure store under one key (smartlink_refresh_token) and the e-mail in AppSettings (SmartLinkEmail, written by SmartLinkClient itself); each client has its own TLS session to smartlink.flexradio.com, nothing global but those two keys (and the desktop's keychain entry, not built on iOS / Android). The radio list and connect go through the account's live session (connect by serial, then the WAN link). Saved radios today are by address or name only (MobileSavedRadios). Steps: (0) small: the client's own e-mail / keychain writes made optional, a disconnect that keeps the sign-in; (a) medium: an account list (e-mail, label; device-bound, not in exported setups), one secure-store key per account (the old key migrated), Add account, a switcher, Sign out per account, a refused sign-in's own e-mail filled in; (c) small-medium on (a): SmartLink favourites in the saved list (account, serial, name): a tap switches account if needed, waits for its radio list and connects by serial; (b) medium-large, later if at all: both accounts signed in at once with one merged list (two background TLS sessions on iOS, and whether the server takes two registrations from one device, unverified). Privacy page: each account's sign-in in the secure store, the e-mails and favourites on the device (also today's e-mail, not mentioned yet). Tests: two real FlexRadio accounts (a second without a radio is enough for sign-in and switching), restart, airplane mode, one signed out and the other kept, a favourite from a cold start, switching while connected (refused or disconnected first), SmartLink bursts, background / resume. Size: (0)+(a)+(c) one medium build.

63. ~~**Fixes:** Pair a Bluetooth MIDI controller… crashes on iOS 27~~ *in build 169* (Apple DTS's workaround; to test on iOS 27 devices in a tester preview) (Rich Yost N2RY, mail with his log 2026-11-12, build 168, iPad Pro 13 M4, iOS 27.0.1: "clicked MIDI tab, selected MIDI device dropdown, app crashed"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-12*: the log's previous run ends in SIGABRT from an Objective-C exception raised in a layout pass inside CoreAudioKit, no "MIDI: opened" before it, so the tap was Radio Setup › MIDI › Pair a Bluetooth MIDI controller… (ios/MidiPairing.mm). A known iOS 27 bug (Apple Developer Forums thread 850584, FB25301268): -[CABTMIDICentralViewController loadView] makes its table view with a NaN height and CALayer raises CALayerInvalidGeometry; it crashes with UIKit's present as well, on iPhone (27.0) and iPad Pro (27.0.1), iOS 26.6.2 works; one user writes 27.2 may have fixed it, Apple has not said. Apple DTS's workaround, confirmed on an iPad Pro with 27.0.1: on iOS 27 give the controller its table view before it is shown (tableView = a grouped UITableView of frame zero) and call its viewDidLoad (without that no devices are listed). Fix: that in showBluetoothMidiPairing under @available(iOS 27, *), non-ARC (autoreleased). Test: on an iPad and an iPhone with iOS 27 (the user's devices, a tester preview), the sheet opens, lists a Bluetooth MIDI device (or none) and Done closes it; on iOS 26 unchanged. Size: small. Marvin Turner W0MET (tester request 2026-11-12) wants a CTR2 on an iPad Pro 13 too.

64. ~~**Devices:** The KPA1500's tune seen to its end~~ *in build 169* (^FT; on the control link ends it, a log line a second; tested with the stand-in) (Rich Yost N2RY, the same log: on the real amplifier the UDP reading and the TCP control link came up at once; a tune at 13 W ran its carrier for 20 s and ended "No tune seen in 20 s"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-12*: the app only watches ^TP over UDP, in turn with the other queries (in practice about every 0.5 s while transmitting), so a full search that is over sooner (the stored settings are tried first) is never seen; the reference's own end signal goes unread: "When the tune completes or is cancelled, the KPA1500 responds with ^FT;" on the TCP link that sent it (Kpa1500Connection parses it but the app does not listen there). It is just as possible that the amplifier never tuned (no RF from the radio at its input, the ATU in bypass); the log has no readings to tell. To build: the ^FT; reply on the control link ends the tune (then ^FL read: 92 no match, otherwise tuned with the SWR read while the carrier is still on), ^TP asked first and every cycle while the tune runs, and one log line a second during a tune (^TP, forward, drive, SWR, ^FL, OPERATE / STANDBY, ATU) so a report tells what the amplifier did. The stand-in (kpa1500-mock.py) answers ^FT; at the end of its tune as the amplifier does, with a --tune-seconds 0.3 case. Ask Rich (back home on Sunday 2026-11-15) what the amplifier showed (ATU IN flashing, the relays) and for a log with Control on. Size: small.

65. **Display:** A compact portrait layout, Larry Averbeck W9FLA **In short:** A second, compact layout for a phone held upright, as a tester drew it: the frequency at the top between the meters, slice letters under it, the values as short texts in three columns. It would be offered beside today's layout, which stays the default. Pre-analysed, not built. **From you:** nothing now; it is a layout, so pictures come first and you agree them before any code. Pick it by number when it is wanted. **Details:** (FB comment on the build 168 post, 2026-11-12: "a complete standalone display option ... while keeping the current setup as the default"; the user, 2026-11-12: a number now, the details changed from Larry's answer). His layout: the frequency centred at the top with the meters either side; under it the slice letters A, B… (grey free, white in use, red the TX slice; a circle round a locked one, a bar through a split one; one tap selects, two lock, three split); the mode and step in the slice flag ("USB - S 500"); the values in three columns, receive (ANT, gain, BW, TNF, DSP), audio (AGC-T, squelch, speaker, record, play) and transmit (tune power, power, TX BW, TX antenna), two lines each of short text (A1 G0 B2.7 AGT35 SQ100); a tap steps a value down, a double tap up, a hold opens its panel, or a tap opens a small − / + that closes 5 s after the last tap; the band row over the step buttons. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-12*: one app, not a version of its own (App Store guideline 4.3 and Google Play's repetitive-content rule; builds, listings, notes, manual and screenshots would all double, and only the main page differs), and no layout question at each start: Controls › View › Portrait layout, Standard (the default) or Compact, remembered. A new AppSettings key, so App setups (17) carries it with no work (a setup is every key that is not device-bound): a "Mobile" setup can be Compact and a "Shack" one Standard. Only a phone held upright: PhoneOperatePage's portrait branch (landscape, width > height × 1.3, and portrait of 480 dp and up keep theirs); the iPad's OperatePage unchanged. Every value is already an Engine property (antenna, RF gain, filter, AGC-T, DSP, TNF, volume, squelch, record, play, RF and tune power, TX filter, TX antenna, the slices, TX slice, lock, split); the work is QML: the slice letters A–D (a tap selects, a tap on a free letter opens a slice there so + Slice goes, a hold opens a menu: Make TX slice and Close slice as in SliceMenu, lock, split; the selected slice blue, the TX slice a red letter, as selected and transmitting are not always one), value chips of 47 × 36 with the value inside (a tap opens − / + beside it, closing 5 s after the last tap; a hold the full chooser a tap opens today; no tap down / double tap up, as every tap would wait for a second and a missed double tap steps the wrong way), AGC-T with receive, the mode and the step as two taps in the slice flag. A mockup (a Design canvas: today, Larry's as written, the proposal to try, its states) sent to Larry on Messenger 2026-11-12 with four pictures, asking his view. Costs: the spectrum 46 px shorter than today at 390 × 844 (B/S still frees 92 px); every later UI change checked and pictured in both portrait layouts (render tool, manual). Open: seven chips a row with Android's larger font or screen zoom (Larry has a Galaxy S26 Ultra) and on a 375-point iPhone; VoiceOver / TalkBack names and the hold as an accessibility action; an iPad mini upright. Size: medium-large.

66. ~~**Display:** The passband runs off the edge of the view as tuning follows it~~ *in build 169* (the passband's far edge kept inside; tested on the FLEX at 1366x1024 and 390x844) (seen 2026-11-12 in the demo video's takes: the operated slice only partly on the display; the user: "Tuo muuten on bugi jos toimii noin"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-12*: MobileEngine::tuneTo, as the view follows the slice, keeps the carrier kPanFollowEdge (a tenth of the span) inside the edge, but an SSB passband lies on one side of the carrier (USB above, LSB below; 2.9 kHz with the user's phone filter, DIGU / DIGL 3 kHz), so at narrow spans it runs past the edge: at 10 kHz the margin is 1 kHz and an LSB passband tuned downwards shows only 1 of its 2.9 kHz (a USB one tuned upwards likewise on the right). The paths through tuneTo: dragging the spectrum (PanadapterView), the step buttons and keys, the MIDI VFO knob (midiTune), CW and RTTY tuning onto a signal (short moves) and DevLink; a typed frequency, a memory, a band, a spot and the FreeDV list are centred already (42). The demo video's render tool used tuneTo for its freq command until a94c1a6b2 (now tuneToCentred). Fix: keep the passband's far edge (the carrier plus the filter's low or high edge) the tenth inside instead of the carrier, and centre the passband when it is wider than the view less both margins; CW and AM (passband about the carrier) unchanged. Test on the FLEX: spans 5, 10 and 50 kHz, LSB down and USB up by drag, held step buttons and the MIDI knob (ctr2-mock), DIGU / DIGL and CW, rendered at 1366 × 1024 and 390 × 844 with the slice at the edge; wide spans as before. No terms. Size: small.

67. **Logging:** A spot from the Log tab to the DX cluster (*(a) in build 170*: Log tab › Spot; *(b) in build 171*: POTA self-spot), Christoph Steinert DM5HF **In short:** A spot sent from the Log tab to the DX cluster, and a POTA self-spot. Released in builds 170 and 171. A real POTA self-spot has not been sent yet: that is for a real activation. **From you:** nothing; or your word (or a tester's try) for one real self-spot during an activation. **Details:** (mail 2026-11-14, build 169, iPad Pro 13 M4, portable POTA with an Aurora: "adding spotting in the log tab for dx spots would be helpful"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-14*: the app already keeps a DX cluster connection (Controls › View › DX cluster, MobileEngine::setupDxCluster, logged in with the user's call), and DxClusterClient::sendCommand sends a line on it; the desktop forwards a spot the same way (MainWindow_Wiring.cpp: "DX <kHz> <call> <remark>", a default remark because DX Spider drops a spot without one). Glue: a Spot button in the Log tab beside Log, with the call and the slice frequency already there and a short comment field; a confirmation showing the exact line before it goes out (a public post under the user's call); dimmed with the reason when the cluster is not connected; the spot also arrives back on the display. Terms: cluster etiquette only (real stations heard, own call). Christoph's answer (mail 2026-11-15): "normal spotting would be good - POTA self spotting superb - if possible". So in steps: (a) the DX cluster spot above; (b) a POTA self-spot from My park (pota.app's spot API, as HAMRS and Ham2K PoLo post), its own pre-analysis first (the API's terms and whether it wants a key). Size: (a) small. *(b) chosen for build 171* (the user, 2026-11-15: "Lisää 1, 2 ja 3"). *Pre-analysis of (b) 2026-11-15*: POTA's spot API is unofficial and undocumented (it may change without notice), and wants no key or sign-in: a self-spot is a JSON POST to https://api.pota.app/spot/ with activator, spotter (both the own call), frequency (kHz as a string), reference (one park), mode, comments and source; OpenHamClock (server/routes/pota-spot.js: its own User-Agent, source "OpenHamClock", POTA's answer shown as it is, 30 s between spots) and hunterlog (src/programs/apis/pota.py) post so, the WRL app likewise; upstream's src/core/PotaClient (in our tree) only reads spots. Here: a Self-spot button by My reference on the Log tab when My park is POTA (and the radio's callsign set): asked first with exactly what goes out ("OH6OKSA at US-1234, 14285.0 kHz SSB, comment", a public post), sent with "OksaSDR/<version>" as the User-Agent and source "OksaSDR" (no borrowed Origin or Referer), POTA's answer under the row, one spot per 30 s. The mode by the slice: USB / LSB SSB, CW, AM, FM, DIGU / DIGL with FTx on FT8 or FT4, RTTY, else DATA. A two-fer (US-1234,US-5678 in My reference) asks which park, one spot each. My park moves from the Log tab's own state to the engine (this session, as now), so Big buttons' log (75) and the spot read the same. Privacy page: the spot's call, park and frequency go to pota.app only on the tap. Terms: POTA's own rules allow self-spotting; the API being unofficial, nothing automatic (no re-spot timer). Test: a stand-in for the endpoint on this PC (the URL settable in the dev build only), the dialog and answers (200, an error text, no network); one real self-spot only during a real activation, with the user's word or by a tester (Christoph). Size: (b) small.

68. ~~**Radio:** The voice keyer F1-F12 and the CW memories M1-M12 on a MIDI controller's buttons~~ *in build 170* (Radio Setup › MIDI › Macros: F1-F12, Stop), Christoph Steinert DM5HF (mail 2026-11-15, CTR2-MIDI: "it would be great to push a 'real' button on the CTR2 to play a DVK F-file"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: Radio Setup › MIDI › Functions (MobileEngineMidi.cpp) has no macro functions yet; the engine's dvkPlay(slot) and sendCwMemory(i) are what the keyboard's F-keys call (OperateKeys.qml), so a learned button calls the same (voice modes the voice keyer, CW the memories, RTTY / PSK the Digi macros, as the F-keys). 72. **Devices:** The KPA1500's tune carrier off as soon as the amplifier says it is done (*in build 171*, the user, 2026-11-15: "Nämä kaikki seuraavaan buildiin") **In short:** The KPA1500's tune carrier is taken off as soon as the amplifier says it has tuned. Released in build 171. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Rich Yost N2RY, mail with his log 2026-11-15, build 170, iPhone, iOS 27.0.1: two tunes ended right, "The amplifier tuned (SWR 1.10)" and "(SWR 1.20)", but the second's reading after ^FT was "out 777 W, drive 13 W", the first's 773 W; the user, 2026-11-15: "Kirjaa ehdotukset roadmappiin"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: after ^FT (or ^TP back to 0) checkKpaTune keeps the radio's TUNE carrier on for kKpaTuneSettleMs (700 ms) so that a fresh ^FL can show a no match (fault 92) and the SWR is read "while the carrier is still on" (64's design). The amplifier bypasses its PA only while it tunes; in OPERATE the PA is back in line once the tune is over, so the 13 W carrier goes out at about 775 W for those 0.7 s, every tune. No harm at SWR 1.1-1.2, but full power was never asked for. In STANDBY nothing changes. Even off at ^FT the carrier would meet the PA for the radio's own delay (tens to hundreds of ms over SmartLink or a VPN), so the fix is the RF2K-S's way: an amplifier in OPERATE is put in STANDBY first (^OS0, the readback awaited, 5 s at most; the manual's full search works in STBY with the PA bypassed), then the tune as now; and as the RF2K-S says, "Put it in OPERATE when you are ready". *As built (build 171)*: in STANDBY the carrier after ^FT is the tune drive into the ATU, not amplified, so the 0.7 s for a fresh ^FL and the tuned SWR stays (the SWR is worth showing); the carrier stops at once if the amplifier reads OPERATE during the tune (^OS asked every cycle with ^TP and ^FL), and an amplifier already in STANDBY is tuned as before. The 20 s limit, a fault during the search and Stop unchanged. *The other amplifiers checked* (the user, 2026-11-15: "huomaa tarkistaa myös tuo KPA ongelma muiden amplifierien osalta"): the RF2K-S (MobileEngineAmp.cpp) asks for STANDBY before the carrier and waits for it, ends when the tuner leaves AutoTuning, and stops the carrier if the amplifier reads OPERATE: never amplified. The Tuner Genius XL: the app only sends autotune (and its abort); the tuner keys the radio through its interlock cable and handles its amplifier's PTT itself, so the app holds no carrier. The Power Genius XL has no tune of its own in the app. The radio's own TUNE button with any amplifier keyed from RCA TX1-3 or ACC TX: Radio Setup's Inhibit during TUNE (this device; off by default, as the desktop's) keeps the chosen outputs open while TUNE or the ATU transmits; the KPA1500 tune goes through the same TUNE, so with its KEY IN output inhibited it was never keyed (Rich's was not). No other change needed. No terms. Test: kpa1500-mock.py made to act as the amplifier after a tune in OPERATE (forward = drive × its gain from the next reading after ^FT), with --tune-seconds 0.3 and a no match: no reading above the drive after ^FT, the result right; a live tune at 10 W on the FLEX with the stand-in; Rich on his amplifier (a log: the tune lines after ^FT show the radio not tuning). Size: small.

73. **Radio:** A MIDI Learn that takes a control another function has, said so instead of silently taking it over (*in build 171*) **In short:** When MIDI Learn catches a control that another function already has, the app says so and asks, instead of silently taking it over. Released in build 171. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Rich Yost N2RY, the same log, CTR2-Max: Learn Slice volume took the VFO knob's CC 100 first, so the knob changed the volume, audio_level 51 to 46, instead of tuning, until he learned Slice volume again on CC 105 and 102; the user, 2026-11-15: "Kirjaa ehdotukset roadmappiin"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: MidiControlManager's dispatch index (src/core) is keyed on the source (channel, type, number) without the function, so of two bindings on one source the last one learned answers and the older stops without a sign. The desktop's typed binding form asks first ("Source Already Bound", MidiMappingDialog.cpp, the overlap rule: the same type and number, the channels equal or either Any), its comment noting that Learn "rarely produces this"; Rich's log shows a learn doing it, as a knob moved by accident or one sending more than one thing (a press with a turn) is learned first. Fix in the app's learn (MobileEngineMidi.cpp's learnCompleted, Radio Setup › MIDI): when the learned source is another function's by the same overlap rule, the row says so ("CC #100 is the VFO knob's") with Replace (the other function unbound) and Learn again (the old binding kept, the learn waits for another control); nothing changes until one is tapped. Import a map… keeps its own rule (a map's later row wins), said in its result if it shadowed any. Inline in the row, not a popup (the keyboard stays with the page). No terms. Test: ctr2-mock on this PC: the VFO knob on CC 100, Learn Slice volume, turn the VFO knob: named; Learn again, then another knob: both work; Replace: the knob sets the volume and the VFO knob row shows none; every layout of Radio Setup › MIDI rendered (phone upright, iPad, the narrowest) with the longer row. Size: small.

74. **Audio:** Receive audio packets put back in order before decoding, upstream's fix for #6051 **In short:** Receive audio packets that arrive out of order are put back in order before they are played (upstream's fix), so mobile networks and VPNs give fewer drop-outs. Released in build 171. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (PR #6992, rfoust, merged 2026-11-15), *widened* to bring the receive path to upstream's (the user, 2026-11-15: "Tehdään näin": #6886 late FFT and waterfall packets no longer drop the frame in progress and pan teardown no longer races the decoder; #6704 a 1-byte datagram to the radio's VITA port 4993 every 5 s keeps a stateful firewall's return path open; #6700 IPv6 binding left out, as its own text says the radio binds IPv4 only; #6855 / #6874 desktop seam refactors left out) (*in build 171*, the user, 2026-11-15: "Lisää 1, 2 ja 3"; the developer's own radio is reached over LTE and WireGuard, where packets arrive out of order). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: today PanadapterStream (src/core/backends/flex) counts loss net of reordering (VitaSequenceTracker) but decodes each audio packet as it comes: a packet that overtakes another makes the decoder conceal the gap, and the late one is dropped (its slot concealed already), so a reordering path sounds like loss. Upstream's VitaRxReorder.h (229 lines, header only) holds a bounded window per stream: in-order packets pass at once, a missing successor is waited for at most 30 ms (bounded by one 16-count sequence cycle and a byte limit), then real loss goes to the existing capped concealment; Opus packets are ordered by their timestamps (a backward clock step over 60 s restarts, as FlexLib), PCM by the 4-bit count with the documented limit (13-15 lost packets followed by a bunched recovery can be discarded uncounted). Display packets (FFT, waterfall, meters) stay immediate. Our PanadapterStream has drifted from upstream's (about 540 lines: upstream's receive-telemetry migration #6874 and the display late-frame lookback are not here), so the PR does not apply; a hand port of the audio part only: VitaRxReorder with the Ordered and Opus timestamp keys (the display-frame key needs the lookback we lack, and display stays immediate anyway), the speaker streams' PCM and Opus through it before decoding, a 30 ms flush timer on the stream's thread, a stream's window cleared on its removal and on format or route changes, one Opus decoder per stream as upstream (today one shared). Upstream's tests/vita_rx_reorder_test.cpp (332 lines) ported for the kept parts and run in the desktop build. Latency: none in order; at most 30 ms only at a gap. Not expected to explain Barry Cohen's 30 s drops (an idea above, waiting for his log). No terms (GPLv3, our engine). Test: the unit tests; on the FLEX over the developer's WireGuard / LTE path and the LAN, PCM and Opus (SmartLink), with the receive statistics in the log (reordered, lost, concealed) before and after over the same minutes, by ear on a phone and an iPad; background / resume and a stream change (a slice closed and opened) for a stuck window. Size: medium.

75. **Display:** Big buttons: a Log button to a big-text log entry, the call and the report **In short:** Big buttons has a Log button to a big-text entry of the call and the report. Released in build 171. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Michael Walker, mail 2026-11-15: "Can you have a button to move to a LOG screen where you just type in the callsign and maybe the signal report?") (*in build 171*, the user, 2026-11-15: "Lisää 1, 2 ja 3"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15*: BigButtonsView.qml (over the operating page, not a popup) has Exit, the slice chips, the mode, the frequency, two meters, − / +, Step and PTT; the Log tab (LogControls.qml) logs with Engine.logQso({call, rstSent, rstRcvd, comment, timeOn, sig, sigInfo, mySig, mySigInfo}). To build: a Log button in Big buttons' top row beside the mode; it turns the overlay's lower part into a log entry in the same overlay: the call in a large field (readingPx), RST sent and received prefilled by mode from Engine.qsoDefaults() (as the Log tab: 59 / 599) as big − / + steppers rather than typing, Log (the call shown on it) and Cancel; the time on taken when the call is first typed, My park from the engine (moved there with 67 (b)), so an activator's QSOs carry the park; a logged QSO shown for a moment, then back to the buttons. The on-screen keyboard: on a phone upright the field and Log sit above it (about the upper half), on its side in one row at the top; with a hardware keyboard Return logs and Esc cancels. After Log or Cancel the page takes the keyboard back (Space PTT, the focussweep), as every text field must. PTT stays reachable (the overlay keeps it at the bottom while the keyboard is down). Test: rendered at the seven Big buttons sizes against build 170 (no empty space, nothing under the keyboard), the focussweep, a test QSO logged by the controls and removed from the test log, Return / Esc on a hardware keyboard, the iPhone and Android keyboards on the devices. Size: small-medium.
