97. **Distribution:** Asking money in the App Store and Google Play, or a voluntary tip in the app **In short:** Asking money for the app in the stores, or a voluntary tip inside the app. Looked into: it depends on the licence (the app is GPL like the AetherSDR engine), and that question was put to upstream on 2026-11-17 with no answer yet. Not chosen for a build. **From you:** nothing until upstream answers; then a decision on a tip jar or a price. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-17: "tällä ohjelmistolla mahdollista pyytää rahaa play kaupassa ja apple storessa", then "Kirjaa"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-17* (not legal advice): (a) The licence: OksaSDR is GPL-3 like AetherSDR, with GPL parts inside (WDSP, FFTW, FlexRadio's smartsdr-dsp among them). The GPL allows a price, but the buyer gets the source (the repository is public), anyone may build and give the app away (our own APK on GitHub already is free), and no further restrictions may be added: a price buys convenience and support, not exclusivity. (b) Apple: the FSF holds the App Store's terms (DRM, device limits) to be further restrictions the GPL forbids; this already applies to the TestFlight distribution, priced or not. Any copyright holder (AetherSDR's contributors, the other GPL parts' authors) could ask Apple to remove the app, as one did with VLC in 2011; a price adds visibility and a motive. The safe way: the upstream maintainers' consent (an App Store exception) before any paid listing (we no longer send upstream PRs, see upstream-not-pursued, but asking is separate). (c) Google Play: its terms do not clash with the GPL; a paid app there is the simpler case. (d) The stores' payment rules: digital features and tips only through the store's own billing (Apple In-App Purchase, Google Play Billing), 15 % commission for small developers; no PayPal donation link inside the iOS app (the Android rule on external donation links to be checked). The stores handle VAT as the seller; the income is the user's taxable income (as a person or through his company); Apple needs the Paid Applications agreement, bank and tax details in App Store Connect (the user's, never entered by me). (e) Store releases first: TestFlight purchases are sandbox only (no real money) and the APK is on GitHub, not Play: real payments need a public App Store release (full App Review, which needs a demo mode for a reviewer without a radio) and a Google Play listing. (f) FlexRadio: the app signs in to SmartLink with FlexRadio's sign-in; commercial use is worth asking FlexRadio first. Some features (Auto AGC-T, our own voice keyer) are SmartSDR+ licence features on the radio, so selling them in an app could strain the good relations (Mike VA3MW welcomed product posts in FlexRadio Enthusiasts). *Asked 2026-11-17* (the user: "Kysytään GPL päätös upstreamistä", then "Tee 1"): a comment on upstream discussion #6409, Jeremy KK7GWY (ten9876) mentioned, whether the project objects to OksaSDR (GPLv3, source public, free) in the App Store (https://github.com/aethersdr/AetherSDR/discussions/6409#discussioncomment-19690807); no answer yet. Recommendation: keep the app free and add a voluntary tip through the stores' own billing (an iOS tip jar with StoreKit, consumable IAPs of a few sizes; on Android Play Billing once the app is on Play), which changes nothing in the licence and limits no one; for a real price, ask the upstream maintainers and FlexRadio first. Size: the tip jar small-medium per platform (StoreKit 2 in an .mm bridge, non-ARC; Play Billing in OksaActivity), the store releases themselves larger (App Review, Play listing, privacy forms). Not chosen for a build.
98. **Distribution:** The App Store and Google Play releases **In short:** Releasing the app in the App Store and Google Play, beyond TestFlight and the beta APK. Much is in hand (the accounts, signing, screenshots tooling); it waits for the licence answer of 97 and for forms only the account's owner can fill. **From you:** when the licence question is settled: the forms in App Store Connect (the App Review contact, App Privacy, age rating, the DSA trader status, the price) and your word to submit. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-17: "Mitä tarvittaisiin, että saadaan nyt molempiin kauppoihin julkaisuun ohjelmisto", then "Valmistellaan apple store julkaisua"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-17*: (a) Apple, in hand: the developer account, the app record and signing, TestFlight's external review passing, the purpose strings, ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption false, mobile/PRIVACY.md, the built-in demo radio (AetherSDR Demo) for a reviewer without a radio. Needed: the GPL decision (97 b), the listing texts, 6.9" iPhone and 13" iPad screenshots, App Privacy (Data Not Collected), age rating, the DSA status (not a trader: free, a private person), the review notes with a public video of the app on a real radio, then App Review (1-3 days); every store update is reviewed, so TestFlight stays for the builds between. (b) Google Play: a Play Console account and identity check (the user's), a closed test of at least 12 testers for 14 days before production (new personal accounts), an AAB instead of the APK, under 200 MB a download (the APK is 210 MB: the Vosk model to an asset pack or a first-use download), the target API to Play's current level (35 now), REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS removed or justified, the mediaPlayback foreground service declared with a video, the listing (512 px icon, 1024 x 500 feature graphic, phone and tablet shots), Data safety, IARC rating; Play App Signing with the existing release key so GitHub and Play builds update each other (the key exported by the user); the package name fi.oh6oksa.oksasdr stays. Google's developer verification for apps outside Play (from 2026 on, country by country) would cover the GitHub APK later too. Size: Apple small-medium (texts, shots, forms), Google larger and at least three weeks (the closed test). *Started 2026-11-17 (Apple)*: the listing texts and the App Privacy / age rating / DSA answers in mobile/appstore/ (README.md, en-US/*.txt); the purpose strings name OksaSDR (three said AetherSDR); PRIVACY.md covers the App Store, points the logbook settings to Radio Setup › Logging (95) and lists the logging program (WSJT-X UDP, 89). The screenshots: mobile/tools/appstore-screenshots.sh (5 iPhone, 4 iPad, rendered on the FLEX; made again with --build N at submission). In App Store Connect (2026-11-17, mobile/tools/appstore_sync.py): version 1.0's texts, the subtitle and privacy URL, App Review's notes and the demo video v5 (720p) as an App Review attachment, Apple only (the user's choice over a GitHub release); the store name OksaSDR (was "OksaSDR Mobile"; the user: "OksaSDR"). Open: upstream's answer to the GPL question (97 b), the user's forms (App Review contact, App Privacy, age rating, DSA, price), the version string and the build (the next one, with the purpose strings), the screenshots uploaded, then the submission.
99. **Fixes:** SmartLink on an iPad or iPhone over a mobile network that closes the radio's connection: the radio connection over TLS 1.3 with Apple's own TLS (*in build 188*, a crash fix in 189) **In short:** SmartLink on an iPhone or iPad over mobile networks that closed the radio's connection: the connection now uses Apple's own TLS. Released in build 188 (a crash fix in 189). **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Mohammed Alkhayarin A71AT, mail with his log 2026-11-18 00:43, build 184, iPad, iOS 27.0.1, the AU-510, Qatar: "the same problem with SmartLink connection to 5G show him back again"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-18*: his log, two runs: the first on his home network (a local session, FT8 on 40 m), then after a disconnect a SmartLink connect, and the second a fresh start on Cellular only; in both every attempt the same as on 2026-11-16: connect_ready, the TLS session up ("TLS 1.2 over IPv4"), the far end closing 30-40 ms later before the radio's version line, the second try with wan validate held back 1 s the same (6 attempts). The iOS build's TLS is Qt's Secure Transport backend (no OpenSSL on iOS), which goes no higher than TLS 1.2; Android (OpenSSL 3) and Linux use TLS 1.3. In TLS 1.2 the server's certificate crosses the network in the clear, in TLS 1.3 it is encrypted. What works for him: OksaSDR on Android over 5G, SmartSDR for iOS on the same iPad, 5G and account (16.11), our iPad app on Wi-Fi; what fails: only our iPad app over 5G, sometimes after it had worked that morning. So the likely cause is a filter on the mobile network (operators in Qatar filter VoIP-like traffic) that classifies the TLS 1.2 connection by what it shows (the radio's self-signed certificate, or Secure Transport's ClientHello) and cuts it; not proven: a test on his side would be the iPad on his Android phone's hotspot with both apps (if the Android app connects and the iPad does not over the same link, it is the client). Found on the way: on iOS Qt's Secure Transport gives no peer certificate with VerifyNone, so the TOFU pin of the radio's certificate ("peer presented no certificate; skipping TOFU check", our own iPad too) never runs on iOS. Fix: on iOS (and the iPad app on a Mac) the radio's TLS connection through Apple's Network framework (nw_connection with TLS 1.2-1.3, the verify block taking the radio's self-signed certificate and doing the same TOFU fingerprint check), behind WanConnection's present interface (connect, write, readyRead, the session line), Qt's QSslSocket kept on the other platforms; the SmartLink server's own connection unchanged (it works). No terms (Apple's framework). Tests: only on a device (the CI builds iOS; .mm non-ARC): the user's iPad over SmartLink to the FLEX (the session line "TLS 1.3", the certificate pinned, a reconnect, the network changes of 84), then Mohammed over 5G. Size: medium. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-18, chosen for build 187)*: src/core/backends/flex/AppleTlsSocket.h/.mm (iOS only, not ARC): nw_connection with nw_parameters_create_secure_tcp, TLS 1.2 to 1.3 (sec_protocol_options), a verify block that accepts the radio's self-signed certificate and keeps its DER (SecTrustCopyCertificateChain's leaf); encrypted() at the ready state with the negotiated version and cipher (sec_protocol_metadata), the peer address and local port from the path; reads by nw_connection_receive, the end of the stream as RemoteHostClosedError, failed and waiting states as errors (as QSslSocket fails at once), disconnectFromHost by the final message then cancel (2 s at most), abort at once; every event handed to the socket's thread for its own connection only (a connection id, the owner cleared under a lock before it goes). WanConnection uses it behind #if defined(Q_OS_IOS) (QSslSocket elsewhere, unchanged) and pins the SHA-256 of the DER as QSslCertificate::digest does, so the TOFU check now runs on iOS; the core links the Network and Security frameworks on iOS. Checked here: the Linux build (the QSslSocket path) and the iOS path of WanConnection.cpp and both mocs compiled with Q_OS_IOS on Linux; the .mm only compiles on the CI. To test on a device: SmartLink on the iPad (the log's "SmartLink: the radio's TLS session up, TLS 1.3 …", "pinned cert fingerprint … first-use TOFU" once, then "matches"), a disconnect and reconnect, a network change (84), then Mohammed over 5G. *Found while testing (2026-11-18)*: WanConnection called its TLS-ready step on QSslSocket's connected(), the TCP connect, before the handshake: so on every platform the session line showed no cipher and a protocol read too early ("TLS 1.3  over IPv4" on Linux), and the peer had no certificate yet, so the TOFU pin was always skipped, not only on iOS. Now on encrypted() (the iOS socket's encrypted() is the same point): on Linux over real SmartLink to the FLEX the session is "TLS 1.2 ECDHE-RSA-CHACHA20-POLY1305 over IPv4" (the radio negotiates TLS 1.2 with OpenSSL 3 too, so the "TLS 1.3" of the Android and Linux logs was that early reading), the first connect pinned the certificate and the second "cert fingerprint matches stored pin". So the iOS change does not bring TLS 1.3 to this radio: what it changes is Apple's current TLS stack and its ClientHello instead of Secure Transport's, which is what still tells the iPad app from SmartSDR for iOS and the Android app on Mohammed's 5G; whether it is enough, only his test can show (his hotspot test, an Android phone sharing 5G to the iPad, would tell client from network meanwhile). *Found after release (2026-11-19)*: build 188 crashed when a SmartLink connection failed before it was up (Barry Cohen N2BJ: his log, two connect_ready, each followed 150 ms later by "OSStatus error -9816 - server closed session with no notification", then EXC_BAD_ACCESS in nw_connection_cancel_inner on the framework's queue). Cause: AppleTlsSocket held the connection by one reference, which its state handler released at the cancelled state; when the framework itself cancelled the connection (a failed handshake, the radio closing, a waiting state), m_conn still pointed at it and finishConnection() then cancelled a freed connection. The same race was open on a normal disconnect and on a write after a failure. Fix: two references, each released by its holder, as Apple's nwcat sample: this object's (from nw_connection_create, released in finishConnection() after the cancel) and the state handler's (an nw_retain, released at the cancelled state); no other code uses the Network framework. Why the radio closed: the user's FLEX does the same to every TLS client that arrives without a SmartLink connect pending (openssl s_client from this PC on the LAN: its default ClientHello, TLS 1.2 only, TLS 1.3 only and X25519MLKEM768 all end in "unexpected eof", no alert), so the radio decides by whether it expects the connection, not by the TLS stack: Barry's radio did not take his connection as the one SmartLink announced. A likely cause, to ask him: his two radios on the account (a FLEX-6600M and a FLEX-8600M) both on TLS port 4994 behind one router, which forwards the port to only one of them; SmartSDR would fail the same way. A possible follow-up after his answer: a clearer message for a TLS connection the radio closes before it is up. The iPad test of 188 (2026-11-19 07:55, SmartLink to the FLEX): "TLS 1.2 ECDHE-RSA-CHACHA20-POLY1305 over IPv4", no "peer presented no certificate" (the pin lines are debug level, not in the devlink log).
100. **Fixes:** The Network & performance panel hangs the app on iOS (*in build 185*) **In short:** The Network & performance panel made the app hang on iOS. Released in build 185. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Scott Stanford W5SLS's two TestFlight crashes on build 181, "When I touch the top of the screen that shows “xxxMB” it crashes"; the developer's iPad on build 184, 2026-11-18, three times; Android beta 98 the same day: no hang). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-18*: the iPad's reports (.ips, mailed by the user) are watchdog kills (0x8BADF00D, "scene-update watchdog transgression ... 10.00 seconds"), the main thread in Qt Quick's polish from the display link: QQuickWindow polishAndSync → QQuickLayout::updatePolish → ensureLayoutItemsUpdated → a layout's own size changed → QQuickLayout::geometryChange → QQuickGridLayoutBase::rearrange → a child layout's geometryChange → rearrange, again and again, with text shaped anew inside it (HarfBuzz over CoreText); the cpu_resource report: 90 s of CPU in 105 s, every sample there. So a layout and a text in it set each other's size without end (Qt 6's polishItems allows INT_MAX rounds, so it never gives up); the functions named from the strings each references in the stripped binary (build 184's symbols artifact has no dSYM). Not reproduced on Linux: the panel opened on the demo radio at 1376x1032, 1032x1376, 1180x820, 440x956, 402x874 and 390x844, fonts default and Liberation Sans, --font-px 13-18, distance-field, native and curve text rendering (144 runs), and on the FLEX at 440x956: the iOS font metrics are what tips it. Fix: the panel's rows without text-driven layout (a fixed row height, the value right-aligned and elided, the note at a fixed width, the per-stream rows updated in place instead of a Repeater rebuilt every second), so no text size can feed back into a layout; and for the next such report the iOS CI keeps the unstripped binary (Qt's own function names) with the dSYM, so a .ips symbolizes. Test: only on a device (the iPad: the badge tapped, the panel open a minute, closed and opened again; an iPhone), as a preview build first. Size: small. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-18)*: NetworkDialog.qml's rows are items of a fixed height (FontMetrics), the value right-aligned and elided over the label, the sections the same, the network badge in an item of a fixed size, the note at a fixed width (the room beside a scroll bar), the four stream rows kept (model 4) with only their values changing; no RowLayout or text-sized item left in the column. Rendered on the demo radio at 440x956, 390x844, 844x390 (scrolls, the bar beside the rows) and 1376x1032: as before. The iOS CI keeps aether-mobile.unstripped (the linked binary of the same UUID before the archive's strip) in testflight-symbols, and that artifact stays 30 days (the others 2). *In build 185 / beta 99 (preview)*; tested on the developer's iPad 2026-11-18 12:13-12:14 (iOS 27.2.1, the FLEX over the VPN): the panel opened from the top row several times, no stall in the devlink log (the user: "Yläpalkki avattu useita kertoja ei ongelmaa"). The CI's unstripped binary was not found where it looked ("unstripped none"): the search path to fix with the next build. *Fixed for build 186*: there is no unstripped copy (Xcode links the app into the archive and strips it there), but the dSYM made before the strip lay beside the build products (build-xcode/mobile/Release-iphoneos), not in the archive, so the export said "Upload Symbols Failed": mobile/tools/ios-symbols.sh puts the dSYM with the shipped binary's UUID into the archive and the artifact.
101. **Fixes:** A slider moved by a finger that only meant to scroll the page (*in build 186*) **In short:** A slider no longer moves when a finger only meant to scroll the page. Released in build 186. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Rich Yost N2RY, mail with his log 2026-11-17, build 182, iPhone17,4, FLEX-6500: "suddenly the waterfall partially disappeared"; his FFT frames/s at 5 and Waterfall speed at 1, both their slow ends, kept in the radio; chosen for the next build, the user 2026-11-18: "Liukusäätimet ja vieritys"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-18* (Qt 6.12's QQuickSlider source): a touch press only arms a Slider and a move along it past the drag threshold drags it, but the release puts the value where the finger lifts even with no drag (handleRelease), so a tap on the track (a finger stopping the page, or one that lands on a slider as a scroll begins and lifts before the page takes it) sets the value under it; a mouse press (a Mac) moves it at once (mousePressEvent calls handleMove). A vertical scroll across a horizontal slider is taken by the tab's Flickable before the slider moves, so the tap is the cause. 30 sliders: 12 plain (the View tab's seven, CW sensitivity, the TX processor, the Info panel's pan, the EQ's vertical bands) and 18 FineSlider (power, gains, levels; − / + beside them). Fix: one AppSlider, a Slider with a MouseArea over it that takes the press: the value moves only by a drag along the slider past the drag threshold, from where it was by the finger's travel (as an iOS slider's thumb; a tap on the track moves an iOS slider no more); a tap does nothing; pressed is true only while dragging and moved() comes on each step, so the sends on release (the View tab's FFT and waterfall pacing, FineSlider's levels) and the live sends work as before; the mouse wheel still scrolls the page (no wheel handler). The EQ's vertical bands in a vertically scrolling tab: to see whether the drag or the page wins. Terms: none. Test: the render tool gets a touch drag through the window system (dragname: a finger, as a phone's, or the mouse): a tap on a slider's track leaves its value; a vertical drag across one scrolls the tab and leaves it; a sideways drag moves it by the travel; − / + and a screen reader's steps unchanged; then the user's iPhone and iPad. Size: small. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-18)*: mobile/qml/AppSlider.qml, used by all 12 plain sliders and FineSlider's own (so all 30); a vertical one (the EQ) drags only from its handle (a finger's width round it), a drag elsewhere scrolls the page. The render tool's new dragname (a finger through the window system as a touch screen, or the mouse) on the demo radio at 390x844, against Qt's own Slider (the MouseArea switched off): a tap at the left end of Black level took it from 51 to 5 before, leaves it now; a mouse press on FFT average took it from 0 to 50 before, leaves it now; a finger's vertical drag across FFT frames/s scrolled the tab both before and now with the value kept; a sideways drag of 60 px moves FFT average by 14 from where it was (before it jumped to the finger); the mic level's FineSlider: taps (finger and mouse) and a scroll keep it, − / + step one, drags of ±40 px move it 12 and back; the EQ: a tap at a band's top keeps it, a drag from the handle moves it (60 px: +9), a drag from the track's foot scrolls the Audio tab (76 px) and keeps it. focussweep on every drawer tab at 430x932: nothing keeps the keyboard. To verify on the iPhone and the iPad.
102. **FTx:** Answer CQ's calls per station set by the user (*in build 188*) **In short:** How many times Answer CQ calls a station can be set by the operator. Released in build 188. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Kurt Hanisch DL5EAG, mail 2026-11-18: "Is it possible to make the auto-response to a CQ call configurable? Currently, the calling sequence is fixed at 5 repetitions", and the same evening: "It would be best if it were freely selectable. Sometimes 2 calls are enough, other times 20 might be needed"; the user, 2026-11-18: "102 Kurtin kutsumäärä" for build 187). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-18*: Answer CQ (MobileEngineDigi.cpp) calls a station kAutoAnswerTries = 5 times, then picks another (one called is left alone 30 minutes); the same limit counts each later message of an Answer CQ QSO sent without its reply (a tapped QSO has 6, the CQ watchdog 10, a Hound 4). WSJT-X has no count, only its Tx watchdog in minutes. Fix: the count a setting, 2-20 (Kurt's range), 5 by default, remembered (MobileDigiAnswerCalls), a row under the presence limit in the FTx settings (More): "Answer CQ calls each station N times" with − / +; the manual's two Answer CQ passages. No terms. Test: --ft8-test autoanswer,dry,calls=2 on the FLEX (two dry calls a station, then the next), the row rendered on a phone and the iPad. Size: small. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-18)*: MobileEngine::digiAnswerCalls (2-20, 5), the FTx settings row (digiAnswerCalls, − / + held repeat), --ft8-test calls=N (put back at the end). On the FLEX, dry, 20 m FT8, calls=2 for 5 minutes: EC3A, EA3DVP, EH5CCE and EA4EQ each called twice ("try 1", "try 2"), then "No answer from …" and the next; frequency, mode, power, TX slice and the setting (5) restored. Rendered at 440x956, 390x844 and 1180x820 (the iPad panel): the row under "Auto stops after …", − / + 5 → 7 → 5.
103. **Devices:** The KPA1500's fan from the Amp tab: − / + for its minimum speed, with the speed shown (*in build 190 / Android beta 102*) **In short:** The KPA1500's fan minimum on the Amp tab (− / +) with the speed shown. Released in build 190. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Rich Yost N2RY, mail 2026-11-19: "possible to add a plus and minus Fan button to control the fan speed with a number indicator? i do a lot of AM, and very conscious of the amp temp"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-19*: the KPA1500 Programming Reference V3 has ^FC, the fan minimum speed (GET ^FC;, SET/RESPONSE ^FCn; n 0 to 5, also ^FC+; ^FC-; and ^FC/; back to the value before the last change; updated in firmware 02.62) and ^FS, the speed the fan runs at now (0 to 5, which can be above the minimum when the amp is hot); ^DW the dwell time (3-255 s). So: ^FS and ^FC in the status poll (the temperature ^TM is there already), on the Amp tab a "Fan" row with − / + (^FC-; / ^FC+;, only with Control on, as the other commands) and the minimum and actual speed shown ("Fan 3 (min 2)"); the speed also offered on the meters as the KPA1500's other readings. The stand-in simulator answers ^FC/^FS too, for the tests; then Rich on his amp. Experimental, as the KPA1500's other control. No terms. Size: small. *Chosen for the next build* (the user, 2026-11-19: "otetaan"). *Done in the dev build (2026-11-19)*: Kpa1500Protocol's Status has fanSpeed (^FS) and fanMinimum (^FC), each one digit 0-5 (anything else left out), buildSetFanMinimum(n) gives ^FCn; (the number itself: every firmware takes it, ^FC+; / ^FC-; only 02.62 and later); Kpa1500Connection::setFanMinimum; the UDP reader asks FS and FC in its slow round (now 11 queries, 5.5 s); MobileEngine::kpaSetFanMinimum, the one KPA1500 command without a question and allowed while the radio transmits (kpaCommandAllowed's whileTransmitting: a fan setting switches no RF and the amplifier still speeds the fans up as the heat sink warms), done when ^FC reads it back, then ^FS read at once. The Amp tab: a Fan reading ("2 (min 1)") under Temperature and with Control on a Fan minimum row, − / + with the read-back value between them (− off at 0, + at 5; usable while transmitting, the tab's other controls not); the notes under the controls say so. The meters: Amp fan (0-5, the six steps as its marks, "Fan 2 (min 1)", named Fan on a narrow face, Amp Fan on a wide one), offered while a KPA1500 is the amplifier read (no RF2K-S set up). The stand-in (kpa1500-mock.py --fan-min N): ^FC / ^FS answered (the speed 2 keyed, 0 in receive, never below the minimum), ^FCn set, ^FC+ / - / / with firmware 02.62 and later. *Found in the tests and fixed*: the command goes over the control link (TCP) and its readback over UDP, so the readback could reach the amplifier first and get the old value; the next one came 5.5 s later in the round, so the tab said "has not shown the change in 3 s" and the next − counted from the stale value (^FC1 sent for 3 → 2). Now a pending command's readback is asked every cycle until it shows (Kpa1500Reader extra queries; a tune keeps its own); this was there for Operate / Standby, ANT1 / ANT2, the ATU mode and the fault clear too (64), only less likely to show. Tested on this PC against the stand-in with the FLEX connected (2026-11-19): kpa1500_protocol_test (FC2, FS4, FS0; FC6, FC+, FC12, an empty FC, FS-1, FS9 refused; ^FC3; parsed; ^FC0; / ^FC5; built, -1 / 6 refused); the user's path at 440x956: Control on in Radio Setup › Amp, − / + from 0 to 2 and back to 0, each step one ^FCn on the TCP link and shown by the readback within a cycle, − at 0 sent nothing; while transmitting (USB, 5 W, no audio, "radio transmitting: yes"): + twice (^FC1, ^FC2), Standby and Bypass off (nothing sent), the state line "Not while transmitting (the fan minimum works)."; the Amp fan meter on the right in transmit; mode DIGU and 100 W put back. Rendered at 440x956, 1366x1024 and 1180x820 (the iPad panel): the rows fit, the meter "Amp Fan 0 (min 0)". Manual: the Amp tab's Fan reading, Fan minimum, the meters' Fan, Radio Setup › Amp's control list; tab-kpa.jpg and kpa-controls.jpg anew (the controls picture scrolled 14 px above the end: ShotPlan's scroll takes -PX). To test on Rich's KPA1500 (firmware, the fans heard). *Tested by Rich Yost N2RY on his KPA1500 (mail with his log 2026-11-19 18:33, build 190, iPhone17,4, iOS 27.0.1)*: "Tested the fan speed up and down, receive and transmit also set the meter to display speed. All appears to work great. Monitored the amp temp thru out test"; his log: 27 fan minimum steps, each shown by the readback (0.02-0.07 s), Tune in STANDBY twice ("The amplifier tuned (SWR 1.00)"). One OPERATE asked 4 s after the second tune (12:02:16) was not read back in 3 s (the first, at 11:19:48, in 0.17 s): to ask Rich whether the amplifier went to OPERATE then. Rich's answer (mail 2026-11-19 21:04): "I did not press anything on the amp itself, The amp tune seemed to work fine I also tuned the flex 6500 once too". So the OPERATE asked 4 s after a tune was not read back in 3 s, while the one asked 10 s after the first tune was at once: perhaps the KPA1500 ignores ^OS1 for a few seconds after a tune (storing the ATU setting); not known, to watch in the next log (no change without it).

104. **Fixes:** A Disconnect over SmartLink, or a switch from SmartLink to the direct connection, logged on iOS as the radio closing the connection (*in build 190*) **In short:** A Disconnect over SmartLink was logged on iOS as the radio closing the connection and left as an error. Released in build 190 and verified on your iPad. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** ("The remote host closed the connection") and kept as the app's last error (builds 188-189, AppleTlsSocket of 99; the developer's iPad 2026-11-19: four such ends in the devlink log, 08:43, 08:52, 16:02 and 16:16, read here first as the radio dropping SmartLink; the user: "ei se ainakaan minulle sitä katkaissut ennenkuin itse katkaisin"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-19*: WanConnection::disconnectFromRadio sends 0x04 and calls disconnectFromHost; AppleTlsSocket goes to ClosingState, sends the final message and the radio closes its side in answer; the receive loop takes that end (is_complete with the final context) for the radio closing and posts handleFailed, which took ClosingState as a live session: errorOccurred(RemoteHostClosedError), so WanConnection logged a socket error, MobileEngine "SmartLink: the remote connection ended after the radio answered" and setLastError("Remote connection: The remote host closed the connection"), shown on the Connect page after the user's own Disconnect. QSslSocket (builds before 188, Android, Linux) reports no error for a close it was asked for. Each end in the log matches the user's own action: at 16:16 no reconnect followed (a Disconnect), at 08:43, 08:52 and 16:02 the direct connection over the VPN came up a second later (the radio's card tapped). Fix: handleFailed in ClosingState ends the session as asked (disconnected() only, a debug line), as QSslSocket does; a session that was up and ends by itself still reports the error. iOS only (the file is not built elsewhere). No terms. Size: small. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-19)*; it builds only in the iOS CI, so to test on the iPad: SmartLink connected, Disconnect (no "remote host closed" in the log, no error on the Connect page), SmartLink then the radio's direct card, and a real drop (the radio's network unplugged, or airplane mode) still reported. *Verified on the developer's iPad, build 190 (2026-11-19 18:42, the phone's hotspot over mobile data)*: Disconnect over SmartLink logged no "remote host closed" and no socket error; connected again at once.

105. **Fixes:** A binding loop in Controls › Prof › App setups: the Export question (*in build 190*) **In short:** A layout warning in Controls › Prof › App setups (the Export question) logged at every start. Released in build 190. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (ProfileControls.qml:229, "QML Dialog: Binding loop detected for property implicitHeight", in Basic's Dialog.qml:16) logged each time the controls drawer was first opened, on every platform (the devlink log: builds 180-189 and Android beta 98; the dev build's runs; the user chose its fix for build 190, 2026-11-19). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-19*: its contentItem was the wrapping Label itself, so the dialog's implicit height and the label's wrapped height were set from each other; the app's other questions (AmpControls' confirm) put the text in a ColumnLayout with Layout.fillWidth and log nothing. Fix: the same layout. No terms. Size: tiny. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-19)*: no binding loop on opening the drawer (the demo radio, 440x956); the question opened from a saved setup at 440x956 and 1180x820 (the text wrapped in the 420-px dialog, OK / Cancel), Delete's question too, the test setup deleted after. The other binding loops in the devlink log (MeterView.qml:128 / 142, BigButtonsView, PanadapterView) are older builds' lines; none in this dev build's runs.

106. **Fixes:** SmartLink to the radio fails on Michael Walker's iPhone on build 189 with "Remote connection: connection failed" (*in builds 190, 192 and 193*: the reason shown, then SmartLink's TLS and UDP through an IPv6-only network's NAT64) **In short:** SmartLink failed on a tester's iPhone on a mobile network that is IPv6 only. The reason is shown, and SmartLink now goes through such a network's NAT64. Released in builds 190, 192 and 193; the tester's own confirmation on his carrier is still awaited. **From you:** nothing; it waits for the tester. **Details:** (mail 2026-11-19 16:48 with a screenshot: iPhone 15 Pro, iOS 27.2, build 189, his Aurora AU-520; the Connection check: this device's network OK at 192.0.0.2/32, 192.168.1.102/24 and 192.0.0.6/32, SmartLink signed in, the radio listed Available, 1 of 1 station slots free, no radio On this network; SmartSDR for iOS connects; SmartLink worked for him on build 184, voice logging from the car). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-19 (first part)*: "connection failed" is AppleTlsSocket's text for a framework state with no error, which is the waiting state (no usable path, before TCP or TLS): so the system held the connection back (this app's mobile data or Local Network off, a path the framework found unusable) rather than the radio refusing it; 192.0.0.2 / .6 are iOS's 464XLAT addresses of an IPv6-only mobile network, 192.168.1.102 his Wi-Fi. Builds 188-189 made the connection with Apple's Network framework (99), which applies the per-app network settings that BSD sockets (Qt, up to 187) did not meet the same way. *In the dev build (2026-11-19)*: a wait now says what its path is (satisfied / unsatisfied and why: mobile data off for the app, Wi-Fi denied, Local Network off; over Wi-Fi / mobile data / wired / other), in the log and on the Connect page. Asked him (reply drafted 2026-11-19): his log right after a failed try, at home on the radio's Wi-Fi or away, OksaSDR's Local Network switch. Size: to see from his log. Tested on the developer's iPad with build 190 (2026-11-19 18:34-18:42): OksaSDR's Local Network permission was off all along, and SmartLink to the radio's public address connected on the home Wi-Fi, on the phone's hotspot shared from Wi-Fi, and on the hotspot over mobile data (after the network change the app made the connection again by itself, TLS 1.2 over IPv4 in 11 s): so Local Network is not what stops a SmartLink connection (it only hides On this network's radios). Not reproducible here: an iPhone's own IPv6-only mobile network (464XLAT, his 192.0.0.x addresses) with Wi-Fi on at the same time; for that his build 190 log, or an iPhone on its own mobile data. If the log shows the wait on a path that is up or can come up, the fix is to give the framework a few seconds in its waiting state instead of cancelling at once (QSslSocket had no such state). *Cause found (2026-11-19, his logs from builds 189 and 191, mail 19:37 and 19:43; his answers: "cellular and other SmartLink applications work", "OksaSDR has always worked")*: on Wi-Fi at 09:52 SmartLink to the radio worked (TLS 1.2 over IPv4); at 12:37 the iPhone moved to mobile data, the SmartLink server connection went over IPv6, and every connection to the radio's IPv4 address failed in the same millisecond as connect_ready: build 189 "connection failed", build 191 (106's line) "no usable network path", no reason, no interface. So on his IPv6-only mobile network (464XLAT) Network framework has no path for an IPv4 literal, where Qt's BSD sockets (builds up to 187) went through the iPhone's CLAT. Not a setting; likely every IPv6-only mobile network (T-Mobile US and others). *Fixed in the dev build*: for an IPv4 address AppleTlsSocket first asks getaddrinfo (AI_DEFAULT, on the framework's queue, never the GUI thread) how the network reaches it, as Apple's "Supporting IPv6-only Networks" asks for IPv4 literals; an IPv6 answer (only NAT64 gives one for an IPv4 literal) becomes the connection's endpoint (nw_endpoint_create_address), logged "an IPv6-only network: the radio's address through its NAT64"; elsewhere the IPv4 address as before. The UDP streams stay Qt's to SmartLink's IPv4 address (as up to build 187), the certificate pin is the radio's whatever the address. Checked here: the helper compiled and run on this PC (no NAT64: empty, the IPv4 path as before); the async part syntax-checked with clang -fblocks against stubs; the iOS build only in CI; to verify by Michael on mobile data. Replied to him 2026-11-19 (the cause, the fix in the next build, Wi-Fi meanwhile), his threads archived. *Build 192 on his mobile data (log, mail 2026-11-19 21:33: "I have no audio and no pan adaptor or waterfall. The Vita49 data is not passing On this Network")*: the TLS session came up over IPv6 (the NAT64 path worked) and the radio took the connection, but not one UDP packet arrived (display check frames 0, audio underruns with no packet at all, "no audio data received for 15008 ms"). The UDP had stayed on an IPv4 socket to the radio's IPv4 address (through the iPhone's CLAT), so the radio saw the TLS session and the UDP come from two different paths, and likely two different public addresses of the carrier's NAT64 (the CLAT has an IPv6 address of its own); up to 187 both went through the CLAT. The assumption above ("the UDP streams stay Qt's ... as up to build 187") was wrong. *Fixed in the dev build (2026-11-19)*: PanadapterStream::wanUdpTarget: when the TLS session reached the radio's IPv4 address through the NAT64 (its peer a plain IPv6 address, not v4-mapped, loopback or link-local), the WAN UDP goes to that same IPv6 address from an IPv6 socket (startWan binds AnyIPv6 for an IPv6 address); everything else as before (IPv4 bind, the public IPv4); RadioModel logs "WAN UDP through the NAT64 like the TLS session". Checked here: wan_udp_keepalive_socket_test with a real IPv6 loopback (udp_register reaches the IPv6 address, VITA over IPv6 confirms the registration and is dispatched, back to IPv4 rebinds IPv4) and the selection's cases, all PASS; the Linux dev build's SmartLink to the FLEX over IPv4 unchanged (TLS over IPv4, frames 54, no NAT64 line). Android and desktop: their TLS peer is IPv4 (Qt, CLAT on Android), so unchanged. *Tested on an IPv6-only network before the release (the user, 2026-11-19: "Rakenna tämä ennen julkaisua Jotta ollaan varma, että se toimii")*: mobile/tools/ipv6only-testnet.sh (a network namespace with IPv6 only, set up by the user's sudo; no packages), nat64-test.py (a NAT64 on a TUN device, tayga's way, the kernel's NAT44 behind it) and dns64-test.py (AAAA as 64:ff9b::A; --no-a answers A with nothing, as iOS hides IPv4 addresses on an IPv6-only network: Linux Qt's HTTP/2 to Auth0 hung on the unreachable IPv4 addresses otherwise, curl did not), the dev build inside it with OKSASDR_TEST_NAT64=64:ff9b:: (the radio's TLS through the NAT64, as AppleTlsSocket from getaddrinfo; Linux and desktop builds only). SmartLink to the FLEX: signed in, the radio's TLS over IPv6, "WAN UDP through the NAT64 like the TLS session", display check frames 56 / 45, 6233 packets in through the NAT64, no underruns; FT8 on 14.074 DIGU at 10 W: "CQ OH6OKSA" sent, "TX ended, peak 8.4 W" (the TX audio over the IPv6 UDP), FT8 decodes after it; restored Decode off, RF power 100 W, slice A 14.074 DIGU TX, disconnected. (SWR 3.2 in that TX, where earlier FT8 runs showed 1.2-1.4: told the user.) Still to verify by Michael on his carrier (with its CLAT).

107. **Display:** The notch panel beside the notch, not over it (*in build 191 / Android beta 103*; on every screen and in 3D *in build 192 / beta 104*), Larry Averbeck W9FLA **In short:** The notch panel opens beside the notch, not over it, on every screen and in 3D. Released in builds 191 and 192. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (FB comment on the build 189 post, 2026-11-19, Galaxy Tab 11A+ in landscape, Android beta 101: "I have a Solar farm nearby that produces a lot of interference requiring frequent use of the Notch feature. Unfortunately, the menu is frozen dead center on the pan and waterfall and the area that I need to see to easily adjust it. The ability to move it out of the way would be useful"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-19*: PanadapterView.qml's tnfPopup.place() centres the panel on the notch (anchorX) at 12 % of the pan's height, so it covers the notch and the signals beside it while its width and depth are set. Fix: put it beside the notch, on the side with more room (its edge a finger's width from the notch), and where the window is too narrow for that (a phone upright: the panel is nearly as wide) under the pan, over the waterfall's lower part; the panel's header also drags it (kept inside the window), for a spot of the operator's choosing, remembered for the session. Test: the render tool at 440x956, 956x440, 1180x820 (the iPad panel), 1366x1024 and an 11" Android tablet's landscape (1340x800): a notch at the left, middle and right, the band and its neighbours visible while the panel is open; the hold-to-add path the same. No terms. Size: small. *Chosen for the next build* (the user, 2026-11-19: "Otetaan 107 ja 108 mukaan"). *Done in the dev build (2026-11-19)*: tnfPopup.place() puts the panel a finger's width plus half the notch's band beside it, on the side with more room (right when both fit and the right has as much), else at the foot of the pan; PanelHeader gets draggable (a DragHandler on the title row, the scene translation, kept 8 px inside the window) and dragged(x, y), which the notch panel keeps (userMoved, userPos) for its later openings this session; no other panel is draggable. Rendered on the FLEX by the hold-to-add path (holdmhz at 14.0740 / 14.0756 / 14.0768-14.0772, the pan 14.0736-14.0776; nothing added, Close each time): 440x956 at the foot, the notch marker visible above; 956x440 right, right and left of the notch; 1180x820 and 1340x800 right, right, left; a drag of the title by 200,250 at 1340x800 and 440x956 moved it (clamped inside) and the next notch's panel opened there. Manual: the Notches paragraph; tnf.jpg anew (the panel beside the notch). *Found in Android beta 103 (2026-11-19, the developer's OnePlus Open; the user: "Androidilla ei aina mene notsia oikealle kohtaan ... laatikkoon päälle", "Sama ongelma etukannen kanssa", "Ongelma on 3D-näytös, 2D-näytös näkyy ihan ok")*: on the inner screen (about 700 dp wide) a notch near the middle left no room beside it, and on the cover screen none either, so the panel went to the pan's foot, where the 3D view draws the notch's handle (the strip under its picture, notch.bandTop = spectrumFraction): it covered the notch (devlink screenshots 19:29 and 19:33). *Fixed in the dev build*: beside the notch only within the pan's own width (also keeps it off a pinned side panel: the iPad's middle notch had overlapped it), else under the pan, else above it, else above or below the notch's handle (a finger's height under it kept clear), only last the pan's foot. Rendered on the FLEX: 3D at 406x903 (the cover screen) above the pan at the left, middle and right, the handle and waterfall in sight; 3D at 700x753 beside, above, beside; 2D at 700x753, 440x956 (above the pan) and 1180x820 (beside within the pan, above it for the middle notch).

109. **Logging:** POTA spots on the display (*in build 195*, with WWFF), Robin Bishop G5OUT **In short:** POTA and WWFF spots on the display. Released in build 195. SOTA spots only after the SOTA Management Team approves (their API terms). **From you:** nothing; SOTA would need you to ask the SOTA Management Team (see 180). **Details:** (FB post in OksaSDR Users, 2026-11-20 about 03:50 Finnish time: "Would it be possible to add POTA spots? I see the logbook already has a facility for entering park numbers, if it could also pull in pota spots it would be absolutely amazing! Save me using a seperate logbook as i mostly POTA!"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-20*: the app has the POTA self-spot (67 b, MobileEnginePota.cpp, posting to api.pota.app/spot/) and Their park / My park in the Log tab, but no feed of other activators. The desktop engine already has one: src/core/PotaClient (upstream's, not yet in the mobile build) polls https://api.pota.app/spot/activator every 60 s (the public feed loggers such as Ham2K PoLo read), one DxSpot a new spotId: the activator's call, frequency, "reference name mode" as the comment, the API's expire as its lifetime, PotaSpotColor (#FFFF00); the desktop sends them to the radio as spots like the DX cluster's (MainWindow_Spots.cpp queueSpotCmd). In the app the DX cluster's spots go to the radio the same way (MobileEngine::setupDxCluster), so its tag on the display, tuneToSpot and the Log tab's spotted call (Engine.lastSpotCall) work for them unchanged. Glue: PotaClient into mobile/CMakeLists.txt; a switch under Controls › View beside DX cluster (off by default, its own colour); its spots through the cluster's path; a tapped POTA spot also sets Their park to POTA with its reference (beside the call it already offers); the spot's mode for the slice (SSB / CW / FT8) as the cluster's. Size: small to medium, one build; tested against the live feed receive only. Later: the same for WWFF (spots.wwff.co) and SOTA (SOTAwatch) with the SOTA idea below. *Sources checked 2026-11-20*: POTA api.pota.app/spot/activator (JSON, 14 spots then: activator, frequency, mode, reference, name, locationDesc, grid4/6, spotTime, spotter, comments, expire; unofficial and undocumented, open by the volunteers' goodwill, light polling asked); WWFF spots.wwff.co/static/spots.json (JSON, 45 spots: activator, frequency_khz, mode, reference, reference_name, remarks, spot_time; no terms found), so WWFF costs little beside POTA; SOTA: the official API moved to api-db2.sota.org.uk (/api/spots/{n}/all/all, /api/spots/epoch; api2 answers "deprecated"), its terms: no commercial use, developers on the SOTA Reflector and its API-consumers group first, and "no AI-generated software may connect to the SOTA API without prior approval": SOTA only after the SOTA Management Team approves (the user's call; ties to 97).
110. **Display:** The waterfall's buttons into the empty row in landscape (*in build 196 / Android beta 107*), Evgeny R0ACS **In short:** On a phone on its side with the band and step rows hidden (B/S), the waterfall's own buttons move off the waterfall into the row that was otherwise empty, so the display is wider (Evgeny R0ACS asked for a wider view on a horizontal screen). Only the phone in landscape: upright the row goes with B/S, and the iPad page hides its whole step row.
