Check: 852x393, 932x430, 667x375, an Android phone and the OnePlus outer screen in landscape, buttons right both ways, split paddles, FT8 (no PTT), B/S on and off, and the focus sweep. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-20)*: PhoneOperatePage's panButtonsBelow (landscape, B/S off): the step row carries − + (zoomOutButtonBelow / zoomInButtonBelow), Band (the band chooser), B/S ▲ (amber frame; shows the steps again), a filler and + Slice / + Pan, mirrored by View › Zoom buttons; PanadapterView's buttonsBelow leaves its − + Band B/S and + Slice / + Pan out (Close N other pans stays), cornerButtonWidth and openBandChooser() for the row; the CW / RTTY decode strip then takes the full width. Rendered on the FLEX: 852x393 (also Right), 667x375 and 800x360 with split CW paddles (667 also Right), 868x390, 932x430, FT8 running (no PTT, no Macros), 393x852 upright unchanged; taps: Band opened the chooser, − then + back to the same span, B/S ▲ brought the steps back and the buttons onto the waterfall; focussweep: nothing keeps the keyboard; the radio back at 14.074 DIGU, the settings as they were.
111. **Distribution:** A check for a newer version in the Android **In short:** The Android app looks for a newer beta on the release page and offers it (your idea); the switch is Setup › App › New versions. Released in Android beta 107 and widened in 133 to a newer build of the same beta. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (and later Linux) app (*in Android beta 107*) (the user, 2026-11-20: "Linux ja android versioihin voisi lisätä toiminnon jolla voisi aina päivittää uusimpaan versioo eli softa tarkistaa onko uutta versiota. iOSin osalta taitaa testflight sen hoitaa"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-20*: iOS, iPadOS and the Mac need nothing: TestFlight tells the tester of a new build and installs it by itself with its Automatic Updates (on by default; TestFlight › OksaSDR), the App Store later the same. Android: the APK comes from GitHub, so nothing tells a user of a new beta. The app knows its beta (AETHER_MOBILE_BUILD_NUMBER, from release.env's TAG in CI), and GitHub's https://api.github.com/repos/OH6OKSA/OksaSDR/releases/latest gives the newest release (tag android-beta-N, the APK's download link; no key, 60 requests an hour per address, a generic User-Agent "OksaSDR"). Plan: at start and then once a day, with a "Check for a new version" switch on, the tag's number compared with the app's; a newer one gives a notice on the Connect page and once on the operating screen: "Android beta N is out" with Download (the APK link opened in the browser; Android's own installer then asks, as at the first install, and keeps the settings, the signing key being the same) and What's new (the release page). No download or install inside the app: that needs REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES, which Google Play restricts, and a Play build (98) must not update itself (Play does), so the check is built only into the GitHub APK (a CMake switch the Play build turns off). Linux: not published yet (91, own use); the same check works once a Linux build is a release asset with its own tag, until then off. Privacy: the request shows GitHub the device's address and the app's version; PRIVACY.md (and Play's Data safety later) says so, and the switch turns it off. Test: an own beta number below and equal to the latest (a development override), no network, GitHub answering 403 (rate limit), the notice's buttons on the OnePlus. No terms beyond GitHub's API rules. Size: small. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-20)*: MobileEngineUpdate.cpp (AETHER_MOBILE_UPDATE_CHECK, a CMake option on for Android only; OKSASDR_UPDATE_CHECK / _OWN_BETA / _URL for development): the first read 15 s after start, then every 24 h and on return to the front when the last read is over 12 h old, User-Agent "OksaSDR"; the tag android-beta-N against the app's own beta, the APK from the assets. UpdateOffer.qml (in Main.qml, over either page; never while transmitting; the page gets the keyboard back): *Android beta N is out* with Later (remembered per beta), What's new and Download (both open the browser). View › **New versions** On / Off, the last check in a line, *Download beta N*. Tested on this PC against GitHub and a stand-in: own beta 105 offered 106 (dialog, Later remembered, not offered again, the keyboard back on the page), 106 the newest, HTTP 403, another tag, no JSON, a refused connection; the View row at 1366x1024 and 393x852. Manual (13. Android › New versions, View), privacy (GitHub), README, the tester guide, Android notes. To verify on the OnePlus with beta 107 (the first beta that has it: it can only offer 108).

112. **Display:** AGC-A in the info line while AGC-T is on Auto (*in build 197*; chosen for it by the user 2026-11-20: "Lisää se seuraavan buildin listalle"), Tommy Pierson W6TPR **In short:** The info line shows AGC-A while AGC-T is on Auto (Tommy Pierson W6TPR). Released in build 197. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (mail 2026-11-20 17:35, iPhone 17 Pro Max, Aurora AU-510M over SmartLink: "when we put AGC in auto, can we have it say the little a letter instead of still saying whatever mode it's in ... I use auto when clicked on my radio and now AGC-A ... I use slow and mine still says AGC-S"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-20*: the info line's AGC word (InfoPanel.qml agcText, the only place the app shows the mode) is "AGC-" and the mode's letter (S / M / F, AGC-OFF). Engine.agcAuto already tells when AGC-T is automatic: radioOn (the radio sets it itself, SmartSDR 4.3's Auto AGC-T, slice agc_threshold_mode=auto, what Tommy's Aurora shows as AGC-A) and follow (the app's own AGC-T that follows the noise, roadmap 48, the same idea where the radio lacks it); the one-shot Auto measurement is not continuous and keeps the letter. Plan: AGC-A while radioOn or follow (not in FM, which has no AGC); the screen reader's name "AGC slow, AGC-T automatic"; a tap opens the AGC panel as now, where the mode and Auto show. The same five characters, so no width changes on any screen. Manual (the info line, the AGC panel). Test on the FLEX: Auto on and off in the AGC panel, Follow on and off, the mode changed meanwhile (the A stays, the panel shows the mode), FM, a phone and the iPad. No terms. Size: small. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-20)*: InfoPanel shows AGC-A while the radio's own Auto AGC-T is on (radioOn) or Follow actually follows; MobileEngine's Follow tick now says so (agcAuto.following: false while it waits for a measurement, measures, transmits, in FM or with AGC off), so Follow switched on before any measurement shows the mode's letter (found in the test: AGC-A had shown while Follow only waited); the screen reader hears "AGC fast, AGC-T automatic". Tested on the FLEX through the AGC panel with the receive audio on: Auto measured 56 (the knee) and the label stayed AGC-F; Follow following: AGC-A; AGC Off: AGC-OFF; fast again: AGC-A; FM: AGC-OFF; DIGU: AGC-A; Follow off: AGC-F. This FLEX has no Auto AGC-T of its own (radioAvailable false), so the radio's path is the same flag, not seen here. Put back: AGC fast, AGC-T 50, Follow off, the test's measurement and per-mode AGC let go.
113. **Display:** A layout the operator arranges: the desktop's Workspace Canvas on a touch screen (*in build 197*) **In short:** Own layouts: the operator arranges the page's blocks on a touch screen, as the desktop's Workspace Canvas (Larry Averbeck W9FLA). Released in build 197; his follow-ups became 116, 117 and 118, all released. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Larry Averbeck W9FLA, FB comment on the build 195 post, 2026-11-20 about 19:00: "AetherSDR has an option for a 'Workspace Canvas' where you can rearrange the display elements for a custom look", "A blank canvas where you can Drag, drop, resize and hide portions of your current display. Also, the ability to Save and recall the setups", "I use the Step buttons frequently but seldom the Band buttons. On a larger display, smaller Step buttons would be easier to use in one corner vs. spread across the full width. My Tab display sits on a shelf about chest high tilted back from 30 degrees from vertical. The ANT1 and STEP lines would be more easily accessible below the Waterfall"; his Galaxy Tab in landscape). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-20, second* (the user on the first, three fixed View settings: "Tuo sortaa täysin ideologiaa mikä oli tuossa ajatauksena eli mietippä uudelleen"): the idea is that the operator, not the app, decides where each part of the screen is and how big, hides what he does not use and keeps several arrangements. **The desktop's model** (RFC #4887, src/gui/workspace): items placed freely as fractions of the surface (NormRect, so a layout survives another window size), moved by a title strip and resized by edge and corner grips, snapped to the surface's edges and the other items' edges and centres, a minimum size per item, named workspaces saved without a save step (a debounced auto-commit), "Classic" the shipping layout as default and reset. Its rules are widget-free and tested headless upstream (CanvasLayout, CanvasInteraction applyDrag / snapping, WorkspaceGeometry, WorkspaceDocument; tests/workspace_*_test.cpp), so the app can build those four files and keep the same rules and the same document format. **In the app:** every part of the operating screen a block: Frequency (mode, step), Info line (ANT, width, AGC, speaker, lock, DSP), Band buttons, Slice row, each meter, Display (spectrum and waterfall), Step buttons, Macros, PTT, Controls (the pinned sections; later each section its own block, and Big buttons' − / +). Each block fills the room it is given and reflows into it (the steps as one row, two rows or a column; the bands wrapping; the info line one to three lines; a meter as an arc or a bar), and declares the smallest size its content fits: the editor never goes under it. **Editing:** View › Layout › Edit (also from the top row's menu): the blocks get frames, a block's title strip moves it, an edge or corner resizes it, snapping as on the desktop; × hides a block, + Add brings a hidden one back; Undo, Reset to Classic, Done. While editing nothing transmits or tunes (PTT, the steps and the display inert), and afterwards the page gets the keyboard back (Space PTT on an iPad). **Layouts:** Classic (today's pages, unchanged and the default) and the operator's own, named ("Shelf", "Car"), each with a landscape and a portrait arrangement, chosen in View › Layout, saved as they change, carried by App setups (17) and stored in the desktop's document format. **Tests:** each block alone at its minimum and at a large size (the render tool), the canvas rules by upstream's headless tests built here too, Classic as now; an arrangement nobody listed is then still made of blocks that fit. Screen readers: move and resize as accessible actions in steps. **Phases:** (1) the tablet in landscape (Larry's Galaxy Tab; iPad, Mac, Android tablets) with the blocks above, Classic and one own layout; (2) named layouts, portrait, phones, single control sections and meters as blocks; (3) several panadapters as blocks. The main work is OperatePage split into self-contained blocks (today they lean on the page: its compact, infoScale, the dialogs, the drawer). To ask Larry with a mock of the edit mode first. No terms. Size: large; phase 1 medium-large. (The first pre-analysis, three fixed View settings for the band row, the steps and the info line, is dropped: it decides for the operator.) *Phase 1 done in the dev build (2026-11-20, not in a build yet; the user: "Se että työtä on ei ongelma keskity vain miettimään ratkaisua sekä testaamaan")*: MobileWorkspace (C++, Engine.workspace) on the desktop's WorkspaceDocument / applyDrag / snapRect, built into the app from src/gui/workspace (WorkspaceGeometry, CanvasInteraction, WorkspaceDocument); one layout = a workspace, "main" its landscape and "portrait" its upright arrangement, block ids land:… / port:… (the document's item ids are workspace-wide), stored in MobileWorkspace (App setups carry it), written 750 ms after a change and when the app leaves the front. OperatePage: Classic (the grid) and LayoutCanvas side by side, the display, the pinned controls, Macros and PTT one instance each, moved between Classic's cell and their block (parent bindings), so the waterfall, a held PTT and their cost stay one; the light blocks (FrequencyBlock, ModeBlock, StepSizeBlock, InfoBlock, ButtonGridBlock for the bands and the steps, the slice row) made for the canvas, each filling its block and telling whether a size fits it (the steps 8 / 4 / 2 / 1 columns, the bands any, the info line one or two rows scaled from probes). A new layout or a new orientation starts from Classic measured on screen (Classic shown for a frame); Classic stays as it was (B/S only there). Edit (View › Layout › Edit / New, the top row's ⋯): frames over the blocks, a drag anywhere on a block moves it, handles straddling the corners and edge middles resize it, snapping to the canvas and the other blocks (the desktop's rules), a resize stops where the content still fits, × hides and + Add brings back, Undo (whole document, 50 steps), Reset (Classic again), Delete (asks), Done; the name in the toolbar renames. While editing nothing transmits or tunes (the blocks' content disabled, OperateKeys and Space PTT off, PTT and the CW key released). A block is never dropped on another: dragged over one it goes back to the last free place on its way (red while it would not stay; the desktop's Tidy, push-down only, made a full screen worse and is not used). Tested at 1340x800 (Larry's Tab) against the FLEX by finger drags: a new layout from Classic, the bands hidden, the slice row moved up and snapped, the display grown, the steps to a 4 × 2 block in the bottom-left corner, the info line and the step size under the display, Macros and PTT beside the steps (his arrangement), a too-small steps block stopped at its fit, Undo, rename, Delete; restarted: the layout back; Classic and the layout switched; PTT held while editing at 5 W: nothing sent; the same layout at 1366x1024, 1180x820, 1024x768 and 1440x900; upright 820x1180 Classic until edited, then its own; the controls hidden: the top row's Controls opens the drawer; Big buttons over a layout and back; the focus sweep and the keyboard back after Done, a dialog and Delete; a phone's View without the Layout section. Classic at 1340x800 the same as before the change. **Gesture tests, second round** (the user: "Onhan kaikki siirtelyt testattu aidosti?"; the first round had not tried the corners, every block's limit, a mouse, portrait or a rotation), each result read back in pixels by a new render-tool verb (layoutitems [land|port]): the display's four corners (the opposite edges stay, one edge snapped to the info line's), Undo four times back to the start; every block's limit (frequency 160, mode 60, step size where its type stays 12 px, info line where its scale stays 0.55, bands one row 34, slice row 320, display 240 × 120, controls 300, Macros 80, PTT 100). **Found and fixed:** two blocks sharing an edge had their handles in one place and the upper block took the other's resize (the bands' bottom moved the slice row; the display's corner went to the steps); now only the selected block (a tap or a drag selects it) shows handles, on top of the others. A fast drag past a limit was refused whole; the limit is now found between the last size taken and the finger (bisection), so a 150 ms fling stops the steps at their 8-in-a-row width. Then: the bands' bottom to 34 with the slice row untouched, the display's corner to its minimum with the steps untouched, an unselected block's handle not there to grab, a mouse corner drag (snapped), portrait: the steps' bottom stopped at the controls (no drop on a block), the steps' edge at their row limit, the info line and the mode dropped over another block went back to the last free place; a rotation while editing ends the edit, the keyboard back, landscape and portrait kept apart. **Third round, wider** (the user: "juuri tämän takia tärkeää, että testit tehdään mahdollisimman laajasti"): every block at its limit after Done, looked at: the display's own corner buttons met at 240 × 120 (now at least 300 × 160) and the info line at 55 % was too small to read and tap (now 70 %); the others fit (frequency 160, mode 60, step size 94 × 33, slice row 320 with bar meters, Macros 80, PTT 100). In the code: an App setup applied wrote the layout to the settings but the app kept the old one in memory and wrote it back at the next background; now the layouts are read again when a setup is applied, written before a setup is saved, and written only when changed. Tested through the Prof tab: a setup saved with a layout, Classic chosen, the setup applied: the layout back at once and still stored after the app went to the background; the test setup deleted through the app. FT8 / FT4 running hides PTT and Macros in a layout too (their place stays empty); two slices in the slice block (the second closed again); 130 % text (the info line's limit measured at that size); Split View 678 x 1024 and a foldable's 698 x 751 (upright: Classic until edited) and back to the layout; the radio left at 14.074 DIGU, 100 W, one slice. **Screen readers in Edit** (the user: "saadaanko tämä testattua?"): each block's title is its button ("Frequency block", "…, selected"; its description where it is in points) and selects it; the selected block gets an arrange bar of eight named buttons (Move left / up / down / right, narrower, wider, shorter, taller; 8 points a press, held to repeat; no snapping; the same limits as a drag), and every step is said (Accessible.announce): the new place, or why not ("at its smallest or the edge", "its content would not fit", "another block is there"); the toolbar buttons named for what they do ("Add a hidden block", "Undo the last change", "Reset to Classic's arrangement", "Delete layout", "Done editing the layout"). While editing the blocks' contents show as a picture (ShaderEffectSource) and are hidden, so a screen reader hears 28 elements (11 blocks, 11 hide buttons, the toolbar) instead of 147, and after Done the 116 controls as before. The render tool logs what is said (a11y on installs an update handler for announcements). Tested only by accessibility actions (a11ypress, as a VoiceOver or TalkBack double tap): New, selecting Steps (said where it is), shorter to its limit ("not moved"), up, narrower, right, Mode right until the info line ("another block is there"), wider refused there, Hide Frequency, + Add › Frequency, Undo, Done (the keyboard back), Edit, Delete layout › Delete. **Found and fixed:** the block's frame and its title shared one name, and the press went to the frame (nothing selected); + Add's menu stayed open after a screen reader's choice (its item was taken away with the list before the menu closed; now the menu closes first, for a finger too); "addMenu is not defined" from the menu's delegate. **With audio** (the dev VM's test sink had vanished, so the receive audio had nowhere to go: made permanent, see the dev notes; the render tool's own slice mute lifted for the test by a new plan verb, mute off / on): the receive audio measured from the sink (RMS 744 on 14.074, 0 before), and the blocks' contents in a layout driving the radio: +1k / −1k in the Steps block (14.074 → 14.075 → 14.074), 40m and 20m in the Bands block (and back to 14.074 DIGU), the info line's volume panel opened by a screen reader's press. **The rest** (the user: "Tee loput testit"): in a layout, every block's own controls against the FLEX: the frequency (its entry dialog), the mode and the step size (their choosers), the slice chip (its power panel), a tap on the display (tuned to 14.075), − / +, Band with the Bands block hidden (the bands chooser), Macros (its panel), a hold on the display (the notch panel beside it, no notch made), the arrows (±1 kHz), 3D in the display block (also as Edit's picture); two layouts switched (each its own blocks), Reset (Classic's arrangement again) and its Undo, Delete with another layout left (Classic shown, the other kept), Edit layout from the ⋯ menu in a narrow window. **A fault of the render tool, and an unintended transmission:** its keys verb did not know <Right> and typed the letters onto the page, whose T key toggles TUNE: a tune carrier at the tune power (10 W) on 14.080 MHz for about 1-2 s, then off (the radio checked: receive, tune=0). Fixed in the tool: the arrows are named keys now, an unknown <name> is refused, and text is typed only into a text field (else the page's letter keys would act: T tune, A ATU). Not tested: a real device's touch and VoiceOver / TalkBack themselves (the platform bridges; only Qt's accessibility interface here), two fingers at once, a Mac trackpad, the Android and iOS compiles (no Qt for Android on this PC; CI): these need a build on the devices (a preview), and the developer link has no gesture or plan command yet to run these tests there. Open: the manual (View › Layout, pictures), the Android and iOS builds (CI), a screen reader's move and resize steps, a mock for Larry. Follow-ups after the release, Larry Averbeck W9FLA (FB comment on the build 197 post, 2026-11-21 about 03:00, with a photo of his first layout on his Tab: the frequency centred on top, the slice row between the two meters, a tall display, Macros, the info line, the step size, six steps and PTT in the bottom row; "The quick implementation and ease of use is beyond my expectations!"): (a) "break up the Slices and Meters entry into Meter 1, Meter 2 and another window to view the slices"; (b) "Allow the slice panels to be displayed in one line vs. the two over two. The first two are displayed OK, but the second two are truncated and the next four are hidden behind the pan": with more slices the Slices block's SliceBar wraps into rows taller than the block and runs under the display: a defect of the block (the canvas only; Classic's slice row is its own), to fix first, e.g. one line in the block with the slices narrower or scrolled sideways, never drawn outside the block; (c) "Consider adding an option for a Light version for my old eyes": a light theme changes how Classic looks, so it is a variant (a View setting, Classic's dark the default); approved by the user 2026-11-21 as a choice: "Vaalea teema pitää vaihtoehto tummalle eli käyttäjä voi valita". Pre-analyses to do (b first, then a, then the light theme).
114. **Display:** Hide controls and Controls in the top row also in an own layout (*in build 198*) **In short:** In an own layout the top row's Hide controls and Controls work as in Classic, so the controls can be closed there too (your finding on the iPad). Released in build 198. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-21, with build 197 open on the iPad: "mistä saan controls ikkunan suljettua", then "Niin pitää ehdottomasti korjata"). Pre-analysis: in Classic the top row's Hide controls hides the pinned panel and Controls opens the drawer, whose Pin Controls pins it again (Engine.controlsVisible). In an own layout with its Controls block the top button was hidden (build 197), so the controls went away only through the editor's ×. Same model as Classic, on the layout: Hide controls closes the layout's Controls block (its place kept, the other blocks not moved), Controls then opens the drawer, and the drawer's Pin Controls shows the block again where it was; if that place is now taken (or the block was never placed: Classic's controls were a drawer when the layout was made), Pin Controls opens the editor with the block selected to place it, never drawing it over another block. Each orientation keeps its own. Classic's behaviour unchanged (classic-check). Tests: hide / drawer / pin in landscape and portrait, the never-placed block, restart, the screen reader's names, the keyboard back on the page, Classic's own flow. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-21)*: hiding first left a hole where the block was, so Hide controls now gives its room to the blocks beside it as Classic gives the panel's width to the display (MobileWorkspace::hideAndFill): the side is the one where the display takes the room in its lane, else the one whose blocks cover the block's edge most; a block grows only within the hidden block's span, and where the display is behind a neighbour in its lane the display grows and the blocks between move (portrait: the display taller, the steps below it); what grew or moved is stored with the layouts (MobileWorkspaceFill) and Pin Controls (or + Add in the editor) puts it back unless edited since. Tested on the FLEX by the controls' own names, read back in pixels: 1366x1024 (Classic's arrangement): hide: bands, slices, display, steps and PTT 964 -> 1338 wide; pin: all back exactly; restart in between: hidden and grown kept; the display edited while hidden (1322 wide): pin keeps its new size, restores the rest, and as the controls' place is now taken opens the editor with Controls selected ("Controls selected, left 978..."); the controls shrunk into the bottom-right corner (978,500 360x444): only steps and PTT grow (the display spans past it), back on pin; a layout made while Classic's controls were hidden (the block never placed): Controls opens the drawer, Pin Controls opens the editor with it selected; 1024x1366 (Classic's portrait, the controls under the display): the display 236 -> 824 high and the steps moved below it (546 -> 1134), PTT and Macros unchanged, back on pin; Classic's own Hide controls / Controls / Pin Controls as before; the screen reader reads Hide controls / Controls; the page keeps the keyboard throughout. The waterfall fills a taller display over a few seconds, in Classic alike.
115. **Display:** The drawer's × in landscape without Pin Controls took a row of its own, otherwise empty (*in build 198*) **In short:** On its side without Pin Controls the drawer's × no longer takes a row of its own; it sits in the first row of tabs (your finding on the foldable's inner screen). Released in build 198. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-21, on the Android foldable's inner screen: "androidissa se controlsin sulkeva ruksi tuo yhden koko rivin lisää controlssiin ja rivi jää muuten kokonaan tyhjäksi"; build 191's 108). Pre-analysis: a tablet-layout window too narrow to pin the controls (the OnePlus Open's inner screen 751x698 in landscape, Split View, small iPads) shows the drawer with the × alone in the top row. Putting it into the section buttons' grid cut "FreeDV" (the cells narrower) or, as a cell of its own, made 5 + 5 + 3 rows (no row saved); so the × sits beside the first row of buttons and the drawer is 48 px wider (Theme.touchTarget + 4) there, the buttons keeping their width and rows. A change to Classic asked by the user. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-21)*, tested by measurement and pictures: 751x698 the grid 347 wide in 6 + 6 rows, the × at its end (was 3 rows), every label whole, the × closes the drawer; with the Amp tab 5 + 5 + 3 and the × in the first row; 698x668 and 930x864 two rows; 1180x820 (Pin Controls fits) and portrait (Close at the bottom) unchanged.
116. **Display:** The Slices block shows the slices in one line and never draws outside itself (*in build 198*) **In short:** The Slices block of an own layout can show the slices in one line and never draws outside itself; what does not fit scrolls sideways (Larry Averbeck W9FLA). Released in build 198. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Larry Averbeck W9FLA, 113 (b), FB comment on the build 197 post: "Allow the slice panels to be displayed in one line vs. the two over two. The first two are displayed OK, but the second two are truncated and the next four are hidden behind the pan"). Pre-analysis: the block holds Classic's SliceBar, whose chips are two columns that grow downward (two over two, four over four); Classic's slice row grows with them, a block has a fixed height, so the third and later chips ran over the display (z under it). In a block the chips go in one line when the width gives each at least the chip's least width (150 px on a tablet), else in as many rows as the block's height holds at the chips' least height (34 px), else in one line that scrolls sideways; clipped to the block always. The chip itself (frequency, mode, the TX tag with the power, the power slider, the slice menu, the off-band outline, the screen reader's name) becomes a component of its own (SliceChip) that SliceBar and the block share, so Classic's chips stay exactly as they are (classic-check). *Done in the dev build (2026-11-21)*: SliceChip (Classic's chip, unchanged by classic-check and by pixels) in SlicesBlock: tested on the FLEX with this app's slices and more put in by status lines with its own client handle (the render tool's status verb takes $OWN): 3 slices in a 534-wide block one line of 175 px; 8 slices 3 + 3 + 2 rows of 175 x 42 inside its 135 px; the block lowered to 68 px: one line of 150 px clipped at its edge, a sideways drag scrolled D..F into view; nothing drawn outside the block in any.
117. **Display:** Meter 1, Meter 2 and the slices as blocks of their own (*in build 198*) **In short:** In own layouts Meter 1, Meter 2 and the slices are three blocks of their own in place of one Slices and meters block (Larry Averbeck W9FLA). Released in build 198. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Larry Averbeck W9FLA, 113 (a): "break up the Slices and Meters entry into Meter 1, Meter 2 and another window to view the slices"). Pre-analysis: three blocks replace "Slices and meters" in own layouts: Meter 1 (the left meter, its pair or two bars as View sets it), Meter 2 (the right one), Slices (116). Each meter sizes its face from its block (the dial's height from the block's, the width capped by it) and offers what the meter offers on Classic (a tap for the meter's chooser). A new layout takes them from Classic's slice row (the meters' and the chips' own places). Layouts made with build 197 keep their look: the first time a surface with the old combined block is shown, it is split into the three at the places the meters and chips had in it, measured on an unseen SliceBar of that size (not guessed), and stored. Classic unchanged. To test: 1, 2, 4 and more slices (status lines put in where the radio has no more), one line / rows / scrolling, the meters' styles (analog, bar, two, analog2) in their blocks and their limits, the split of a 197 layout in both orientations, the screen reader's names, Classic by classic-check. *Done in the dev build (2026-11-21)*: MeterBlock (the face's height from the block, as wide as that face needs, scaled down only where the block is narrower; at the top as on Classic), the three blocks seeded from Classic's slice row (SliceBar's leftMeterItem, chipAreaItem, rightMeterItem) and build 197's block split on first show (splitBlock, measured on an unseen SliceBar). Tested: a new layout from Classic is pixel for pixel Classic's slice row (the meters 208 x 135 and the chip 210 x 55 at the same places, landscape 1366x1024 and portrait 1024x1366; only the live needle differs); a stored build 197 layout split into meter1 0,182 208x135, slices 215,182 534x135, meter2 756,182 208x135, as a new one, in landscape and in portrait (0,168 / 215 566 wide / 788), stored; Hide controls (114) now lets the slice chips take the row's room and moves Meter 2 to the right edge as Classic does (a lane's display, else its slice chips, take the room); the meter styles bar / two / analog2 in the blocks (analog2 at 208 px two bars, as Classic's rule); the screen reader's "Meter 1 block", "Slices block", "Meter 2 block" and their Hide buttons.
118. **Display:** A light theme beside the dark one, the user's choice (*in build 199*) **In short:** A light theme beside the dark one, chosen in Setup › App › Theme (Larry Averbeck W9FLA; your condition: a choice, with dark as the default). Released in build 199. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Larry Averbeck W9FLA, 113 (c), FB comment on the build 197 post: "Consider adding an option for a Light version for my old eyes"; the user 2026-11-21: "Vaalea teema pitää vaihtoehto tummalle eli käyttäjä voi valita", chosen for build 199: "1 ja 2"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-21:* the colours are Theme.qml's tokens (background #0f1419, surface, surfaceRaised, text, textDim, accent, transmit, the tags, warning, passband; the buttons by Engine.buttonStyle, "lit" by default and no longer offered in the UI), used 619 times, but 130 colour literals sit in 21 QML files (PanadapterView 27, DigiControls 22, AnalogGauge 10, RadioSetupPage 8, LoggingSetup 8, NetworkBadge 7, InfoPanel 7, BarGauge 5 ...) and 26 named colours / Qt.rgba, Main.qml sets the Controls palette (menus, popups, text fields), and C++ draws the spectrum's axis band and grid (SpectrumItem) and the 3D view's background. Plan: Controls › View › **Theme**: *Dark* (the default, as now) and *Light*, switched at once and remembered (AppSettings MobileTheme; in App setups too). Theme.qml gets the light set of the same tokens and new tokens for the literals that are not the display's (the status greens "#00cc66", the network and CPU colours, the white text on red / blue tags, Main.qml's palette, the meters' faces); every dark value stays exactly as now, so Classic and every dark screen stay pixel-identical (classic-check at all sizes is the proof). Stays dark in Light: the display (spectrum, waterfall, 3D and what is drawn on them: slices, filters, notches, spots, the FTx and digi overlays, the axis band), as a radio's screen: the waterfall's palette is made on black, SmartSDR's is dark too, and its overlay colours stay readable without a second set. Follows the theme: the frequency, the info line, the rows and buttons, the meters (a light face with a dark scale and needle, like a panel meter), the slice chips, the panels and the drawer, dialogs and menus, Radio Setup, Connect, Log, Big buttons, App setups and the manual viewer. Contrast: text 4.5:1 and large text / icons 3:1 against the light surfaces, measured from the shots; the status colours get darker light variants (a green that reads on white); the red TX and blue slice tags keep white text (4.5:1 already). The system bars: dark icons on a light page (iOS status bar style, Android's light status / navigation bars), checked on the devices. Not in this step: *Follow the system* (Qt's colour scheme hint, later on request) and a light display. Tests: classic-check (Dark unchanged, 27 sizes, DIGU and USB); the same sweep in Light at every size with the panel plan and the focus sweep (every drawer tab, the pinned panel, popups, dialogs, Radio Setup pages, Big buttons, Log, Connect), side by side with Dark, every text's contrast read as numbers; switching back and forth on the page and after a restart; the screen readers' names unchanged; the manual: a Theme paragraph under View and two Light pictures (the operating page, the View tab). Size: medium to large (about 21 QML files and Main.qml's palette; no C++ while the display stays dark). *Done in the dev build (635eb649e)*: Theme.qml's light set and a fixed dark set (Theme.dark) for the display; PanadapterView's items on the display (19 uses) on the dark set, its four popups following the theme; the meters' faces repainted with dark ink on a light face; the info line, the network badge, the press highlights, Main.qml's palette and the status greens (25, now Theme.good) follow; the drawers opaque in Light (the page's text showed through at 0.96); Light asks Qt for the light colour scheme (QStyleHints::setColorScheme: iOS's status bar dark on the light page), Dark asks nothing as before; the View tab's Theme row placed before Text size; the render tool's theme verb (also in link plans) and render-sweep.py --theme. Contrast computed for every light token against the page, panels, raised rows and buttons: text 13.7-18.2, dim text 5.4-7.2, accent 5.3-7.1, red 5.0-6.7, amber 5.0-6.7, green 5.0-6.6, white on the blue / red tags 4.9-5.0. Rendered on the FLEX in Light with no QML warning: the page at 390x844 and 1366x1024, all twelve Controls tabs in the phone's drawer, the band, mode, AGC, filter and meter choosers, Radio Setup, the stations dialog, Macros, Big buttons, the layout editor (a copy made and deleted), the Connect page; the switch back to Dark at once. Manual: a Light theme section with its picture (theme-light), Theme in the View tab's list; README feature and status rows; TestFlight and Android beta 110 notes. classic-check against build 198 (after its fix, 6be92169c: the first run had swept build 198's QML twice, found by a light sweep that came out dark): layout decisions 0, panels moved 0, masks live content plus the Theme row itself where the pinned View tab reaches it (2560x1375; a shorter scroll bar at 1440x900, 1710x1045, 1728x1050), approved. The light sweep at all 27 sizes (render-sweep --theme light): no QML warning, layout decisions identical to Dark's (layout-diff 0 / 0), every size light around the dark display, the bar meters (844x390) with dark scale numbers on a light track. On the Android emulator (Android 15, the preview APK, run 136; the system in its light mode) with MobileTheme=light: the Connect page light, the status bar's clock, Wi-Fi and battery dark and readable, the navigation handle dark (the light colour scheme reaches Android's bars). The operating page could not be shown there: connecting started the display, whose float16 conversion the emulator's ARM translation aborts on this host ("CHECK failed: host_platform::kHasF16C", libndk_translation; an emulator limit, not the app's). Found on the way, not from 118: in Dark with the system in light mode the status bar's icons are dark on the dark page (the emulator's first screen; the same on iOS by its default style); a fix would ask the system for its dark scheme in Dark too, a change to Dark's own look, so a separate item to decide. On the user's iPad (preview build 199 over the developer link, 20:42-20:46; the user had set Light by hand at 20:40): the theme switched both ways on the device; the screen reader's elements 125 named, 0 unnamed in Dark and Light; pictures (Metal) of the light page with the 3D display, the View tab's Theme row, the band, AGC and meter choosers, Radio Setup, Dark again and Light again: every panel light and readable, the display dark, Dark as before; transmitting blocked during the plan and lifted after. The iOS status bar cannot be in an app's picture; Qt's light colour scheme sets the window's overrideUserInterfaceStyle (qiostheme.mm), which UIKit's default status bar style follows, the same request that turned Android's bars dark on the emulator.
119. **Devices:** Android logs the keys and the push-to-talk broadcasts the app does not use, so a handset's own PTT key can be read from a log (*in Android beta 110*) **In short:** Android's log lists the keys and push-to-talk broadcasts the app does not use, so a handset's own PTT key can be read from a tester's log (Rob Metallo VA3MET's UNIWA F400). Released in Android beta 110; it led to 120 and 121. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (41, Rob Metallo VA3MET's UNIWA F400 on Android 15: its key works with Zello, QSO 1 and Pocket SDR; chosen for build 199: "1 ja 2"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-21:* a handset's PTT key reaches apps as a key event (often a vendor key code or KEYCODE_UNKNOWN with a scan code) or as a broadcast that push-to-talk apps register for; Zello documents com.zello.ptt.down / com.zello.ptt.up and com.zello.intent.buttonExtra1.down / .up for handset makers (support.zello.com, hardware partner integration). OksaActivity.dispatchKeyEvent passes today's keys on (the Ulanzi dial's only while it is on) and logs none. Plan, logging only, nothing keyed: (a) every key event the app does not use logged once per press (key code and its name, scan code, the device's name and vendor / product, down / up, repeat), the first 40 of a run, then a count; (b) a receiver registered while the app runs (RECEIVER_EXPORTED on Android 13+) for Zello's actions and the handset actions in common use (to be listed from the vendors' and the PoC apps' public documents before coding), logging the action and its extras. Rob then presses the key once and sends Log › Send to the developer; the PTT itself is the next step (41 (a)'s learned *PTT key*, off by default, under Transmit from the controller). Tests: the render tool cannot run the Java side and no Android device is attached here, so on the user's OnePlus with the beta: keys the app does not use (the volume keys while the dial is off, a Bluetooth keyboard's F-keys if one is at hand) must show their lines in the devlink log, and Space PTT and the Ulanzi dial work as before; the receiver's registration logged at start. iOS: unchanged. Size: small. *Done in the dev build (d8013beeb)*: OksaActivity logs keys from devices other than a full keyboard and keys Android cannot name (down / up, key code and name, scan code, the device's name, vendor:product, built-in or external) and a receiver (RECEIVER_EXPORTED on Android 13+) for com.zello.ptt.down / up, com.zello.intent.buttonExtra1.down / up, android.intent.action.PTT.down / up (RugGear and MediaTek handsets), com.sonim.intent.action.PTT_KEY_DOWN / UP and com.symbol.button.L2, the first 40 lines of a run, into the app's log through a native registered from C++ (android/KeyLog.cpp; until then logcat); nothing keyed. Java compile-checked against android.jar (API 36) with a QtActivity stub; the desktop build passes. On the emulator (Android 15, x86_64 with arm64 translation) with the preview's CI APK (Android run 136): "key log: on ... listening for 9 push-to-talk broadcasts" at start, all nine actions sent with adb am broadcast logged with their extras (source=test), KEYCODE_UNKNOWN logged, Space from the virtual keyboard not logged; but the emulator's own volume key was not, as its hardware keys come from "AT Translated Set 2 keyboard", a full keyboard (type 2), which the first rule skipped wholesale. A handset may report its buttons the same way, so the rule is now: a full keyboard's typing keys (letters, digits, punctuation, Space, Tab, Enter, arrows, editing keys, modifiers, F1-F12, Esc) are not logged, every other key is (volume, media, camera, headset, vendor codes). Retested on the emulator with Android run 137: "key log: on (every key but a keyboard's typing keys); listening for 9 push-to-talk broadcasts"; all nine broadcasts logged with an int extra (keycode=999); KEYCODE_UNKNOWN, VOLUME_UP, CAMERA, HEADSETHOOK and MEDIA_PLAY_PAUSE logged (from the virtual keyboard, a full keyboard), each still handed on (the system's volume dialog showed); Space, A, F1 and DPAD_LEFT not logged. The emulator console's own key events (emu event send) reach no app in a window-less emulator, so they say nothing either way. The OnePlus and Rob's F400 with the released beta.
120. **Fixes:** A held F-key **In short:** A held F-key (or ⌘ / Ctrl with a digit) starts its voice macro, CW memory or Digi macro once, not again with every repeat of the key (found in Rob Metallo VA3MET's log). Released in build 200 / Android beta 111. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (or ⌘ / Ctrl with a digit) no longer starts the voice keyer, a CW memory or a Digi macro again with each auto-repeat (*in build 200 / Android beta 111*) (Rob Metallo VA3MET's F400 log, 2026-11-21: its PTT key is F2, and one long press sent "dvk playback_start id=2" 32 times; slot 2 was empty, so nothing went on air). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-21:* OperateKeys.qml and the Log tab's fields pass event.isAutoRepeat to Engine.operateKey, which only skips its log line and the macro-repeat cancel for a repeat, then plays the slot again (dvkPlay toggles start / stop; sendCwMemory queues the memory again). Plan: a repeat is taken (the key stays the app's) and does nothing. Tests: the render tool's keys with auto-repeat on the FLEX, receive only: F2 held in a voice mode, a CW memory held in CW, a Digi macro held in RTTY: one start each, the log's lines counted; Esc still stops. Size: small. *Done in the dev build:* Engine.operateKey takes a held key's auto-repeat and does nothing (F1–F12 and ⌘ / Ctrl with a digit only; the arrows and the rest return before it, so a held arrow still tunes on). *Tests on the FLEX* (the render tool's keyhold: the press, an auto-repeat every 50 ms, the release; mode, power and frequency put back after each run): CW, F1 held 2 s with the transmit block on: one refusal (a short press: one); USB, F2 held 2 s: handled once ("key F2 -> voice keyer"; the radio's ten voice keyer slots are all empty, duration 0, so nothing could go on air); RTTY, F1 held 2 s: one "text TX refused" for 40 repeats. Esc's path is unchanged (not a repeat).
121. **Radio:** A learned *PTT key*: any key a button sends becomes PTT, held as long as the key (*in build 200 / Android beta 111*) **In short:** A PTT key of your own: Setup › Transmit › PTT key › Learn takes any key a button sends (a handset's side key, a Bluetooth button, a foot switch) as PTT (Rob Metallo VA3MET, Rich Yost N2RY). Released in build 200 / Android beta 111. A phone's volume keys and a headset's button are 128. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (41 (a); Rob Metallo VA3MET's UNIWA F400, whose side key sends F2 from its keypad "mtk-kpd", press and release both; Rich Yost N2RY's Bluetooth button). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-21:* Space is PTT today on the page (OperateKeys.qml; GCKeyboard's held Space on iOS); a key other than Space reaches the page's handler as itself (F2 = the voice keyer's slot 2). An application-wide key filter (as the Mac's key filters) takes the learned key before anything else, wherever the keyboard is (a drawer open, a text field): press = PTT on (setPtt with the reason "PTT key", so the transmit lock and the rest hold it as PTT), release = off, its auto-repeat ignored; *Tap on / off* toggles on the press as Space does; the key no longer acts as itself (F2 no longer plays slot 2). Not yet: Android's F2 from the emulator's keyboard (the preview's APK), Rob's F400 (the beta). *Verified 2026-11-22 on Rob's F400 (Android 15, beta 112, his log by mail 16:25):* the learned key kept after the update ("PTT key: F2" at start); four transmissions by the side key, each key down → "xmit 1" within 35 ms and the built-in microphone streaming, key up → "xmit 0" at once, 65-85 W peak, no key left down; DAX transmit was on and the app turned it off so the phone's microphone was heard, as designed.

123. **Logging:** A contact deleted or edited in the log is deleted or changed in the **shared ADIF** file too (*in build 200 / Android beta 111*) **In short:** A contact edited or deleted in the Log tab is changed or deleted in the shared ADIF file too (Christoph Steinert DM5HF). Released in build 200 / Android beta 111; Christoph confirmed it. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (78; Christoph Steinert DM5HF, mail 2026-11-21 19:09). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-21:* MobileEngineSharedAdif.cpp only appends (a queue of ADIF records, flushed on a worker thread; iOS through the security-scoped file, Android through the ContentResolver, "wa" or a whole rewrite where a provider cannot append); deleteQso / editQso change the app's own log only. Plan: deleteQso and editQso queue an operation for the shared file (delete ORIGINAL / replace ORIGINAL with NEW, kept with the pending appends and in the same order); the flush applies it by reading the file, finding the record that matches the original (CALL, QSO_DATE, the first four digits of TIME_ON, BAND, MODE; the first such record from the end), removing it or putting the new record in its place, and writing the whole file back (the same writers as today's rewrite), re-read just before the write so a record another program added meanwhile stays; no match = nothing changed, said in the status line and the log. Tests here: a local ADIF file in the render tool's run (the desktop path): log three QSOs, edit one, delete one, the file read back record by record; a record another program added between stays; Android's ContentResolver path on the emulator with a file in the Downloads provider. Size: medium. *Done in the dev build:* QsoLog::replaceInAdif (the app's own log's replaceRecord now uses it too, unchanged) with a loose match for the shared file, where an edit changes only the fields the edit changed in the file's record (the other program's own fields, QSL states and the time's seconds stay); deleteQso / editQso queue the change (sharedAdifChange) with the appends, in order and kept over a restart; the flush applies one at a time, re-reads the file and says in the status line whether it was done or the contact is not in the file; the edit dialog says the shared file follows, and its list of logbooks not changed no longer names it. *Tests* (the desktop path, an isolated home, a file written as another program would: a header text, lower-case tags on one record, six-digit times, FREQ 14.2, APP_ fields, LOTW_QSL_RCVD, QSL_SENT): K1ABC's comment and report edited: only those fields changed, the lower-case record found, QSL_SENT and the seconds kept; W1AW's time and name edited: the APP_ field and LoTW state kept; DL1XYZ's name changed to Jörg: the UTF-8 lengths right (every tag's length checked); DL1XYZ deleted: only that record and its line break gone; OH2XX (not in the file): the file unchanged, the status said so; G4ABC (the other program's own) stayed; a deletion made with the file away waited ("1 wait") over a restart and was done when the file came back. Not yet: Android's ContentResolver rewrite ("wt", as today's rewrite for a provider that cannot append) on the emulator with the preview; iOS uses the same file path as the appends. *On Android* (the user's phone, beta 111, 2026-11-22, the shared file in the phone's own storage, the log shared there first: 17937 QSOs): OH8NW/P's comment edited and edited back: "shared ADIF: edit OH8NW/P 20261122 0926 done" both times, 17937 read after each and with Read now (the ContentResolver rewrite; which mode, "wt" or "w", is not logged); from Google Drive the file could not be chosen at all (129). *On a device:* Christoph's Mac with build 200 (mail 2026-11-22): "Delete contact deletes also the contact in the shared log - perfect!"; RUMlog's own log keeps it ("this is clear to me": what RUMlog has taken in, by the WSJT-X UDP message or an import, is its own; that message has no deletion).

127. **Fixes:** Closing a slice closes its panadapter too when no other slice is left on it (*in build 202 / Android beta 112*) **In short:** Close slice also closes the slice's panadapter when no other slice is on it, so empty panadapters no longer hold the radio's four (your finding on the iPad); and when the radio has none to give, the app says why. Released in build 202 / Android beta 112. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-22, the iPad with build 200: three slices closed left three empty panadapters, hidden as the app shows one; they held the FLEX-6600M's four panadapters, and the Android phone could not connect: its "display panafall create" was refused, 0x50000009, and it showed only "The radio allows 4 panadapters"; the user: "aina kun suljetaan pitäisi panitkin mennä kiinni"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-22:* MobileEngine::removeSlice sends only "slice remove"; the panadapter stays, as in SmartSDR and the desktop (there by design, #3086: a click on an empty panadapter makes a slice there), but the desktop shows every panadapter with its own ×, while the app shows one: an empty one is invisible, streams to the device and holds a slot, and only *Close N other pans* frees it. Plan: (a) removeSlice remembers the slice's panadapter; when the radio confirms the slice gone (sliceRemoved) and none of the app's slices is left on it and it is not the app's last panadapter, RadioModel::removePanadapter (the "display pan remove" + "display panafall remove" pair; only this station's panadapters are in the model). A split's transmit slice shares its panadapter, so split off closes nothing more. (b) A refused panadapter says why and what to do, as the slice limit's message: the radio's panadapters are in use by other stations (another device, SmartSDR): close one there, then connect again. Tests (the FLEX, receive only): four slices on four panadapters, three closed: one panadapter left (panCount 1), the slot free for a second station; a slice closed that shares its panadapter: the panadapter stays; the shown slice closed when alone on the shown panadapter: the app moves to another and the old panadapter closes; split on and off; the last slice still refused; the iPad and Android with the preview. Size: small. *The user chose (a) for build 201* (2026-11-22: "a"); (b) waits. *Done in the dev build:* MobileEngine::removeSlice remembers the slice's panadapter (m_closingSlicePans, cleared at each connection), and the sliceRemoved handler, after selectActiveSlice, closes it (closeEmptiedPanadapter) unless it is gone, the last, the shown one or a slice is still on it; logged "slice N closed: its panadapter X closed too". The render tool's slices line now gives each slice's panadapter and the count. *Tests* (the render tool against the FLEX, 1366×1024, receive only; the radio restored the tool's own earlier slices on connect): A and B on one panadapter, C on another shown: A closed: both panadapters stay (2), no close; B closed, alone on the hidden one: it closed ("display pan remove" + "display panafall remove"), 1 left, the radio's free panadapters 1 → 2; + Pan, its slice shown and alone closed: the app moved to C, that panadapter closed, 1 left, free 1 → 2; C, the last: its menu; two panadapters, SPLIT on (the TX slice beside B on its panadapter) and off: both stay (2), nothing closed. Not here: the menu's Close slice on an iPad or Android device (the same Engine.removeSlice), a slice closed by another station (not ours to close). *(b) done in the dev build:* panadapterLimitReached says, when the app has no panadapter yet, "The radio did not give this app a panadapter. Its %1 panadapters may all be in use by other programs or your other devices (SmartSDR, another OksaSDR): close one there, then connect again.", else "The radio allows %1 panadapters, and all are in use (here or by other programs)."; the manual's trouble table has the row. Not provoked on the radio: it takes a third station while four panadapters are held, and the FLEX takes two GUI programs.
128. **Radio:** The *PTT key* from the phone's own volume keys and a Bluetooth headset's button on Android **In short:** On Android the PTT key cannot be learned from the phone's volume keys or a Bluetooth headset's button (your test with beta 111). The cause is known: Qt leaves the volume keys to the system, and Android gives a headset's button to a media session, which the app does not have. You asked for the volume keys and a headset's call button to be analysed as a whole; that analysis is not finished. **From you:** nothing now; when the analysis is done you get the options (which keys, hold or tap, what App Store review allows) to pick from. Size: medium. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-22, testing 121 on his Android 16 phone with beta 111: "mikään nappi ei rekisteröidy ei bluetooth kuulokkeista tai volume"). Found: the activity's key log has every volume press (KEYCODE_VOLUME_UP / DOWN, "gpio-keys" and "pmic_resin", down and up), but Learn never gets them: Qt for Android leaves the volume keys to the system (its QT_ANDROID_VOLUME_KEYS switch), so they never reach the window's key events the PTT key filter takes; the headset's button is not even in the key log: Android gives media buttons to a media session, and the app has none. Rob's F400 side key (F2 from the keypad) does reach the app, as his log showed. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-22:* (a) volume keys: OksaActivity's dispatchKeyEvent hands a volume key to the engine natively (as the Ulanzi dial's keys) while Learn waits or when it is the learned key, and takes it (the volume then stays); the rest of the time the volume keys stay the volume. (b) a headset's button: a media session for the radio's audio (the foreground service already plays it) whose media-button callback gives KEYCODE_HEADSETHOOK / MEDIA_PLAY_PAUSE with down and up; many headsets send both at the release, so Tap on / off is the mode for them. The user's headset: a press did nothing at all on the phone (2026-11-22), while the app had the headset's microphone open ("TX stream ... device: Bluetooth", the Mic meter's check): with its microphone in use the headset is on the call profile (HFP), whose button goes to the phone's call handling, not to a media session; only on the media profile (the app's sound to the headset, the microphone the phone's own) does play / pause reach one. A second try with the Controls closed and the microphone closed ("mic check: microphone closed", 11:11:02), Learn waiting, two of the headset's buttons: still nothing in the key log; the app's sound went to the headset on the hands-free route too ("audio output Auto -> Bluetooth (hands-free ...)"), so the headset stayed on the call profile and its buttons went to the phone's call handling. So (b) needs more than a media session: the media route (A2DP) for the sound, or a self-managed call (Android's Telecom ConnectionService, as calling apps get a headset's answer / hang-up); to analyse apart, after (a). iOS: Apple keeps the volume keys; a headset's play / pause comes as one toggle command (MPRemoteCommandCenter), so only Tap on / off there, later. Tests: the user's phone (both volume keys, his Bluetooth headset), learn, hold, release, the volume unchanged while learned and back after Clear, the app in the background (nothing keyed). *The user, 2026-11-22:* 128 apart, analysed as a whole: a headset's answer / hang-up button is the same on any headset, and the volume-down key as PTT would work on most phones, with the feel of a handheld radio in the hand. To analyse together: (a) the volume keys; (b) the headset's call button through the call profile (Android's Telecom self-managed call, iOS's CallKit, and what App Store review says of a call that is not one); the Tap on / off and hold modes; the app in the background. Size: medium. Not chosen yet.
129. **Fixes:** A file from Google Drive **In short:** On Android a file from Google Drive, OneDrive, Dropbox or Nextcloud can be chosen: the shared ADIF file, an ADIF import, an app setup, a MIDI mapping (your finding with Drive). Released in build 202 / Android beta 112. **From you:** nothing; a word if choosing a cloud file ever fails on your phone. **Details:** (or OneDrive, Dropbox, Nextcloud) can be chosen on Android: the shared ADIF file, an ADIF import, an app setup, a MIDI mapping (*in build 202 / Android beta 112*) (the user, 2026-11-22, beta 111 on his Android 16 phone: the shared ADIF file chosen from Drive left "No file chosen", twice, and Import contacts (ADIF) with the same file did nothing: "ADIF import: cannot open QUrl(\"\")"; the log: "QML FileDialog: Cannot set  as a selected file because it doesn't exist"). Found in Qt 6.12's sources: QML FileDialog's setSelectedFiles (qquickfiledialog.cpp) refuses a file QQmlFile::urlToLocalFileOrQrc gives no path for, and on Android that function gives one for a content: URL only from the phone's own storage (com.android.externalstorage.documents, the Downloads and media providers, qqmlfile.cpp's isLocalContentAuthority); a cloud provider's URL (Drive's com.google.android.apps.docs.storage) gets none, so the choice is dropped and onAccepted gets an empty URL. The imports that worked (beta 33's 19331 contacts, beta 73's app setup) were files on the phone. iOS and the Mac are not affected (Christoph's shared file on the Mac works). The shared ADIF file's Drive check, open since build 143, is how this shows. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-22:* on Android the four Choose / Import buttons open Android's own picker (ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT from OksaActivity, its result handed to C++ natively as the content: URL), not QML's FileDialog; the engine's setSharedAdif, importAdif, importAppSetup and midiImport take the URL as today (QFile reads any content: URL through Qt's Android content engine; the shared file's lasting grant and ContentResolver writes as now). Elsewhere QML's FileDialog stays. Tests: the emulator (a file in Downloads through the new picker, each of the four); the user's phone with the preview: the shared ADIF file from Drive (chosen, read, an edit and its undo rewriting it, Read now's count unchanged), an ADIF import from Drive (0 new, all already in the log). The rewrite then logs its mode, and after a "w" (a provider that does not truncate) reads the file's length back: a shorter rewrite would otherwise leave the old end behind (Drive is where that matters; the phone's storage takes "wt"). Size: small to medium. Not chosen yet. *Done in the dev build:* MobileEnginePicker.cpp (pickDocument, documentPickedNative, the documentPicked signal) and OksaActivity.pickDocument / onActivityResult (request 0x129, Qt's own is 1305; read, write and persistable grants offered); LoggingSetup's Import contacts (ADIF) and Choose…, the Profiles tab's app setup Import and Radio Setup › MIDI's Import a map… ask pickDocument first and open their FileDialog only where it returns false (iOS, the Mac, the desktop); only the page that asked takes the file. *Tests:* the desktop build (the FileDialogs unchanged there, every button opens it); the Java compiled against android-36's android.jar with a QtActivity stub; Android itself needs the preview's APK (the user's phone: Drive).
130. **Logging:** Editing or deleting a contact no longer freezes the app for seconds in a large log (*in build 202 / Android beta 112*) **In short:** Editing or deleting a contact in a large log no longer freezes the app for seconds; the log is written in the background (your phone, a log of 17 937 contacts). (written in N ms)", sends the change to the shared ADIF file and emits qsoLogChanged; qsoLogBusy (Saving the change… under the list, no contact opened meanwhile). *Tests* (the render tool against the FLEX, 1366×1024, an 18000-contact log in its own documents folder, the shared file a copy): edit 234 ms, the edit back 261 ms, deletion 245 ms on the worker, no GUI stall logged; the shared file followed each (18000, 18000, 17999 read); the Log tab: 17999 contacts, the deleted one gone from the list; both files 17999 records, the contact gone. 136. **Spots:** Spots from the Reverse Beacon Network and PSK Reporter, and where you are heard yourself (*in build 204 / Android beta 113*) **In short:** Spots from the Reverse Beacon Network and PSK Reporter, and Heard me: who hears your own CQ (your idea). Released in build 204 / Android beta 113. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-22: "pskreporterista pystyä nostamaan spotit koska se on tärkeä paikka hakea digimodejen spotteja", "Vai olisiko reverse beacon network kaikista paras koska pystyisi myös itsensä spottaamaan ... mahdollisimman laaja ja hyvä spotti lähde tarvittaisiin"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-22:* the widest source differs by mode: for CW and RTTY the RBN (hundreds of CW Skimmer stations, telnet.reversebeacon.net port 7000; FT8 / FT4 skimmers on 7001), for FT8 / FT4 and the other digital modes PSK Reporter (every WSJT-X / JTDX / OksaSDR station reports what it decodes, far more receivers than RBN's digital port). Both also tell where you are heard: RBN spots your call when you call CQ on CW, RTTY or FT8, PSK Reporter has every reception report of your digital signal; the app already sends its own reports to PSK Reporter (FTx). The engine has both clients: the desktop AetherSDR runs RBN as a second DxClusterClient (the same telnet protocol as the cluster, its own startup commands) and PskReporterClient (HTTP queries at most once per 5 minutes, or the live MQTT feed mqtt.pskreporter.info over TLS, its own MqttClient, no Qt module). The app already puts the DX cluster's spots on the radio with "spot add ... source=", so new sources go the same way. Plan in steps: (a) RBN as a source in Setup › Spots (on / off, CW-RTTY and / or FT8-FT4, the login your call), a Spots shown button RBN with its count; (b) PSK Reporter's live feed for digital modes; (c) *Heard me*: your own call's RBN spots and PSK Reporter reports, by band: how many hear you, the farthest, the best SNR, on the Spots tab (134). The volume is the design question (RBN gives tens of spots a second in a contest): only the bands with a slice, only spotters within View › From (132: a station heard near you is one you can hear), one spot per call and band per 10 minutes, a cap on the radio's list, the oldest out first. The alerts (132) then fire from RBN and PSK Reporter spots too, e.g. a VK9XY on FT8 heard in OH. Before building: read RBN's and PSK Reporter's usage terms (the query API's 5-minute rule; the MQTT feed's fair use) and how the desktop filters RBN. Tests: both feeds live on the FLEX, a contest weekend's rate, the radio's spot list size and the other clients' panadapters, an alert from an RBN spot, Heard me with a low-power CQ on CW and FT8 (TX live). Size: large, three steps. *Done in the dev build* (MobileEngineSpotFeeds.cpp, MiniMqtt, AdifDxcc.h): RBN through the cluster (set/skimmer cw rtty psk; accept/rbn by the skimmers' place per From, or this station's call), PSK Reporter's MQTT feed (receivers in this DXCC; this station's call), Heard me seeded from the query API at most every 5 minutes; caps per band, alert spots always; a call the radio has within 1 kHz from any source not added again (the user: no duplicates from many places); a cluster spot from farther than From allows kept off the radio (the user: e.g. one heard only in VK). Tested on the FLEX (login OH6OKSA-7): 233 RBN and 124 PSKR spots in 90 s with the caps (600 a minute without), 3 cluster spots a minute with From on EU, a 10 W FT8 CQ heard by EA3IDY shown in Heard me (2865 km); RBN's FT8 left out (all on the dial frequency).
137. **Spots:** Tune on a spot alert (*in build 204 / Android beta 113*) **In short:** An alert's Tune, and a tap on a spot, set the whole radio for the spot: frequency and mode, and for FT8 / FT4 the band's frequency with FTx decoding (your idea). Released in build 204 / Android beta 113. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (and a tap on a spot) sets the whole radio for the spot's mode, FT8 / FT4 included (the user, 2026-11-22: "kun se valitaan tulee radion asettua siihen tilaan minkä spotti tieto tuotti eli esimerkiksi taajuus, mode digu, sitten rtty tai pskxx tai ft8 tai ft4, cw, lsb, usb, fm, am jne"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-22:* the alert's Tune runs tuneToSpot, as a tap on a spot label does. It already sets the mode from the spot's mode or comment (SpotModeResolver, always on: no setting in the app): CW, USB, LSB, AM, SAM, FM, NFM; RTTY to the radio's RTTY mode (or DIGL / DIGU when the RTTY tab is set so) with the mark tuned onto the signal; PSK31 / PSK63 to the Digi tab's PSK in DIGU on the carrier; a spot without a mode by the band plan (the CW part CW, the data part DIGU, else USB above 10 MHz, LSB below). Missing: FT8 / FT4 only get DIGU on the spot's own frequency (the DX's dial plus its audio, e.g. 14.07523), not FTx's way: FTx is not switched to the spot's protocol, the slice not on the band's FT8 / FT4 frequency (MobileEngineDigi's kBandFreqs: 14.074 / 14.080 ...) and decoding not started; and no mode opens its Controls tab. Plan: an FT8 / FT4 spot (by its mode, or a frequency within 3 kHz above the band's FT8 / FT4 frequency) puts the slice on that frequency in DIGU, FTx's protocol to FT8 / FT4, decoding on, the receive focus onto the spot's audio (spot − dial, e.g. 1230 Hz) and the transmit Hz as a tap on a decoded call sets it, and shows the FTx tab, so the station's CQ comes up in the list for a one-tap answer; RTTY and PSK show the Digi tab, CW the CW tab. One function for the alert and the panadapter tap. Tests on the FLEX by test spots (spottest spot=CALL,MHZ,SPOTTER,SOURCE,MODE), each read back as numbers (slice frequency, mode, FTx protocol and Hz, the tab shown), by the alert's Tune and by a tap: FT8 14.07523, FT4 14.0815, FT8 on 40 m 7.0755, RTTY 14.085, PSK31 14.0705, CW 14.025, USB 14.250, LSB 7.150, AM 3.885, FM 29.600, a spot without a mode in each band-plan part; the radio's state restored after. Size: medium. *Done in the dev build:* tuneToSpot's FT8 / FT4 branch (ftxDialForSpot: the band's FT8 / FT4 frequency within 3 kHz, or a named one's kHz below), controlsSectionRequested opens the mode's tab (the alert's Tune opens a closed drawer). Tested on the FLEX by the alert's Tune with 14 test spots, every mode read back (see the commit). *Fixed in the preview* (the user, build 203's preview, 2026-11-22: VK9XY's PSK Reporter spots at 1.835497 and 10.138417, the tune right (dial 1.835 / 10.136) but "kuuntelu ei virittynyt juuri sille rx kohdalle millä asema lähetti"; then "Tarkista myös rtty, pskxx"): the FTx RX mark now goes onto the station's audio frequency (not during a QSO); a digital mode named anywhere in a spot's comment counts (the resolver read only its first and last word), BPSK31 / PSK alone as PSK31, BPSK63 as PSK63, RTTY45/50/75 as RTTY; PSK125 / QPSK go to DIGU with the signal at 1000 Hz. Tested on the FLEX by the alert's Tune: *CQ RTTY test* RTTY, *BPSK31 CQ DX* and *CQ PSK* PSK31 at -1000 Hz, *PSK125 CQ* DIGU, *TNX FT8 QSO* at 10.1384 FT8 dial 10.136 with the RX mark at 2400 Hz, mode RTTY50 RTTY, CW CW.
138. **Fixes:** Every disconnect logs its cause (*in build 204 / Android beta 113*) **In short:** Every disconnect logs its cause, and the Connect page says why a connection ended when it was not your own Disconnect (Rob Metallo VA3MET's and your own drops with beta 112). Released in build 204 / Android beta 113. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Rob Metallo VA3MET, mail 2026-11-22 16:51, UNIWA F400, Android 15, beta 112 over SmartLink: "It crashed on me a couple of times", with a log; the user's Android 16 phone the same day, beta 112's preview: the connection went idle 3-11 s after returning from Share the log's sheet, twice, and connected again). Found in Rob's log (09:33-09:41 EST): no crash in it: at 09:33 the app went to the background, Android froze it for 134 s ("GUI thread stalled ... app not in front"), back in front it signed in to SmartLink again and connected (as designed); at 09:41:21, 1 s after "CW keyboard paddles: page keyboard focus out" and 2 s after his CW CQ ended, an orderly disconnect (cwx clear, cw key 0, stream remove, "connection state idle"), connecting again 3 s later. Nothing in the log says who closed it: the operator's Disconnect (the ⋯ menu takes the page's focus), the radio, SmartLink or the app. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-22:* disconnectRadio and RadioModel::disconnectFromRadio log no reason, nor does a socket the radio closes. Plan: one line "disconnect: <why>" on every path (the operator's Disconnect with the button or menu, a station conflict's choice, the radio closing the TCP link or its error, a keepalive timeout, SmartLink's own drop, the app's restart of a stale session, a shot plan), and the connect page says the cause when it was not the operator. A real crash shows as "previous run ended: reason ..." at the next start, and Send to the developer then attaches the previous run's log: Rob asked to use it right after the next one. Tests: each path on the FLEX (Disconnect by button and menu, the radio's own client disconnect, the network cut on the dev VM, the app in the background on the emulator), the line and the connect page's text read back. Size: small. *Done in the dev build:* disconnectRadio(why) from every button and menu; RadioModel::linkLost for the keepalive; "disconnect: <cause> (asked|not asked)" and the connect page's *The connection ended: …*. Tested on the FLEX: the app's own, the radio's client disconnect, the radio unreachable (5 pings) and back.

141. **Logging:** The Log tab for a fast run (*in build 206 / Android beta 115; follow-ups in build 211 / Android beta 117*) **In short:** The Log tab for a fast run: a line with the run's contacts and rate, the operator's name in the recent contacts, and After logging › Back to the call (Christoph Steinert DM5HF, after a POTA run of 126 QSOs). Released in build 206 / Android beta 115, the keyboard follow-ups in build 211 / beta 117; you checked both choices on the OnePlus and the iPad. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Christoph Steinert DM5HF, mail 2026-11-22 17:41, after his first POTA activation with OksaSDR alone, SSB, 126 QSOs in 1.5 h: "worked very very well"): (a) the number of contacts on a small green line, as Setup's lines, to see the run rate; (b) the other station's name in the recent contacts instead of the park reference; (c) the cursor back in the call field after Enter logs the contact, so no mouse or trackpad is needed. *Pre-analysis 2026-11-22:* (a) the recent list's heading says "Log: N contacts"; a line under Log (this session's count and the rate per hour, e.g. "126 this session · 84 / h") is new. (b) the recent list shows time, call, band / mode and the park; NAME when the contact has one, the park otherwise. (c) after logging the app drops the keyboard on purpose (logTab.dropKeyboard) so that Space keys PTT again on an iPad or Mac keyboard (roadmap: iPad Space PTT is core); so a choice "After logging: back to the call" (off by default), and with it on the page's Space PTT waits until the field is left (Esc).
