Size: small. Chosen for build 206 (the user, 2026-11-23). *On devices 2026-11-23 (the user):* the run line and the names right; Back to the call made no difference on the iPad with its keyboard (Qt on iOS let the field go after Return's default Done): fixed in build 207 / Android beta 116 (the call field's key Return with the choice on). Beta 117 on the OnePlus (the user): Back to the call right on the outer screen; Keyboard away gave Android's Next (a Tab to the next field, nothing logged): its key is now Done. The iPad on build 209: both choices right with the Magic Keyboard.
142. **Usability:** The on-screen keyboard can be put away from a text field (*in build 204 / Android beta 113; follow-ups in build 211 / Android beta 117*) **In short:** The on-screen keyboard can be put away from a text field (Done, a tap outside), and the page makes room above it so PTT stays in sight (Robin Bishop G5OUT). Released in build 204 / Android beta 113, with follow-ups in the builds after it, the last in build 214 / Android beta 119. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Robin Bishop G5OUT, FB 2026-11-22 after his first field test with build 202, iPhone Air and iPad Air 3: "only one actual issue, the text boxes dont allow me to dismiss the keyboard, when i want to alter something or check things"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-22:* an iPhone's keyboard has no key to hide it, and a tap outside a Qt Quick text field does not take its focus away, so the keyboard stays over the page until the field's own Return (single-line fields) or a button that drops it (the Log tab's Log). Plan: a tap anywhere outside the field (the page, a panel's background) takes the focus away and hides the keyboard, as iOS apps do; a ⌄ key in the field's row where a keyboard would cover what is checked; the page gets the keyboard focus back afterwards (Space PTT on an iPad with a keyboard). Also from the same field test: two parks entered comma-separated in My park went to POTA fine; ADIF's MY_POTA_REF (a comma-delimited list) could be written beside MY_SIG_INFO for POTA. Tests: every text field (Log tab, Setup, macros' editors, the Digi tab's text) on an iPhone and iPad size, the keyboard shown and put away by a tap outside, nothing typed lost, focussweep. Size: small-medium. *Chosen for build 203* (the user, 2026-11-22: "Tuo 142 pitää tutkia ja korjata tähän mukaan"). *Done in the dev build:* TextFocusRelease (a press outside the focused field lets go of it, since build 140) now also hides the on-screen keyboard; a **⌄ Done** key at the top centre while the keyboard is up on iOS and Android (a popup above the drawer and Setup, so a press on it closes neither), letting go of the field, the page taking the keys again. *Fixed in the preview* (the user, build 203's preview on the OnePlus Open, 2026-11-22: "142 kohta Android ainakin ihan persiellään", "Etukannen tilassa sitä ei näy ollenkaan"): at the window's top it went out of sight when the system slid the page up for a field low on the screen (the outer screen's drawer), and Android's font had no "⌄" (a box); and on the iPad with a hardware keyboard it showed at the top with no on-screen keyboard ("Eikä tuo iPadissäkään kovin järkevältä näytä"); now plain *Done* by the focused field: below it at its right end where the keyboard leaves that in sight, else above it, the first such spot with nothing to press under it, following the field every 200 ms while the drawer slides or scrolls; on iOS only with a keyboard at least 150 px tall (a hardware keyboard leaves iOS's shortcut bar only); the render tool's `keyboard on HEIGHT` draws a stand-in keyboard over the window's bottom. The user then on the iPad without its keyboard: "virtuaalinäppäimistöllä ei PTT-painamme onnistu silloin, kun logikirjoitus päällä" and "näppäimistö peittää melkein koko vesiputouksen": now while the keyboard is up the page ends above it (Main.qml keyboardCover, from Qt.inputMethod.keyboardRectangle; 0 where the system made the window shorter), the bottom controls drawers grow by it and pad their content above it (Qt places a bottom Drawer at the window's edge whatever its margins), Setup is centred in what is left when at least 420 px remain (a phone in landscape keeps the system's slide), the focused field is scrolled into its Flickable's sight once, and a press on PTT (keepsTextFocus) keeps the field and the keyboard (TextFocusRelease finds the item under the press as Qt would: the popups' layer and empty containers passed through). Tested in the render tool with stand-in keyboards of 200-380 px at 412 x 915, 393 x 852, 840 x 904, 915 x 412, 852 x 393, 1366 x 1024 (Log pinned too) and 1024 x 1366: Done beside Rcvd or in the field's end, PTT above the keyboard, a PTT press keeping the field (TX blocked); the real keyboards' sizes and the systems' slides on devices still to see (each placement logs "keyboard: Done …" with the keyboard's rectangle). Tested first in the render tool (a stand-in keyboard, shot plan `keyboard on`): the Log tab's call focused, Done at the top, after it the field let go, the drawer still open and the page's keyboard focus back (keyboardOnPage true) at 390 x 844 and 1366 x 1024; on a device still to see. *On devices 2026-11-23 (the user, build 206 / Android beta 115):* Done works, but it is not seen unless the Controls drawer is at its full height (in a lower drawer the keyboard covers it), and with the on-screen keyboard up PTT is not seen; the device log has no "keyboard: Done …" line at all (the QML console line does not reach the developer link), so the placement on a real keyboard is still unread: to fix with a placement that stays in sight in every drawer height, its line in the engine's log. Big buttons' Done fixed in build 207 / Android beta 116 (the call field's end), its place logged by the engine; the lower drawer to read from that line on the OnePlus. Android's keyboard in device pixels (no room made; PTT under the keyboard on the opened OnePlus upright): scaled in build 208 / Android beta 117. Beta 117 on the opened OnePlus: no keyboard at all, in either orientation (the outer screen fine): with the keyboard's room counted the window fell under the phone layout's 500 high, the page was built anew and the field let go; the phone / tablet class now from the whole height (Theme.classHeight), tested in the render tool at 698 x 751 and 751 x 698 (Big buttons' Log and the Log tab keep the field and the keyboard; upright the Log tab's PTT row shows only its top part above a 316 keyboard). Run 149 on the OnePlus: upright right; the device log showed Qt's keyboard rectangle changing without its signal and inputMethod.visible lagging (typing: "keyboard 0+0, the system's 1413+1027"; the room only after Enter), and the reveal scroll staying after the keyboard went (the call out of sight under the tab buttons, a devlink screenshot): Android now reads the rectangle every 150 ms while a field has the focus and counts the keyboard up while it has a height; reveal() gives its scroll back when the keyboard goes. Run 149 on the inner screen: PTT under the keyboard in both orientations (the outer screen fine): with the keyboard's room the page read as landscape (the Controls drawer from the side, the page's PTT row pushed under the keyboard by the display's 320 minimum); portrait, the panel pinning and the tight height now from the whole window (Theme.classHeight), the display's minimum 100 while the keyboard is up, and an upright drawer takes the whole page above the keyboard. Render tool with a 316 keyboard: 698 x 751 the drawer's PTT above it, 751 x 698 the page's PTT row above it, 1024 x 1366 (a 400 keyboard) the drawer's PTT above it; the field focused in each. Run 149: the keyboard's Done logged nothing in any mode (the user): Qt's QtInputConnection hides Android's keyboard on a Done / Go Enter before the key reaches the field (the log: Enter reached QQuickLoader after "let go"), and Next makes Enter a Tab; every field with onAccepted (17: the Log tab's call and spot line, Big buttons' Log, CWX, RTTY, the address, SmartLink's password, the memory name / frequency / rename, the offset, the layout rename, the profile and setup names, the radio setup save, the CQ target) now gives Android Return, iOS unchanged. Build 210 on the iPad (the user, the log: Done at 20:17 only let the CWX field go, Enter sent "sss"): Done now finishes the field as Enter (its accepted() when it has text), then hides the keyboard; it keeps the field through the press (keepsTextFocus), as the press-outside release had taken it first. Render tool (demo radio, the FLEX's slots taken by the user's devices): Keyboard away and Back to the call both logged by Done, an empty field only lost the keyboard, Big buttons' Log logged.
143. **Radio:** Voice macros with the radio's microphone in use (*in build 204 / Android beta 113*) (*chosen for build 203*) **In short:** Voice macros also work while the radio's own microphone is in use: the app switches the input to this device for the macro and back right after it (Christoph Steinert DM5HF). Released in build 204 / Android beta 113. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Christoph Steinert DM5HF, mails 2026-11-21 18:37 and 2026-11-22 19:11: POTA with a Heil BM17 headset on the radio's microphone input and VOX, "the DVK mic change is so important to me - all in one app"; the desktop AetherSDR plays the radio's DVK with the radio's mic input but loops no CQ). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-22:* with the radio's input chosen the app's PTT is off (transmitAvailable needs mic_selection=PC), so neither the app's own clips nor (needlessly) the radio's licensed voice keyer could be started; the FLEX-6600M here has no DVK licence, so the app's clips are what most use. *Done in the dev build:* the radio's voice keyer plays whatever the mic input; an app clip with the radio's input switches the input to PC, keys 400 ms after ("mic input PC" before "xmit 1": the model takes the input at once, so keyed at once the radio got xmit first), plays, and puts the radio's input back however the press ends (the clip's end, Stop, the controls drawer, a disconnect); Repeat does it again for each send, so the headset and VOX answer in the gaps; not while the radio transmits by its own PTT or VOX; 3 s without the switch: an error and the input back. Tested on the FLEX at 0 W with a test tone in F3 (shot plan `voiceclip`, `micinput`, `dvkplay`): MIC → PC → xmit 1 → xmit 0 → MIC, from F3 and from the Macros panel, and from the drawer's opening; the clip's own end could not be seen on the dev VM (no microphone, so the clip does not advance): on a device with the preview. Size: medium.
144. **Fixes:** Android: a Bluetooth headset silent after the first transmission (*in Android beta 114; follow-ups in build 211 / Android beta 117*) **In short:** Android: a Bluetooth headset went silent after the first transmission; receive now comes back on the headset after each one (Mattias SM6YEC). Released in Android beta 114, follow-ups in build 211 / beta 117. Mattias confirmed on 2026-11-25 with beta 120 and his usual profile loaded: receive works. What he noticed then, the two seconds before receive returns and the call link's sound, is 201. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Mattias SM6YEC, mail with his log 2026-11-22 22:30, beta 112 on a Samsung Galaxy S24+ with Android 16, a FLEX-8400 over SmartLink, a Jabra Evolve2 65 and a second headset: "After the first transmission, the headset goes silent when I release PTT. Restarting OksaSDR restores the audio"). Pre-analysis: his log shows receive on "Bluetooth (hands-free, reported as Jabra Evolve2 65)", PTT opening the microphone on the headset (TX stream on "Bluetooth"), "receive playback back: PTT released" and from then on audio underruns (188 in 10 s): on Android opening the microphone can end a receive stream opened before it (applyAudioOutputDevice's own note), and the release only un-ducked receive. Fix: on Android, with the device's microphone in use and a Bluetooth hands-free / LE headset as the communication device (Platform.headsetRoute), receive opens again 300 ms after PTT is released (AudioEngine::reopenRx, as after a route change). Size: small. The user, 2026-11-22 23:05: "tee korjaus yönaikana ja PuJu". Not testable on the dev VM (no Bluetooth); to see in the next log from a headset. *On a device 2026-11-23 (the user, OnePlus, beta 115, a Bluetooth headset):* receive comes back after PTT only when the headset's Media audio is allowed in Android's Bluetooth settings; with it off (hands-free only, as Mattias's route "Bluetooth (hands-free …)") receive stays silent, though the log says "receive opened again on the Bluetooth headset after transmitting" and a receive stream started at 24000 Hz on "Bluetooth" (the user: so it was before too). Also in the log: a short press (0.1 s) is released before the microphone has opened on the headset (the TX stream starts 0.3-0.5 s after the press on Bluetooth, after the release) and then no reopen is tried at all; the release itself took up to 0.4 s (the JNI route query while Android set the link up). To analyse: keep the hands-free link and the communication device across the microphone's close, reopen receive once the TX stream has really stopped (not on a 300 ms timer), and a microphone that opens after the release closed at once. Mattias's own case (hands-free only) is so likely not fixed yet. *Build 207 / Android beta 116:* the hands-free route taken again after PTT, receive reopened and the link watch armed, also after a short press; the headset's state in the log. On the OnePlus with Media audio off and on still to see. Mattias told 2026-11-23 to turn Media audio on meanwhile. Beta 116 on the OnePlus: the fix works (log); a short tap left the headset chirping (the microphone opened after the release): not opened then, build 208 / Android beta 117. Mattias (2026-11-23 17:28): Media audio and Calls both on, still silent on beta 115: beta 117 to try, his log asked for. Beta 117 on the OnePlus: the not-opened start helped only when the start had not begun (9 taps logged "not opened"); a tap whose microphone was already opening still chirped, now and then with Media audio on too, while a press of a second or more never did: a headset's microphone now stays open a second from the press and receive comes back after it closes (the radio let go at once). *After beta 118 (Mattias, mail 2026-11-25 00:34)*: "Bluetooth audio worked in Auto mode. The receive audio came back after I released PTT. Then I loaded the profiles I use with SmartSDR on my iPad, and the problem returned": something a profile sets brings the fault back. Asked the same night for the log right after it and the kind of profile (Global, TX or Mic). A guess to check against that log, not a finding: a profile that turns VOX on makes the app keep the headset's microphone open (voxHoldsMicrophone), which would hold the hands-free link as a transmission does. To pre-analyse with the log: what a profile load changes that the headset path depends on (the mic input, VOX, DAX), and whether the app should follow it. He plans a video of the app on his channel (SM6YEC ON AIR).
145. **FTx:** A tapped decode answered in the period already running, as WSJT-X's late start (*in build 206 / Android beta 115*) **In short:** A tapped FT8 / FT4 decode is answered in the slot already running, as WSJT-X's late start (George Molnar KF2T). Released in build 206 / Android beta 115. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (George J Molnar KF2T, mail 2026-11-23 03:00, build 204 on an iPad Pro 11 and an iPhone 16 Pro: "manually clicking on a CQ queues up a transmission, but it does not air until a full sequence has gone by — after the station's next CQ, or perhaps when they have already started a new QSO"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-23*: digiAnswer only sets the state and the TX parity (the slot after the CQ's); TX starts from onDigiSlot at a slot's start only, and startDigiTx refuses past 2.0 s. A decode shows as the CQ's slot ends (~14.5 s), so the tap always falls in the slot of ours already running and the call goes out 30 s (FT4 15 s) later. CQ (Auto: the next slot) and the auto-sequence (decoding starts 1 s before the slot ends) are not hit. WSJT-X (widgets/mainwindow.cpp guiUpdate, Modulator/Modulator.cpp): it keys in its own period while under 75 % of it has gone by (fTR < 0.75, "Allow late starts") and the modulator skips the waveform already past (m_ic = (mstr - delay_ms) × rate), so the signal stays in time (its start cut, DT right). Fix: a tap (digiAnswer, the Hound's call and a decode heard on another slice too) starts TX at once when the running slot is ours and under 75 % gone; startDigiTx's late start drops the samples already past instead of the lead; FT8 and FT4. Test on the FLEX at low power: taps 3 / 6 / 10 s into the slot (DT read by a second receiver or PSK Reporter), 12 s waits for the next slot, the try count, Hound, another slice. Manual: FT8's Tap a decode. Size: small. Chosen for build 206 (the user, 2026-11-23). Done: digiLateStart() from digiAnswer (a tap, the Hound's call, Answer CQ), startDigiTx's late start removes the samples already past and ramps the cut in over 1/8 symbol; the dry run and the on-air test in the build paragraph.
146. **FTx:** The next message sent in the slot already running when its decode comes after the slot began **In short:** In FT8 / FT4 a reply decoded just after our own slot began would still go out in that slot: the message on the air is swapped for the next one, as WSJT-X does. Found on the air in 145's test, where a late reply cost two extra cycles. Pre-analysed, not built. **From you:** nothing now; pick it by number for a build. Its test is real QSOs at low power over the cabin's link. **Details:** (found in 145's on-air test, 2026-11-23: F1MXE's report came 1.4 s into our slot, by then our call had gone out again, and the QSO took two extra cycles). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-23*: decoding starts 1 s before the slot ends plus the receive latency (over the cabin's LTE about 1 s more), so a reply can arrive after onDigiSlot has keyed the old message. WSJT-X restarts the transmission with the new message's tones when the message changes while transmitting (mainwindow.cpp m_restart, set in processMessage after the auto-sequence; the modulator keeps its place, so the new message goes on from that point). Here: when handleDigiDecodes changes m_digiTxMsg while the old one is on air and under 75 % of the slot has gone by, the clip is swapped (AudioEngine without unkeying) for the new message from the same place, as 145's late start; tests on the FLEX with a dry run and on the air. Size: small-medium. *Design 2026-11-24 (read in the code, not built: left out of build 212 so that it gets its own on-air test)*: the swap belongs in AudioEngine, which alone knows where the clip is (`swapTxDigital`: from its own position the old samples fade into the new over 1/8 symbol, the rest replaced; the two clips laid out alike, so startDigiTx remembers its lead or late-start skip as a sample offset); MobileEngine calls it from handleDigiDecodes when m_digiTxMsg has changed while m_digiTransmitting, the slot is ours and under 75 % gone, sets m_digiSentMsg, adds the new text as a TX row and counts no new try; not in Hound (the R+report goes on the Fox's frequency) and not when the audio frequency would change. The dry run needs a decode injected into our own slot (a test option of --ft8-test), as a dry transmission gets no answers; the splice checked by numbers (the largest sample step at the join), then real QSOs over the cabin's LTE, where the replies come about 1 s into our slot. Not chosen for a build.
147. **Audio:** Receive audio without pops when its queue overflows **In short:** Receive audio without a pop when its queue overflows, as after a gap on LTE or SmartLink (upstream's fix). Left out of build 212 on purpose: it changes every user's receive path and a pop cannot be heard on the test machine, so it wants a build of its own. **From you:** nothing now; pick it by number when a build can be given to it alone. **Details:** (upstream #6993, ad2a2c4b1) (upstream review 2026-11-23 (the user: "Katso onko upstreamissä jotain meille", then "Kirjaa nämä"): 475 upstream commits after the merge base 7864ef544 to f61dfbbb0, about 100 FLEX-relevant read; *pre-analysis*: when the receive queue overflowed, AudioEngine spliced the history of NR / EQ / the resampler, a pop; upstream keeps feeding them and drops only the oldest ready output. The bursts after an LTE or SmartLink gap hit this path. Applies to src/core/AudioEngine.cpp/.h with every symbol present; beside our processMixedRxAudioData change (medium conflict risk). Tests: a burst replayed (netchange, SmartLink on the mobile network), the underrun lines and the receive monitor's RMS. Size: small. Left out of build 212 (2026-11-24): the receive path of every user, with a medium conflict risk and no way to hear a pop on the test machine; it wants its own build with the upstream tests (RxOverflowCapture, audio_engine_rates_test) run first. Not chosen for a build.
148. **Devices:** PGXL / TGXL readings on the meters for every device handle (*in build 212 / Android beta 118*) **In short:** Power Genius XL and Tuner Genius XL readings on the meters also with the long device handles of SmartSDR 4.3 (from upstream). Released in build 212 / Android beta 118. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (upstream #6658, b2ac93b14) (upstream review 2026-11-23 (the user: "Katso onko upstreamissä jotain meille", then "Kirjaa nämä"): 475 upstream commits after the merge base 7864ef544 to f61dfbbb0, about 100 FLEX-relevant read; *pre-analysis*: the meter list's `num=` is parsed signed (toInt, base 0), so a handle at or above 0x80000000 fails and MeterModel::isTgxlMeter / isAmpMeter never match: the amplifier's and tuner's readings missing from the meters on about half of the devices. Upstream parses it unsigned (FlexKvCarry.h, MeterDef.h, FlexBackend.cpp, MeterModel.cpp). Applies cleanly. Tests: genius-mock with a high handle, the meter choices. Size: small.
149. **CW:** A `"` in CWX text sent as it is (*in build 212 / Android beta 118*) **In short:** A quotation mark in a CW text or memory is sent as typed (from upstream). Released in build 212 / Android beta 118. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (upstream a06694743) (upstream review 2026-11-23 (the user: "Katso onko upstreamissä jotain meille", then "Kirjaa nämä"): 475 upstream commits after the merge base 7864ef544 to f61dfbbb0, about 100 FLEX-relevant read; *pre-analysis*: an embedded quote ended the CWX command's text; upstream sends it as 0x1E and decodes it back in the macro status (CwxModel.cpp, unchanged in the fork). Applies cleanly. Tests: a memory with a quote, sent with TX blocked and the radio's echo read. Size: small.
150. **CW:** NetCW's client_handle in 8 hex digits as FlexLib (*in build 212 / Android beta 118*) **In short:** The CW key's client handle in FlexLib's eight-digit form, so the radio always matches it (from upstream). Released in build 212 / Android beta 118. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (upstream #6612, bc2526e89) (upstream review 2026-11-23 (the user: "Katso onko upstreamissä jotain meille", then "Kirjaa nämä"): 475 upstream commits after the merge base 7864ef544 to f61dfbbb0, about 100 FLEX-relevant read; *pre-analysis*: the phone's paddles go through sendNetCwCommand; a handle with fewer digits may not be matched by the radio. One line, applies cleanly. Tests: keyboard paddles on the FLEX with a handle under 0x10000000 if one comes, the sidetone and the radio's keying. Size: small.
151. **Spots:** Another client's spot click makes no blank spot (*in build 212 / Android beta 118*) **In short:** Another program's click on a spot is no longer taken for a new, blank spot (from upstream). Released in build 212 / Android beta 118. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (upstream 874ca62ce) (upstream review 2026-11-23 (the user: "Katso onko upstreamissä jotain meille", then "Kirjaa nämä"): 475 upstream commits after the merge base 7864ef544 to f61dfbbb0, about 100 FLEX-relevant read; *pre-analysis*: a `spot N triggered pan=…` status, sent when SmartSDR or another client clicks a spot, created or updated a spot with no call; upstream ignores it for that purpose (RadioModel.cpp). Applies cleanly. Tests: a spot clicked in SmartSDR / the desktop while the phone shows spots. Size: small.
152. **Band plan:** IARU Region 1 rows corrected (*in build 212 / Android beta 118*) **In short:** Band plan: the IARU Region 1 and SSA rows that are all-mode read ALL again, and Band plan lock sets no mode in them, so a DIGU slice tuned in stays DIGU (from upstream, with our own correction). Released in build 212 / Android beta 118. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (upstream #6573, 2109c0da3) (upstream review 2026-11-23 (the user: "Katso onko upstreamissä jotain meille", then "Kirjaa nämä"): 475 upstream commits after the merge base 7864ef544 to f61dfbbb0, about 100 FLEX-relevant read; *pre-analysis*: some Region 1 rows were drawn digital-only; the JSON and BandPlanManager parts apply cleanly (the desktop SpectrumWidget part left out). Tests: the band plan strip on 40, 30, 20 and 17 m against the IARU R1 plan, a render of the strip. Size: small. *Found in the tests 2026-11-24*: the rows' new ALL label made Band plan lock set phone there (a DIGU slice tuned to 1.8405, FT8's 160 m row, became LSB), where it had set nothing while they read DIGI: an all-mode row whose usage note names digimodes sets no mode (MobileEngine segmentUse).
153. **Radio:** A memory recalled onto our slice only (*in build 212 / Android beta 118*) **In short:** A memory is recalled onto the slice the app shows, not onto whichever slice the radio holds active (from upstream; reproduced on the FLEX). Released in build 212 / Android beta 118. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (upstream #7502, 55f891971) (upstream review 2026-11-23 (the user: "Katso onko upstreamissä jotain meille", then "Kirjaa nämä"): 475 upstream commits after the merge base 7864ef544 to f61dfbbb0, about 100 FLEX-relevant read; *pre-analysis*: `memory apply` acts on the radio's active slice, so with two slices the other one could move; MobileEngine::recallMemory sends `memory apply` then `slice tune m_slice`, the same pattern. Port by hand: `slice set <id> active=1` first (upstream's FlexStoredControls.cpp is not in the fork). Tests: two slices, the other active, a memory recalled, both frequencies read back. Size: small.
154. **Controllers:** MIDI settings saved safely (*in build 212 / Android beta 118*) **In short:** MIDI settings are written in one piece, so an interrupted save cannot damage the file, and a file that cannot be read is rewritten from the bindings in use (from upstream). Released in build 212 / Android beta 118. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (upstream #6630 a87f11c6c, #6846 efd58619d) (upstream review 2026-11-23 (the user: "Katso onko upstreamissä jotain meille", then "Kirjaa nämä"): 475 upstream commits after the merge base 7864ef544 to f61dfbbb0, about 100 FLEX-relevant read; *pre-analysis*: save() writes with a plain QFile and can leave the MIDI bindings empty after a failed write; upstream saves atomically and does not overwrite a file it could not read. The first applies cleanly, the second builds on it. Tests: a mapping saved, a read-only / broken file, the bindings after restart (desktop and Android). Size: small. *In the app 2026-11-24*: MidiSettings now refuses a preference save while the file cannot be read; a phone user cannot reach the file to restore it, so the app then rewrites it from the bindings in use (MobileEngineMidi saveMidiPreferences), as before the refusal.
155. **Devices:** TGXL's manual relays numbered 0 / 1 / 2 again (*in build 211 / Android beta 117*) **In short:** Tuner Genius XL: the manual C1 / L / C2 buttons move their own relays again; the tuner numbers them from 0 (Ben Morris KT4O). Released in build 211 / Android beta 117. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (upstream review 2026-11-23 (the user: "Katso onko upstreamissä jotain meille", then "Kirjaa nämä"): 475 upstream commits after the merge base 7864ef544 to f61dfbbb0, about 100 FLEX-relevant read; *pre-analysis*: our 6c9391f98 (2026-11-04) moved `relay=` to 1 = C1, 2 = L, 3 = C2 after 4O3A's PDF and was tested only on genius-mock, which was built from the same PDF. Upstream #7227 (eb827a714, 2026-11-19) keeps 0 = C1, 1 = L, 2 = C2 from a pcap of 4O3A's own program against a real TGXL (firmware 1.2.17, upstream #469) and reverted its own one-based change, which had also been tested on an emulator only. So on a real TGXL our C1 button likely moves L, L moves C2 and C2 sends a relay that does not exist. Fix: the relay numbers back to 0..2 (TunerModel, genius-mock), the rest of 6c9391f98 (operate / bypass set, state read, SWR) kept. Needs a tester with a real TGXL to confirm: asked in the group 2026-11-23 (the user: "Esitä tämä kysymys ryhmässä niin saadaan 100% varmuus"), https://www.facebook.com/groups/1468327561855382/posts/1501255525229252/ (C1 + pressed once: which bar moves, and the TGXL's firmware). Size: small. Not chosen for a build. *Confirmed and fixed in build 208 / Android beta 117:* Ben Morris KT4O on a TGXL with firmware 1.2.44 (FB 2026-11-23): C1 + changed L, so the device numbers from 0; TgxlConnection sends relay 0..2, genius-mock follows, the engine logs each step with the relays before and after.
156. **Layout:** The controls beside the display upright on a big screen (*in build 211 / Android beta 117*) **In short:** On a big screen held upright the pinned controls sit beside the display, the arrangement you called the best view; before, they jumped there from under the display while typing in the Log tab. Released in build 211 / Android beta 117. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-23 evening on the iPad with build 210: typing in the Log tab upright the pinned panel jumped from under the display to the side, and the side arrangement is "paras näkymä sekä pystyssä että vaakassa", "Vaihtoehto 2 paras ipad ja mac"). *Pre-analysis*: OperatePage chose by orientation alone: upright the pinned panel under the display (bottomFits, 1050 pt) or the drawer from the bottom; in landscape the panel at the side (sideFits: the band buttons, at least 560, beside a 320 panel) or the drawer from the right. A new stacked = portrait && !sideFits keeps the controls under the display only where the side does not fit: a 13" iPad (1024 wide) and a Mac window upright take the side arrangement, pinned or the drawer from the right; an 11" iPad (834) and a mini (744) keep theirs (482 / 400 left for the band buttons). 157. **SmartLink:** Forgot password? on the SmartLink sign-in (*in build 215 / Android beta 120*) **In short:** A *Forgot password?* button on the SmartLink sign-in form (Bob Manley WB3BOB). It asks FlexRadio's sign-in service to mail a password reset link; the password is changed on FlexRadio's page, never in the app. Released in build 215 / Android beta 120; the live test passed before the release and the user confirmed it on a device the same evening. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Bob Manley WB3BOB, TestFlight 2026-11-23: "Can't reset password"; the user: "Onko muuten tuo reset password mahdollista lisätä meidän softaan?"). *Pre-analysis*: the sign-in uses Auth0's password-realm grant on frtest.auth0.com (Username-Password-Authentication, SmartSDR's client id); the same tenant's /dbconnections/change_password with the e-mail typed sends FlexRadio's reset mail, as SmartSDR's Forgot password does; the app never handles the new password. A button under the sign-in, the reply shown. Test: one real reset request to the user's own SmartLink address, with the user's yes. Size: small. *Drafted and tested against a stand-in, 2026-11-25 (the user's night order: on toward the next build from what was promised; kept out of the tree until agreed)*: the service's own documented call (Auth0's Authentication API, Change Password: POST /dbconnections/change_password with the e-mail, the connection "Username-Password-Authentication" and the client id, read on auth0.com the same night: 200 with a line of plain text when the mail has been sent, 400, 404 or 429 otherwise), added to the shared core's SmartLinkClient beside the sign-in, as the tenant and client id live there. On the Connect page's sign-in form (signed out, or *+ Account*) a **Forgot password?** button under the password field, lit once an e-mail is typed; it asks first ("Send a password reset e-mail? FlexRadio's sign-in service mails a reset link to ADDRESS. The new password is set on its page, not in this app."), and the answer shows under the button: "Sent. If ADDRESS has a SmartLink account, a reset link is on its way: set the new password on the page it opens, then sign in here." (the service does not always say whether the account exists), or why not in amber. The form is one button's row taller (about 50 pt). Tested with a stand-in for the service on this machine (mobile/tools/auth0-mock.py, OKSASDR_TEST_AUTH0), nothing sent out: the request carries exactly the three fields to that path; sent, no such account (404), too many requests (429), a refused request (400), no answer (given up after 15 s) and no service at all each give their line; rendered at 440 x 956, the user's phone size at 115 % text and 1366 x 1024. The real service, with the user's yes (2026-11-25: "157 saat lähettää"): asked at 08:43 from the app's own form for the user's own SmartLink address, the service took the request (HTTP 200, *Sent…* under the button) and its mail *Reset your password* (from FlexRadio's no-reply address, "Password Change Request ... SmartLink Login", the link on the sign-in service's own host) arrived the same minute; the link was not opened, so the password is unchanged. Not tested: iOS and Android, the on-screen keyboard over the form. Classic check of the whole next tree: no change (the form shows only signed out or adding an account). Chosen for the next build. *On a device (the user, 2026-11-25 evening, after the release):* "157 ok".

162. **Radio:** Save over the profile in use without typing its name (*in build 215 / Android beta 120*) **In short:** Saving a profile again under its own name without typing the name: *Save* with the name field empty saves over the profile or App setup chosen above, after a question that names it. Released in build 215 / Android beta 120; the user confirmed it on a device the same evening. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (Larry Averbeck W9FLA, OksaSDR Users 2026-11-24: "Often, I find a small change that I would like to intentionally save using the same Profile name. (I do not like the Flex Auto Save capability.) As currently implemented, I have to retype that Profile name in order to re-save it. Would you consider adding a Re-Save option for the three Profiles and for the App setups"). *Pre-analysis* (ProfileControls.qml, MobileEngine::saveProfile): Save is enabled only with a typed name. A Global profile of that name is overwritten (`profile global save`); a TX or Mic profile is refused when the name exists ("delete it first"), as the app only sends `profile transmit|mic create` and upstream holds that the radio cannot overwrite those (its #2712; its own reference lists a `save` verb beside create: to try on the FLEX with a scratch profile whether `profile transmit save "name"` and `profile mic save "name"` replace, else delete and create in turn as the re-save, the profile made active again); an App setup of that name asks *Replace the setup?*. *Tested on the FLEX-6600M (SmartSDR 4.3.16), 2026-11-24, with a scratch profile ZZ_OKSA_TEST* (the user: "162 voi tallentaa päälle testeissä"; the app's own client through the render tool's new `radiocmd`; the tune power 10 → 11 and the mic level 50 → 49 as the marks, both put back): `profile transmit save "name"` is no command (50000016), but `profile transmit create "name"` and `profile mic create "name"` on a name that exists answer 0 and write the current settings over that profile, with the radio's Auto Save on and with it off (loaded back, the scratch profile held 11 and 49 while Default kept 10 and 50), and make it the current one. So the radio can save over a TX or a Mic profile, upstream's #2712 and our "delete it first" are wrong, and the re-save is the same one command for all three kinds (`profile global save`, `profile transmit create`, `profile mic create`). Seen on the way: `profile mic load` answers 50000078 and loads all the same; a plain TCP client cannot set the tune power (50000043), the app's client can. The radio as it was after the tests (15 TX and 25 Mic profiles, Default current, tune power 10, mic level 50, Auto Save on). Idea, to agree with the user first: Save with the name left empty saves over the profile or setup picked above, after a question that names it; no new control. Tests on the FLEX with scratch profiles only (create, change one setting, re-save, load another, load back, read the setting), each of the three kinds and an App setup; the user's own profiles untouched. Size: small. *Drafted and tested 2026-11-25 (the user's night order: on toward the next build from what was promised), kept out of the tree until the pictures are agreed*: Save is lit also with the name field empty when a profile or setup is chosen above; it then asks *Save over “name”?* ("TX profile: the settings now in use replace the ones saved in it.") and saves on Yes; a typed name that exists asks the same, a new name saves at once as before; an App setup goes to its *Replace the setup?* as before; the engine no longer refuses an existing TX or Mic name. The two hints say it ("Save with no name saves over the profile chosen above."); nothing moves on the tab (the user's phone size at 115 % text and an iPad's side drawer compared with build 214: the hints' text and the lit Save buttons are the only differences). On the FLEX, as the tester works (the radio's Auto Save off for the test, on again after): a scratch TX profile made on the tab; tune power 10 to 11 and loaded away and back without a save: 10 again (the change was lost, so the test can tell); 11 again, Save with no name, Yes: `profile transmit create` sent, loaded away and back: 11; 12, Save, No: nothing sent, loaded back: 11. A scratch Mic profile the same way with the mic level (50 / 49): 49 came back. Global: the question names the profile in use, No sends nothing; Yes was not pressed (it would have written over the user's own profile; the command is the one Save sent before). An App setup in the test's own settings: saved, the theme changed, Save with no name, *Replace the setup?*, Yes: saved again; deleted. The scratch profiles deleted, Default in use, tune power 10, mic level 50, Auto Save on. Chosen for the next build (the user, 2026-11-25: "162 re-save ok"). *On a device (the user, 2026-11-25 evening):* "162, 169 ja 170 kunnossa".

165. **Connect:** An iPhone or iPad finds the radio on its own network **In short:** An iPhone or iPad does not find the radio on its own network: iOS needs Apple's multicast entitlement for that, which is why iOS testers connect by IP address or SmartLink. You sent Apple's request form on 2026-11-24; the code change is small once Apple grants it. **From you:** Apple's answer: a word when it arrives, if it comes to a mailbox I do not watch. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-24 at the cabin, after verifying 29 on Android: "lähiverkkohaku pitäisi silloin toimia eikä kukaan ole valittanut testaajista ettei toimisi"). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-24*: it cannot work in today's builds, and nobody complains because the instructions send iOS testers to the IP address or SmartLink (TESTING.md; the manual's "connect by IP address for now"). A FlexRadio announces itself with UDP broadcasts to port 4992 and discovery only listens for them (RadioDiscovery binds AnyIPv4:4992). Apple's TN3179 (Understanding local network privacy, Multicast operations): on iOS "to send or receive multicast or broadcast traffic, sign your app with the com.apple.developer.networking.multicast entitlement", and receiving an incoming UDP broadcast is on its list; enforced from iOS 16 (Apple's Local Network Privacy FAQ). The iOS build is signed automatically with no entitlements file (mobile/CMakeLists.txt sets no CODE_SIGN_ENTITLEMENTS, mobile/ios has none), so the broadcasts never reach the app and "Searching this network…" stays. The Local Network permission (NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription in Info.plist) is another thing and is in place. What it takes: (1) the account holder asks Apple for the Multicast Networking entitlement (a form on developer.apple.com: what the app does with it, here finding a FlexRadio by its discovery broadcasts; Apple answers by mail); (2) the capability on the App ID, an entitlements file with the key in mobile/ios and CODE_SIGN_ENTITLEMENTS in mobile/CMakeLists.txt (the CI signs automatically with -allowProvisioningUpdates, which makes the profile); (3) a test on an iPhone or iPad at a radio's own network: the developer's radio is at the cabin, so the next visit there or a tester at home; (4) the manual, the README, mobile/README.md and TESTING.md lose "connect by IP address for now". The request was sent by the user on 2026-11-24 in the evening ("Multicast lomake lähetetty"; in the morning still "165 ei ole haettu"): Apple's answer comes by mail and is watched for. The text for Apple's form (developer.apple.com/contact/request/networking-multicast: the app, why it must receive the radio's broadcasts, that it only listens) was drafted for the user the same day. It says the app sends no broadcasts, which holds from 167 on, so 167 goes into the build that carries the entitlement at the latest. Not known: whether the iPad app on a Mac is held to it (macOS itself does not require the entitlement). Size: small in code; the wait is Apple's. Not chosen for a build.
166. **Diagnostics:** The log says when a radio is found on the network and which way a connection was made (*in build 214 / Android beta 119*; previewed as 213) **In short:** The log says when a radio is found on the network and which way a connection was made. Released in build 214 / Android beta 119. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (found 2026-11-24 verifying 29: the developer link's log of the user's test at the cabin showed the multicast lock and a local connection, but neither the radio appearing nor the card tapped; a tester's "it does not find my radio" would be read from nothing too). Now: `discovery: a radio on this network: MODEL vVERSION at ADDRESS` when a radio first comes to the list and `discovery: the radio at ADDRESS is no longer on this network` when it goes (no serial number, no nickname); at a connect `connect: a radio found on this network, ...`, `connect: by address, ...`, `connect: over SmartLink, MODEL` or `connect: the demo radio` (a favourite already logged its own line). Tested 2026-11-24 with the render tool: announcements of a test radio (mobile/tools/discovery-announce.py: the radio's discovery text to this machine's UDP 4992, a made-up serial) put the card on the Connect page and both discovery lines in the log; a tap on that card connected to the real FLEX with the found-on-this-network line; a connect by address and one over SmartLink logged theirs; slice A 14.074 DIGU each time. Not tested: on a device. Size: small.
167. **Network:** No discovery probes for radios the app does not serve (*in build 214 / Android beta 119*; previewed as 213) **In short:** The app no longer sends discovery probes for radio families it does not serve; it only listens for a FlexRadio, which is what the request to Apple (165) states. Released in build 214 / Android beta 119. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (found 2026-11-24 while writing 165's request to Apple, which says what the app sends). The shared discovery source the app starts looks for every family: it listens for a FlexRadio's broadcasts (UDP 4992) and also sweeps for Hermes Lite 2 and ANAN radios, a broadcast probe to UDP 1024 every five seconds on each network the device is on, for as long as the app runs, connected or not; so since its first build. Captured on the dev machine with build 212's binary on the Connect page: three broadcasts every 5 s (63 bytes to the subnet's broadcast address and to 255.255.255.255 for the HL2 sweep, 60 bytes for ANAN). A FlexRadio client has no use for them: traffic and wake-ups on every network, the same on a café's Wi-Fi, and an answer would list a radio the app cannot work. The privacy text (mobile/PRIVACY.md, Local network discovery) says the app listens for discovery broadcasts; it has been sending these as well, so the fix makes the text true. iOS blocks them without the multicast entitlement, so they would begin there with 165. Fix: LocalDiscoveryOptions::families names the families to look for (empty: all, as upstream); the app asks for "flex" only, so the other sources are never made. Tested: the same capture with the new binary shows no broadcast from the machine in 14 s; a FlexRadio is found as before (166's tests). The desktop's discovery test (radio_discovery_source_test) passes with a new case: every family by default, "flex" alone when named. Not tested: on a device. Size: small.
168. **Layout:** Texts that do not fit on a narrow phone at a large text size: the rest of the app **In short:** Texts that do not fit on a narrow phone at a large text size, in the rest of the app after 164: every page, tab and dialog was measured at your phone's numbers and at the worst case. The finds are few and small: some placeholders, the Network panel's labels, the PTT key row, and Setup's fourteen tabs taking three rows on a phone on its side. **From you:** your picks among the finds and how to mend each; I show them with before and after pictures first (nothing to do before those). **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-24, after 164: "Muiden sivujen tarkistus kapealla ruudulla ja suurella tekstillä"). *Checked 2026-11-24, by numbers*: the render tool's new `cuttext` lists every text on screen that is elided, painted past its box or ending outside the window. Run on the FLEX at the user's own phone's numbers (344 x 764, text 115 %, from its developer-link log), at 164's tester's (360 x 780, 130 %), at the worst case (344 x 764, 135 %) and at a baseline (360 x 780, 100 %), with an Android phone's fonts (mobile/tools/render-fonts-android.conf) and default text size: the main page, the ⋯ menu, thirteen choosers and dialogs, the Macros panel, all thirteen Controls tabs and all fourteen Setup tabs scrolled through (some 190 views a run); then the dialogs again for texts above or below the window, and a phone on its side (764 x 344 and 780 x 360 at 115, 130 and 135 %, 44 views a run). **Found.** At every text size, 100 % too: the Spots tab's *Spot sources: …* summary button (318 of 343 px at 360 pt), the placeholders of Setup › Logging's two address fields (196 of 242 and of 277 px) and of Setup › Spots' callsign field (139 of 184 px); by design, the radio's name in the top row ("192.1…") and a CW memory's text on its button. From 130 %: Network & performance's labels (*Audio packet gap*, *Busiest threads* and its value; at 135 % also *Recent loss (10 s)* and *App CPU, one core = 100 %*), the Mem tab's *Name for the current frequency* placeholder, and Setup › Transmit's *PTT key*, which paints 10 px past its box into its value ("PTT keyNone"). At 135 % only: the Log tab's *Their park* (3 px), a FreeDV station's grid and distance (8 px), a cluster's address in Setup › Spots (2 px). Nothing else: no chooser or dialog ends outside the window, upright or on its side (the frequency keypad fills a 344 pt high window to the last row), and no text paints over another but *PTT key*; on its side the finds are the CW memory's text, the Mem placeholder (2 px at 135 %) and *PTT key*. Seen in the pictures, not a matter of text: on a phone on its side Radio Setup's fourteen tab buttons take three rows and leave the page about 100 px of the 344. A false alarm on the way, kept as a lesson: a render at 344 pt showed the whole page 18 px wider than the window, which a phone does not (its cover-screen shots in the developer link): Android draws the frequency's "monospace" with Roboto, the desktop with a wider font; hence the font mapping. To agree with the user, with pictures (164's way): which of these to mend and how (shorter placeholders, the Network panel's labels on two lines or a narrower value column, *PTT key*'s row, Setup's tabs on a low screen). Size: small each. Not chosen for a build.
169. **Layout:** What is hidden gives its room away: the FTx list to the bottom on every device (*in build 214 / Android beta 119*; previewed as 213) **In short:** What is hidden gives its room away: with PTT hidden in FT8 / FT4 the decode list and the controls run to the bottom on every device, and List only really is the list (your findings on the OnePlus Open and the iPad, after Michael Walker VA3MW asked for a full screen of decodes). Released in build 214 / Android beta 119; the user confirmed it on the devices on 2026-11-25. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-24, on the OnePlus Open and the iPad while FT8 ran, after a tester asked for a full screen of decodes: "alareunassa turhaa tyhjää", "sama ongelma sisänäytöllä", "aina tulisi maksimoida tilan käyttö ... skaalautuminen on aidosti dynaamista laite kuin laite"). The rule, part of 160: nothing hidden keeps its room; with PTT hidden (FTx running) or the band and step rows off (B/S) their place goes to what needs it, and the buttons that stay arrange themselves by the room on that device and in that orientation. The user's examples: the inner screen on its side, + Slice and + Pan beside B/S so the list runs all the way down; the inner screen upright, the bottom row's buttons a little smaller so all fit at the left. *Measured on the user's devices over the developer link, 2026-11-24 (beta 118, build 212)*: (a) the phone's cover screen (343 x 764), the Controls drawer at full screen with the FTx list: Close and ▼ end 88.5 pt above the window's bottom, the page's own bottom row under them ends 60 pt above it; a render of the same state without a phone's safe areas leaves 13 pt, so the cause is in how the drawer and Android's insets add up (the Basic style's Drawer pads its content by the safe area; not yet pinned down, wants the numbers from the device); (b) the inner screen (698 x 751), the drawer at the side: it ends at 567 pt as it does to leave the PTT row free, though there is no PTT, with the waterfall's last strip and + Slice / + Pan under it; (c) the iPad, controls pinned: the FTx tab has no Scroll › List only at all (the choice is given only where the drawer passes its height), so the list scrolls away with the whole tab. *To draft and show in pictures before any of it is pushed*: with no PTT row the side drawer and the pinned panel run to the window's bottom, the bottom row's buttons gathered left of them; List only on every device (the main buttons stay, the list takes the rest, More opens the settings); the phone's gap gone. Size: medium. Not chosen for a build. *Found 2026-11-24 evening, the cause of (a) and of half of (b), a defect since build 203*: Qt lays the page inside the status bar and the navigation bar (ApplicationWindow pads its content by the safe area since Qt 6.9), so the page is shorter than the window by both with no keyboard anywhere; roadmap 142's sums took "the window's height less the page's" for the on-screen keyboard. So on every device with bars: a phone's drawer lifts Close and PTT by the status bar (41 pt on the OnePlus Open's cover, 38 on a 360 x 804 phone: Close ends 85 pt above the window's bottom, where the page's own row ends 50 above it); the tablet page's side drawer starts at the window's top but counts its height from the page's and ends the status bar too high also with PTT showing; upright on the smaller iPads the drawer is always the short one made for the keyboard; and with the keyboard up the page ends the navigation bar's height above it (the "musta raita" of beta 117, of which beta 118 took 12 pt). No render showed any of it, a desktop window having no bars: the render tool got them (plan verb safearea TOP BOTTOM) and gave the phone's numbers back (Close at 672, the phone 675). A second finding: List only takes the list's room only where the tab's view is 520 pt or more; the cover screen's 489 never reaches it, so the whole tab scrolls there (what the user took for List only), and the 360 x 804 phone's 529 does: the fixed part (Decode, the bands, the CQ row, the state, Show) then takes 373 pt and the list is four rows ("eihän list only tilassa näy normaalisti kaikki nuo painikkeet"). *Drafted, shown to the user in five before / after sheets and approved the same evening ("1 kelpaa ja 2 tee preview")*: (1) the sums use the keyboard's own cover and the bars' own insets, the page ends at the keyboard's top, the bottom rows stand on the navigation bar's inset (8 and 12 pt lower); (2) the pinned panel spans the bottom row whenever that row is there (it spanned it only with PTT showing): 93 pt more list on the iPad in both orientations; (3) the bottom row a button's height while FT8 / FT4 hides PTT, as on the phone; (4) the side drawer to the window's bottom while PTT is hidden, the row's − + Band B/S + Slice + Pan gathered left of it and smaller where six do not fit at their size (the inner screen upright: 314 pt for about 346); (5) List only shows the slot, the CQ row with More and the QSO's state, then the list: Decode, FT8 / FT4 and the bands show until decoding runs, Show and the settings under More; from a 260 pt view on; in the pinned panel too when FTx is the only section shown; the CQ row's type gives way where five buttons do not fit. Classic changes only while FT8 / FT4 runs (2-5) and on devices with bars (1); a desktop window without FT8 is as it was. *The preview on the user's OnePlus Open the same evening (beta 119 as the run's APK)*: the numbers came out as rendered (the outer screen 343 x 764 with bars 39 / 44: Close at 671-715, level with the page's Controls; the inner screen in a voice mode: the side drawer 11 pt above the PTT row; with the keyboard up the page ends at the keyboard and Close 5 pt above it; List only twelve rows). The user asked how decoding is stopped when List only hides Decode ("mites pysäytät kun sitä nappia ei näy"): More, then Decoding; offered the row always in sight at the cost of a list row and a half, the user chose to keep it under More ("A vaihtoehto"). The Controls tab falling back to DSP at each change of screen became 170. One freeze of 16 s or more right after the phone was opened (20:19:25, the app killed and started again) did not come back in four more openings nor in the render tool; its cause is open. *From the same test (in build 214)*: the side drawer that ends above the PTT row kept the margin of a drawer reaching the window's edge at its bottom, 23 pt of nothing between its last line and PTT (the user, marking it in red on a screenshot: "controls laatikon alalaitaa ennen ptt ... jos tuota mustaa tyhjää siinä ei olisi teksti näkyisi kokonaan"); it is half a spacing now, as the phone's drawer has, and the tab's view 18 pt higher (391 → 409 in the render at 698 x 751). And on the iPad with build 213 the pinned panel offered no List only though FTx was its only section ("ei ole list only valintaa???"): the panel counted the names in the stored list of pinned sections, and a name of a section this radio does not offer now (Amp with no amplifier set, a tab of an older build) is in that list without being on screen. Reproduced in the render tool with "Digi,Amp" stored (no Scroll row) and mended by counting the sections that show (the Scroll row is there). *The iPad's other results with build 213* (window 1366 x 967 on its 1366 x 1024 screen, no bars, so the bars change nothing there): the pinned panel and the side drawer as drawn (9, 11-13 and Space PTT with the Magic Keyboard in order), with the keyboard up the page ends at the keyboard in both orientations and PTT keys upright. *Open, and older than 213 (first seen in build 210)*: in landscape with the on-screen keyboard up and the Log tab's call field in use, PTT is at 417-483 and its copy over it, two presses keyed (both within a second of the keyboard coming up), and eight more were logged at y 483-495, just under PTT, which lets the field go and puts the keyboard away ("vaakassa oikeassa paikassa mutta ei toimi sulkee näppiksen", "väitän kosketusalue ongelmaksi"); a press meant for the Sent field landed on the label under it the same way. Qt's own view scroll for the keyboard is not it (read in qiosinputcontext.mm: it scrolls only when the cursor is under the keyboard, which the call field is not). The next build logs each such press as the screen has it with the window's place on the screen, to see whether the view and the touches disagree; an iPad screenshot of that state asked from the user. Seen beside it: "TX 200 Hz" painted under Zoom in an upright iPad's 320 pt panel (to 168). *The freeze found (2026-11-24, late; mended in build 214, the preview never reached the testers)*: reproduced in the render tool at the inner screen's size (698 x 751) and the iPad's (1366 x 967): FT8 decoding with eight or more decodes in the list, List only on and the drawer on another tab (Log), and the app stands still for good. The drawer keeps every tab loaded and its stack sizes only the tab shown, so a tab not shown is as high as its own content, and the FTx tab was given that as the height of its view. It took it for room for the list (List only from a 260 pt view), which made the tab lower, which took the room away, every decode came back as a row, and so on without end. On the phone that evening the tab had gone back to DSP when the phone was opened (170): this case. Mended: only the tab shown is told a view's height, and the list no longer works its height out from its own place in the tab (the tab is made as high as the view, and the list is the one item in it that stretches). The pictures before and after are the same at four sizes (the strip through the rows identical), and the tabs were changed back and forth with 45 decodes without a stall. Three decodes did not freeze it, nor the phone's half-height drawer (a view under 260 pt). *PTT above the keyboard in landscape, more data and a mend (in build 214)*: thirty presses on the iPad that evening, sixteen keyed and fourteen came in at y 483-507, up to 24 pt under the button, six of them inside the keyboard's frame (it starts at 494 and passes a touch on a little under its top edge). Read in Qt 6.12's iOS plugin: the window is maximized to the screen's safe area (its view at y 32, which is why the page has no bars), a touch is the view's own point, and the keyboard's place (494 = 526 − 32) shows no view scroll, so nothing was found there that could set the view and the touches apart (a reading, not shown on the device). Two things put the keyboard away: the app's own rule (a press outside the field and PTT lets the field go) and Qt's hide-the-keyboard gesture, which takes the field's focus when a touch moves onto the keyboard's frame. Now every PTT that can stand above the on-screen keyboard (the page's, its copy over the side drawer, the drawers' own; the phone's too) takes a press up to 32 pt under its lower edge while the keyboard is up, and holds as long as the finger is down; Qt's gesture is not given a touch that begins on what keeps the field (iOS; it cannot be tried without the device). For the next test the log says where each press on PTT landed (how far down the button, or under it), and the developer link can switch on press marks: each press written to the log with what is under it and drawn as a ring for two seconds, so the tester sees where the app takes a press against where the finger was. *On a device (the user, 2026-11-25 evening):* "162, 169 ja 170 kunnossa".
170. **Controls:** The Controls drawer keeps its tab and stays open when a foldable changes screens (*in build 214 / Android beta 119*) **In short:** The Controls drawer keeps its tab and stays open when a foldable is opened or closed (your finding). Released in build 214 / Android beta 119; the user confirmed it on a device on 2026-11-25. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-24, the first minutes with the preview of build 213 on the OnePlus Open with FT8 running: "kun hyppää etu ja sisäkannen väliä controls häviää valitusta tilasta esim ftx ... controls hyppää aina dsp painikkeeseen kun näyttöä vaihdetaan"). *Pre-analysis*: the outer screen shows the phone page and the inner one the tablet page; Main.qml's Loader builds the other page anew when the window crosses that line (a fold, a rotation on some sizes, a resized window), and each page has its own drawer and ControlsPanel, whose tab is a plain property starting at 0 (DSP). Nothing of it is new in 213. *Made*: the engine keeps the drawer's tab (Engine.controlsSection, set by the panel, taken up by a new one) and whether the drawer is open (Engine.controlsDrawerOpen: a new page opens its drawer again; a page going away does not count as closing it; not where the controls are pinned; closed again at every new connection). Kept while the app runs, not across a restart. Rendered by resizing 343 x 764 ↔ 698 x 751 with FT8 running: FTx stays up through both changes, a drawer closed on one screen stays closed on the other. Size: small. *On a device (the user, 2026-11-25 evening):* "162, 169 ja 170 kunnossa".
171. **Fixes:** PTT gives way to FT8 / FT4 only where they key (*in build 214 / Android beta 119*) **In short:** Decode takes the slice to DIGU, and PTT gives way to FT8 / FT4 only while the transmit slice is the one they key (your finding: PTT had vanished in a voice mode). Released in build 214 / Android beta 119. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-24, the preview of build 213 on the OnePlus Open, test 8 in a voice mode: "ptt hävisi nyt kokonaan"). *Pre-analysis from the phone's log*: a memory had put slice A on 3.699 LSB, which ended decoding as it should ("decoding off: slice A left DIGU / DIGL for LSB"); the user then pressed Decode there. Decode starts decoding on the TX slice in whatever mode it is, the pages hid PTT and Macros whenever decoding ran (PttButton.digiHidden = decoding on and not RADE), and the guard that ends decoding acts on a change of mode, which never came: LSB, "0 decodes" every slot, no PTT for three and a half minutes, until More › Decoding. Not new in 213, but List only now folds Decode away while decoding runs, so nothing on screen said why. The same rule took PTT from a voice slice while another slice decoded. *Made*: Engine.digiOwnsTx (decoding on and the slice FT8 / FT4 key in DIGU or DIGL); PTT, Macros, the bottom rows and the drawers follow it, and List only folds Decode and the bands away only then, so with decoding on for a slice in LSB they show and say so. The display already went by the slice's mode (PanadapterView.digiMode). Rendered at 343 x 764 and 698 x 751: Decode pressed in LSB keeps PTT and Macros (the drawer's and the side drawer's copy too), DIGU hides them, back to LSB ends decoding and brings them back. The user's rule on reading the cause: "decodaukset liittyvät aina tiettyihin mode valintoihin muulloin ne eivät voi jäädä päälle" (FTx, CW and the Digi modes each with their own). CW's and the Digi tab's decoders already follow the mode; FTx's Decode did not, so Decode pressed on a slice outside DIGU / DIGL now takes that slice to DIGU where it is, as a band button does on its dial frequency, and leaving DIGU / DIGL ends decoding as before. Rendered at both sizes: Decode in LSB, the slice in DIGU with the FT8 filter and PTT hidden; LSB again, decoding off, the voice filter and PTT back. Size: small.
172. **Spots:** A watched station's last five spots behind an arrow on its alert **In short:** A watched station's last five spots behind an arrow on its alert, the newest first, and the time after which a station alerts again chosen as the age of the spots shown is (your ideas while hunting a station). Pre-analysed, not built. **From you:** one answer: is the list wanted for new-DXCC alerts too? And a yes to my reading of the time: one chosen time in place of the fixed 30 minutes, the same for every repeat. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-24, hunting a station while testing the preview of build 213: "kun spotteja tulee useampi koskien asemaa jota metsästän tulisi spotissa olla painike nuoli alaspäin josta näkyisi kaikki spotit ja uusin ylimpänä 5 viimeisintä"). *Pre-analysis*: the alert banner (132, SpotAlertBanner) shows one spot: the call, MHz, mode and country, why it alerts, the spotter and the time, with Tune and × (the next alert; "N more" counts other alerts waiting, at most 20). A spot of the same call on the same band within 30 minutes of its alert is dropped unseen (checkSpotAlert's m_spotAlertSeen by call and band), so a station that moves or is spotted again by others leaves no trace; a spot on another band raises a new banner and pushes the old one into the queue. Nothing keeps a station's spots together. *To make*: the engine keeps, per alerting call, its last five spots (time, MHz, mode, spotter, the spot's index) also when the 30 minute rule raises no new banner; the banner of that call gets ▼ when there is more than one, which opens the list under it, newest first, each row with its time, frequency, mode and spotter, a tap tuning to that spot as Tune does to the newest; the banner's own line follows the newest spot. Still not a popup (it must not take the keyboard from the page, Space PTT). The user the same evening, on the 30 minutes: "hunting spoteissa pitää myös pystyä valitsemaan tuo aika ja sillä kuten muissakin spoteissa. Jos ollaan jo spotin bandilla uusi hälytys noudattaa asetettua aikaa myös uusinnan suhteen": the time after which a station alerts again is to be chosen, as the age of the spots shown is (the Spots tab's Any age / 5 min / 15 min / 30 min), and a new alert on the band one is already on keeps to that time too (the developer's reading: one chosen time in place of the fixed 30 minutes, the same for every repeat; to confirm when it is built). Still to settle: whether the list is wanted for new-DXCC alerts too. Size: small to medium. Not chosen for a build.
173. **Fixes:** FT8 / FT4 after FreeDV decodes only 750 to 2250 Hz when DIGU is chosen by hand (*in build 215 / Android beta 120*) **In short:** After FreeDV the radio kept a narrow receive filter for DIGU, and FT8 then heard only part of the band. *Decode* now opens the filter to 3 kHz each time it starts. Tested on the FLEX. Released in build 215 / Android beta 120. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (found 2026-11-25 in the release tests on the FLEX). RADE works in DIGU with its own filter (750..2250), and the radio keeps that as DIGU's filter; RADE off puts the slice back to the mode it had, but DIGU chosen later from the mode chooser comes up with RADE's filter, and Decode then starts without opening it. Seen: after a FreeDV test slice A in DIGU had 750..2250; Decode on left it so; put back with `filt 0 0 3000`. *Pre-analysis*: widenDigiFilter() (it acts only on a filter narrower than 300..2700) runs when a band button or Decode itself changes the mode (171), not when Decode starts on a slice already in DIGU / DIGL: call it at every start of decoding. RADE's end could also give DIGU its filter back before it leaves the mode. Test on the FLEX: FreeDV on and off, DIGU from the mode chooser, Decode: 0..3000 and decodes across the passband; a filter the user narrowed on purpose while decoding stays (only the start opens it). Size: small. *Done 2026-11-25 (the user's night order: on toward the next build)*: Decode calls widenDigiFilter() at every start on a slice already in DIGU / DIGL. Tested on the FLEX, receive only, by the app's own filter chooser: DIGU narrowed to 1000..2000, Decode: opened to 0..3000; narrowed to 750..2250 while decoding: stayed so over three slots, with decodes, whose reports then read some 30 dB too high (the same three stations -7, -5 and -7 dB with the open filter, +24, +27 and +27 with the narrow one: the noise the reports are measured against is cut away; mended as 195); Decode off and on: opened again; DIGL narrowed to -2710..-1710, Decode: -3000..0. FreeDV itself was not run again after the fix: that the radio brings DIGU back with the narrow filter was shown with a filter command and a mode change. RADE's end giving DIGU its filter back was left out (the start of decoding covers it).
174. **Devices:** TGXL SO2R and the Antenna Genius **In short:** Further steps for a Tuner Genius XL in SO2R with an Antenna Genius (Ben Morris's station): the active channel shown and switched, the readings per channel, and the open points of the earlier audit of the 4O3A devices. The Antenna Genius itself is on the Amp tab since build 154. It needs a tester's logs, since your station has neither device. **From you:** nothing now; pick it by number. **Details:** (2026-11-04, the user: after build 153, with Ben Morris's setup: an SO2R TGXL on an Antenna Genius SO2R): the active channel (status active=1|2) shown and switched (activate ch=1|2), the A / B readings per channel, and the Antenna Genius (4O3A's AG TCP/IP API, github.com/4o3a/genius-api-docs, port 9007) read, then controlled. Built from Ben's app log (Log › Copy after Controls › Amp), not from the documents alone. With it the audit's open points: antA's base, the PGXL setup write and its authcode from a stale read, the PGXL sequence past 255, the meters and interlock a real PGXL creates on the radio (genius-mock to create them too).
175. **Desktop:** Tailnet shim setup **In short:** Setting up upstream's Tailnet shim (the radio on your own Tailscale network) from the app. Kept for the record only: you placed it with the desktop program. The app already connects to a radio on a tailnet by its name or address. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (noted 2026-11-04, the user: it belongs to the desktop; installing it from a mobile device is not on the roadmap now, kept for the record): upstream AetherSDR's flex-tailnet-shim is a Docker waveform inside a FLEX-8000 / Aurora that puts the radio on the owner's tailnet (a single-use Tailscale auth key, an allow list, sharing the station's 4O3A devices), set up once over the radio's LAN from the desktop's Waveforms › Remote Access. OksaSDR already connects to a radio on a tailnet by its MagicDNS name or 100.x address (the radio list, build 151); what the app would add is only the install and provisioning (download and upload of the 8 MB image, the provisioning API on port 48992, the admin token in the Keychain).
176. **Usability:** From Ian Gilchrist VK1HF's own build **In short:** What is left of Ian Gilchrist VK1HF's own build (2026-11-09): Sign out as one tap with a confirmation, the CW repeat's pause timed from the radio unkeying, and a receive jitter buffer over SmartLink (Off / 100 / 200 / 400 ms). His other points are done or have their own numbers (62, 56). Ideas, not pre-analysed. **From you:** nothing now; pick any of the three by number when wanted. **Details:** (mail 2026-11-09, his code GPLv3, offered as a branch): two SmartLink accounts with favourite radios that connect in one tap (now 62); Sign out as one tap with a confirmation (our two-tap "Sign out?" is easy to miss on a phone); the CW repeat's pause timed from the radio unkeying (cwx sent= at the batch's end, then TX off for 800 ms) rather than from the queue emptying; an RX jitter buffer over SmartLink (Off / 100 / 200 / 400 ms, upstream discussion #6537). Size: medium. **Details:** (Michael Walker, the same mail, with a Siri Shortcut sketch: dictate the phonetics, keep each word's first letter; layers 0 and 1 now 79). *Pre-analysis 2026-11-15* (the user: "pohdi samalla Michael Walkerin Siri asiaa ja onko se Gemini android puolella", then any AI, Claude's free app among them: which is the most sensible): iOS App Shortcuts need no setup and work locked and in CarPlay, but a phrase cannot carry free text (Apple DTS: no arbitrary string parameters in the phrase; one AppEntity / AppEnum at most), so it is two turns: "Hey Siri, log a contact in OksaSDR", Siri asks the call, the operator spells it; a Swift App Intent inside the Qt / CMake Xcode project needs a spike first; iOS 27's Siri AI tools are not available to TestFlight testers. Android: Google Assistant left phones in September 2026; App Actions are Assistant only; Gemini calls third-party apps through AppFunctions (Android 16+) only in a private preview, so today "Hey Google, open OksaSDR" and the app's own voice entry. Claude (or ChatGPT) as the front end: a remote MCP connector (the free plan has one) is called from the vendor's cloud, so it would need our own relay to the phone, with sign-in, hosting and a transmitter beside a cloud path, and no wake word: not sensible for this. The microphone shared with the radio audio and PTT to test first (iOS's session interruption, Android 10's capture sharing). Sources: Apple's App Shortcuts HIG and developer forum threads 777854 and 832277; developer.android.com/ai/appfunctions; support.claude.com's connectors article.
