181. **Fixes:** What Mohammed Alkhayarin A71AT's log of 2026-11-17 showed (*b and c in build 216 / Android beta 121*) **In short:** What Mohammed Alkhayarin A71AT's log of 2026-11-17 showed. (b) After an audio interruption in the background the receive audio could not start until the app came to the front: it is now tried again by itself, every five seconds and at once in front. (c) The 3D view switched off as too slow when the app was merely busy: the profile-load case was mended in build 184 (94); the rest, seen on a tester's phone after an audio route change, is mended too: the graphics chip is judged by how long a frame itself takes to draw. Both released in build 216 / Android beta 121. A wrong held-for line in the log was mended earlier (84 k). **From you:** for (b) a try on your iPad: the app to the background, a call or another app's sound in between, and receive after it; nothing for (c). **Details:** (build 182, iPad mini A17 Pro, iOS 27.0.1, the AU-510 over SmartLink, MIDI PTT on a CTR2 at up to 520 W; no words in the mail): (a) SmartLink connected every time, once on the second try (84 f, 00:29). (b) With the app in the background an audio interruption began (10:13:50, a call or another app's audio); the radio dropped the session at 10:14:22, the reconnect in the background (84 j) made it again in 7 s, but the receive audio could not start ("failed to open RX audio sink", the play-and-record session not allowed to start in the background: "Session activation failed"), so iOS suspended the app again and the radio dropped that session too; brought to the front at 10:15:11 it was back in 7 s. To weigh: after an interruption the reconnect waits for the front, or the audio starts again as playback only in the background. (c) The 3D view was switched off as "too slow on this GPU" in both runs, each time just after a global profile was loaded (10:02:26; 00:30:18, a second after the app went inactive): on an A17 Pro that is the busy GUI thread (the profile's status burst) or the inactive app, not the GPU; the five late frames should not count then (a warm-up after a profile load and while inactive). (d) The "held ... 2147483647 s" line again (in the build 183 list). *(c), 2026-11-25:* the case of this log, a profile load or the app away from the front, was mended already as 94 in build 184 (this item's summary said none was). What was still open showed in Mattias SM6YEC's log of 2026-11-25 (a Galaxy S24+, beta 120): "3D view too slow on this GPU (frames 531 ms behind the data): switching to the 2D waterfall" eleven seconds after an audio route change, the app in front and no profile loaded. The judgement knew only of rows waiting for a frame, which a busy GUI thread causes as well as a slow GPU. *Chosen for the next build by the user; done, not in a build yet:* each frame is timed on the thread that draws it (from the scene graph beginning it to its end with the swap), and a row that waited over half a second counts against the GPU only when the frame before it took over 250 ms to draw; a late row after a quick frame clears the count. *Tested on the FLEX in the render tool with the 3D view on, ten holds of the GUI thread of 0.9 s each:* on the test machine's software GPU the frames really are slow (drawn in 630 to 1050 ms, the rows waiting as long) and the view is still given up, as before; with the frames given as drawn in 20 ms (AETHER_3D_DRAW_MS, a development switch) 67 rows waited over half a second and none was counted. Whether it would have kept 3D on Mattias's phone is not known: his frames' own times were not in the log; they are from now on (AETHER_3D_WAIT_LOG shows them). *(b), 2026-11-25, chosen for the next build by the user; done, not in a build yet:* a receive sink that cannot be opened is no longer final. The first opening at a connect and the one after an audio interruption now use the retries the route changes had (six, half a second apart); when they run out the audio engine says so (rxOpenFailed) and the app tries again every 5 s, and at once when it comes to the front, with the play-and-record session set up first; a line in the log for each step. *Tested on the desktop with the demo radio* (AETHER_RX_OPEN_FAILS, a development switch that makes openings fail): with ten failing openings, seven fail, "RX could not be opened again", the 5 s retry, three more fail, then "RX stream started" and "receive opened again"; a normal start opens once with no failure. On the FLEX: receive starts, PTT keys from the page and from the drawer. *Not tried on an iPad*: an interruption in the background (a call, another app's sound) with the app connected is the user's to try. *On the user's iPad with build 216 (2026-11-26 08:25-08:45, their word: "181. toimi"; the developer link's log):* two interruptions with the app in the background (08:30:00 and 08:33:27); receive started again 0.2 s and 0.6 s after the app came back to the front ("audio session back: restarting receive audio"). In the second the interruption ended while the app was still in the background, and iOS refused the session there ("setActive deferred (app not in front yet): Session activation failed"), so the iPad stayed silent for 20 s until the app was opened. The user on that: there must be no silence in the background (**215**).
182. **Devices:** The Tuner Genius XL's own amplifier keying in the app: its *PTT OUT when tuning* **In short:** The Tuner Genius XL's own amplifier keying settings in the app (PTT OUT when tuning, the PTT IN source), which decide whether an amplifier is keyed while the tuner tunes (your question on Tune TX Suppression). Not pre-analysed: it needs readings from a tester's TGXL (Hanno Vogels DK3HV), since your station has no tuner or amplifier. **From you:** nothing; waits for a tester's readings. **Details:** (setup `tuneptt1` / `tuneptt2`) and the PTT IN source (`flexradio ... source=LAN|RCA`), read and set over its port 9010 link (4O3A's TG XL API Commands, upstream discussion #2595) (the user, 2026-11-17, on SmartSDR 4.3.19's Tune TX Suppression with another maker's amplifier before a TGXL: "Tarkista"). What was found: the radio's own suppression (the per-band switch in Radio Setup › Transmit › TX band settings since build 158) holds the RCA TX1-3 outputs off only while the radio itself is in TUNE; the TGXL's autotune keys the radio over its hardware interlock line, so the radio sees an ordinary hardware PTT, not TUNE (upstream #6458), and the suppression may not cover it. With an amplifier keyed through the TGXL (radio RCA TX -> TGXL PTT IN, source RCA; TGXL PTT OUT -> amplifier), the TGXL's *PTT OUT when tuning* off keeps the amplifier in bypass while it tunes; an amplifier keyed straight from the radio's RCA TX is keyed during a TGXL autotune whatever the radio's switch. To verify with a tester's TGXL (Hanno Vogels DK3HV): the radio's status during an autotune (`transmit tune=` vs `interlock ... source=`), and the TGXL's `setup read` values. The developer's station has no amplifier or tuner. Not pre-analysed yet.

185. **Fixes:** FreeDV not transmitting at first **In short:** A tester reports that on his iPhone FreeDV does not transmit on the first try after the app starts. It does not show elsewhere: on the FLEX with the Linux build it transmits at once, and the user's own check on a device on 2026-11-25 was ok. So it is something of that tester's iPhone or setup, and only his log can tell. **From you:** nothing; waits for the tester's log. **Details:** (Scott Stanford, TestFlight feedback 2026-11-21 20:16, build 199, iPhone 16 Pro Max, iOS 27.2.1: "When I fist open the app and turn on FreeDV, it will not transmit til I select FM then go back to FreeDV then it will transmit"). To pre-analyse: what turning on FreeDV does to the slice's mode and the radio's TX path on a fresh start (DIGU, the waveform, mic selection, DAX) against what a mode change to FM and back does; reproduce on the FLEX from a fresh start (low power), the devlink log if Scott sends one. *Tried 2026-11-25 on the FLEX at 5 W with the render tool*: FreeDV on from a fresh start and PTT, then FM and back and PTT again: both keyed the radio the same way ("DAX transmit on", the radio transmitting), but the dev machine has no microphone ("mic delivered 0 Hz"), so no modem signal and 0 W either time: the difference Scott sees cannot show here. It needs his log (Log › Send to the developer right after a first PTT that sends nothing) or a device with a microphone. *Tried on the FLEX-6600M, 2026-11-25, with the released build (the Linux one) and a 3 s test clip as the audio, a voice macro through the FreeDV encoder on 14.236 at 5 W*: it transmits at the first try here. Right after a start, FreeDV on, the clip: "PTT released: voice macro done (peak 4.1 W)", 539 modem packets in 2.8 s; then FM and back to FreeDV: the same numbers. So the encoder, the DAX transmit stream and the radio's DAX source are in order from the first press, and what is left for an iPhone is its microphone's capture after FreeDV comes on (FreeDV takes the capture to its own 24 kHz path; a mode change makes it again). His log decides it, at the first FreeDV press: the line "TX audio: N packets ... mic delivered N Hz of nominal N Hz" (no microphone rate = iOS's capture; a rate with no packets = the encoder's path). Waits for that log (Log › Send to the developer right after it happens). A first attempt the same night told nothing and went wrong in one thing, see 199: the dev VM had no microphone source at all, the clip (which runs on the capture's clock) never started or ended, and the radio stayed keyed about ten seconds with no audio, through the plan's change to FM, until the plan opened the drawer; the forward power read 0.0 W throughout. The VM has a virtual microphone since (silence on a steady clock), so voice macros and FreeDV can be tested on the air here. *The user's own check on a device, 2026-11-25 evening:* "185 ok": FreeDV transmitted at the first try there (the developer's reading, confirmed by the user: "185 juuri noin"); so not reproduced on the FLEX nor on the user's device, and the tester's log is what is left.

The wait shows on the Connect page's status line, where *Check the connection* is: "Waiting for NAME…" with *Cancel*; nothing else moves on the page. A card tapped meanwhile wins. Not found in time, the radio list stays as without the choice, which is his "go to the current screen if the last radio isn't available". *Tested on the FLEX with the draft, 440 x 956*: by address: the choice turned on while connected, the app started again with no connect option: connected by itself; Disconnect: no second connect. SmartLink: connected once over SmartLink, started again: signed in, waited for the list, connected over SmartLink. The demo radio used in between: not remembered. Not tested: a radio in use by another station (the question), two SmartLink accounts (the switch), a device (a cold start after iOS or Android ended the app, coming back from the background), VoiceOver / TalkBack on the waiting line. Open, for his answer and the user: away from home, should a radio last used on the LAN be taken over SmartLink by itself when it is not found (the draft does not: it keeps the way used last, so home on the LAN after a trip waits 8 s for SmartLink's list instead; the last way wins)? Decided by the user on 2026-11-25 ("189 mukaan, mutta jos verkko on vaihtunut niin ei yritä smartlinkillä eli juuri kuten olit ajatellut"): no, the way used last is kept, as drafted; a radio last used on the LAN is never taken over SmartLink by itself. Chosen for the next build. 190. **Upstream:** Upstream maybes from the review of 2026-11-23 **In short:** Upstream maybes from the review of 2026-11-23: ten smaller upstream changes that might be worth taking later (RTTY decoder safety, the ATU on transverter outputs, a TX off delay, the voice keyer on 3.x firmware and others). None is needed now. **From you:** nothing; pick single ones by name if one starts to matter. **Details:** (the user: "Katso onko upstreamissä jotain meille"): the AGC-T sweep stopped when no audio arrives (#6711; our flat-curve check already restores AGC-T); no NaN filters in the RTTY decoder with Mark / Space at or below 0 Hz (#7390); the ATU locked out on transverter outputs (#7016, conflicts, QML dimming too); static_net_params not sent over SmartLink or a VPN, where a wrong address strands a remote radio (#7310, as an idea for MobileEngine); ggmorse's printf / fflush out of its decode loop (9636edbf1); RTTY lock and level following copy quality (#7427, conflicts, changes statsUpdated); a TX off delay so the end of speech still airs (#7059, an idea for our PTT path); DVK on 3.x firmware (#7200); the nickname on 4.3.16 (#6470); TGXL port labels (#6709).

I think it would be useful to have it on the left side or spanning the entire length of the display"; told what helps today: Big buttons' PTT across the bottom, TX › PTT › Tap on / off, a learned PTT key). Today the phone's bottom row is Controls, Macros, PTT from the left in both orientations, with no setting; only the waterfall's buttons change sides (Setup › App › Zoom buttons). (B) *Wide* gives PTT the page's width upright, with Controls and Macros in a row of a button's height above it (the drawer: Close and the height arrow in that row): the waterfall gives up 49 pt at 440 x 956 (its buttons at y 726 against 775); on its side the page has one row for everything, so Wide is as Right there. With a phone's bars (41 / 44) at 344 x 764 and 115 % text the drawer's PTT lands on the page's in all three (y 651), Wide's Close on the page's Controls (y 602). On its side with the step row hidden by B/S, and at 804 x 360: no text cut, nothing overlapping. 192. **Distribution:** The word "aethersdr" out of what others see (*the log's channels and the demo radio's name in build 216 / Android beta 121*) **In short:** The word aethersdr out of what others see. Released in build 216 / Android beta 121: the log's channel names (oksa.… at the start of nearly every line) and the demo radio as OksaSDR Demo. The station name is OksaSDR already. **From you:** nothing before the App Store release. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-24: "haluan tuon aethersdr sanan pois bundle ID fi.oksa.aethersdr", then: at the App Store release, "silloin vois muuttaa koska emme halua aethersdr näkyvän"). Checked: Apple does not let a bundle ID change once a build is uploaded (App Store Connect Help, App information: "You can't change this property after you upload a build"; 212 builds are), so it means a new app record with a new bundle ID (Android's is fi.oh6oksa.oksasdr already): a new Apple ID of the app, the 96 external and 6 internal TestFlight testers invited again, settings carried by App setup export and ADIF only, the name OksaSDR free only once the old record is renamed, the capabilities (In-App Purchase, Multicast once granted) on the new App ID, a new Beta App Review; cheapest before the App Store release, which is the user's plan. The same word elsewhere: the app tells the radio `client program AetherSDR` (the shared core, src/models/RadioModel.cpp), and the demo radio is named "AetherSDR Demo" on the Connect page and in the header. *The user, 2026-11-25 evening:* the word still shows in many places in the log ("aika monessa paikkaa esiintyy vielä aethersdr"). Counted in that evening's log from the phone (beta 120, 2827 lines): 2546 lines begin with a log channel's name aether.… (aether.mobile, aether.connection ...; 71 such names in the code, the shared core's among them), which only the log shows; the word itself is on 36 lines: 27 name this station ("stations on it: AetherSDR": the station name's default, which the radio and its other stations see; Radio Setup › Radio › *This station's name* changes it), 9 are "client program AetherSDR". Three separate steps, none decided: (a) the log shows the channels as oksa.… (the log's own lines only, the shared code untouched); (b) the station name's default becomes OksaSDR (for a station that never set one: also for those who have the old default?); (c) the program name told to the radio: the shared core also knows its own older session by that name (RadioModel, the stale-session check), so the two change together, and a session of an older build is then not recognised as its own. *Decided by the user, 2026-11-25 21:17:* (a) is changed now; the program name (c) "pitää olla tietenkin OksaSDR", but "ehdottomasti Apple-muutoksen yhteydessä", with the new app record. *(b) needed nothing, and what was said of it above was wrong:* the station name's default has been OksaSDR all along on a phone and a tablet (main.cpp names the station after the app where the device gives no name of its own; the user's phone and iPad sent "client station OksaSDR" that day, and names of their own, OksaSDR-OnePlus-Open and OksaSDR-iPad). The 27 lines counted as the station's name were the log's own line "stations on it:", which listed the programs, not the stations: the program name once for each session of this app. *(a) as built (not in a build yet)*: the log writes the channels as oksa.… (oksa.mobile, oksa.protocol ...) on the Log page, in the run's file and in a log sent to the developer; the categories keep their names in the code, the shared core's too, and in a desktop terminal. The line "stations on it:" lists the stations by their names, as it says, and names another program on the radio after them (SmartSDR, a Maestro). *Tested on the FLEX over SmartLink (a connect and a disconnect)*: 129 of the run file's 138 lines begin with a channel oksa.…, none with aether.…; "stations on it: omarchy" while connected (the test machine's station), empty before and after. What is left for (c): the line of the command itself, "client program AetherSDR", and the connection check's "On the radio now: AetherSDR (station)". *The demo radio, 2026-11-25, chosen by the user for the next build:* named OksaSDR Demo on the Connect page and in the header (SimBackend::demoModelName in the shared core, one line; the mobile manual with it). Seen in a demo run: the header reads OksaSDR Demo. 193. **FTx:** The way to a full list of FT8 decodes on a phone is hard to find **In short:** The way to a full list of FT8 decodes on a phone is hard to find (Michael Walker VA3MW asked how to get a full screen of decodes). It exists as Scroll › List only, and build 214 gave that list its room; but the Scroll row still sits among the settings it folds away. **From you:** one choice, from pictures I make first: List only as the phone's default once FTx runs, or the list keeping its share when the settings are open. **Details:** (Michael Walker VA3MW, mail 2026-11-24 with a screenshot of his iPhone: the Controls drawer at full screen, the FTx tab with its settings open and one decode row in view: "is there a way to get a full screen of the FT8 decodes ... I must be missing a button to push"). It exists: Scroll › *List only* folds the settings away and the list takes the rest of the drawer, *More* beside Halt TX brings them back; answered so the same day. But the Scroll row sits among the settings it folds, and with the settings open the list keeps four rows of which one shows above Close. To think about with the user: List only as the phone's default once FTx runs, or the list's share kept when the settings open. Size: small.
194. **Fixes:** A memory recall's repeater keys left out for a memory that is not FM **In short:** A memory that is not FM is recalled with its repeater keys too, which the radio answers with a harmless not-in-FM code. Analysed on the FLEX: nothing is lost and nothing shows to the operator. Leaving the keys out would only make the log cleaner. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (the radio's 50000061; for a cleaner log only) (found 2026-11-24 in 153's tests): after `memory apply` and the retune the shared recall policy sends the memory's own `repeater_offset_dir`, `fm_repeater_offset_freq`, `tx_offset_freq`, `fm_tone_mode` and `fm_tone_value` in one `slice set`, and for an HF LSB memory the radio answered 50000061. *Analysed on the FLEX-6600M the same day* (each key alone, by mobile/tools/flexcmd.py and the render tool's `radiocmd`): outside FM the radio answers 50000061 to `repeater_offset_dir`, `fm_tone_mode` and `fm_tone_value` and sets them all the same (its status showed 88.5, CTCSS_TX and DOWN, each put back); the two offset frequencies answer 0. In FM and NFM every key and the whole command answer 0. So the code says "not in an FM mode", nothing is lost, an FM repeater memory gets its shift and tone, and nothing is shown to the operator. No change needed; the FM keys could be left out for a memory that is not FM, for a cleaner log only.
195. **FTx:** FT8 / FT4 signal reports measured against the noise inside the receive filter (*in build 215 / Android beta 120*) **In short:** With a receive filter narrower than 3 kHz the app gave stations FT8 / FT4 reports 17 to 26 dB too high, also in the reports it sent. The decoder now measures its noise inside the filter. Tested on recorded and live 40 m signals. Released in build 215 / Android beta 120. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (found 2026-11-25 in 173's tests on the FLEX: with the filter at 750..2250 the same three stations read +24, +27 and +27 dB where they read -7, -5 and -7 with 0..3000). The reports are measured against the noise of the whole audio band, and a filter narrower than that band cuts the noise away outside its passband, so every report comes out too high, the ones sent to the other station too. 173 opens a narrow filter when decoding starts; one narrowed while decoding runs still does this. Ideas: measure the noise only between the slice's filter edges (the app knows them), or say on the FTx tab that the filter is narrower than the decode band. To pre-analyse: how the decoder takes its noise floor (mobile ft8 engine) and how WSJT-X does it and behaves behind a narrow filter (its source read first). *Done 2026-11-25*: the decoder took its noise floor as the 30th percentile of every bin from 200 to 3000 Hz and of every block of the slot. A filter narrower than that band empties part of the bins (750 to 2250 Hz nearly half), and the percentile fell among them, some 25 dB under the noise the signals stand in. A slot the decoder joined late has its first part empty, with the same effect on a smaller scale. Now the floor is taken inside the slice's receive filter (each decoding slice hands its filter's edges to its decoder, mirrored in DIGL, 50 Hz inside each edge) and only over the blocks that had audio. *Measured on real 40 m audio recorded from the FLEX (the decoder's own slot dumps, decoded offline with --ft8-wav, which takes pass=LOW-HIGH: now)*: fully heard slots through 0..3000 give exactly the released code's reports (13 of 13 and 18 of 18 lines the same); through 750..2250 the released code read the same stations 17 to 26 dB higher than 30 s before with the open filter (-7 to +18, -4 to +21, -7 to +19), the new code within -1 to +4 dB of them (-7 to -5, -4 to -1, -7 to -3); stations outside the filter read as weak as it leaves them; a slot with its first quarter emptied read 6 to 8 dB too high with the released code and 1 to 3 dB under the whole slot with the new one (a quarter of each signal is gone). *Live on 40 m with the app's filter chooser*: two slots open, three narrowed to 750..2250: the same stations within about 3 dB on average across the change (4 to 9 stations a pair). Not done: the candidate search still looks at the whole band; WSJT-X's own way was not read (the measurements above were the test).

197. **Fixes:** Closing the app over SmartLink starts a reconnect in its last moment **In short:** When the app is closed during a SmartLink session it begins a reconnect in its last moment (build 214 does too). No harm has been seen. **From you:** nothing; you set it aside for later on 2026-11-25. **Details:** (found 2026-11-25 in 189's tests; build 214 does the same). The log of a session over SmartLink ends "disconnect: the app closing (asked) over SmartLink" and then "connection state connecting": the disconnect made when the app goes away is not marked as asked for, so the dropped-session retry begins while everything is being taken down. No harm seen (the process ends a moment later). To check: that nothing is sent to the SmartLink server at that point, and that an app ended by the system in the background does not leave a half-made session; then mark the closing disconnect as the operator's. Size: small. Not chosen for a build.
198. **Desktop:** Linux: a crash at exit inside PipeWire while Qt Multimedia closes the audio output **In short:** The Linux version crashed once while quitting (once in 163 test runs), inside the system's audio library, after everything had been saved. **From you:** nothing; you set it aside for later on 2026-11-25. **Details:** (seen once in 163 render-tool runs on the dev VM, 2026-11-25, the run that had just disconnected from SmartLink). SIGSEGV in libpipewire, called from Qt Multimedia's teardown out of the application object's destructor in main(), right after Qt's own warning "QObject: shared QObject was deleted directly. The program is malformed and may crash." The session had ended and the settings were saved; nothing was lost. To pre-analyse: which object is deleted directly (the audio sink the engine owns against Qt Multimedia's shared device objects), the order in which the engine lets go of its audio before the application object goes, and whether L37's users see a crash report when they quit. Not chosen for a build.
199. **Fixes:** A voice macro keeps the radio keyed when the device's microphone delivers nothing (*in build 215 / Android beta 120*) **In short:** The app's own voice macro rides on the device's microphone audio: when the microphone gave none, the macro never ended and the radio stayed keyed. Now such a macro is ended after 3 seconds, PTT is let go and a notice says so. Released in build 215 / Android beta 120; the user confirmed on a device that a voice macro goes out as before. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (found 2026-11-25 in 185's test on the dev VM, which has no microphone). The app's own recording is played by putting its samples in the microphone's place as the capture delivers its blocks (AudioEngine::onTxAudioReady); the macro keys the radio first and unkeys when the clip has run out. With no blocks coming the clip neither plays nor ends: the radio stayed keyed about ten seconds with no audio, until something else let PTT go (the Controls drawer opening). On a device the same would follow a capture that stops under a macro or never starts: the microphone taken by a call or another app, an audio interruption, a refused permission. With the macro on repeat nothing ends it. To pre-analyse: a guard (no progress in the clip for about a second ends the macro with a notice and lets PTT go), or the clip on its own clock as the FT8 / RTTY audio is (playTxDigital), and what the radio's own TX timeout does meanwhile. Size: small. *Done 2026-11-25 as a guard in the engine (dvkPlay)*: no transmit audio three seconds after keying, or no end three seconds past the clip's own length, ends the macro and its repeat, lets PTT go and shows "The voice macro was not sent: this device's microphone gave no audio, so PTT was let go." *Tested on the FLEX in USB at 5 W on 14.250*: with the dev VM's new virtual microphone a 3 s and a 7 s clip went out whole (peak 4.0 and 4.1 W, 297 and 700 packets, "voice macro done"), the guard silent; with the microphone source taken away a 3 s and a 10 s clip were each ended at 3 s ("ended by its guard: no transmit audio 3 s after keying", PTT released, 0.0 W), the notice on the display. Not tested: a capture that dies in the middle of a clip (the second check), a repeat cut by the guard, FreeDV with no microphone, a phone whose microphone a call takes. The clip on its own clock was not done (the guard covers the keyed radio; the macro itself still needs the microphone's audio path). *On a device (the user, 2026-11-25 evening):* "199 ok".

200. **Fixes:** The test modes that end right after their test abort at exit: the stall watch's thread still ran (*in build 215 / Android beta 120*) **In short:** The decoder's self-test and the other test modes of the development build ended with an abort instead of a clean exit, though the test itself had passed; the released build 214 did the same. Fixed: the stall watch is now stopped on every way out of the program. No user ever saw it; a test script can now trust the exit status. Released in build 215 / Android beta 120, by the user's word of 2026-11-25. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (found 2026-11-25 preparing the next build: `--ft8-test selftest` printed its 42 lines, every message decoded as sent, then "terminate called without an active exception", exit 134; the same with the released 214's binary and each build of the night between). The core dump's stack: `exit()` running the static destructors into `std::terminate`, with one thread asleep in `sleep_for`: the stall watch's (mobile/src/StallWatch.cpp, a static `std::thread` started early in main and stopped in the ordinary shutdown), still joinable when a test mode returns from main straight after its test. An ordinary quit ends in order (the render runs exit 0). To do: stop the stall watch on every return from main (a guard object in main), then the self-test's exit status can be checked in the scripts. Other than 198, which is inside the system's audio library and rare. Size: small. *Chosen by the user 2026-11-25* ("Seuraavaan buildiin tuo 200 korjaus jos ei tähän ehdi") and *done the same morning*: a guard object right after `StallWatch::start()` in main stops the watch on every return (stopping twice is harmless: the ordinary quit has stopped it already). Tested without a radio, each against the build before it: `--ft8-test selftest` exit 0 where it was 134, the same 42 lines, types 12 of 12; `--ft8-wav` on a recorded slot exit 0 where it was 134, the same 11 decodes; `--voice-selftest` 47 of 47, exit 0 as before (it ends before the watch starts); an ordinary start and quit on the Connect page exit 0 with the shutdown lines in order. Not tested: a device (no test mode runs there, and the ordinary quit is unchanged).

201. **Audio:** Android, a Bluetooth headset: receive back sooner after PTT, and the call link's sound (*in Android beta 121*, not run on a device before it) **In short:** On Android a Bluetooth headset used as a hands-free set (sound and microphone through it, no media audio set on in the phone) had three faults: about a second of silence after each PTT, receive lost whenever the microphone closed (a tab closed, the app to the background), and after the background no receive at all until the route was changed by hand (Mattias SM6YEC; measured and reproduced on your OnePlus Open with a Jabra). Released in Android beta 121 on your word, without a run on a device: in front the microphone is held open so the headset's link stays up and receive is back at once; a link that drops is taken again by the app itself, five times at most; back in front the route of a headset without media audio is renewed once. The held microphone streams to the radio all the time (see 208). **From you:** the try on your phone with beta 121, media audio off: PTT with Controls closed, quick taps, the background and back, the headset off and on, Bluetooth media and back to Auto. I read the developer link's log beside it; say when you start. **Details:** (Mattias SM6YEC, mail 2026-11-25 14:23 with his log: beta 120 on a Samsung Galaxy S24+ with Android 16, Galaxy Buds2, a FLEX-8400 over SmartLink, VOX off, his usual profile: "Receive audio works. After transmitting for a few seconds and releasing PTT, it takes about two seconds for the audio to come back", and the receive audio "sounds more compressed and a bit digital. With wired headphones connected via USB-C, it sounds normal"; this closes the open end of **144**: with his usual profile loaded receive comes back). From his log: the output Auto took the headset's hands-free link with media audio on; a 3.3 s transmission at 62 W: receive opened again 1.21 s after the release, the link was reported up 15 ms later and receive opened once more, some 1.5 s in all; a press of 0.3 s: the microphone kept open 0.7 s more (144's rule for short presses), receive opened again 1.7 s after the release and once more 0.55 s later with the link, some 2.5 s in all. In the code (MobileEngine, the release of PTT): the microphone's capture is stopped, 300 ms later the hands-free route is taken again and receive reopened, and the link watch reopens it once more when Android reports the link up after that. To analyse: where the 0.9 s between those 300 ms and the reopening goes (stopping the capture on the call link, the route call), whether receive can wait for the link and open once, whether the 300 ms is needed, and whether the link can be held through a transmission so that nothing reopens at all; and the sound: whether *Auto* could play receive over the music link and take the call link only while transmitting (the switch costs time at each PTT and may clip the first syllable), against the choice that exists (*Bluetooth media*: the music link with the phone's microphone). Tests need a real headset on a phone: the user's OnePlus, and Mattias with a preview. Size: medium. Not chosen for a build. *Mattias SM6YEC, mail 2026-11-25 21:10 with a screenshot (beta 120, a Samsung SM-S926B, Android 16):* with the Bluetooth headset receive did not come back after PTT that afternoon; with Controls open (his screenshot: the TX tab) "everything worked perfectly", and "as soon as I closed the settings panel, the receive audio disappeared again", back again when reopened; sometimes only closing the app, waiting and starting again brings receive back. Audio output › *Bluetooth media* gave no receive at all. Wired USB-C earphones worked very well (several DX contacts from his office). *Read from the code, not yet tried on a device*: the TX tab holds the device's microphone open while it is shown, for its mic level (TxControls micCheckShown → Engine.holdMicCheck; the eSSB tab does the same), and an open microphone is what keeps a headset's call link up on Android: so on his phone receive is heard over the headset only while the microphone is open, and the link does not come back by itself once it is closed, after PTT as after closing the tab. That points at the mend for this item and for the thread left open in 144: with Audio output at Auto and a Bluetooth headset in use, hold the call link (the microphone open, its audio not sent) for the whole connection, not only around PTT; it should take the silence after PTT with it. To check on a device first (the user's phone with a headset shows the delay too), then with Mattias. Bluetooth media with no receive is a separate thread: he was asked for the headset's make and model and a log right after trying it. Answered 21:16 (the cause as read, Controls left open on the TX tab or the wired earphones until a beta has the mend). *The user, 2026-11-25 21:30:* on their own devices a Jabra Evolve 65 and a Jabra Evolve2 65 work ("Minulla Jabra Evolve 65 ja evolve 2 65 toimivat"). So receive lost over the headset is not general: it is Mattias's phone (a Samsung SM-S926B) or his headset, whose make and model he was asked for; a mend that holds the call link has to be tried with these two Jabras first, so that what works is not broken. *The user, 2026-11-25 21:35:* the delay is there with their Jabras too, and too long: "kaveri ehtii aloittaa puhumaan monesti, niin jää osa kuulematta"; they recall it was made longer against the crackle when the link was restarted too quickly at each press. In the code (MobileEngine::releasePtt): the microphone opened on the headset's hands-free link ends the receive stream; at the release the microphone stays open until a second from the press (against the chirp), receive is opened again 300 ms after it closes and once more when Android has the link up: 1.5 to 2.5 s of silence. Mattias has a Jabra Evolve2 65 as well (144), so the difference between his loss of receive and the user's delay is the phone. The way out of both: no switching at PTT at all, the link held for the whole connection (the microphone open and not sent) and the reopening at the release left out. A check without a build, asked of the user: with the TX tab open (which holds the microphone open already), does receive come back at once and clean after the drawer's PTT? *Mattias, mail 2026-11-25 21:38 with a log (beta 120, a FLEX-8400 over SmartLink):* the headphones are Bowers & Wilkins Px7 S2e (the log of 13:14 the same day names Galaxy Buds2 as well); *Bluetooth media* still gives no receive. In the log: 20:35:23 Auto took the Px7 as a hands-free headset ("hands-free link down, media audio on"), the link came up 0.5 s after receive opened and receive was opened again on it; 20:35:37 he chose Bluetooth media: "audio output Bluetooth -> Android's media route", the microphone to the built-in one, "RX stream started at 24000 Hz device: Bluetooth" 0.3 s after the change, and nothing more until he sent the log 11 s later: no error, no sound. My reading, not proven: the stream opened while Android still had the call link of the Auto route up, and stayed on a link that then went down (a receive stream stays where it opened; the app opens it again when a link comes UP, not when one goes down). Asked of him 21:42: Bluetooth media left chosen, disconnect and connect again, so that receive opens on the music link from the start. If that plays, the mend is to open receive once more after leaving the hands-free route. *Measured on the user's OnePlus Open with a Jabra Evolve2 65, beta 120, Audio output Auto, from the developer link's log, 2026-11-25 21:49-21:51:* with the microphone closed after PTT (two presses of 1.1 and 1.5 s): receive opened again 0.45 s after the release with the call link down, the link came up and receive was opened once more at 0.95 s, the stream running at 1.1 s. With the microphone held open ("mic check: microphone open for the Mic meter" from 21:49:59; five presses of 1.6 to 3.8 s): the link stayed up, one opening at 0.34 to 0.40 s, the stream running at 0.44 to 0.51 s. A tap of 0.1 s: the microphone kept open until a second from the press, receive opened again 1.26 to 1.29 s after the release, the stream at 1.41 s; a row of quick taps works (each new press takes the pending reopening with it). So on this phone the held microphone already halves the silence, and what is left is the app's own waiting (the second from the press, 300 ms, the reopening), none of which is needed while the link is held. *The user, 2026-11-25 21:55, the same phone and headset (Auto; the headset's media audio off in Android's Bluetooth settings):* everything worked and nothing crackled, but with the app in the background the headset went silent ("kun kävin taustalla ... kuulokeääni hävisi"). In the log: 21:53:07.484 "app state: Qt::ApplicationInactive" and in the same millisecond "mic check: microphone closed" (the Mic meter's check holds the microphone only while the app is in front), then nothing opens receive again; back in front at 21:53:26 the microphone opened again. So it is the same thing as Mattias's closed TX tab: whenever the microphone closes, the headset's call link drops for a moment, and only a PTT release takes the route again and opens receive (releasePtt); a microphone closed for any other reason (the mic check ending: the tab closed, the meter changed, the app to the background) leaves receive on a dead link. The service that keeps the app alive in the background is of the type mediaPlayback only (AndroidManifest), so Android does not let the microphone stay open there. *The mend, three parts, none built:* (1) in front, with a headset as the Auto route, the microphone stays open for the whole connection and is sent only at PTT: no switching, no waiting, the reopening at the release left out; (2) after ANY closing of the microphone while a headset is the route, the route is taken again and receive opened again, as after PTT (the app to the background, where the microphone cannot stay open); (3) to decide: the service also of the type microphone, so that the link is held in the background too (and a PTT key works there with the device's microphone), which Google Play asks to be declared. Also seen in that log: "CwSidetoneQAudioSink: no supported float/int16-stereo rate on device Bluetooth" (the CW sidetone has no stream on the headset's call link). *The user, 21:58, corrected the above:* after the visit to the background receive never came back, PTT or not ("ääni ei kuulunut enää missään vaiheessa ... PTT:n painelu ei vaikuta mitään"), while the microphone and the transmission worked. In the log: from the first press after the return (21:53:36.094) every opening of the receive stream on the headset fails inside Android, "qt.multimedia.android.aaudiostream Opening stream failed: AAUDIO_ERROR_INTERNAL" and "qt.multimedia.android.audiosink Stream null", 35 times by 21:58 (13 in 21:53, 22 at the three presses of 21:57-21:58), once also our own "AudioEngine: failed to open RX audio sink on any negotiated format" (21:53:40.001); the app all the same logs "RX stream started at 24000 Hz device: Bluetooth" each time and goes on as if receive played. Before the background visit the same reopening had worked some fifteen times. So there are two faults of the app here whatever Android's reason is: it does not see that the stream did not open (Qt's sink has no stream), and it has no way back but a restart of the app, which is what Mattias described ("close the app completely, wait a while and restart"). To the mend, part (4): after an opening, check that the sink really has a stream; if not, take the route down and up again and open once more, and say so on the page if receive still cannot play. Asked of the user: whether Audio output › Speaker and back to Auto brings receive back without a restart. *The user, 22:03:* Audio output › Speaker and back to Auto (22:01:44 and 22:01:47) brought receive back to the headset without a restart, "ja mielestäni näin on toiminut aina"; in the log the first five openings after the return to Auto still failed (AAUDIO_ERROR_INTERNAL within 0.3 s, the call link down), then "RX stream started at 24000 Hz device: Bluetooth" at 22:01:48.014 with no failure after it (my reading from the missing link-up line, that it was still silent, was wrong: the user heard it). So the way back exists and is the route taken down and up with the opening tried again until it holds: part (4) does that by itself. The user also: with the headset's media audio ON in Android's Bluetooth settings it plays normally, in the background too, as they recall; to be confirmed by them. That fits 144's notes (hands-free only was the case that went silent) and means the faults here belong to a headset used over its call link alone. *The user, 22:07, the same phone and headset with the headset's media audio now ON in Android's settings:* silent in the headset from the connect on (Auto, 22:04:32), also after Speaker and back to Auto (22:04:56-57); receive began only when Audio output › Bluetooth media was chosen (22:05:03), and it stayed when Auto was chosen after that (22:05:24) ("Ääni lähti tulemaan vasta, kun vaihdoin Bluetooth-medialle ... alussa ... kuulokkeet olivat hiljaiset"). The log shows no error in any of the four: Auto each time "stream on Bluetooth", "call link up" 0.8 to 1.6 s after the route was taken, the stream opened again 0.7 to 1.3 s after that; the silent ones and the one that played look alike. So with media audio on the stream can be open and silent, which a check of the sink alone (part 4) would not see: where the sound goes is Android's routing (the music link and the call link of one headset, a media stream while the audio mode is "in communication"), not known from the log. *Not understood yet*; the expectation that media audio on is the trouble-free case does not hold on this phone today. Before any code: read how the Auto route and Qt's stream meet (the stream's usage, the order of opening and of the link), then a plan with the tries it needs on the phone in both settings. *The user's aim, 2026-11-25 22:10:* the headset is to work as a hands-free set, sound and microphone both through it, with no media audio set on in the phone's settings; chosen for the next build with the parts (1), (2) and (4). *As built, not in a build yet and not run on any device:* a keeper looks at the headset's route twice a second while it is the Auto route (MobileEngine::headsetKeeperTick). (1) In front the microphone is held open for the link, with the same open microphone a Mic meter has (it is not asked for from here, and not while VOX is on); a PTT release then closes nothing, and with the link up receive is let back at once, the second from the press and the reopening left out. (2) A link found down for 1.5 s, whatever closed the microphone, has its route taken again and receive opened on it, twice; then the route is let go and taken 0.7 s later, every 3 s, then every 10 s. (4) Back in front after the background the route is let go and taken again once (about 1.5 s of silence at each return: what Speaker and back to Auto did by hand on the user's phone); after six tries with the app in front a notice says that Android does not open the link and what to try. A headset switched off while it was the route has the route worked out anew. Bluetooth media chosen while a headset's call link was the route opens receive once more 1.5 s later (Mattias's silence). The receive sink's own retries are 181 b's. The link's state is the hands-free link's own (Android's broadcast), not Android's word for the communication device, which read "earpiece" in the log right after the route was taken. *Checked here*: the engine, the Android route code and the Java helper compile for arm64 Android with NDK 27.2 and Qt 6.12 with no new warning; on the desktop, where none of it runs, receive and PTT are as before on the FLEX. *To try on the user's phone with the preview (Auto, media audio off)*: the silence after PTT with Controls closed; a row of quick taps; the app to the background and back; the headset switched off and on while connected; Bluetooth media and back to Auto; and what the log says at each (the keeper's lines). *Made more careful before the release (the user's PuJu, 2026-11-25 23:00, with none of it run on a device):* the keeper mends only a link it has itself seen up (a headset or phone that gives no link state is left as before), five tries at most for one drop, then it leaves the route alone until the link's state changes or the app comes back to the front; the round after the background is only for a headset with its media audio off (the case seen); and after PTT with the link held receive is still opened again, as since beta 114, but at once instead of 0.3 s and more later. So what the build does with a headset is what was seen to work on the user's phone that evening with the Mic meter holding the microphone open, plus the mending of a drop. *First run on a device (the user's OnePlus Open with the Jabra Evolve2 65, media audio off, Android beta 121, 2026-11-26 08:02-08:15, read from the developer link's log):* the microphone was taken into the hold 3.4 s after the connect; after each of 25 PTT presses receive ran again 96 to 213 ms after the release (median 118 ms; 1.1 s in beta 120); in the background the microphone closed as meant, the link dropped half a second later and the keeper had it back in 3.0 s, and once more in 4.5 s after a second drop there. Two faults: one drop of the link by itself was not mended (**214**), and the renewing of the route on coming back to the front ran with the link already up, a needless second and a half of silence (also 214). The user on the open microphone: it is how the other Flex programs work, so that the level can be set without transmitting; the TX tab's own asking for the microphone is merged with the hold (one microphone, opened once), which the user checked for. *iOS (the user's iPad, build 216, 2026-11-26):* there the microphone is open only while the TX or eSSB tab is shown (the log: "mic check: microphone open for the Mic meter" three times, closed between), as designed: the hold was made for Android's Bluetooth link alone. The user: "201 ios myös jatkuvasti mikki auki" (**216**). *On a second phone (Mattias SM6YEC, a Galaxy S24+, mail of 2026-11-26 10:03):* Auto in beta 121 for about an hour on an 80 m net, with several different Bluetooth headsets: "Auto worked reliably throughout the session". What he still met: 214 (see there), no listening with the screen off or another app in front (**218**), and Bluetooth media silent as before (211).

202. **Devices:** The amplifier's state on the main screen, and a tap from its meter to the Amp tab **In short:** A tester asked for a small amplifier monitor on the main screen: output power, SWR and the amplifier's state at a glance, without opening the Amp tab over the waterfall. Power and SWR are there already: each of the two meters offers the amplifier's readings, and with the style *2 bars* one meter shows both; he was told how. New would be the state (Operate, Standby, Off, Fault) on the main screen and a tap from an amplifier meter straight to the Amp tab. Not pre-analysed. **From you:** nothing now; it is a change on the main screen, so pictures come first when it is picked. **Details:** (Sergio Landoni LU7YS, mail 2026-11-25 16:16, build 215 on his iPhone with a KPA500 and a KAT500: "whenever I want to check the amplifier's actual output power and SWR, I need to open Controls, select the Amp tab and sometimes scroll through the panel. This also covers a significant part of the spectrum and waterfall", "an optional Mini Amp Monitor directly on the main operating screen", three readings: output power, SWR and status, "a selectable meter in the existing meter area, a small horizontal bar above the spectrum, or even a compact numerical display", "tapping the indicator could also open the full Amp panel", for every amplifier the app serves; the screenshots he mentions did not come with the mail and were asked for again). What exists (MeterView's chooser, since the amplifiers were added): with an amplifier set up every meter offers, for receive and for transmit, *Power*, *Refl*, *SWR*, *Temp*, *Volts* and *Current* under *Amplifier* (a KPA500 on its own scales, Power 0-600 W), and a KAT500 adds *SWR* under *Tuner*; the two meters with *2 bars* give four readings on the air. What does not exist: the amplifier's state anywhere on the main screen (it is on the Amp tab: OPERATE / STANDBY / POWER OFF and a fault in red), a fault told on the main screen, and a way from a meter to the Amp tab (a tap on a meter opens its chooser). To analyse, with pictures for the user first: where a state fits without taking room from the waterfall (a short tag in an amplifier meter's name line such as OPR / STBY / OFF / FAULT in the state's colour; or a tag in the slice row beside TX), what a fault does on the main screen (a notice, as other faults), a button in the meter chooser to the Amp tab rather than changing what a tap does, and the same for every amplifier (RF2K-S, KPA1500, KPA500, Power Genius XL) and both page layouts. Size: small to medium. Not chosen for a build.

203. **Fixes:** Android: a foldable's outer screen turned on its side: the app stood still and the system ended it (*the stall watch's stacks in Android beta 121, from 8 s in beta 122*; the cause open) **In short:** On the user's OnePlus Open with Android beta 120, turning the closed phone from upright to its side while connected stopped the app for good, twice within two minutes: the picture broke up, nothing answered, and Android ended the app. After that the same turn worked more than a dozen times in a row, on the home network and over SmartLink, so it does not happen every time. The render tool has not shown it even with the phone's own numbers. The phone's log said that the app's main thread stopped, not where; from Android beta 121 the stall watch says where once the app has stood still for 16 seconds (both stops lasted longer), so the next time it happens the log has the place. The cause is not known yet. **From you:** a word on what was open on the screen at the two stops (the main page only, the Controls drawer, Setup?), if you remember. Then nothing special: use the phone as always with the developer link on; if it stops again, start the app again and I read the place from the log. And a yes for one test when the phone is free: over the developer link I hold the app still for 17 seconds to see that the place really comes into the log on your phone (Android may offer to close the app meanwhile). **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-25 19:50, right after build 215 / Android beta 120: "Kun puhelimen etukannel kääntää pystystä vaakaan, niin se sekoaa ... ja kaatuu"). *From the phone's log over the developer link*: the app's main thread stops at the window's Expose event and does not answer again ("GUI thread stalled 16033 ms ... last window event Expose" at 19:48:41; from 19:50:04 on for four minutes, 256 s counted, right after "onSurfaceChanged(): valid Surface received"), and the system ends the app ("previous run ended ... o-kill(40)" at 19:48:56 and 19:58:06). Both times the page was the phone's (343x764, bars 39 and 44, text 115 %), the connection over SmartLink, the slice on 7.373 MHz LSB, the page built a few seconds before (a fresh connect at 19:48:22; the phone closed at 19:50:00). The turn that works: the window 764x343, bars top 39 and none at the bottom, the page 681x304, as the outer screen on its side has insets at both ends (a camera cutout and the navigation bar): eight times by the radio's address at 20:07-20:09 and five times over SmartLink at 20:11-20:14, with the same page (meters as two bars each, the band and step rows hidden, the display in 2D). Betas 117, 118 and the preview of 119 turned the outer screen over SmartLink too (the panadapter asked at 671 points wide, the last on 2026-11-24 20:37), so nothing says yet that build 215 brought it. *Not reproduced in the render tool* (2026-11-25, on the FLEX, upright to its side and back twice each): by its address with PTT at Right, Left and Wide and the 3D view on; with the page at 681x304; then, with the side insets the tool had never had (below), by its address and over SmartLink, with the phone's own page (two-bar meters, B/S hidden) on 7.372801 LSB, and with the Controls drawer open. The longest stop was 2.2 s, at the turn's Expose with the drawer open; the render tool runs Qt 6.11 on a desktop, the phone Qt 6.12 on Android, where the surface comes and goes on a third thread. *Built for it*: (1) the render tool's safearea verb takes the side insets (safearea TOP BOTTOM LEFT RIGHT, the OnePlus Open's outer screen on its side: 39 0 39 44; drawn as dim strips), which no picture or check had before: a phone on its side was always rendered with nothing at its ends. (2) The stall watch logs where the threads are on Android as it did on Linux only: the main thread, the render thread and Android's own UI thread from their stacks, and the Java side of them, when the main thread has not answered for 4 s and once more at 16 s (the same place twice is a thread that waits; another place one that still works). The lines also go into the note the next start reads ("previous run: stall stack GUI #0 ..."), since a stopped main thread sends no log. A run that ends in order now leaves no note (a slow second right before a quit was reported at the next start as a stall). (3) A plan line stall MS holds the main thread on purpose, also over the developer link, so that the stacks can be seen to come on a device (stall 4500). *Tested on the desktop with the demo radio*: a 3 s hold is named in the log ("QThread::sleep ... ShotPlan::run" under "GUI thread stalled 1000 ms"); a run killed during an 8 s hold leaves the note and the next start reads it back as 32 lines and clears it; a quick run with a slow start leaves none. The Android side compiles with the release build's NDK 27.2 and Qt 6.12 with no warning, and the Java part runs on a desktop JVM; *not run on a device yet*: that is what the preview is for (stall 4500 over the developer link, then the log). *The user later the same evening:* it came while trying PTT's places (191): "191 oli muuten ok mutta vaakakäännössä kaatui"; started with the phone already on its side it did not come, and after that not at all, with Wide chosen and over SmartLink too ("Jumitumista ei tule enää edes Smart-linkillä. En tiedä mistä se johtuu"). In the render tool the page built upright with Wide (the phone's own page, the side insets) turned without a stop as well. *Before the release (the user's PuJu, 2026-11-25 23:00, with none of it run on a device):* on Android the stacks are taken only when the main thread has stood 16 s, and once more at the next report, so that a slow start or connect is never walked (a tester's handset stands 4 s at every connect, and a walk gone wrong there would end an app that was only slow); nothing of the walk runs on a device before such a stall, not even the unwinder's first set-up. The test line takes up to 20 s for it (stall 17000). *The test line on a device, 2026-11-26 14:45:27 (the user's yes: "Voit tehdä tämän testin nyt"; the first preview of beta 122, OnePlus Open):* `stall 17000` over the developer link. Reports at 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 s; at 16.06 s the stacks, 56 lines: the GUI thread with names (nanosleep, QThread::sleep, ShotPlan::run, ShotPlan::step, the event loop, main), the render thread (waiting in Qt Quick), Android's UI thread (idle in its looper) and the Java threads. "GUI thread answers again after 17243 ms". Android did not kill the app (the screen was not touched). A side effect worth knowing: the radio closed the connection as the app did not answer it for 17 s ("The remote host closed the connection (not asked)"), and the app connected again by itself 5 s later with the headset and the slice as before. So a real freeze of 16 s or more now leaves its place in the log; a shorter one, or one the phone's own killer ends first, still does not. *In the third preview of Android beta 122:* the stacks are taken at 8 s already and again at 16 s (they were held to 16 s while the walk was unproven on a device); 221's two stops of 15.5 and 15.8 s the same day ended just short of 16 s and left no place.

204. **Layout:** PTT › Wide with the phone on its side: PTT in the middle of the row (*in build 216 / Android beta 121*) **In short:** With Setup › App › PTT at *Wide*, a phone on its side showed PTT at the right as with *Right*: build 215 had made Wide an upright thing only, so the choice did nothing there and read as broken. Now PTT stands in the middle of the page's one row with the other buttons at its two sides, as the user drew it: Left, Right and Wide in the middle mean the same upright and on its side. Tested on the FLEX; released in build 216 / Android beta 121. **From you:** a look on the phone: Wide with the phone on its side, B/S hidden and shown. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-25 evening, build 215 / Android beta 120 on the OnePlus Open: "Vaidi valittu. Pystyasennossa se toimii, mutta vaakassa ei"; their drawing in the row's free room between B/S and + Slice, "tuossa olisi ollut paikka siinä keskellä vaan laittaa se ja siirtää ne muut painikkeet vasemmalle ja oikealle, niin kuin kaikissa muissa malleissa"; the two states shown to them, "204 noin ok"). How it was: PhoneOperatePage's pttWide held only while the page was not on its side (roadmap 191 (B): "so Wide is as Right there"), agreed from upright pictures only; three alternative models drawn for it first were not wanted: the pattern had the answer. *As built*: pttWideSide (Wide, on its side, no split paddles). With the band and step rows hidden (B/S) PTT takes the place of the empty room between − + Band B/S and + Slice / + Pan, Macros and Controls after them; with the step buttons shown it stands between the steps down and the steps up (the step buttons are two halves of one list now) and has a third of the row at least, the steps keeping to their own width. The row's own PTT at the right is hidden then; the Controls drawer's row is as it was. *Tested on the FLEX at the phone's numbers* (764x343 with the insets 39 0 39 44, the page 681x304, text 115 %, two-bar meters; the page built upright with Wide, then turned): PTT 280 pt wide at x 228 with B/S hidden (150 at the right before), 201 pt at x 218 with the steps shown (the steps 40 pt each, 47 before); a hold on it keyed and let go in both ("PTT pressed 22 pt down its 44 pt", "radio transmitting: yes (this app)", "PTT released: button released"; 14.250 USB at 5 W, no audio, the radio put back to 14.074 DIGU and 100 W); with the drawer open the page's PTT is hidden and the drawer's own keys; turned back upright PTT is across the page again (335 pt). Right and Left on its side are where they were (150 pt at x 565 and at x 44), and with Right the row is the same to the pixel as before the change. *Owed at the build*: the manual's picture of PTT's places gets the row on its side; the classic check over every size; the notes' line. *Confirmed on the device (the user, 2026-11-26, the phone turned on its side at 14:32 with PTT Wide): "204 ok".*

205. **Audio:** Android in a car: the car's hands-free kit is taken for a headset and nothing is heard; a SmartLink session through a phone's hotspot dropped **In short:** A tester wants receive audio through his truck's speakers (Android Auto). With Audio output at Auto the app takes the truck's Bluetooth, a hands-free device, for a headset and sends receive to its call link, which the truck does not open: no audio. In the same log a SmartLink session through his phone's hotspot was dropped after 12 seconds with no panadapter data at all, while a connection made on the network held. He was told to try Audio output › *Bluetooth media* and to take beta 120, and asked for the device, the way it reaches the radio, and one more log. Nothing is changed in the app yet. **From you:** nothing now; it waits for his answer. **Details:** (Rob Metallo VA3MET, mail 2026-11-25 21:18 "Re: PTT" with a log, beta 118, Android 15, text 115 %: "Trying to connect the audio to android auto in my truck. The demo works fine but when I connect to my flex it keeps on dropping the connection and no audio comes through. By the way it's tethered to my phone for internet"). *From his log*: "audio output Auto -> Bluetooth (hands-free, reported as Uconnect-…)", "headset: communication device earpiece, hands-free link down, media audio off, audio mode 3", the receive stream opened on "Bluetooth" at 48000 Hz, "QAudioSink stopped with error IOError (the route changed): restarting RX", opened again at 24000 Hz; the GUI thread stood 3.7 to 4.4 s right after each connect (4.2 s with the built-in speaker as well, that morning). The first session of the log was on the network (the display check: 121 frames) and was ended by him after 16 s; the second, over SmartLink (the server over IPv6, the radio's TLS session over IPv4), had "display check: WAN ... frames:0" and ended with "link lost: no answer from the radio to 5 pings" 12 s after the connect, then "remote session dropped, reconnecting". *Open questions*: whether a car kit with no media link should count as a headset at all in Auto (it has no microphone link the app can use either); what Android Auto needs (a media session?) for receive to play through the car; what holds the GUI thread 4 s at each connect on his device (the stall watch's stacks of 203 would say); whether the SmartLink drop was the hotspot's path (no UDP either way) or the stall at the wrong moment. Related: 201 (*Bluetooth media* gave Mattias no receive at all on beta 120), 144, 128. Answered 21:38 and archived.

206. **CW:** Android: no CW sidetone stream on a Bluetooth headset's call link **In short:** With a Bluetooth headset as the Auto route the app's own CW sidetone cannot open its sound on the headset: the log says the headset's call link offers no format the sidetone uses. Seen in the user's log while they tested the headset in a voice mode; not heard or tried in CW yet, so what an operator hears there (nothing, or the radio's own sidetone through receive) is not known. **From you:** nothing now; a word if you key CW with the headset and miss the sidetone. **Details:** (found 2026-11-25 in the developer link's log of the user's OnePlus Open with a Jabra Evolve2 65, beta 120: "CwSidetoneQAudioSink: no supported float/int16-stereo rate on device \"Bluetooth\"" at 21:53:39 and 21:58:00, each time right after receive was opened again on the headset). The call link carries mono speech at 8 or 16 kHz; the sidetone sink asks for stereo. To look at when picked: a mono format for the sidetone on such a device, or the sidetone mixed into the receive stream there. Related: 201.

207. **Fixes:** A warning in every log at each start: "QQuickItem::stackAfter: Cannot stack ..." (*in build 216 / Android beta 121*) **In short:** From build 213 to 215 every start of the app wrote one warning line into the log, on every device. It came from the render tool's stand-in for a device's bars, which was made on devices too although it never shows there. Nothing was wrong on the screen; the line was noise in a tester's log. Mended in build 216 / Android beta 121: the stand-in is made only when the render tool asks for bars. **From you:** nothing. **Details:** (found 2026-11-25 in the developer link's logs: android119 twice, android120 at every start, ios213 and ios215 once each: "default QQuickItem::stackAfter: Cannot stack QQuickRectangle_QML_… (… z=998) after QQuickRectangle_QML_…, which must be a sibling"). Main.qml's Repeater for the test bars (plan verb safearea) makes its rectangles in the window's overlay on every start; the model can be empty unless a test inset is set, so that a device makes none. *Done 2026-11-25, chosen for the next build by the user ("Otetaan kaikki nuo ehdottomasti 4 tähän buildiin mukaan"), not in a build yet:* the stand-in is one item behind a Loader, made only while a test inset is set. On the FLEX with no inset set the line is gone (0 against 3 in a run of the build before); with insets set the strips draw as before and no warning comes either. *Confirmed on devices, 2026-11-26:* no such line in the phone's log (beta 121) nor in the iPad's (build 216).

208. **Audio:** Android: hold a Bluetooth headset's call link without sending the microphone's audio to the radio **In short:** Since Android beta 121 the app holds a Bluetooth headset's microphone open while it is in front, because the headset's call link stays up only that way (201). The microphone's audio then goes to your radio all the time, as it does with VOX or with the TX tab's Mic meter shown: the radio transmits only at PTT, but over mobile data the stream is up to some 50 MB an hour, and the microphone is live towards the radio whenever the app is in front. The link needs the microphone open on the phone only; the stream to the radio could wait for PTT. Not built: it changes the transmit audio path, so it wants its own test on the air and on your phone. **From you:** nothing now; pick it by number if the data use or the open stream matters to you. **Details:** found on 2026-11-25 in the last reading before build 216's release. *What happens now:* the keeper (headsetKeeperTick) sets the hold, updateMicCheck opens the microphone exactly as for the Mic meter, and startDeviceMicrophoneCapture starts the transmit stream to the radio with it (AudioEngine::startTxStream; the radio takes Opus only on that stream: 70 kbit/s in 10 ms packets, with the packet headers about 115 kbit/s, some 50 MB an hour; the shared engine's own comment says its desktop program sends during receive too, for VOX and the radio's metering). *What it would take:* the capture opened without the stream for this one holder (or the stream's packets held back until PTT or VOX asks for them), and the stream started at the press: the microphone is open already, so the audio is there; what has to be measured is whether the first syllable is whole when the stream to the radio starts only at the press. The privacy text's microphone section, the manual's headset paragraph and the Android notes of beta 121 say what happens now; they change with this. *2026-11-26, with 218:* the link's drop at the closing of the microphone turned out to be the Qt library's own bookkeeping, not a rule of Android's. If 218's cure holds on a device, the link needs no open microphone at all, and this item is then a choice (the microphone open for its meter, as 216 asks, or closed to save the data) and no longer a need.

209. **Keyboard:** Space as PTT can be switched off (a Mac with VoiceOver) (*in build 221; Android and Linux from the next beta*) **In short:** With a hardware keyboard Space was PTT always, whatever was held with it: Control-Option-Space, VoiceOver's own "press this control", keyed the transmitter like a plain Space wherever the key reached the app (Justin Mann AI5OS, a Mac with VoiceOver, TestFlight feedback on build 215). Built, both parts: (1) Space pressed with Control and Option together or with Command keys nothing, with no setting; (2) Setup › Transmit has a new row, *Space is PTT* Off / On, On as before and the default. The iPad's own key path could not be run here, nor a Mac. **From you:** the try on your iPad with the Magic Keyboard: Space as PTT as before; Control-Option-Space and Command-Space key nothing; *Space is PTT* off and on. The Mac with VoiceOver is the tester's to try. **Details:** (TestFlight feedback, 2026-11-25 22:13 Finnish time, a Mac on macOS 27.2.1 with build 215: "Can you make the ptt button an assignable key so that the spacebar is not used on mac OS? I sometimes use voiceover to click a button, and ptt gets in the way.") *What is there now* (read in the code and the manual, 2026-11-25): the page's key handling (OperateKeys.qml) keys PTT on Space, held or as a switch with PTT *Tap on / off*; the learned PTT key (roadmap 121, build 200) takes any key a button or a keyboard sends and keys the same way, and the manual says of it "Space stays PTT too". On an iPad Space is read through GameController (the paddle sides); on a Mac the keys come as plain key events. *To weigh:* what a screen reader's own keys do reaches the app or not (VoiceOver's VO-Space should press the control under its cursor through the accessibility interface, with no key event to the app, while a plain Space is the app's to handle: to be seen on a Mac); the screen-reader work of roadmap 27 is the wider frame; the choice must work the same on an iPad, a Mac, Android and Linux, and the iPad's GameController path has to obey it too. Not tried on a Mac: there is none here, so the try is the tester's. *The user, 2026-11-26, on their iPad with a Magic Keyboard:* Space as PTT works as it should there; what Justin presses is not known. The reply went to him that morning (the user's "ls Justin"): the learned PTT key, that a switch for Space is on the list, the fixed keys, and three questions: what he presses to click a button (VoiceOver's VO-Space or plain Space with the focus on the button), which keyboard, and where VoiceOver gets in the way most. *His answer, 2026-11-26 10:07:* he does not say what he presses with VoiceOver; he sets his own keys in every program and names Option-Space or Shift-Space for PTT. The learned PTT key takes one key, the first one pressed (MobileEngine::pttKeyEvent keeps the key and its scan code, no modifiers), so a combination cannot be learned: with Option-Space it would learn Option. He was told so, and to pick a key he uses for nothing else until 210 is built. *Read in the code, 2026-11-26 (the user: "Avaa tuota 209 tarkemmin"):* two paths key PTT from Space and neither looks at the modifiers. On an iPad and an iPhone GameController reports the physical key (SideKeys.mm, GCKeyCodeSpacebar; MobileEngine::onSideKey keys when the page has the keyboard and the app is in front): it very likely sees Space whatever VoiceOver does with Control-Option-Space, so the same fault is to be expected with VoiceOver and a keyboard on an iPad. On a Mac (the iPad app on a Mac: sideKeysAvailable() is false there), Android and Linux the key comes as Qt's key event to OperateKeys.qml (case Qt.Key_Space), again with no look at event.modifiers; whether VoiceOver's key reaches the app there is not known, his report says PTT does get in the way. The learned PTT key keys wherever the keyboard is (an event filter on the whole app), Space only while the page has the keyboard. *What (1) cannot cover:* VoiceOver with Caps Lock as its key, or with its keys locked, sends a plain Space: for that the switch (2) is the sure one. *Where (2) is read:* OperateKeys.qml's press and MobileEngine::onSideKey; the row is an OnOff of the Setup page (Off / On buttons, as its other rows) under PttKeySetup in Setup › Transmit, whose heading "This device: PTT key (not in CW)" then needs another wording, as Space keys in CW too; the hint under the PTT key and the manual's key table say "Space stays PTT too" and "Space: PTT (hold)" today. (1) on the iPad's path reads the Control, Option and Command keys' own state in the same handler (Command, GCKeyCodeLeftGUI / RightGUI, is not listened to yet); a paddle key alone (CW paddles on the Control or the Option keys) must not stop Space, hence Control and Option together; only the press is held back, a transmission under way is never cut by a modifier. *Not proposed: Space off by itself while a screen reader runs:* a blind operator with a keyboard may well want Space as PTT, and a key that changes its meaning unseen is hard to explain; (1) covers the reader's own key instead. *With 210:* a key map of one's own would take this row in as Space's entry, and his Option-Space for PTT becomes a combination given by hand there. *Tests when built:* the Qt path by the render tool on Linux (Space alone, with each modifier, the row Off and On, Tap on / off, CW), the focus sweep (the page gets the keyboard back), the iPad's path by the user with the Magic Keyboard, the Mac's by the tester. *What was built (2026-11-26, the user's pick):* the setting MobileSpaceIsPtt (Engine.spaceIsPtt); OperateKeys.qml asks it and Engine.spaceHeldBack(event.modifiers) at Space's press, and a Space that was not PTT lets nothing go at its release (it would have let go a PTT held another way); MobileEngine::onSideKey asks the setting, and SideKeys.mm reports a Space pressed with those keys as a kind of its own (SideKey::SpaceHeldBack), reading the keys' own state at the press with GCKeyboardInput's buttonForKeyCode, so nothing is remembered that could go stale while the handlers are off. Qt hands Command on as Control and Control as Meta on Apple's systems, the other way round elsewhere: spaceHeldBackWith has both, and the Setup hint names the keys by the device (Ctrl and Alt together, or the Search / Windows key, on Android and a PC). The row has a heading of its own, "This device: the space bar", as the PTT key's says "not in CW". *Tested on Linux against the FLEX, every transmission refused (the plan's txblock):* Space, Shift-Space, Ctrl-Space and Alt-Space keyed; Ctrl-Alt-Space, Meta-Space and all three together did not; the engine's half of the iPad's path through a test line (sidekey) the same; the row Off: neither path keyed, On: both again; the truth table for both key mappings (spacemods); the page held the keyboard after Setup closed; then one real transmission by Space at 1 W in USB. No text cut in Setup › Transmit at 1194x834, 834x1194, 393x852, 852x393 and 343x764. *Not run:* SideKeys.mm's own lines (an iPad or iPhone with a keyboard), a Mac, VoiceOver.

210. **Keyboard:** Own keyboard shortcuts (a Mac, and any hardware keyboard) **In short:** The app's keys are fixed: arrows tune, + and − zoom, F the frequency, T tune, A the ATU, F1 to F12 the memories and macros, Esc stop, Space PTT. A tester asks whether hotkeys can be made for other functions on the Mac (Justin Mann AI5OS, the same feedback as 209). They cannot today; a MIDI controller can be mapped (Setup › MIDI), a keyboard cannot. An idea, not pre-analysed: a key map of one's own, perhaps the MIDI mapping's list of functions with a key learned for each. **From you:** nothing now; pick it by number. **Details:** (TestFlight feedback, 2026-11-25: "Is there a way to create hotkeys for various functions in the mac version of this app that I'm not aware of?") The keys are listed in the manual's section 11 (Keyboard and Apple Pencil) and handled in OperateKeys.qml and MobileEngine::operateKey; the functions a MIDI control can be bound to are the natural list for keys too, and the learned PTT key (121) shows the way a key is learned. For a VoiceOver user a key for each function is worth more than for others: it saves the walk through the controls. *His scheme (mail of 2026-11-26 10:07), for when this is built:* ⌘1 to ⌘0 for the bands from 160 to 6 m, Option-Space or Shift-Space for PTT, Control-Shift with a letter (U, L, A, F and so on) for the modes, "and other variants as I need them". ⌘1 to ⌘0 are taken today (they do what F1 to F10 do: the CW memories, the Digi tab's macros, the voice macros), so a key map of one's own has to let a fixed key be given to something else, and a key with a modifier has to be learnable. He has used the app on a Mac only so far, asked whether FT8 is in the app (told: Controls › FTx › a band button, a decode pressed answers the station; how VoiceOver reads that tab is not known), and offers accessibility work on VoiceOver in the code (27). He was told the source is public and to say first what he has in mind; whether changes from outside are taken in is the user's to say.

211. **Audio:** Android: Audio output › Bluetooth media gives no receive on a Galaxy S24+ with Bowers & Wilkins Px7 S2e **In short:** On Mattias SM6YEC's Samsung Galaxy S24+ with Px7 S2e headphones, Audio output › *Bluetooth media* plays no receive audio at all: not after choosing it while connected, and not from a fresh connect with it already chosen (his two tries of 2026-11-25). On your OnePlus Open with the Jabra the same choice plays. His log shows the receive stream starting on Android's media route with no error. The cause is not known; Android beta 121 opens receive once more a second and a half after the choice, which his second try says is not the cure. **From you:** nothing now; it waits for his log of a fresh connect and his word on whether it differs with Controls closed (asked 2026-11-26). **Details:** (mails of 2026-11-25 21:38, with the log of beta 120, and 23:36: "I tried disconnecting from the radio and reconnecting with Bluetooth media still selected.
