Unfortunately, there is still no receive audio.") *In the log:* 20:35:37 "audio output Bluetooth -> Android's media route (a Bluetooth device's media link when connected)", then "RX stream started at 24000 Hz" 0.3 s later, no failure line after it; the headset's state line earlier in the same connection says the headphones' media audio is on. *What the app does for this choice* (read in the code): it lets Android's communication device go and sets the audio mode back to normal (Platform.useHeadset(false)), opens receive on the system's default output as a media stream, and takes the phone's own microphone for transmit (applyAudioOutputDevice). *My first reading was wrong:* that receive had opened while Android still held the call link; a fresh connect has no call link to wait for and is silent too. *To look at when his log comes:* whether the phone's own microphone was open at the time (the TX tab shown keeps it open for its meter, and an open microphone can move a phone's playback to another route), which output Android reports for the stream, and the media volume. Nothing here can show it: no Samsung, and no headphones of that kind. *2026-11-26 10:03, with beta 121:* "Bluetooth media still produces no receive audio." No log with it; the log of a fresh connect and the Controls-closed try were asked for once more. *2026-11-26, with 219:* the likely cause is found in Qt's own device list: it shows the app one Bluetooth device of a headset, the first Android lists; where that is the call device, Bluetooth media opens on a device whose link is not up. 219's cure (both devices listed, Bluetooth media on the music device) should cover this; to be tried by Mattias when it is in a beta.

212. **Audio:** Android: Bluetooth headphones go silent when the TX tab opens, until the app is started again (a Samsung tablet) **In short:** Juan Carlos Reig Cucarella EA5WA loses receive in his Bluetooth headphones on a Samsung tablet every time he opens the TX controls, and gets it back only by closing the app completely; he says it has been so since the first versions. Opening the TX tab opens the microphone for its level meter, which changes Android's audio route. It is the mirror of Mattias SM6YEC's phone (201: receive lost when the microphone closes) and may be one with 211 (Bluetooth media silent on that phone). Android beta 121 holds the headset's microphone open from the connect on, so on his tablet it either cures this or takes receive away from the start; he was told it may go either way and asked to try. The cause is not known: no log, no model and no word on his Audio output setting yet. **From you:** nothing now; it waits for his log and answers. If beta 121 turns out worse on devices like his, the held microphone of 201 has to become a choice or be taken back: that would be yours to decide then, from his log. **Details:** (a post in the OksaSDR Users group, 2026-11-25 about 22:36 Finnish time, seen and answered in the watch of 01:30: "Everytime I open TX controls, I loose the audio in my Bluetooth headphones. To recover it, I have to close completely the app and reopen it. Is anyone experiencing this same issue?") *Answered under his post*: what opening the TX tab does, that beta 121 changes this part and was tested on one phone only, and what to send: the log right after the sound is lost (⋯ › Log › *Send to the developer*), the tablet's and the headphones' model, and what Controls › Audio › Audio output is set to. *What is known from the other Samsung* (Mattias's Galaxy S24+, betas 118 to 120): with Audio output on Auto, receive plays through the headset's call link while the microphone is open and is lost when it closes; with Bluetooth media nothing plays at all (211). Juan Carlos's is the other way round, which fits a tablet on Bluetooth media (or an older route) where the open microphone moves or silences the playback: to be read from his log, not assumed. *The watch missed his post for three hours*: it had looked at the newest release post, the notifications and the requests, not at the group's own feed; the feed's newest posts are read at every hourly check from now on.

213. **Meters:** The radio's main fan speed on the meters (John N1JM) (*in build 221; Android and Linux from the next beta*) **In short:** A tester asked for a bar graph of the main fan speed (TestFlight feedback on build 216, an iPad; his radio is a FLEX-8600). Built: *Fan* is one more choice on every meter, in receive and in transmit, wherever the radio reports the reading (its own MAINFAN, in RPM). The scale goes to 2000 RPM and grows when the fan runs faster, to 4000 and then to the radio's own top for the reading: that top is 8192 RPM on the FLEX-6600M, whose fan idles near 800, a tenth of such a bar. **From you:** nothing; a look at the meter on your own devices when the build is out. **Details:** (TestFlight feedback, 2026-11-26 06:31 Finnish time, an iPad (A16) on iOS 27.2.1, build 216, installed on his iPhone too: "Would be nice to have a bar graph of the main fan speed.") *What is there* (read in the code and the manual, 2026-11-26): the meter kind *Amp fan* (MeterView.qml: 0 to 5, "Fan 3 (min 2)"), offered for a KPA1500 only (MobileEngine's list of meter kinds), and the Amp tab's *Fan* row and *Fan minimum* for the Elecraft amplifiers; the Power Genius XL's fan mode on the Genius tab is a setting, not a reading. *If he means another device:* what its protocol reports of the fan decides whether a bar can be made (the rule that every added device offers its readings on the meters applies). His station is not known here: he has not written before. *2026-11-26, after reading his tester request of 2026-11-07 (a FLEX-8600 with firmware 4.3.16 over Tailscale):* "the main fan" is the radio's. The FLEX-6600M's meter list, asked over its command port: 4 RAD +13.8A Volts, 5 RAD +13.8B Volts, 6 RAD MAINFAN RPM ("Main radio fan RPM"), 10 TX- PATEMP degC. The meters' receive choices today are S, Mic, PA temperature and supply voltage (the manual's Meters section); the fan would be a fifth, and the scale's top has to be read from the radio (the reading's own hi value) so that a FLEX-8600's fan and a 6600's both fit. The earlier guess that he might mean an amplifier's fan is dropped; the reply sent says the radio's. *What was built (2026-11-26, the user's pick: "Otetaan iosille nuo 209, 213, 215, 216"):* MobileEngine::refreshMainFan reads RAD / MAINFAN through the core's meter model once a second while connected (the model keeps every reading's value but announces only the ones it knows by name; the core is not changed); the meters offer *Fan* only while the radio defines the reading, and a remembered choice of it falls back when it does not; MeterView's reading, MeterMarks' round marks (0, 500 ... 2000); "808 RPM" shortens to the number where a face has no room for it. *Tested on the FLEX-6600M:* "main fan: 808 RPM (the radio's scale for it: to 8192)"; chosen on both meters in receive and in transmit, as a bar and as an analog face, at 1194x834, 834x1194, 393x852, 852x393 and the OnePlus cover's 343x764 at 115 % text: no text cut. *Not seen:* the fan above 2000 RPM (the scale's growth), and a FLEX-8600, his radio.

214. **Audio:** Android beta 121: a headset link that drops by itself is not taken again, and the route is renewed needlessly at the front (*in Android beta 122*) **In short:** In your first try of beta 121 (OnePlus Open, Jabra Evolve2 65, media audio off) the headset's call link dropped by itself once, two and a half minutes after the connect, and receive stayed on the phone's speaker until Audio output was set to Speaker and back to Auto. Why it dropped is not known: it did not happen again that morning. That it stayed was a fault of 201's keeper, which took one refusal from Android for "no headset any more"; in beta 120 the next PTT would have taken the headset again, so beta 121 is worse there for every tester with a Bluetooth headset. Mended in the preview of beta 122, with three more things the same try showed: the app keeps asking for a headset Android still lists, looks for one that went off the list, leaves a phone call's route alone, and does not renew the route at the front while the link is up. Not run on a device yet. **From you:** what the first preview did not show, tried with the second one: the headset switched off and on while connected, Bluetooth media and back to Auto, and the same with the headset's media audio on. Then your word for the release to everyone. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-26 08:10: "Bluetooth tippui itsestään kuulokkeista eikä palautunut"; no voice prompt from the headset, and no drop since.) *The log:* 08:04:39.791 the receive stream ended with "the route changed", 21.7 s after the last PTT release, with nothing done in the app in between (the TX tab was open, which changes nothing while the hold is on); 08:04:41.462 the keeper: "headset link down for 2002 ms (communication device earpiece, hands-free link down, media audio off, audio mode 0)": Android had already taken the call mode back; the taking again found no headset to take (Platform.useHeadset(true) came back empty, twice within 3 ms: no call device listed, or Android refused it), and the keeper's rule for an empty answer worked the route out anew: "Auto -> no Bluetooth headset (Android's own route)". From then on nothing looked for the headset: the keeper only runs while a headset is the route. 08:06:25 the user chose Speaker, 08:06:26 Auto: the headset was there (link up 1.65 s after receive opened, the microphone held again). *Why the link dropped is open:* the app's log cannot say; Android's own log could, and the phone is not reachable by adb from here. Either the headset let the call link go, or Android took the call mode from the app (it had: mode 0). *The renewing at the front:* 08:07:11.972 "back in front with the Bluetooth headset: its route let go and taken again (… hands-free link up …)": the keeper had mended the link in the background already (back in 3.0 s and 4.5 s), so the round only cost a failed receive opening and two seconds until the microphone was held again. *The mend, built on your go ("push", 2026-11-26 08:20), commit "214 Android, the headset keeper after its first run on a device":* (1) an empty answer when taking the route again is a try that failed, not the end: the tries go on as for any drop, and the phone's own route is taken only when Android's list of call devices has no headset any more; while a headset is listed and the route is Auto, the keeper keeps looking for it at a slow pace even after the tries are used up, and at every return to the front; (2) the round at the front only when the link is down; (3) the same on a PTT release: a headset that Android lists is taken again, as beta 120 did. (4) During a phone call the keeper leaves the route alone. *Seen in the same try:* the user made a phone call with the app in the background (08:17:10, and once before at 08:07:03): the call took the headset's link, the keeper read that as a drop ("headset link down for 1999 ms (communication device Bluetooth hands-free, hands-free link down, media audio off, audio mode 2): the route taken again, try 1 of 5") and asked for the route in the middle of the call; "link back after 4502 ms" was the call's own link, and receive was opened on it. The user reported no harm from it, but an app must not reach for the call route while the phone is ringing or in a call (Android's audio mode 1 or 2): the keeper waits, and takes the route again as after a drop when the mode is back. What the app's receive should do during a phone call (play on, as it does now, or fall silent) is not decided here. *The same on another phone (Mattias SM6YEC, a Galaxy S24+ with beta 121, 2026-11-26):* "if I do something on the phone that causes OksaSDR to lose its Bluetooth audio connection, I sometimes have to close the app completely, wait a little and then reopen it to get Bluetooth Auto working again". He was told the mend is built and being tried, and that Speaker and back to Auto should bring it back meanwhile. *The preview of beta 122 on the user's phone, 2026-11-26 10:05 (the first minutes, from the log):* the mend in the background in 2.5 s; a phone call seen and left alone ("a phone call (audio mode 2): the headset's route left alone until it has ended"), the link back 0.5 s after it by itself; back in front with the link up nothing was renewed and the microphone was held again at once. *The rest of that try (10:05 to 10:16, from the log; OnePlus Open, Jabra Evolve2 65, Auto, media audio off):* three visits to the background, the link back at the first try each time (silence 2.4, 2.2 and 4.2 s, the last with a second break of 0.7 s because receive was opened twice as the link came up: 218); a phone call with the app in the background and one ringing with it in front, the route left alone both times, the link back by itself half a second after the first and by the round at the front 1.2 s after the second; back in front with the link up nothing was renewed; 11 PTT presses with receive back 94 to 128 ms after the release (median 114 ms); the screen dark for 4 min 46 s with the link up the whole time and no microphone open. *Not seen:* Android refusing the route to a listed headset (the fault this item is about: it did not happen in the try), a headset switched off and on, Bluetooth media and back to Auto, media audio on. *The user's second try on the first preview, 14:31 to 14:46 (the log; from 14:34:24 with the headset's media audio on, off again by 14:40:49):* the headset switched off at 14:32:54 (the sound on the phone's speaker within a second, "Android lists no Bluetooth headset any more", the microphone closed) and on again (14:33:06 "Android lists a Bluetooth headset again, its route taken", receive in the headset and the microphone held 1.2 s later); the same once more at 14:43:10 / 14:43:21; some twenty changes between Auto, Speaker and Bluetooth media, also 1.4 s apart, with the link up 0.4 to 0.6 s after each return to Auto; eight PTT presses in Auto with receive back in 92 to 152 ms, and a burst of quick taps at 14:40:51; the app to the background at 14:34:05 and 14:39:19 with the link back in 3.0 and 3.5 s (the delay 218 is to remove; this build did not have 218), and a phone call at 14:39:25 left alone, the link back 3.5 s after it. The user: "214 auto, speaker ja bluetooth toimivat myös vaihdot", with two faults that are not this item's: **219** and **220**. *Still not seen:* Android refusing the route to a listed headset. *The fourth preview (Android run 159) on the user's phone, 2026-11-26 16:22 to 16:33:* the headset switched off and on three times (twice with media audio on, once off): off Android's list and the phone's own route each time, taken again by the keeper within a second of being listed; six changes of Audio output; eleven quick PTT presses with receive back in 10 to 98 ms (once 0.9 s after the burst). The user: "3. 4 toimii". Android refusing the route to a listed headset was still not seen.

215. **Audio:** iOS: receive goes on in the background after an interruption has ended (*in build 221, not run on a device*) **In short:** On an iPad or iPhone, a call or another app's sound interrupts OksaSDR while it is in the background. When that ended and nothing else played, OksaSDR stayed silent until the app was opened, and with nothing playing iOS suspended the app and the radio dropped the connection: iOS refuses to start the app's microphone session from the background. Your word (2026-11-26): there must be no silence in the background. Built as the likely way: at the end of an interruption in the background the app takes a playing-only session (when iOS says to resume, or nothing else is playing), receive opens again on it, and the microphone's session comes back when the app is in front. It could not be run here: there is no iOS on the test machine. **From you:** the try on your iPad or iPhone: the app in the background with receive playing, then a call, an alarm, Siri or another app's sound for a moment: receive back when it ends, without opening the app; then the app to the front: receive goes on and PTT works. I read the log beside it. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-26 about 08:43, on 181's result: "181 taustalla ei sais olla hiljaisuutta jos mikää muu äänilähde ei tuota audiota niin silloin pitäisi kuulua oksaSDR".) *The iPad's log, build 216:* 08:33:11 the app to the background; 08:33:27.186 "interruption began"; 08:33:29 the receive sink cannot be opened; 08:33:36.732 "interruption ended"; 08:33:36.739 "setActive deferred (app not in front yet): Session activation failed"; nothing more until 08:33:56 when the app came to the front and receive started in 0.6 s. *What is known:* the session is play-and-record (the microphone is part of it); iOS's documented rule for recording is that it cannot be started from the background, which would explain the refusal (to be confirmed from the error's code). 181's own retry every 5 s cannot help, as iOS suspends the app once its audio has stopped. This was weighed in 181 already ("or the audio starts again as playback only in the background"). *To find out first:* the error's own code (the log has only its text: the code is to be logged), whether iOS still runs the app at the moment the interruption ends (it did at 08:33:36: the two lines came), and that a playing-only session started there keeps the app running and the radio's connection up. *Not to break:* PTT and the microphone when the app is back in front (the session put back before the microphone opens), a Bluetooth headset's route, CarPlay or a hands-free kit (205), and 216 if the microphone is to stay open. *The same chain took the radio's connection too (the user, 2026-11-26 09:03: "Miksiköhän ipad yhteys tipahti kun sovellus jäi taustalle"):* 08:44:09 the app to the background; 08:44:28.661 "interruption began" (something else took the audio for ten seconds: a call, an alarm, Siri or another app's sound); 08:44:38.654 "interruption ended"; 08:44:38.661 "setActive deferred (app not in front yet): Session activation failed"; then not a line for seventeen minutes: with no audio playing iOS had suspended the app, and the radio closes the connection of a client that no longer answers. 09:01:58.532, as the app was opened: "socket error: The remote host closed the connection", and the app connected again by itself in 5.5 s (09:02:04.363). So 215 is also what keeps the connection: with receive playing again in the background the app is not suspended. *What was built (2026-11-26, AudioSession.mm):* at AVAudioSessionInterruptionTypeEnded the play-and-record session is tried first, as before; when that is refused and the app is in the background, a session of the Playback category is taken if iOS's ShouldResume option is set or no other audio is playing (isOtherAudioPlaying), and the handler that reopens receive runs. Only there, where iOS has said the other sound is over: a retry on a timer would take another app's sound away, and today's refusal is what keeps the app quiet while the user's music plays. The playing-only session is left alone in the background (configureAudioSession, a change of Audio output), gives way to play-and-record when the app is in front (the did-become-active notice, or the first thing that asks for the session there), and no microphone is opened on it (audioSessionPlaybackOnly). The error's code is in the log now, with what iOS said ("interruption ended in the background: iOS says resume ..., other audio playing ..."). A playing-only session has no Speaker choice: with a Bluetooth device connected and Audio output Speaker it plays where iOS routes media until the app is in front. *Left to see on a device:* the whole of it; and after a long call, when the radio has dropped the connection of a client that did not answer, whether the app's reconnect gets to finish in the background before iOS suspends an app that plays nothing.

216. **Audio:** The microphone open the whole time on iOS too, as SmartSDR (*in build 221 for iOS; Android and Linux to come*) **In short:** On Android with a Bluetooth headset the app holds the microphone open while it is in front (201), and you found that right: it is how the other Flex programs work, so that the level can be set without transmitting. Built for an iPhone, an iPad and a Mac: the microphone is open the whole time the app is connected, with this device's microphone as the radio's input, and in front, once the microphone has been allowed. iOS shows its orange microphone mark for as long, and the microphone's audio goes to the radio all the time (some 50 MB an hour over mobile data); the radio still transmits only at PTT. In the background it is closed. Your word was for every device; Android and Linux are not in this build, as an open microphone moves Android's audio routes and that needs a preview on the phone. **From you:** the try on the iPad and the iPhone: the Mic meter moving in receive with the TX tab closed, iOS's orange mark, PTT as before, the app to the background (the mark goes) and back. For Android and Linux: nothing now, it comes with the next Android beta's preview. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-26 08:25-08:45, trying build 216 on the iPad: "201. iosissa mikki ei ole koko ajaan auki vaan ainoastaan kun controls tx on auki", and to the question whether it should be: "201 ios myö jatkuvasti mikki auki"; earlier that morning of the Android hold: "se on jo kokoajaan auki joka on itse asiassa se kuin muutkin flexin softat toimii jotta tasoa voi säätää ilman lähetystä".) *What is there:* MobileEngine::updateMicCheck opens the microphone for VOX, for the Mic meter of a shown TX or eSSB tab (holdMicCheck) and, on Android, for a Bluetooth headset's link (201); a fourth reason, "connected with this device's microphone as the radio's input", is a small change there. *To settle with the user:* the two questions above; in the background (iOS lets a recording app go on there and shows the mark; Android does not let the microphone stay open); what the main meters' Mic choice shows then (it would be live everywhere, which is the point); the privacy text's microphone section, the manual and both notes; and 208 (the link held without the stream), which this goes against: with the microphone always open the stream is the wanted thing. *The first answer (the user, 2026-11-26 14:4x):* "216 kaikki laitteet käsittääkseni näin ne myös muissa softissa on jotta ääniketjua voi vapaasti muokata ilman lähetystä": every device. The second (plain or a switch) was not said; read from the reason given as plain, always open, as the other Flex programs, and told so to the user with its price (the microphone mark for the whole connection, some 50 MB an hour over mobile data); a switch only if they ask for one. *What was built (2026-11-26, the user's pick; plain, no switch, as read from their reason):* a fourth reason in MobileEngine::updateMicCheck, "mic check: microphone open while the app is connected and in front": this device's microphone as the radio's input, a radio that can transmit, VOX off (VOX has its own reason), the app on the screen (active or inactive, so a banner or the control centre does not close and open it), and the microphone already allowed: this reason alone never asks, so a listener is not asked at the first connect, and once the microphone is allowed (the first PTT or TX tab) it opens. On iOS only (micAlwaysOpenHere; OKSASDR_TEST_MIC_ALWAYS=1 turns it on elsewhere for a test). The TX and eSSB tabs' own hold lets go in the background already (they ask for the app to be active). *Tested on Linux with the test switch, against the FLEX:* open at connect with the stream to the radio started, closed at the app's suspend, open at its return, kept through inactive, no change when the TX tab opens or closes; without the switch nothing of it happens; a real transmission by Space at 1 W in USB with the microphone already open: keyed, released, the microphone stayed open. *Not run:* iOS itself (the session, the orange mark, a Bluetooth headset, CarPlay). *Android and Linux, when built:* the phone's own microphone open beside Bluetooth media is 220's suspect (the robot sound), and Qt sets Android's audio mode when a microphone opens (218): a preview first. *Left for the next iOS build:* the permission text iOS shows ("OksaSDR sends your voice to the radio when you transmit") was written for a microphone open at PTT; with it open the whole time it should say that the microphone's audio goes to the radio while the app is connected and in front (the privacy text and the notes say so already).

217. ~~**Display:** The waterfall waits while you transmit: reported as a fault on the iPad~~ (*no change: the user's decision, 2026-11-26*) **In short:** With build 216 on the iPad you saw the waterfall stand still during your own transmission while the spectrum above it went on. That is the radio's setting *Show my transmission in the waterfall* (Radio Setup › Transmit), which is off on your radio: the iPad said so when asked. Off, the waterfall waits and goes on after, as SmartSDR's does; on, your transmission is drawn in it. It was made so in an earlier build after your finding that the transmit rows lit the whole span on 20 m. The 3D view keeps moving during a transmission whatever the setting says, which is not the same thing told twice. **From you:** nothing: you decided on 2026-11-26 that it stays as it is ("217. ei muutoksia mennään näin"). **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-26 about 08:42: "Sellainen virhe ios, että alapuoli vesputouksesta ei juokse kun itse lähettää yläpuoli kyllä"; their transmissions in the log at 08:38:57 to 08:40:06, 74 to 82 W.) *Asked of the iPad over the developer link:* setupget showTxInWaterfall=false. *The code:* SpectrumItem draws rows from the trace during a transmission only when the radio's setting is on ("Off, the waterfall simply waits for the transmission to end, as SmartSDR's"), and drops the radio's rows for 400 ms after it; Spectrum3DItem makes rows from the trace whenever no waterfall line has come for 400 ms, with no look at the setting. The manual says it under the display's list ("Off, the waterfall waits while you transmit and goes on after"). The same on Android: the code is shared.

218. **Audio:** Android: a Bluetooth headset goes on playing without a break when the app leaves the front (the screen off, another app) (*in Android beta 122*) **In short:** With a Bluetooth headset the sound stopped for two to four seconds every time the app left the front or the screen went dark, and then came back by itself (your phone, the preview of beta 122; a tester had asked to listen with the cover closed, Mattias SM6YEC). The cause is found: when the microphone closes, the Qt library takes back its own request for the headset, and the app's request went with it, so the headset's link dropped and had to be taken again. Mended in the code, not yet run on a device: the app now asks for the headset through a channel of its own that Qt's bookkeeping does not touch, so the link should stay up when the microphone closes; if it drops all the same, it is taken again at once and not after a second and a half; and receive is no longer opened twice as the link comes up. No microphone is kept open in the background and no new permission is asked: your word, as PTT cannot be pressed there anyway. **From you:** the try with the second preview (your word for it: "toinen esiversio", 2026-11-26): the headset playing, the screen dark, another app in front, and back; every Audio output choice once (Auto, Speaker, Bluetooth media), since the route code is changed; PTT with the headset. I read the log beside it. Then your word for the release to everyone. **Details:** (Mattias SM6YEC, mail of 2026-11-26 10:03: "One thing that would be really useful is the ability to close the phone's cover, turn off the screen or switch to another app while still listening to the radio", and "It would be fantastic if Bluetooth audio could remain active in the background and automatically recover if the connection is interrupted". The user, the same morning, on my first proposal to keep the microphone open in the background: "En ymmärrä ihan tätä koska silloinhan ei voi kuitenkaan painaa ptt. Kuuloke toimii ainakin minulla aina myös taustalla tai näyttö pimeänä siinä vaan on viive".) *The log of the user's phone (the preview of beta 122, the screen made dark at 10:11:43):* the microphone closed; the link was seen down 0.5 s later ("communication device earpiece, hands-free link down, audio mode 0"); the keeper asked for the route at 2.0 s (it waits 1.5 s on purpose), the link was up at 3.0 s, receive ran at 3.2 s, stopped once more for 0.7 s and ran for good at 4.2 s; after that the link stayed up for the 4 min 46 s the screen was dark, with no microphone open. The two visits before: 2.4 s and 2.2 s. *The cause, read in the Qt library's own Android code (Qt 6.12, QtAudioDeviceManager in Qt6AndroidMultimedia.jar):* when a microphone is opened on a Bluetooth headset, prepareAudioInput sets Android's audio mode to normal, asks for the headset as the communication device and counts one; when that microphone is closed, releaseAudioDevice counts down and at nought calls clearCommunicationDevice. Android keeps such requests apart by the AudioManager they come through, and the app's own request (Platform.useHeadset) went through the same AudioManager as Qt's, so Qt's clearing took it too. That explains why the link dropped after every transmission before beta 121 (roadmap 144, 201), why holding the microphone open cured it at the front (201), and why it dropped each time the app left the front. Receive (the output side) takes no part: Qt's native code does not call prepareAudioOutput at all, and the link held through eleven reopenings of receive with the microphone open (the PTT presses of the same try). *The cure (Android only):* Platform.audioManager: the app's route calls go through an AudioManager of the app's own (from a context of its own), so Qt's clearing takes only Qt's request; Platform.holdHeadsetMode: the app's call mode is taken again just before the keeper closes the held microphone (Qt had set it to normal when the microphone opened), which is the state the log shows holding for minutes in the background; the keeper takes the route at once, without its 1.5 s, when the link drops within 3 s of its own closing of the microphone; its rounds that let the route go close the microphone too, since Qt's request now stands beside the app's while the microphone is open; and the link watch opens receive again only if no opening that was under way has landed on the link meanwhile (MobileEngine::reopenRxOnHeadsetLink). Compiled for arm64 Android and the desktop build made; nothing of it can run here. *What the try has to show, in the log:* "headset route asked through an AudioManager of the app's own: yes" once after the connect; after "mic check: microphone closed" in the background no "headset link down" line at all, and no break in the sound; if "headset link down for 0 ms ... try 1 of 5" comes instead, the request was taken all the same and the quick way back ran (then about a second of silence instead of three). At the front everything as before: PTT, a phone call, a headset switched off and on. *To weigh:* below Android 12 the older calls are used, and one of them (setBluetoothScoOn) is not kept apart by AudioManager, so there the link may still drop and is then taken again at once; a transmission without the held microphone (the microphone not allowed, or another microphone chosen) should now leave the link up as well, which nothing has shown yet; 208 follows from this. *First proposal, dropped:* the microphone kept open in the background by a background service of the microphone kind. It is not needed (the link holds without a microphone), it would have asked a new permission and shown Android's microphone mark with the screen off, and the user saw no sense in it. Mattias was told on 2026-11-26 that listening in the background waits for such a permission: that was wrong, and is to be put right in the mail about the build that carries this. *On the user's phone, the second preview (Android run 157), 2026-11-26 15:11 to 15:16:* "headset route asked through an AudioManager of the app's own: yes" at the connect; the app to the background at 15:12:23 (12.6 s), 15:12:41 (about 40 s) and 15:13:32 (7 s): the microphone closed each time and the link did not drop, no line of a drop or of receive opened again, the microphone held again at once at the return. The first preview at the same step: 3.0 to 3.5 s of silence every time. Also seen: after a change of Audio output the phone's own microphone was no longer opened for a moment (220's suspect), and "receive had opened on the headset's link meanwhile: left as it is" once (15:16:03). Not caused by this, as far as can be told, but found in the same try: **221**. *The third preview (run 158):* the same code with its call to Android now made through the bounded call of 221; the background visit is tried again on the fourth preview before the release. *The fourth preview (run 159), 2026-11-26:* the app to the background four times in Auto (29, 6, 5 and 7 s): the microphone closed, no link drop and no reopening of receive, at the return only the microphone opened again. The user: "pieni katko", a small break is still heard. It is not in the log (nothing is opened again); the likely moments are the change of Android's audio mode when the app takes its call mode before closing the microphone, and Qt's setting it back when the microphone opens at the return. To look at later: whether the mode needs changing at all once the app's own request stands.

219. **Audio:** Android: Audio output changed from Speaker to Bluetooth media leaves the sound on the phone's speaker **In short:** On your phone every Audio output choice works and so does changing between them, except one: from *Speaker* to *Bluetooth media* the sound stays on the phone's speaker; only from *Auto* does it reach the headset. The cause is found: the Qt library shows the app one Bluetooth device of a headset only, the first Android lists, which on your phone is the call device; the music device is not there at all, so *Bluetooth media* cannot open on it. A first attempt in the third preview of beta 122 could not work for that reason, and it made one case worse (with Bluetooth media chosen, the headset switched off and on lost the sound until Audio output was changed), so it was taken out again. The cure: the app's own copy of that Qt class lists a headset's call device and its music device both, and *Bluetooth media* opens on the music device. That changes the device list on every phone, so it gets a build of its own with every choice tried, wired and USB headsets too. Likely the same behind 211 (Bluetooth media silent on a Galaxy S24+) and 220 (the robot sound). Not built. **From you:** nothing now; your word when it is picked for a build (I propose the next Android build after beta 122), then the try of every Audio output choice and change with a Bluetooth headset, media audio on and off, and with a USB-C or wired headset if you have one. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-26 about 14:44, the first preview of beta 122: "auto, speaker ja bluetooth toimivat myös vaihdot paitsi kun speakerilta vaihdetaan bluetooth media niin se ei toimi katso loki".) *The log:* Speaker to Bluetooth media at 14:34:31, 14:34:37, 14:34:40, 14:34:47, 14:36:30 and 14:40:04, the headset's media audio on for at least the first five (switched on at about 14:34:10, off again by 14:40:49); each time "audio devices: output the system default / microphone Built in microphone", one or two openings refused at once (as at nearly every change of route), then "RX stream started at 24000 Hz device: Bluetooth" (once at 44100 Hz) 0.1 to 0.8 s after the change, and nothing more; the user moved on 1.4 to 4.5 s later each time. From Auto the same lines, then at 1.5 s "receive opened again on the media route, the headset's call link let go" and a second start. *The code:* MobileEngine::setAudioOutput opens receive once more after 1.5 s only when the choice left a headset's call link (the cure of beta 121 for a Galaxy S24+ where a stream opened while the call link was still up stayed silent); applyAudioOutputDevice opens Bluetooth media on Qt's default output, which Qt names "Bluetooth" for a headset's call device and its music device alike (types 7 and 8), so the log cannot say which of the two the stream was given. *To do:* first the proof: a line after each opening that names the device Android plays the app's sound on (AudioManager.getActivePlaybackConfigurations gives it for the app's own players); then the cure it points to, likely the second opening after every change to Bluetooth media. The same line would serve 211 and 212, where a log cannot say today what the tester hears. *The user's answer, 2026-11-26 15:0x:* "219 jäi kaiuttimeen": the sound stayed on the phone's speaker, it was not silent. *Qt 6.12's own code (QtAudioDeviceManager, read from the jar):* the outputs are sorted by a priority per device type: wired and USB headsets 1, Bluetooth A2DP (type 8) and Bluetooth SCO (type 7) both 2, the built-in speaker 3; the sort keeps Android's own order among equals, and audioDeviceTypeToString gives "Bluetooth" for both. QMediaDevices::defaultAudioOutput is the first of that list. So "the system default" that Auto and Bluetooth media open on (applyAudioOutputDevice) is the call device or the music device as Android lists them, and the stream carries that device's id. *The cure in detail:* Platform gives the ids of the connected headset's call device (TYPE_BLUETOOTH_SCO or TYPE_BLE_HEADSET) and music device (TYPE_BLUETOOTH_A2DP, the LE Audio media types); applyAudioOutputDevice picks the QAudioDevice with that id (Qt's device id is Android's), falling back to today's default when none is listed; the log line after each opening names the device Android routes the app's player to (AudioManager.getActivePlaybackConfigurations). To be tried on the phone with media audio on and off, from each choice to each other, and with the headset connected before and after the app's connect. *The second preview, 2026-11-26 15:1x:* unchanged, as expected (not mended in it). The user: "jos puhelimen media päällä niin ei kaiuttimelta siirry bt medialla, toimii vain auto bt media". *The third preview on the phone, 2026-11-26 15:54:* "audio devices: Android's music device 57 is not among Qt's outputs: Qt's default" at every change to Bluetooth media; the user: "Edelleenkään speaker => bluetooth media ei toimi". *Qt's own code (the app's copy, mobile/android/qt-patches/QtAudioDeviceManager.java, getAudioDevices):* after the sort it keeps the first Bluetooth device (A2DP or SCO, equal in rank, so Android's order decides) and skips every further one, as it keeps one built-in microphone. So Qt's one "Bluetooth" output is the call device or the music device as Android lists them; once that day, right after the headset had come back, it was the music device for two seconds ("output Bluetooth (57)") and then the call device again. *What the attempt did wrong (taken out, commit "219 taken out again"):* with Bluetooth media chosen it looked the device up again at every change of Android's outputs and opened receive again; when the headset came back that put receive on Qt's default "Bluetooth", the call device, whose link is not up in that choice: silence until the user chose Auto ("bt medialla kun sammuttaa kuulokkeen ... ääni hävisi ja ei palautunut mihinkään", "painamalla auto tuli kuulokkeeseen"). Before, the sound stayed on the phone's speaker. The line "receive plays on" never came: Android gives no device for the app's players. *The cure in detail:* in the app's copy of the class, both Bluetooth kinds listed, the call device ranked before the music device (so Qt's default with a headset stays the call device, which Auto uses) and named apart (the music device "Bluetooth media"); applyAudioOutputDevice opens Bluetooth media on the device of that name and falls back to the phone's speaker by name when there is none (media audio off, the headset gone), so that the sound never lands on a call device whose link is down; the look-up at a change of outputs comes back with that fallback; patch-qt-usb-audio.sh tells a jar that already has the change by a mark of this version, not by "USB headset" (a cached Qt would otherwise keep the old class). What Bluetooth media has really played on when it came from Auto (the call link kept up, most likely) is to be seen in the same try. *Also part of this (the user, 2026-11-26 16:00, the third preview):* with Bluetooth media chosen, the headset switched off brings the sound to the phone's speaker (2.3 s at 15:59:09), but switched on again the sound does not go back to the headset ("kuulokkeiden käynnistyksessä ei palaudu"): at 15:59:44 Android listed the music device (id 405), Qt did not, and nothing opened receive again. With the cure the music device is looked up whenever Android's outputs change: on it when it is there, the phone's speaker when it is not. *The fourth preview (run 159), without the attempt:* Bluetooth media with the headset switched off at 16:30:49 put the sound on the phone's speaker in 2.8 s and it stayed there, as before the third preview (the user: "4 toimii").

220. **Audio:** Android: Audio output changed from Auto to Bluetooth media sometimes leaves the sound like a robot **In short:** On your phone the sound in the headset was at times robot-like after changing Audio output from *Auto* to *Bluetooth media* in the app. The app's own audio buffer did not run dry once in that try (it would say so in the log), so the fault arises after the app, in Android or the headset, most likely while the headset goes from its call link to its music link. One thing the app does at that moment looks wrong and is my suspect: it opens the phone's own microphone for a moment although nobody asked for it (the held microphone is moved to the new device just before it is closed). Not mended; the log does not show which of the changes sounded like a robot. **From you:** when it happens again, the time, and whether the sound mended itself or only at the next change. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-26 about 14:44: "Samoin autolta bt medialla vaihto apissa välillä vei audion robotiksi".) *The log, Auto to Bluetooth media with media audio on:* 14:34:20, 14:34:52, 14:35:01, 14:35:07, 14:35:53, 14:36:19, 14:37:38 and 14:40:09. No "audio underrun" line in the whole try. In four of them the first opening after the change came up at 44100 or 48000 Hz and the second, 0.4 to 1 s later, at 24000 Hz; in the others both at 24000 Hz. Each time, right after "mic check: microphone closed": "TX stream started ... device: Built in microphone" (the capture restarted on the new microphone by applyAudioOutputDevice, then stopped by the closing that was already asked: at the next PTT it starts anew, so it does not stay open). Qt sets Android's audio mode and asks for a communication device at every opening of a microphone (see 218), which is the last thing wanted while the headset changes links. *To do:* no restart of a microphone that is about to close (the keeper's hold released before the devices are changed), the device line of 219 to see where the sound goes, and the user's times. *2026-11-26, with 219:* coming from Auto, Bluetooth media very likely plays on the headset's call link, which is on its way down at that moment (Qt's one Bluetooth device is the call device on this phone): a robot-like sound fits a link in that state. 219's cure moves the sound to the music link; this item is then tried again. In the second and third previews the phone's own microphone was no longer opened at the change (the other suspect).

221. **Fixes:** Android: the app stands still for 15 seconds when a Bluetooth headset with media audio on is switched off (*in Android beta 122, seen working on the phone*) **In short:** With the headset's *Media audio* on in Android's Bluetooth settings, switching the headset off while connected froze the app for about 15 seconds, twice; the radio closed the connection meanwhile and the app connected again by itself. With media audio off the same step is clean: the sound moves to the phone's speaker and back to the headset when it is on again. The likely cause: the headset keeper asks Android's audio service for the headset's state twice a second from the app's main thread, and when a headset with media audio disappears that service is busy for some 15 seconds, so the question, and the whole app with it, waits. Not proven: the stacks are taken at 16 s and both freezes ended at 15.5 and 15.8 s. The questions came with beta 121, so the released beta 121 very likely has this too; most testers have media audio on. Not mended. **From you:** the short check on the fourth preview (the headset off and on with media audio on, the screen dark, PTT), then your word for the release to everyone. **Details:** (the user, 2026-11-26 15:17, the second preview of beta 122: "Sammutin kuulokkeen ja media ääni oli puhelimen asetuksissa päällä niin kippasi softa", and "Jos puhelimen media ei päällä niin ei kippaa vaan irtoo normaalisti ja siirtyy kaiuttimeen ja kun kuulokkeet taas päälle niin menee kuulokkeisiin".) *The log (OnePlus Open, Jabra Evolve2 65, Auto, the microphone held):* 15:14:07.5 the GUI thread stops answering; 15:14:11.06 "AudioEngine: sink appears zombie (bytesFree stuck at 0 for 2000 ms), restarting RX" (the audio thread's stream stands too, and its restart does not return until 15:14:22.9); 15:14:22.96 "GUI thread answers again after 15475 ms"; 15:14:23.2 the radio has closed the connection; 15:14:24.2 "headset link down for 15610 ms ... Android lists no Bluetooth headset any more"; connected again at 15:14:29.9. The same at 15:15:14.5 (15812 ms, "Stream device not correct" at the end). With media audio off at 15:15:50.8: "QAudioSink stopped with error IOError (the route changed)" at once, the headset off the list 0.9 s later, no stall; the first preview's two such steps (14:32:54, 14:43:10) were with media audio off as well and clean. *The reading:* the keeper's tick calls AudioManager.getMode (since the first preview, 214) and getCommunicationDevice when the link is down (since beta 121, 201) on the GUI thread; both wait in Android's audio service for locks its own worker holds while it takes a Bluetooth device down, which with a music link (A2DP) on a headset that has just gone lasts long. Whether the second preview's own request (218) lengthens that is not known; the first preview was never tried with media audio on at a switch-off. *The cure:* every call the keeper makes to Android (asking and requesting) on a worker thread of its own, the tick working from the last answers and skipping while one is out, so that a slow audio service costs sound for a while and not the app and the radio's connection. With it 203's stacks at 8 s (the walk is proven on the device since 14:45 that day), which would have shown both freezes. To be tried: the headset switched off and on with media audio on and off, at the front and in the background, and out of reach. *What was built (commit "221 Android: calls to Android's audio service are waited for only a moment"):* ScreenAwake.cpp runs every call to Platform's audio functions on a thread of its own and waits 150 ms for a question, 1.5 s for a route change; a question that is not answered gives what it last knew, a request goes on there, and the keeper skips its tick until the call has returned; Qt's device lists are read through the same call (Qt reads them from Android on the asking thread, and the device change is told on the GUI thread at that very moment: as likely a place for the stop as the keeper); the link's own state is read first, without a question. Which call stood on 2026-11-26 is still not known; if the app stands again, the stacks at 8 s say. *On the user's phone, the third preview (Android run 158), 2026-11-26:* the headset switched off with media audio on at 15:53:18, 15:55:27 and 15:57:30: "Android's audio service has not answered in 150 ms: the app goes on without it until it does", then "... answers again (a call took 15112 ms)", 4426 ms and 13413 ms. No "GUI thread stalled" line in any of the three, the volume keys answered at once, and the radio's connection stayed up (the second preview lost it both times). So the stop was a call to Android's audio service, as read. The sound reached the phone's speaker 19 s and 16 s after the switch-off: Android's own 13 to 15 s, in which the audio thread's stream stands too ("sink appears zombie"), and 2 to 3.4 s of the keeper's tries after it. *Left to do, small:* when the service answers after a long wait, the keeper could look at once whether the headset is still listed and save those last seconds. *The fourth preview (run 159), 2026-11-26 16:23 and 16:26:* the headset switched off with media audio on twice more: Android's audio service out for 15.0 and 12.6 s, no stall of the GUI thread, the radio's connection kept, the sound on the phone's speaker 18 s and 13 s after the switch-off; with media audio off (16:30) 1.5 s. The user: "Kuulokkeen katkaisusta kaiuttimeen kesti melko pitkään mutta tuli".

Earlier ideas and reports, kept for their history: each line either names the number its idea took ("now N"), is done, or is a report with nothing to do. Every open idea has a number above since 2026-11-25 (174 to 196 on that day, and 50 again; later ones follow).
- SmartLink closed on the mobile network (now 99) (Mohammed Alkhayarin A71AT, mail with his log 2026-11-16, build 178, iPad, iOS 27.0.1, an Aurora AU-510, Qatar: "running perfect ... with a 5G network ... but suddenly it stopped working", the radio listed and signed in, only the home network connects). The log: 17 SmartLink attempts, every one connect_ready and the TLS connection to the radio's public address open, then closed from the far end 30-40 ms after wan validate on the cellular network (Qt's "The remote host closed the connection", also what an injected reset gives); at 17:31 on Wi-Fi the same SmartLink connection worked, at 17:33 back on cellular every attempt closed again. The app does the same on both, so the mobile network (operators in Qatar filter VoIP-like traffic) or the home router's port forward is the likely cause. Asked him (reply 2026-11-16): SmartSDR for iOS over the same 5G, OksaSDR on another Wi-Fi, changes at home, the iPad's own SIM or a hotspot. His answer (2026-11-16): SmartSDR for iOS connects on the same iPad, 5G and account, and so does OksaSDR beta 92 on his Android phone over 5G (his video: MOE on 20 m and 15 m, 19-65 ms); it worked on the iPad over 5G that morning and stopped after a disconnect and reconnect, Wi-Fi at home still works. So it is ours and on the iPad; nothing in the connect path changed between builds 174 and 178. Asked him next: Wi-Fi off in Settings, the iPad on the phone's hotspot, Sign out and in. Item 84's log lines (ports, UPnP, the hole punch port, the end before or after the radio answered) for his next log. His log also had LogControls.qml:1066 "Unable to assign [undefined] to bool" (the POTA dialog's park row before its info came): fixed with !! as the dialog's other rows.
- ~~A demo video of the app in operation~~ *done*: made in the dev build by a ShotPlan script (version 5: the iPad tour, SSB, CW, RADE and RTTY on the air, the phone part) and published in the OksaSDR Users group on 2026-11-13. The idea as it was: (Kent Winrich K9EZ, FB question 2026-11-12: "Has anyone created a video showing the software in operation?"; answered with the manual for now). Two ways: the dev build at iPad or iPhone size against the FLEX, driven by a ShotPlan script (repeatable, redone after big builds), recorded with gpu-screen-recorder or frame grabs, the radio's audio with pw-record, captions (SRT) and tap marks made from the same plan by ffmpeg; or the iPad's own screen recording by the user, partly driven over the developer link (band, mode, tune, zoom, drawer), the file copied to this PC and cut and captioned here. Not a release item; publishing (YouTube, the group) asked first.
- The Ulanzi D100 dial as a control on the iPad and Android, as on the desktop (now 83) (Christoph Steinert DM5HF, mail 2026-11-14, build 169, iPad Pro 13 M4: "would it be possible to add the Ulanzi D100 like in AetherSDR (more convenient use for portable due to the internal accumulator)", for the voice keyer). The desktop reads the dial's key stream (src/core/UlanziChordDecoder: the knob as volume up / down keys, the top row and press as media keys, the side buttons as Ctrl chords) through evdev / IOKit / Windows HID, none of which an iPad app has. To analyse: how the D100 connects (Bluetooth or a 2.4 GHz dongle), which of its keys iPadOS hands to an app at all (volume and media keys are normally the system's) and which Android does (KeyEvent sees volume and media keys); the Ctrl chords would arrive as keyboard keys on both.
- Keyboard keys for TUNE, the ATU, a two-tone test and a PTT that stays keyed (now 85) (Christoph Steinert DM5HF, mail 2026-11-16 evening: his D100H on the Mac through Ulanzi Studio, which turns each button into a key: the knob already tunes by ← / →, F1-F3 play the voice keyer, Space is PTT while held; "If now in combination the buttons could work, that would be perfect", his buttons labelled VOX, PTT, ATU and 2 tone tune, and a photo of the D100H beside his cabled CTR2). The app has keys only for tuning, zoom, frequency entry, Space PTT, F1-F12 / ⌘1-⌘0 and Esc; to analyse: which functions get keys (TUNE, ATU tune, two-tone, PTT toggle with a time limit, VOX when 19 exists), fixed keys or chosen ones (the desktop has Configure Shortcuts), and whether Ulanzi Studio holds a key for the length of a press (Space PTT) or sends a tap. Serves any keyboard and a Stream Deck on a Mac too.
- The DX cluster after a network change (now 86) (the developer's iPad, build 180, 2026-11-16): after each switch between Wi-Fi and the hotspot the cluster (dxc.nc7j.com) closed the new connection right after login, several times ("closed 2 times right after login"), until the session from the old network had timed out on the server: most likely a second login with the same call refused. To analyse: a log line naming it, the retry spaced out, or a different SSID (call-1) while the old session lingers; how other clusters treat a duplicate login.
- The slice kept through a reconnect on a one-station radio (now 87) (Rich Yost N2RY, mail with his log 2026-11-16, build 180, iPhone17,4, iOS 27.0.1, FLEX-6500 over SmartLink: "Was on 18.130ish USB, walked outside and switched from WiFi to 5gUW, app connection dropped for like 5 secs then came back to CW to 14.111"). The log: the network change handled as meant (14:08:57 cellular, a new SmartLink server connection, signed in 1.2 s later, the radio back at 14:09:04.9), but the radio (one station) still held the app's session from Wi-Fi with its slice on 18.130 USB; the slice create on the pan the radio restored was refused (5000002a), the app recreated the pan, evicted the stale session, and its slice recovery made a new slice, which the radio put on its default 14.111 CW. To do: keep the slice's frequency, mode and filter when a session drops, and give them to a slice the app has to make again after a reconnect (only then; a slice the radio kept stays as it is).
- OksaSDR on the desktops (Linux now 91): Linux, Windows and Intel Mac from the same code (the user, 2026-11-17 night: "kyllästynyt desktopilla kun pitää olla monta ohjelmaa"; a study, no build asked). Study in the doc "OksaSDR työpöydälle" (claude.ai/artifact/GsLyWL4td6qyFv4qca9La6): the app already runs on Linux as the dev build; upstream's appimage.yml, macos-dmg.yml (arm64 + x86_64, macOS 14.4) and windows-installer.yml can be pointed at aether-mobile; the work is packaging and signing, a real secret store (qtkeychain), audio device choice and desktop habits (windows, shortcuts); Windows needs the POSIX headers (unistd.h, dlfcn.h) behind QLibrary; Intel Macs only through a native macOS build (iPad apps need Apple silicon; macOS 26 is the last for Intel). Suggested order: Linux for own use first (the user's daily ThinkPad, on Linux, then the cabin radio PC, which runs upstream AetherSDR 26.8.3 today), then a native Mac, Windows last (tested by testers or a Windows VM: the ThinkPad's old Windows partition would take the daily machine away). Open questions in the doc.
- Big buttons: a Log button to a simple log screen, just the callsign and maybe the report (Michael Walker, mail 2026-11-15: "Can you have a button to move to a LOG screen where you just type in the callsign and maybe the signal report?") (now 75). The Log tab has the fields already; to analyse: a big-text overlay or the drawer's Log tab, and the on-screen keyboard's room on a phone.
- Upstream's fix for #6051 (PR #6992, merged 2026-11-15, rfoust) (now 74): RX audio packets put back in order (a 30 ms wait for a missing one) before Opus / PCM decoding, so reordering on LTE / WireGuard / Wi-Fi is not concealed as loss. Ours (PanadapterStream's VitaSequenceTracker) only counts loss net of reordering. To analyse: its VitaRxReorder.h and the PanadapterStream changes against our copy, with Barry Cohen's iPhone drops above in mind.
- From Christoph Steinert DM5HF (mail 2026-11-15, build 170 on a Mac: the DVK on the CTR2, Esc and the DX cluster Spot work; (a), (c) and (b) now 76, 77 and 78): (a) a clear button in the Log tab's callsign field; (b) editing a contact already logged (a tap or double click on it in the list): today a logged QSO cannot be changed in the app; (c) a Name field beside Comment, filled with QRZ.com's first name (ADIF NAME is already filled from the lookup; "no prio"); (d) F1-F12 in RADE play the voice keyer as analog SSB although RADE is on: *in build 171*: the radio's voice keyer is held off in RADE (it plays through the radio's analog chain); the app's own recordings already go through the RADE encoder; (e) the arrow keys do not move the cursor in the FreeDV tab's FreeDV Reporter message field (on a Mac's keyboard); *in build 171*: AppKit's function-key characters mapped and navigation keys logged, to be confirmed by his log.
- The CTR2's buttons for the Tuner Genius XL's Tune and a PTT that lets go (the Tune now 93; the PTT solved, see 183) (Hanno Vogels DK3HV, mail 2026-11-17, build 182, iPad Air 5, iOS 27.2.1, a CTR2-MIDI over Bluetooth, a Tuner Genius XL; thanks for the tuner on top of the Amp tab): (a) "tune" on button 3 "is like MOX ... as long as I push the button, it is transmitting", while the screen's Tune "ask[s] for TGXL and start[s] the short tune process". The MIDI functions TUNE and ATU are the radio's (TUNE keys the radio's tune carrier on and off as the TUNE button on the TX controls; ATU runs the radio's own ATU, nothing without one); the tuner's Tune on the Amp tab (Engine.tgxlTune) has no MIDI function. To weigh: a MIDI function "Tuner Genius XL Tune" (the same call, without the screen's question, under Transmit from the controller), or TUNE / ATU going to the TGXL when the radio has none. (b) PTT on button 4 "is not a push to talk; it has a hanger and PTT keeps pushed". PTT (hold) follows the note on and off (Mohammed A71AT's CTR2 lets go every time in his log of 2026-11-17: "PTT released: MIDI released"); a PTT that stays keyed fits MOX (on / off) learned on the button, or a button that sends only on a press. To ask: which function the button has on the MIDI tab (PTT (hold) or MOX), and what Last message shows on a press and on letting go.
- The View tab's display sliders moved by a finger that only meant to scroll (now 101) (Rich Yost N2RY, mail with his log 2026-11-17, build 182, iPhone17,4, FLEX-6500 on his LAN: "suddenly the waterfall partially disappeared ... Tried to restart app but didn't come back"; the screenshot: the 3D view above, the waterfall black). The log: 23 FFT frames and 1 waterfall row in the first five seconds against 103 and 44 in the run before, as FFT frames/s at 5 and Waterfall speed at its slow end would give; both are kept in the radio, so a restart did not help. A Slider takes the value at the point pressed and is sent on release (DisplayControls.qml PanSetting), so a touch on its track while scrolling the drawer sets it. To weigh: a slider in a scrolling tab moved only by a horizontal drag past a threshold (not by a press on the track), or by its handle; the same for every slider in the drawer. Also in his log: the KPA1500's TCP control link timed out every 13 s while its UDP readings answered at times. Rich's answer (2026-11-17): "the waterfall speed was like 1, after resetting it its was fine" (confirmed); port 1500 was taken by his Node-RED flow for the UDP side, Tailscale next.
- Two crashes on build 181 from Scott Stanford W5SLS (now 100) (TestFlight feedback 2026-11-17 21:48 and 21:56, iPhone 16 Pro Max, iOS 27.2, mobile data): "When I touch the top of the screen that shows “xxxMB” it crashes" (the top row's network badge with RAM ... MB, which opens Network & performance) and "I was just about to disconnect and program froze"; no crash log from the API. The panel opened and refreshed on the dev build at 440x956 against the FLEX (2026-11-18) without a fault. He has build 184 installed since. *Reproduced 2026-11-18 on the developer's iPad* (build 184, iOS 27.2.1, a hardware keyboard, the FLEX over the VPN): a tap on the top row froze everything and iOS ended the app, twice (08:21, 08:22); the next start logged "previous run: GUI thread stalled 32181 ms ... last frame 32059 ms before", nothing logged after the tap. The same panel opens fine in the dev build on Linux (440x956 with the FLEX, 1376x1032 and 1032x1376 without), so it is iOS-only and the GUI thread hangs (a loop or a lock), not a crash in itself. Wanted: the iPad's crash report (.ips, the watchdog's main thread stack); build 184's testflight-symbols artifact holds only the stripped binary (no dSYM: the archive's dSYMs folder was empty), so our own frames will not symbolize: the CI copy of the dSYM to fix with it.
- Build 184 heard from (2026-11-18): Hanno Vogels DK3HV: the Tuner Genius XL Tune from the MIDI button "works perfect after replacing the function" (93), with SmartSDR 4.3.19 on his radio; Michael Walker: voice logging "worked great today from the car" (mail and the FB post); Mohammed A71AT: the microphone handed to another app and back "very fast and effective".
- ~~The KPA1500's OPERATE not read back in 3 s once~~ *its log lines in build 195* (Rich Yost N2RY, his build 190 log 2026-11-19: at 11:19 and 11:25 OPERATE after a tune showed in 0.04-0.17 s; at 12:02, 4 s after a tune and 40 s after the control link came up again, "has not shown operate in 3 s" with the UDP readings still answering; Rich, mail 2026-11-19 21:04: "I did not press anything on the amp itself", the tune fine). The log cannot tell whether it came later or not at all: to log the OS value last read when the wait ends, and "shows operate after N s" when it comes late.
