Amplifiers and the tuner (planned 2026-10-29, its own build; asked by
Sebastian Schu DG5CST for the RF2K-S, Bill Arcand for the Tuner Genius and
Dennis Daniel for the FlexRadio amplifier and tuner):
- **Step 1, the RF2K-S read only (build 148 / beta 61):** RfkitProtocol /
  RfkitConnection taken from upstream (#6596, tested there on an RF2K-S,
  firmware G200C267) with their tests, plus a read-only mode (only GET
  requests reach the wire; the control calls are refused, tested on the
  loopback stand-in) and a poll period (500 ms on the phone, 3 requests a
  tick). Radio Setup › **Amp**: the address (host or host:port, 8080 by
  default), Read the amplifier on / off, Forget. Controls › **Amp**, the
  13th button, only once an amplifier is set up (rows of seven then): name,
  OPERATE / STANDBY shown, forward / reflected / SWR bars on the
  amplifier's scales, temperature, voltage, current, band, frequency,
  antenna, tuner, interface, status, firmware, the amplifier's error.
  Read while the app is on screen and connected to a radio. Tested with
  mobile/tools/rf2k-mock.py (the API's documents, keying cycles; counts
  any PUT / POST): readings live, refused, not an RF-KIT, timed out, link
  lost, off and back, background and back, forgotten; 0 writes in every
  run. Sebastian checked it on his RF2K-S (2026-11-03, port 8081, 615 W
  at 8 W drive, SWR 1.06): "The readings are all fine!"
- **Step 2, control (build 149 / beta 62):** Radio Setup › Amp › **Control
  the amplifier** (off by default; Forget turns it off; with it off the
  connection stays read-only). The Amp tab: **Mode** Standby / Operate,
  **Antenna** 1-4 (the ones the amplifier lists as set up; EXT shown, not
  switched, as upstream), **Reset error**. Each a confirmation naming the
  change (the action button names it too), closed by itself when the
  radio keys, the link drops or Control goes off; refused while the radio
  transmits (no relay switching under RF); one at a time (Asking the
  amplifier…); never latched, the tab shows the readback; a refusal shows
  the amplifier's reason, a lost answer says the state is unknown until
  read. Forward gets the desktop's 3-second peak marker; the poll is
  250 ms while transmitting. The meters offer the amplifier's six
  readings. Next: Sebastian's check of the controls; then the 4O3A Power
  Genius XL / Tuner Genius XL (Dennis Daniel) when someone can test.
- Controls gets an **Amp** tab (the twelfth button went to Digi in build
  140; the Amp tab is the 13th and shows only when set up): the operating
  side, the state, Operate / Standby, Tune, the meters. Radio Setup's tab
  holds the setup (addresses, turning them on).
- Radio Setup gets an **Amplifier & tuner** tab:
  - **4O3A Power Genius XL**, found through the radio: model, state,
    Operate / Standby (relayed by the radio, so over SmartLink too).
  - **4O3A Tuner Genius XL**, found through the radio: Operate / Bypass and
    Tune (the radio's `tgxl autotune`). Later, with the TGXL's own
    connection and its code: a surer autotune, the relays and the 1x3's
    antenna.
  - **RF-KIT RF2K-S**, the amplifier's own REST API (upstream #6596,
    26.10.4; RfkitProtocol / RfkitConnection taken as they are): address
    (or host name) and port, free to choose (Sebastian's is 8081: his
    router uses 8080), the link's state, Operate / Standby, internal
    antenna 1-4, error reset (asks first); band, frequency, tuner and
    interface shown. Not over SmartLink: the phone must reach the
    amplifier's address (same network or VPN).
- The meters can show them, offered while one is there: amplifier power,
  SWR, temperature and current (RF2K-S voltage, PGXL drive); the tuner's
  power and SWR. The engine has the PGXL / TGXL meters already
  (MeterModel ampMetersChanged, ampVitalsChanged, tgxlMetersChanged); take
  upstream's #6658 with it (SmartSDR 4.3 amp / tuner meter handles
  unsigned).
- Testing: no hardware here. The RF2K-S against a local mock of its API
  first, then Sebastian on his amplifier (he offered, 2026-10-29). PGXL and
  TGXL need a tester with one; not released untested on a real device (Mike
  VA3MW agreed: "He needs the equipment to test and integrate").
- The gate for anything that changes a device's state (the user,
  2026-10-30: the smoke must stay in the amplifier):
  1. Read-only first: power, SWR, temperature, state. No command that
     changes the device goes out in the first step.
  2. A written test plan for every control command (what it does, the
     device's reply, the error handling), and mock tests with the failures
     too (link lost, a wrong reply, a slow device).
  3. Off by default, and first only in a preview build for the tester with
     the device; to everyone only after the tester's check. (The RF2K-S
     control went to every tester at once, off by default, by the user's
     choice on 2026-11-03 after Sebastian's read-only check.)
  4. Every state change (Operate, antenna, error reset, tune) behind a
     confirmation, never one stray tap.

- **Radio Setup › Amp by manufacturer** (planned 2026-11-05, the user):
  choose the manufacturer first, which opens only its own devices as
  sub-sections: 4O3A (PGXL, TGXL, Antenna Genius), RFKit / RF Power
  (RF2K-S), later SPE, Elecraft and others. Several manufacturers can be
  on at once (an RF2K-S and an Antenna Genius); the ones in use list
  first. Controls › Amp and the meter choices stay as they are, showing
  the devices in use. Devices already set up land under their maker after
  the update. Each new manufacturer the same way: the address or a
  serial-to-TCP bridge, read only first, control off by default and every
  command confirmed.
- **Elecraft KPA500 and KAT500** (asked 2026-11-05 by Sergio Landoni
  LU7YS, a tester with both on a FLEX-6600, who offers to test): status,
  operate / standby, power, tuner status, antenna and bypass, through a
  serial-to-TCP bridge at the station (ser2net on a Raspberry Pi or a
  serial-to-Ethernet adapter); both have Elecraft's documented serial
  command sets. As the RF2K-S: a stand-in from the documents first, read
  only, then control; the readings on the meters. Asked him how they are
  connected and whether he has a bridge (no answer yet). Fits as the first
  new maker of the manufacturer layout above. His answer (mail 2026-11-19
  21:08, iPhone 14 Pro, build 192, operating his FLEX-6600 from the car
  over Starlink that morning): both are on Elecraft's USB-to-serial cables
  to his Windows PC, where AetherSDR (desktop) runs them; from the phone he
  sees nothing of the amplifier (power, SWR, status, a fault); wanted: the
  KPA500's readings, Operate / Standby and above all Clear Fault, the
  KAT500's status and tuning; he will test on the real devices (a stay in
  London ahead); he also noticed that PTT is released when he switches to
  another app. Upstream (main, fetched 2026-11-19) has the whole of it:
  src/core Kpa500Connection (serial or a raw TCP serial bridge, ser2net,
  no Telnet / RFC2217), Kpa500FaultHistory, Kpa500Maintenance,
  Kat500Protocol, Kat500Maintenance, docs/architecture/
  elecraft-kpa500-kat500-design.md: so the engine can come from there over
  raw TCP, as the KPA1500's. To settle in the pre-analysis: how the serial
  ports reach the network at his station (a Raspberry Pi with ser2net and
  the two cables, or serial-to-Ethernet adapters; a COM port the desktop
  holds cannot be bridged at the same time), the Amp tab and meters for the
  KPA500 as for the KPA1500, the KAT500 as a tuner section, control off by
  default and asked first.
  His second answer (mail 2026-11-19 22:33): the bridge exists and works
  with AetherSDR's Network option: a Raspberry Pi Pico W (MicroPython, two
  MAX3232 RS-232 modules) on the station Wi-Fi gives raw TCP to the KPA500
  on port 2000 and the KAT500 on 2001 (one TCP client per port at a time,
  the TCP client before the Pico's own web panel on 8080); used remotely
  from the radio club and while travelling (Clear Fault from AetherSDR
  saved a KPA500 protection trip after high SWR, hundreds of km away). He
  also offers the Radio Club de los Andes LU8YE's two FLEX-6300s for tests.
  So the transport is settled (raw TCP, as upstream's Kpa500Connection /
  Kat500Model connectToHost); one client per port means AetherSDR on his PC
  and the phone take turns. Asked him (2026-11-19) how the phone reaches the station LAN
  when travelling (VPN?).
  *Decision (the user, 2026-11-19: "Sen verran muutosta että tehdään
  suoraan käyttöön eli ei pelkkiä lukuhommia")*: no read-only first step;
  the first build gives the KPA500's readings (power, SWR, temperature,
  voltage, current, status, faults) on the Amp tab and the meters with
  Operate / Standby and Clear Fault, and the KAT500's status (SWR, antenna,
  bypass, mode) with Tune and the antenna, behind Control in Radio Setup ›
  Amp as the other amplifiers (each asked first, none while transmitting,
  the tab showing only what the device reads back). Size: upstream's core
  is about 1 700 lines for the KPA500 (Kpa500Connection, Protocol,
  FaultHistory, Maintenance) and 1 650 for the KAT500 (Kat500Model,
  Protocol, TxController, Maintenance), with their tests; on our side the
  engine part as MobileEngineKpa (about 800 lines), Amp tab and meters,
  Radio Setup › Amp fields for both addresses, stand-ins for both devices
  from Elecraft's command references, manual and pictures: one build of
  its own, tested against the stand-ins, then on Sergio's devices.
  *Done for build 194 (2026-11-19 night, the user: "Tee build 194, PuJu")*:
  upstream's Kpa500Protocol / Connection / FaultHistory / Maintenance,
  Kat500Protocol / Maintenance / Types / Model and their seven tests from
  upstream/main 8b004e825 (all pass; the no-serial path compiled for iOS
  and Android); MobileEngineElecraft.cpp (links as the KPA1500's: read
  while on screen and connected; Control off sends no change and leaves the
  band alone; the KPA500's Operate / Standby, fault clear, fan minimum 0-6,
  band following the TX frequency (Kpa500Connection's follow, never while
  transmitting); the KAT500's antenna (one not enabled on the band refused),
  mode, bypass, fault clear and Tune as upstream's Kat500TxController:
  KPA500 to STANDBY, the radio's ATU bypassed, F, T, the TUNE carrier at
  10-100 W until FT or idle, its result read; 30 s, >110 W, OPERATE, a
  fault, Stop end it and cancel the search with CT); the meters' Amp
  readings (KPA500 after RF2K-S and KPA1500; 0-600 W, refl from fwd and
  SWR) and Tuner SWR (KAT500 without a TGXL); the logged power in OPERATE;
  Amp tab: AmpControls for the KPA500, Kat500Controls.qml; Radio Setup ›
  Amp › Elecraft sections; the devices' poll debug kept out of the log.
  Stand-ins: mobile/tools/elecraft-mock.py (both devices on raw TCP ports,
  one client each, a control FIFO, --keyed, --nomatch-on). Tested with the
  FLEX (LAN): read only (nothing changed), Control on, Operate / Standby,
  fan 2 → 3, ANT 2 / 1, Manual / Auto, bypass on / off, a KPA500 fault 07
  cleared, the band 20 m → 40 m → 20 m, Tune refused in BYPASS mode; Tune
  keyed at 10 W on 14.345 (quiet, checked first): tuned (SWR 1.05, the
  KPA500 put in STANDBY and the radio's ATU bypassed first), no match (and
  the fault cleared), Stop during the carrier (CT sent); the radio's ATU
  came back by itself on the next QSY (memories: TUNE_SUCCESSFUL), then
  14.074 DIGU, 100 W, tune power 10 W as before. Rendered at 1180x820,
  440x956, 375x667 and 700x753; the KPA1500's pictures unchanged (only the
  stand-in's numbers differ). Manual (Amp tab, Radio Setup › Amp, meters),
  pictures tab-kpa500, kat500-controls, setup-elecraft; README.

## Waiting

- **Apple (asked 2026-11-24):** the Multicast Networking entitlement for 165 (an iPhone or iPad finding the radio on its own network); the answer comes by mail to the developer account.
- **Resolved for build 194 (2026-11-19):** SmartSDR v4.3.19's Tune TX Suppression (Tune Supp per band in TX Band Settings) is the radio's own per-band `interlock band N tune_tx_suppress` (upstream #6524), which the app has had since build 158 as Radio Setup › Transmit › TX band settings › **RCA TX1-3 held off during TUNE (the radio's own)**, shown when the radio reports it; the FLEX on 4.3.16 reports it for every band (tune_tx_suppress=0). The manual now names SmartSDR's Tune Supp there.
- **Upstream (aethersdr/AetherSDR):** no longer pursued (2026-10-22); OksaSDR
  goes its own way and takes upstream changes only when useful.
- **Verified 2026-11-24:** LAN discovery on Android at the radio's own network (the
  developer at the cabin, where the radio is: Android 16, beta 118, the radio on
  SmartSDR 4.3.16).
- **Verified:** the TX start without a delay since build 68. The whoosh at the start came back with receive on a phone's speaker (Jeff Stinger, the user on Android beta 54): fixed in build 141, to verify on a device.
